# Engine reference

The engine in `engine/` as built. The reasons behind it are in [architecture](architecture.md), and the version plan is kept privately. All of it is **[Fact]**: it is checked by `engine/tests/`.

Every file path the engine takes or returns (C API, project JSON, requests) is UTF-8 text. The engine opens, creates, renames and deletes files through `src/utf8_path.h` (a `std::filesystem::path` built from the UTF-8 bytes, and `_wfopen` on Windows), so names outside ASCII work on Windows too, where a narrow `fopen` or `std::filesystem::path(std::string)` would read them in the ANSI code page; file system errors there are returned, never thrown. Returned paths use `/` on every platform. **[Assumption]** The `caw` CLI is the exception on Windows: it passes `argv` on as given, which there is in the ANSI code page, so CLI paths outside ASCII are not expected to work on Windows (untested).

## Layout

| Path | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `engine/src/` | The portable core: `detmath` (a forward to the public `include/caw/detmath.hpp`), `pattern` (note patterns), `engine` (the mixer), `component_chain` and `note_events` (native effects and instruments), `patch` and `patch_nodes` ([patches](#patches-built-not-yet-used), not yet used), `builtin_components`, `builtin_instruments` and the other `builtin_*` (the built-ins), `project`, `render` (exports), `render_stems` (song renders: mix and stems), `wav`, `json`, `capi`, `stress_scenarios` and `stress_run` (the Benchmark's projects and timed runs, [below](#the-benchmark)), `capi_stress` |
| `engine/include/caw.h` | The C API (`CAW_API_VERSION`, 44) |
| `engine/src/scan/` | The [pre-load scan](#pre-load-scan) of native libraries: its own ELF64 and PE32+ reader, findings, capability declarations, the policy, SHA-256 and the verdict cache |
| `engine/platform/` | Audio output through miniaudio, and a stand-in for builds without a device; the platform parts of `src/thread_tuning.h` (`thread_tuning_posix.cpp` for Linux and Android, `_win.cpp`, `_none.cpp` elsewhere) |
| `engine/cli/` | The `caw` tool |
| `engine/plugin/clap/` | The CLAP wrapper that exports a component library as a `.clap` plug-in, and its test host |
| `engine/plugin/vst3/` | The VST3 wrapper that exports a component library as a `.vst3` bundle, and its test host |
| `engine/tests/` | Unit tests and tolerance tests against reference renders (`tolerance.h`; the references in `references/*.cawref`) |
| `engine/bench/` | `caw_bench`, which times the engine like a phone's audio callback ([performance](performance.md)); its scenarios are `src/stress_scenarios.cpp`'s, shared with the app's Benchmark. `caw_patch_bench` times a linear [patch](#patches-built-not-yet-used) against the chain |
| `engine/third_party/miniaudio/` | miniaudio 0.11.25 (2026-03-04), unmodified, public domain or MIT-0 |
| `examples/` | The C++ starters (`examples/cpp/`) and the two example patterns the app bundles (`acid-line.caw`, `chords.caw`) |

Build and test (Linux or macOS, CMake 3.22+ and a C++20 compiler):

```
cmake -S engine -B engine/build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release
cmake --build engine/build && ctest --test-dir engine/build --output-on-failure
```

Optimised builds (Release, RelWithDebInfo, which Android Gradle's release uses, and MinSizeRel) are link-time optimised by default with Clang (the Android NDK's included) and MSVC, when CMake's `CheckIPOSupported` finds it works; not by default with GCC or MinGW, where it measured slower; never in Debug or `-DCAW_SANITIZE=ON` builds. `-DCAW_LTO=ON|OFF` overrides the default (speed phase 1, [engine performance](engine-performance.md#1-free-wins)).

`-DCAW_CLAP=ON` (off by default; it fetches the CLAP headers from GitHub) also builds the CLAP export tests: the minimal example and a test bundle as `.clap` plug-ins, and `caw_clap_tests`, a small CLAP host CTest runs ([native module SDK](native-module-sdk.md#export-as-a-clap-plug-in)). `-DCAW_VST3=ON` does the same for VST3 (it fetches the VST3 interface headers): `.vst3` bundles and `caw_vst3_tests` ([native module SDK](native-module-sdk.md#export-as-a-vst3-plug-in)).

## Patterns

The .caw module language, its compiler and VM were removed (#160, owner 2026-11-10): sound is C++ (built-in components and the user's own, [native module SDK](native-module-sdk.md)). What stays of the text format is the note pattern, the note-clip format the app edits (#158).

A project module holds a pattern (`kind: pattern`): notes, not code. A track picks one as its pattern (or plays several as clips), and the track's instrument plays its notes. Patterns are plain text, so a person or an AI agent can write one:

```
---
kind: pattern
name: Acid line
length: 16          # in 16ths, or `length: 2 bars`; without it, the whole bars its notes need
---
// at   note(s)    len   vel   extras
0       A1         2     1
3       A1         1     0.8   slide
13/3    E2         1     0.6   prob 0.5
4.5     C3,E3,G3   1/2   0.9   ratchet 2
```

- **Columns:** `at` and `len` are in 16ths and may be decimals or fractions (`13/3` lands exactly on a triplet). Notes are names (`A1`, `F#3`) or MIDI numbers, with fractions for microtones; commas make a chord. `vel` is 0–1 and optional (default 1).
- **Extras:**
  - `prob P`: the note plays with chance P. The choice comes from the project seed, the note and which repeat of the pattern this is, so every project loop plays the same way and exports match playback.
  - `ratchet N`: the note is split into N equal repeats (2–64; owner, 2026-11-19); a repeat shorter than a sample is not played, so a note sounds at most as many repeats as it lasts samples.
  - `slide`: the next note starting exactly where this one ends takes over the held note: no new note-on, the pitch glides over half a step. In a chord, sliding notes pair with the next chord's notes in written order.
  - `bend`: per-note pitch curve in semitones, written as time/value points; optional segment bend handles use `~`. A bend plus the note's vibrato depth reaches ±96 semitones (eight octaves, `kMaxBend`; #226, [decisions](decisions.md)). The engine sends a pattern note's pitch to the instrument held between MIDI −60 and half the sample rate as a MIDI note (`Engine::safePitch`: 135.08 at 48 kHz), so no oscillator is driven past Nyquist; `the_built_in_instruments_stay_finite_at_extreme_pitches`.
  - `vib`: vibrato depth, rate in Hz and optional fade-in.
  - `press`: per-note pressure/volume curve; `timbre`: per-note timbre curve. These are preserved in the pattern model and emitted as MPE expression on MIDI export.
- **Playback:**
  - The pattern sets the track's loop length; a note running past the end is released there.
  - Notes reach the instrument as note events on their exact frames; how many sound at once, and how a stolen or released note ends, is the instrument's own (each built-in's, or the user's component).
  - A tempo change releases held notes.
  - **Chasing (#226, `Engine::chaseNotes`):** where playback lands inside a note, the note sounds from there instead of waiting for the next note-on: Play and Play from a step, the loop and loop range going back, each pass of a layer that loops at its own length (and a section's jump or loop, which the app plays as those), an unmuted or woken track, and offline renders and exports (the same path, so an export equals playback; `a_chased_note_exports_as_it_plays`). The note starts at frame 0 of that chunk with its pitch, bend, vibrato, pressure and timbre as they are at that point of the note (not its start), and its expression's 16-frame control grid carries on from the note's own start, so the chased note sounds as the note played through would from there, at any block size. The instrument starts its voice there with its own attack (that is the anti-click ramp; a held note is released at the loop or pass boundary first, as before). The sampler starts the sample from its beginning (a retrigger, not an offset into the sample; [decisions](decisions.md)). It is one pass over the pattern's events before the playhead with a fixed list of at most 128 open notes (no allocation; only at those moments). Tests in `engine/tests/clip_playback_test.cpp`.
  - Notes that start at or after the pattern's length never play, and the project result warns about them.
  - **A pattern edit while a note sounds** (owner, 2026-11-10, #170): pattern text has no note ids, so `setProject` matches the old pattern's notes to the new one's on the control thread (`matchPatternNotes`, `engine/src/pattern.cpp`): first notes with the same start, length, pitch and ratchets, in order (a curve, velocity or chance edit keeps the note); then each changed note takes the edited note that overlaps it in time, nearest in pitch, then start, then length (a move, stretch or transpose that still overlaps its old place). Past 2^24 pairs of changed notes that second pass is skipped. The map goes to the audio thread with the pattern (and is retired with it). On the track's next run, a held note whose matched note still covers the playhead keeps sounding, with no new trig or note-on, and takes the new note's curve from the playhead, gliding from the pitch heard to where the new curve is over 5 ms; it then ends where the edited note ends. A note the edit deletes, or moves off the playhead, is released (its release, not a cut); a note the edit adds starts at its next start. A note's 16-frame control grid keeps its phase across the edit. Two notes at the same pitch overlapping the same place are told apart only by start and length, so a drag that swaps their order can hand a sounding note to the other one. With no edit nothing changes, so playback and export match (their renders unchanged).
- **Limits:** note collections grow dynamically; there is no fixed 4096-note parser cap. Pattern step indices use the signed 32-bit representation boundary rather than a fixed 4096-step product cap; note-expression curves are dynamically sized. Ratchets remain limited to 8. Errors give a line and column. Kit rows (`[kick]` lines) are reserved for kit patterns and are an error for now.

### MIDI files

A pattern can be imported from, and exported to, MIDI files for use in Reaper and other DAWs (code in `engine/src/midifile.cpp`).

- **Import:** `caw midi import IN.mid -o OUT.caw [--track N] [--channel C] [--name NAME] [--no-bend] [--no-slides]`, or `caw_pattern_from_midi(data, size)`, which returns `{"ok","error","pattern"}`.
  - It reads Standard MIDI Files (format 0 and 1) and MIDI 2.0 Clip Files (`.midi2`). All tracks and channels are merged unless one is chosen.
  - Times come from ticks, so tempo changes don't move notes. Whole steps are written as numbers, small fractions exactly (`13/3`), and anything else to 1/1000 step.
  - Notes with the same start, length and velocity become one chord line. Velocity is vel/127, or vel/65535 for a clip file.
  - The pattern is the whole bars of the file's first time signature (4/4 without one).
  - A pitch bend sounding at a note's start becomes a microtonal pitch, assuming ±2 semitones.
  - A note that overlaps the next one by at most 1/8 step becomes a `slide`, so ordinary legato playing comes in as slides. `--no-slides` turns that off.
  - Limits: 16 MB and 65536 events; notes and steps are bounded only by the signed 32-bit step representation (a caller may pass a lower note allowance). Format 2, SMPTE-timed and malformed files give an error.
  - **Tempo and meter (API 31):** `caw_pattern_from_midi` also returns `tempo_map` (every tempo event of every track, or a clip's Set Tempo, as stepped points; 120 BPM until the first; BPM held to 1–999) and `time_signatures` (4/4 until the first; a change off a bar line, or a denominator over 32, is left out), in the project JSON's shape. More than 4096 tempo changes or 1024 signatures is an error.
- **Export:** `caw midi export PATTERN.caw -o OUT.mid|OUT.midi2 [--bpm B] [--beats N] [--clip]`, or `caw_pattern_export(source, bpm, beats_per_bar, CAW_MIDI_SMF|CAW_MIDI_CLIP, path, error, len)`.
  - Both formats use 960 ticks per quarter and carry the tempo, the meter and the pattern's name.
  - **With a tempo map (API 31):** `caw_pattern_export_song(source, points, n, sigs, n, format, path, error, len)` writes a tempo event at each point and a meter event at each signature (`MidiSongMeta`, `patternToSmf` / `patternToMidiClip`). MIDI files have no ramps, so a ramp becomes a tempo event every 1/16 note (at most 1024 a ramp), each at the tempo that keeps its stretch's exact length; a `.mid` tempo holds at most 0xFFFFFF µs a quarter (about 3.6 BPM). An MPE export's vibrato runs at the tempo where its note starts. Changes at or after the pattern's end are left out. A tempo the format can't hold (under about 3.58 BPM in a .mid, 1.4 BPM in a clip file) or a .mid pattern longer than its 28-bit delta times (1,118,481 steps) is refused with an error (`checkMidiExport`, also on `caw_pattern_export` and the CLI). The one-tempo export writes the same bytes as before (tested).
  - Ratchets are written as their repeats, every note is written whatever its `prob`, and a slide is a legato overlap of 1/16 step. Notes are cut at the pattern's length, as in playback.
  - `.mid` is a format 1 file. Microtonal notes round to the nearest note and play on a channel of their own (2–9, 11–16) with a pitch bend; RPN 0 sets the bend range to ±2 semitones. If the pattern has note expressions (bend, vibrato, pressure or timbre), export uses an MPE lower zone with per-note pitch bend, channel pressure and CC74 timbre; each member channel's bend range (RPN 0) is the narrowest of 12, 48 and 96 semitones that holds the pattern's widest bend plus vibrato (#226).
  - `.midi2` follows the MIDI Clip File specification (M2-116-U, read 2026-10-28): MIDI 2.0 notes with 16-bit velocity, and microtones through the Note On Pitch 7.9 attribute.
    - **[Assumption]** Some layout choices aren't fixed by the spec. Everything is on group 0, channel 1. Tempo, time signature and clip name sit right after Start of Clip. Note Off has velocity 0x8000.
    - **[Assumption]** Which DAWs read clip files is unverified ([architecture](architecture.md#variables-and-patterns-proposal)).

## Playback

- **Project:** BPM, beats per bar, loop length in bars, seed and master volume, and a tempo map and time signatures (below). The project loop is the song step it ends on as a sample on the tempo map (`round(steps × 60 × sr / (bpm × 4))` with one tempo), and every layer restarts when it wraps.
- **Tempo map and time signatures (batch 1.0.3, #207, C API 31; `src/tempo_map.h`):**
  - **Units.** Positions are beats (quarter notes) from the song start (1 beat = 4 steps); tempo is BPM in quarter notes per minute. The project JSON's `tempo_map` (`[{"beat", "bpm", "ramp"}]`: 1–4096 points, BPM 1–999, the first at beat 0; its BPM becomes `bpm`) and `time_signatures` (`[{"bar", "beat", "num", "den"}]`: 1–1024, `num` 1–32, `den` 1/2/4/8/16/32, the first at bar 0; `beat` is recomputed from the bars and must agree within 1e-6) are checked and fail closed with an error that names the problem. Without them a project is one tempo (`bpm`) and one `beats_per_bar`/4 signature.
  - **Maths.** A segment runs from a point to the next at that point's tempo, or, when the next point has `ramp`, linearly in BPM over beats to it. Closed forms: a constant stretch takes 60 / bpm s a beat; a ramp from bpm0 with slope k BPM a beat reaches d beats in `60 / k × log1p(k × d / bpm0)` s, inverted by `bpm0 / k × expm1(k × t / 60)`. `tempo_map.cpp` is built strict, its functions out of line, as `sample_time.cpp` is, and its `log1p` and `expm1` are its own (`tempomath`: series and frexp/ldexp, within a few ulp; the C libraries' differ between glibc, bionic and MSVC), pinned to their bits in `the_ramp_maths_gives_the_same_bits_on_every_platform`, so ramps and their rounding ties are the same on every platform. Points that never change the tempo merge, so a single-tempo map is one segment and gives `step × 60 × sr / (bpm × 4)` to the bit: older projects render bit-identically (the goldens and references are unchanged, and `one_tempo_projects_render_the_same_bits_with_or_without_a_map` checks it).
  - **What follows the map** (each a sample `floor(frameAt(step) + 0.5)`): the loop and loop range, Play from a step, every layer's pass (pass k of an n-step layer starts on song step k × n, its notes on song step k × n + at), note expression lengths (each sub-note's own span on the map) and slide glides (samples per step where the note is), track volume and pan lanes and component lanes (the song step at each sample, worked out once a chunk with one segment lookup, `TempoMap::stepsAt`: on a dense-automation song, 16 tracks with volume, pan and component lanes, a ramped map cost 106 ms per second of audio before and 86 after, against 80–88 for one tempo; Linux x64 VM, GCC 13 Release, 2026-11-14), the components' transport (the tempo, beat and the signature's beats per bar at each block's first sample, so the synced delays, choruses, gates, the Flagship's synced LFOs and the project's synced modulators follow the tempo at the playhead), audio clips (`audio_clips.cpp`, through the map the Engine hands its `LiveAudio`), offline renders and stems (`stepFrame`, tails), the one-shot render, and recording (a request's optional `tempo_map`, else the Engine's own when it changes tempo, sets `start_steps` and the punch points; while recording the map holds: the live setters refuse, and a project update keeps the map with a warning). An audio clip that follows the tempo (musical) follows the map, its rate worked out per 64-frame chunk (API 32, [Audio clips](#audio-clips)); a fixed one plays at the file's speed, keeping its seconds when the app sends its `length_steps` from the map.
  - **Metronome and count-in.** With one tempo and one `beats_per_bar`/4 signature the metronome clicks as before. Otherwise it clicks on each tick of the signature (a 1/`den` note), accented on each bar, on the first sample at or after the tick's exact time (worked out per chunk). The count-in ticks at the signature and tempo where playback starts (1/`den` notes, accented every `num`); `caw_engine_play_count_in_bars` counts whole bars of them, so it starts on an accent (the app sends bars).
  - **Real time.** The control thread builds a `SongTime` (map and signatures, with a serial) for each project update (`Transport`) and each `caw_engine_set_tempo_map` / `_set_time_signatures` / `_set_bpm` (`SetSong`); the audio thread swaps it in from the command queue and hands the old one back to be freed (`tempo_map_swaps_and_playback_on_the_map_never_allocate`). A change of tempo ends held notes, as a BPM change did. While playing, a change from one tempo to another keeps the sample position (as before the map); any change involving a map keeps the beat (floored to a sample, and a playhead inside the loop range stays inside it). The C API's settings last until the next project update, which sets the project's map again.
  - **Position.** `caw_engine_position` gives the transport's sample, beat, seconds, the tempo there, and the 0-based bar, beat in the bar and signature, on the map the audio thread plays: it publishes them at the end of each callback behind a sequence lock (a map sent but not yet played doesn't show).
  - **Shared vectors.** `engine/tests/references/tempo_vectors.json`, which the app's tests read too: `tempo` (five maps: constant, stepped, the contract's 120→96 ramp, a ramp up, mixed) with `beat_seconds` pairs from the engine's `TempoMap`, and `bars` (three signature lists) with `bar_beat` rows `[beat, bar (0-based), beat in the bar in the signature's own beats]` from `TimeSigMap`; both sides agree within 1e-9. `caw_tests --update-references tempo_vectors_shared_with_the_app_match_the_engine` rewrites the file.
- **Loop range** (REAPER's loop points, 2026-11-10): with `loop_range` on, playback that reaches `loop_end_steps` goes back to `loop_start_steps` (both rounded to samples like the loop), every layer restarting there as at the song's end. Played from after the range, the song plays to its end and then goes back to the range's start. Off (the default, and every older project), the whole song loops.
- **Tracks:** any number, up to what the Engine's memory budget allows (`maxLayers()`). A track is a native C++ instrument (`instrument`) playing its pattern or clips, then its native effects (`components`); a layer without an instrument plays nothing and takes no slot. A track loops at its pattern's length in 16ths, or its own `length_steps`; 0 means it follows the project loop. Each loop start falls at `round(k × length)`, so a 3-bar pattern against a 4-bar loop stays in time.
- **Notes:** notes start and end on the exact sample (`round(at × samples per 16th)`). At a pattern restart, held notes are released, but the instrument and its effects carry on, so tails ring across it. Editing a pattern swaps it in at the next block without rebuilding the sound. A note that is already sounding follows the edit from the playhead, gliding onto its new curve, or is released if the edit deleted it or moved it off the playhead ([Patterns](#patterns)).
- **Chain changes:** a new instrument, or a changed list of effects, is built and activated on the control thread, then crossfaded in over 20 ms while playing (held notes move to a new instrument); new values for the same components ramp over each parameter's smoothing, without a rebuild.
- **Play costs about one callback (A3, 2026-11-13):** a chain's first-time work happens when it is built, on the control thread: an instrument of built-ins is reset there (one with a loaded component is still reset on the audio thread, since the native ABI does not say reset may run on another thread) and its components' values are resolved there, a reset with nothing run since is a no-op (`ComponentChain::reset`), and a block where only the play state changed keeps the values of a component no lane drives (a Play still ends knob overrides). So the first callback after Play no longer clears every drum voice twice or resolves every parameter ([engine performance](engine-performance.md#a3-the-first-callback-after-play)); `startPlay` and the stopped monitoring scan visit only the slots in use (A4, #133).
- **One node per track:** the mix graph runs each track as one node (its instrument, the sends into it, its inserts, volume, pan, sends and latency delay), each folder bus after what feeds it, then the master: two nodes per slot. A layer's audio goes to its output bus (its `output`, else its folder's bus, else the master): the graph's one edge per slot. A send or key is pulled (speed phase 5a, 2026-11-12): the source writes each route into a buffer of its own route table, and the destination (a bus, or since API 36 a track, summed after its instrument and before its inserts) sums its routes when it runs (`pullRoutes`), in a fixed order (its sources in the order they run, each one's routes by index), so the float sums keep their bits. A feedback send (API 36) orders nothing: its source writes a ring of two chunks (`FeedbackRing`) at its frames on the route clock (`routeFrame_`, every frame the mixer has run) and its destination reads the frames 64 before, so it is exactly one chunk late at any device buffer size, whichever of the two runs first or both at once on the mix pool; the cycle fallback in `updateRouting` stays a safety net for a loop the control thread never sends. Each node has its own scratch, the project modulation's per-chain output is per slot, and what a node hands back waits in its own list until the graph has run (`flushRetires`). The executor sorts the order into dependency levels (each send and key a dependency, the master alone and last; phase 5b) and runs them level by level. Since phase 5c the nodes of a level can run at once on the mix pool (`src/mix_pool.*`, worker threads with the callback thread joining in; the output is the same bits as on one thread): the platform starts it with the device (`startLiveMixPool`; `MixPool::defaultWorkers()`: the fast cores less the callback's, at most cores − 2 on 4 or fewer cores, at most 4; used once the measured load stays above 0.4, with fewer workers or none while the callback waits on them more than they work), offline renders use it unless a device runs (checked every callback), and a node holding a third-party native component stays on the callback thread. Since phase 5d the live pool's workers tune themselves to the callback thread (`src/thread_tuning.h`: priority never above the callback's, the fast cores of a hybrid CPU, MMCSS on Windows, an Android performance hint; see "Audio threads" below and [engine performance](engine-performance.md#5-parallel-mixer)).
- **Mix:** each layer has volume, constant-power pan, mute and solo, smoothed over 5 ms. Track and master volume are linear gains from 0 to +12 dB (3.981, `kMaxMixGain`; owner, 2026-11-10: faders like REAPER's), in the project JSON (`volume`, `master`) and the live controls alike; more is held at +12 dB. Volume automation lanes reach the same +12 dB (API version 7; before it they stopped at 0 dB), so a lane written from a fader keeps its level. The master chain is master volume, then a 5 Hz DC blocker, then a safety limiter with a ceiling of −0.2 dBFS, always last.
- **The live output's last stage (API 35, #244; `src/output_safety.h`):** after `Engine::process`, in the device callback only (`DeviceRender`, `src/device_render.h`, the one path `platform/audio_miniaudio.cpp`'s output callback takes), two optional steps that renders, exports, freezes and stems never run: the **mono check** (both sides become (L + R) / 2) and the **safety limiter** for ears and speakers, on by default. The limiter is stereo-linked and has no look-ahead, so it adds no latency and the latency figures and the loopback ping are unchanged: its envelope follows the louder side's peak at once and falls back over 150 ms; under the knee at -4 dBFS the gain is exactly 1 (a quiet mix passes bit for bit), above it the level bends along k + (c - k)(1 - e^(-(e - k)/(c - k))) toward the -1 dBFS ceiling c, which it never reaches, and a non-finite sample is silenced. It runs before the latency ping is mixed in (`LiveAudio::afterCallback`). It sits after the master stage's own -0.2 dBFS limiter, which every render keeps, so golden renders and exports are unchanged; live and export then differ only when the live output goes over -4 dBFS with the limiter on, or with the mono check on, which the owner chose (2026-11-18, [decisions](decisions.md)). It allocates and locks nothing, and costs a few operations a sample (`caw_rt_tests`: `output_safety_rt_test.cpp`).
- **Overload, and coming back (shedding recovers on its own since 2026-11-12, the owner's Bluetooth report):** the host reports how much of each audio callback was used, how many frames it covered and how many the engine rendered for it (`reportLoad(load, frames, rendered)`, `engine.cpp`; through the device FIFO a callback renders more or fewer frames than it plays, so the meter counts the load per frame rendered and the FIFO's alternating render sizes are not taken for overload; a callback counts as over for shedding when that load passes 85% or when it missed its own played frames' deadline (its raw load reaching 1: a 256-frame callback rendering 320 frames at its whole budget reads 0.8 a frame), `a_callback_that_misses_its_played_deadline_counts_toward_shedding`). Everything goes by time, so every callback size behaves the same. The CPU meter is an average that keeps 0.9 of itself per 10.7 ms. **Shedding:** the share of the last ~0.25 s spent in callbacks over 85% of their budget (an average with a 0.25 s time constant, weighted by each callback's length) passing 70%, about 0.3 s of nearly all-over callbacks, mutes the newest audible layer (faded as a mute is), flags it in the meters, and starts 1.07 s before another could go. One late callback, however late (the first after Play, a device restart or reroute, a background burst), counts only its own length, so a single spike sheds nothing; before, a stall could push the average over 85% for 0.21 s by itself. **Coming back:** with the meter under 60% for 2 s, the newest shed layer returns (fading in), if the meter now plus what that layer was seen to cost (the meter's drop in the 1.07 s after it was shed, at most an equal share of the load then, so a load that fell for another reason doesn't make it look too costly ever to return) stays under 75%, or else on trial after 8 s more of quiet (a cost seen while the device was throttled reads high, and a song shed to silence must not stay silent); then another 2 s for the next. The hysteresis (shed over 85%, back only under 60% and only to under 75%) keeps a returning layer from crossing 85% again, and a layer shed again within 10 s of coming back waits twice as long each time (2, 4 ... 32 s, plus 8 s on trial), so a layer the device can only just not carry comes back on trial a few times a minute at first and then about every 40 s, not every 2.3 s. **Reset:** Unmute (`caw_engine_clear_overload`), the app going to or coming back from the background (`AppState.lifecycle`), and every device open (`requestSheddingReset`, an atomic the next callback reads) bring every shed layer back and forget the load history (every per-callback average, the CPU meter included), so a throttled stretch never thins the song for good. **A new device's warm-up (#234, 2026-11-17):** every device open and AAudio reroute also asks for a warm-up (`requestDeviceWarmUp`, an atomic): the callbacks of the device's first `kDeviceWarmSeconds` (0.5 s) of frames enter no average (the CPU meter, the over-budget share, Auto's), though the performance hint still gets their times. They are the stream starting, not the song: a shared or Bluetooth stream fills its buffer back to back at first on a core whose clock has not risen, and since the app closes an idle device and Play reopens it, they fell while playing. The meter moves about 9.9 × a stall's seconds (its weight per callback is 1 − 0.9^(length ÷ 10.7 ms)), so 0.2 s of frames at 9 times their budget read 780% (`a_new_devices_first_callbacks_stay_out_of_the_cpu_meter`). **[Assumption]** 0.5 s covers a stream's first fill and the clock's rise; a load still over after it sheds as before. An engine that has rendered offline (`setProjectOffline`) never sheds. A removed layer, track or folder bus, stops counting as shed when its slot frees. **With the mix pool** (speed phase 5, merged 2026-11-13): `reportLoad` takes the pool's times first, before every early return (no frames, an offline engine, a shed), so the pool sees every callback: the callback's wait for workers and the workers' task time (`MixPool::takeTimes`), and the callback's real time against the frames it played for the performance hint (`reportWork`: the device's deadline). Auto's decision follows, on the load per frame rendered, as the meter is, so the FIFO's alternating render sizes don't swing it. While Auto turns the pool on, its first callbacks' cost is the pool starting, not the song: for `kMixSettleSeconds` (0.7 s: one of Auto's worker steps, `kMixWaitSeconds`, plus 0.2 s, so a pool that costs more than it gives can shrink first) they don't count toward the over-budget share, nothing is shed and Auto adds no worker, and a load still over after that sheds as before (`mix_pool_auto_turning_on_holds_shedding_off_while_it_settles`). An overload while the callback waits on the workers (Auto's wait share over `kMixWaitShare`) turns Auto off first, for `kMixRetrySeconds`, and the over-budget share starts again, so only a song still over on one thread loses a layer (`mix_pool_overload_while_waiting_on_workers_turns_auto_off_before_shedding`). **Tail guard** (Claude's choice, 2026-11-13, for the owner to confirm): `reportLoad` also keeps the share of callback time that missed its played frames' deadline (raw load 1 or more, `kMixTailLoad`; not shedding's 0.85, which a busy pool's p99 can sit near with nothing missed), a bias-corrected 2 s average (`kMixTailSeconds`), separately for callbacks that used the pool and for those that ran serial (before Auto turned on, during its retry waits, and levels with one busy node); with 2 s of pooled samples, a pooled share over the serial one by more than 0.02 (`kMixTailMargin`) for 1 s (`kMixTailHoldSeconds`) turns Auto off for `kMixRetrySeconds`, as the wait rule does, so a worker the system preempts now and then (rare late callbacks the mean wait hides) can't keep the pool on (`mix_pool_auto_goes_serial_when_the_pool_makes_late_callbacks`; `mixTailFallbacks` counts it, `caw_bench --mode auto` says so). A shedding reset also clears Auto's wait and work averages, its over and under times and the pooled late share (the serial one is kept), and with Auto still on it settles again; a new live device (`startLiveMixPool`, a reopen) starts Auto on one thread (`mix_pool_a_new_live_device_starts_auto_serial_again`). `caw_engine_shedding_stats` (API 27) counts the layers shed (the telemetry's `dropouts`), those that came back on their own, and those shed now (`CawMeters.overloaded`, which now falls as layers return). The per-callback cost outside the layers is small at the smallest periods ([Fact, measured 2026-11-11, x86-64 Release]: 0.011 µs a callback stopped, 0.175 µs with one layer playing at 16 frames against 4.3 µs at 512). Tests: `overload_shedding_goes_by_time_not_callbacks`, `shed_layers_come_back_on_their_own_once_the_load_drops`, `a_layer_the_device_can_only_just_not_carry_does_not_come_and_go`, `a_single_late_callback_sheds_nothing`, `an_offline_render_never_sheds_and_a_shedding_reset_brings_everything_back`, `odd_callback_sizes_through_the_device_fifo_shed_nothing_under_the_threshold`, `a_shed_folder_bus_that_is_removed_is_no_longer_counted_shed`, `shedding_and_its_recovery_never_allocate_on_the_audio_thread`.
- **Threads:** the control thread parses patterns, builds chains and sends messages to the audio thread through a lock-free queue. The audio thread hands what it replaced back the same way, so it never allocates, locks or frees. Parameter and mix values are atomics.
- **Determinism:** output must be perceptually identical, not bit-identical, across platforms, live against export and runs; timing, seeded choices and discrete behaviour stay exact (owner, 2026-11-12, [decisions](decisions.md)).
  - The output doesn't depend on the device's buffer size, and a second engine in the same process gives the same bits (both tested under the build's flags).
  - **Sample positions are strict:** every step or second the engine turns into a sample index goes through `src/sample_time.h` (samples per step, ratchet repeat steps, `floor(x × scale + 0.5)`, layer passes, clip shifts, the metronome's beat offset), and `src/sample_time.cpp` is built with the strict flags in every build, its functions out of line (`noinline`), so neither inlining nor link-time optimisation merges them into fast code. Without it Arm64 fused `at + part × sub` into one rounding and put a ratchet repeat on a half sample one sample away from x86-64 and MSVC (44.1 kHz, 60 BPM, a note at step 2, length 1, ratchet 6: repeat 5 at 31237 strict, 31238 fused). The callers: Engine timing, loop range, play-from, layer passes, note spans and event lookup, pattern ratchet events, arrangement clips, audio clips (start, end, source offset), punch in/out, render and stem ranges and tails (one `stepFrame`, `render.h`), MIDI export's ratchet ticks, the creative effects' song-beat frame (`StepClock`), the note-FX fades, the preview glide, the play tail, the dynamics look-ahead and the test signal's period. Tests: `sample_timing_test.cpp` (ratchets at awkward rates and tempos against a strict oracle, `tests/timing_oracle.cpp`; fails on an Arm64 FMA build without the helper), a sweep of the helpers, MIDI export's note-on ticks against the strict step and the song-beat frame against the strict formula.
  - **Floating-point flags (speed phase 2, 2026-11-12):** the engine's own targets (`caw_core`, `caw_engine`, the CLI, tests, bench) build with `CAW_FP_FLAGS` = `-ffp-contract=fast -fno-math-errno -fno-trapping-math -fno-signed-zeros -fassociative-math` on GCC and Clang (the Android NDK's), and the same set through `/clang:` on clang-cl (the Windows engine since 2026-11-13, with `/clang:-O3`; a strict file negates each one, since `/fp:precise` alone does not); `-DCAW_FP_FLAGS=...` overrides it. Never `-ffinite-math-only`: the mix's NaN/Inf guards (`route_math.h`, `finiteOr0`, the limiter) stay, and `fp_mode_test.cpp` feeds NaN and ±Inf from a track (the test bundle's `poison` component) and checks the mix stays finite and audible; a full `-ffast-math` build fails it. `-freciprocal-math` is left out (no gain). Fused multiply-adds happen on Arm64 only: the x86-64 baseline (SSE2) has no FMA and is not raised, and there is no runtime dispatch: x86-64-v3 measured 15–29% faster with GCC but no faster with MSVC, then the compiler of the shipped x86-64 build; clang-cl, shipped since, is not yet measured there (A5, [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a5-cpu-features)). MSVC's cl, still buildable, keeps `/fp:precise`: `/fp:fast`'s NaN handling is unchecked. Measured gains are in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#2-floating-point).
  - **`CAW::SDK` stays strict:** a component built against it gets `-ffp-contract=off -fno-fast-math -fno-trapping-math` (`/fp:precise`), so the Library gives the same bits in a component on every platform. The SDK's interface flags skip targets with the `CAW_ENGINE_FP_FLAGS` property (otherwise, coming last on the command line, they would override `CAW_FP_FLAGS`). The Library's exact bits and signed zeros, as components get them, are tested in their own executable, `caw_exact_tests` (`library_kernels_test.cpp`, `library_exact_test.cpp`): it builds with `CAW::SDK`'s strict flags and links nothing of `caw_core`, so `caw_tests`, `caw_rt_tests` and the CLAP and VST3 host tests (`caw_clap_tests`, `caw_vst3_tests`) hold only code built with the engine's flags (an inline function compiled both ways in one program leaves the linker one copy for every caller). Their one strict file, `tests/timing_oracle.cpp`, includes no engine or SDK header. Fast-built tests read sign bits from the bits (GCC folds `std::signbit(x)` to `x < 0` under `-fno-signed-zeros`) and keep expected sums in order through a `volatile` (Clang vectorized a 8192-term one in another order).
  - **Code the flags would break:** `detmath.hpp`'s Cody-Waite range reduction (`detail::reduce`, in `detSin` and `detExp`) is kept as written: `#pragma clang fp reassociate(off)` for Clang, an empty asm holding the partial result for GCC; reassociated, it doubled the sine's error at every |x| and put `detExp` 1e-13 off (`fp_mode_test.cpp` checks both under the build's flags). With FMA (Arm64) the reduction is exact. `positiveZero` (`detmath.hpp`) turns −0 into +0 in its bits, as `lib::wrap01` needs, where `x + 0.0` would be folded away.
  - **Flush-to-zero:** every `Engine::process` call (live callbacks, every export and render, the bench, the C API), every sample-pack render and the stem render's loop (its stems' master chains and loudness meters) holds `ScopedFlushDenormals` (`src/fp_mode.h`): x86 MXCSR FTZ and DAZ (FTZ only on 32-bit x86), Arm64 FPCR.FZ (float and double); it clears only the bits it set when it ends, so a host's thread keeps its mode and its exception flags. **[Assumption]** every x86-64 CPU supports DAZ. Each control-register access is a volatile asm with a memory clobber (MSVC: the intrinsics between compiler barriers), so work on values loaded after the guard starts and stored before it ends runs flushed; a value kept in a register across either end needs `ScopedFlushDenormals::fence(v)` (GCC otherwise moved a register-only multiply before `msr fpcr`). Other targets (32-bit Arm, MSVC on Arm64) keep their mode. The component test suite (`caw test-component`) renders without it on purpose, so its "denormals" warning still shows a component's own subnormals (what a CLAP or VST3 host that doesn't flush would get). The kernels' own denormal guards stay: they settle state at exact 0 far above 1e-38 (which lets silent nodes sleep), and they protect components and plug-in exports outside the engine.
  - The built-ins use the Library's own `sin`, `exp` and `log` (`caw/detmath.hpp`); per-sample tanh curves (saturator, delay feedback, drum drives, filter drives, distortion) use `lib::tanhApprox` and Flagship's voice filters `setApprox` (speed phase 3, 2026-11-12, [engine-performance](engine-performance.md)), and control-rate exps, pows and sin/cos are worked out again only when their inputs move. Block-split bit tests (`note_fx_test.cpp`) hold the modulation path to `denormalizeParameter`'s bits, so `denormalizeOffsets` hoists the curve's log but keeps `detExp`.
  - Tolerance tests against reference renders hold the sound, not the bits ([Tests](#tests), "Reference renders"); every reference passes unchanged with the flags above on x86-64 GCC 13 and Clang 18 and on Arm64 GCC 13 (FMA; run under qemu, 2026-11-12). **[Fact, 2026-11-12, x86-64 GCC 13 Release]** the earlier probes (`-mfma -ffp-contract=fast`; `-ffast-math -fno-finite-math-only -mfma`) also null below −129.7 dBFS.

## Project file (JSON)

The app, the CLI and exports share one format:

```json
{ "caw_project": 1, "name": "Night Glitch", "bpm": 140, "beats_per_bar": 4, "loop_bars": 4,
  "seed": 7, "master": 0.7,
  "automation": [
    { "target_type": "instrument", "layer": "l1", "target": "flt1_cutoff",
      "points": [ { "step": 0, "value": 900 }, { "step": 64, "value": 2400 } ] }
  ],
  "modules": [ { "id": "p1", "name": "Riff", "source": "---\nkind: pattern\n---\n0 A2 2 1\n" } ],
  "layers": [
    { "id": "g1", "name": "Group", "folder": true, "bus": true,
      "volume": 0.9, "pan": 0, "mute": false, "solo": false },
    { "id": "l1", "name": "Lead", "parent": "g1",
      "instrument": { "id": "org.caw.synth.flagship", "params": { "flt1_cutoff": 900 } },
      "components": [ { "id": "org.caw.delay.pingpong", "params": { "mix": 0.3 } } ],
      "clips": [ { "id": "c1", "pattern": "p1", "start_steps": 0, "length_steps": 16,
                  "source_offset_steps": 0, "loop": true } ],
      "volume": 0.8, "pan": 0, "mute": false, "solo": false,
      "automation": [ { "target": "volume", "points": [ { "step": 0, "value": 0.8 }, { "step": 64, "value": 0.4 } ] } ] }
  ],
  "audition": { "instrument": "org.caw.synth.flagship", "pattern": "p1", "length_steps": 0 }, "audition_only": false }
```

- Optional project-level `automation` lanes target a component's or instrument's parameter: `target_type` `"component"` (with the `layer`, empty for the master chain, and the chain `component` index) or `"instrument"` (with the `layer`), and the parameter `target`. Each lane has song-time `points` with `step` and `value`, joined by each point's curve to the next (below), holding its first and last value outside its range; the project loop extends far enough to include the latest arrangement or automation point. Lanes of other types (the old `param` and `global`) are read but drive nothing.
- `modules` are patterns: `{"id", "name", "source"}`. The result has one entry per module: `ok`, `kind` (`pattern`), `error`, `line` and `col` when it doesn't parse (it keeps its last good version playing), the pattern's `name`, `summary`, `notes`, `length_steps`, `length_bars`, `last_end` and `warnings`. A module that isn't a pattern (an old .caw sound module) fails there with line 1.
- A layer's `pattern` names one pattern module; its notes play the instrument, and its length replaces `length_steps`.
- A layer's `instrument` (a component entry, as in `components`) is the native C++ instrument that plays its notes (see the C API's native components, below). Old keys the engine no longer reads: a layer's `chain`, the project's `globals`, `voices`, `swap`, `seam_crossfade`, `key_root` and `key_scale`.
- An arrangement layer can instead contain \`clips\`. Each clip references a reusable pattern source and has \`start_steps\`, \`length_steps\`, \`source_offset_steps\`, \`loop\`, an optional \`loop_steps\` and an optional \`mute\` (a muted clip plays nothing, while its layer still follows the arrangement instead of running free). \`loop_steps\` is the clip's own loop point (owner, 2026-11-11, REAPER-like): the clip repeats its source from the offset every \`loop_steps\` steps, so notes past it never play and a loop past the pattern's end plays silence up to it; 0 or absent loops at the pattern's length, as before (\`compileArrangement\`, off the audio thread; \`arrangement_clip_loop_steps_repeat_the_source_at_the_clip_loop_point\`). A clip whose length is not a whole number of repeats plays its last repeat's own start, cut at the clip's end (\`a_clip_longer_than_its_repeat_plays_the_repeat_s_start_cut_at_its_end\`: 4 bars in 5 and 3 in 8). A note the clip cuts (at its end, its loop point or its source offset) keeps its expression where it was: it plays the part of its bend, vibrato, pressure and timbre curves that its kept span had in the written note (\`NoteExpression::from\` and \`span\`, set there; the text never sets them), not the whole curve squeezed into what is left (#226: a four-bar bent note repeating every four bars in a five-bar clip played all four bars' bend in bar 5; \`a_bent_note_cut_at_a_clip_s_end_keeps_its_bend_where_it_was\`). The engine has no clip automation: the app unrolls a clip's lanes into the layer's and the project's lanes (see [App](app.md)). Clip instances may overlap; all of their notes play the track's one instrument and chain.
- The control thread compiles clips into a sorted absolute-time event stream when the project changes. The audio callback does no pattern parsing or timeline allocation: it splits its current block at note boundaries and renders the events at their sample positions. The scheduler follows project transport and loop boundaries; it does not depend on Flutter timers.
- Folder layers may set `folder: true`, `bus: true|false` and `parent`. A parent must be an existing folder; cycles and invalid parents are rejected. Bus folders sum their descendants' stereo output once, then apply the folder's volume and pan before routing to an ancestor bus or master. Organization-only folders do not process audio. A bus folder runs its own `components` (a reverb bus) on that sum, before its volume and pan; an organization-only folder takes none.
- **Sends (#175, API 9):** a layer's `sends` is a list of at most 8 `{"to": bus folder or track id, "level": linear gain 0..+12 dB, "pre": bool, "mute": bool, "feedback": bool, "limiter": bool}`, a copy of the layer's sound into a bus folder's input beside its normal route, or (API 36) into a track's, summed after the track's instrument and before its inserts (the track keeps its instrument). A "return" is a bus (or track) fed by sends; there is no other kind. Post-fader (the default) taps after volume and pan, pre-fader after the layer's effects and before them; a muted or soloed-out layer sends nothing either way. A bus folder can send too. `caw_engine_set_send(engine, layer, index, level, pre, mute)` changes send `index` while it plays (ramped, no project update); a switch between pre and post fades the send to silence, changes tap and fades back (5 ms each way), and a send pointed at a different bus starts there from silence, with none of the old bus's audio still in its delay line. Soloing a layer keeps open every bus it is heard through: its sends' buses and theirs, and the buses above them. A send into a track opens that track the same way (owner, 2026-11-18: as in REAPER), so soloing a layer that sends into track B makes B audible, B's own instrument included.
- **Outputs (API 36):** a layer's optional `output` names where its audio goes: an enabled bus folder's id (any one, not only an ancestor), `"@master"` (straight to the master even inside a bus folder; layer ids never start with `@`, and a layer with the id `@master` is refused), or absent/`""` for the folder's bus as before (its nearest ancestor folder with a bus, else the master). `parent` keeps meaning the folder it shows in, so soloing or muting a folder still reaches the layers shown in it. An output to a missing layer, to anything but an enabled bus folder, to itself, or from an organization-only folder is refused.
- **Feedback sends (API 36):** `"feedback": true` (default false) is the only way a route may close a loop (a bus into itself, two buses into each other, a track back into a track that feeds it). The send reads its source's output from one 64-frame chunk before (exactly 64 frames, counted from the frames the mixer has run, so the output never depends on the device's buffer size) and runs in no order with its source. `"limiter"` (default true; read on a feedback send only) passes the return through a DC block (about 10 Hz) and a soft clip (unity below 0.5, a tanh knee that never reaches 0 dBFS), so a loop at any gain stays bounded; false turns both off, and a loop above unity then runs away until its return reaches ±16 (+24 dBFS), where it is held (never near ±FLT_MAX, which could poison the inserts after it). Stop clears every feedback send (a stopped preview never brings back a loop from before the stop), and so does each Play. Each Play starts a feedback send from silence, as an export does. A send turned into a feedback send (or back) while it plays fades in from silence, as a send pointed elsewhere does. The DC block is tuned to the sample rate, so a new device rate rebuilds the send's table. `caw_engine_set_send` moves a feedback send's level, tap and mute like any send's; a project update that changes its limiter or destination rebuilds its route table, and the ring carries on.
- **Samples (API 11):** a component entry may name `"samples"`: at most 256 `{"slot": n, "path": file}` or `{"slot": n, "builtin": name}`. The engine decodes them when it builds or updates the chain (the control thread; the audio thread only takes the new buffer), and keeps a slot whose source didn't change. Only a component with sample slots takes them (`org.caw.drums`: slot n is pad n, A1 = 0); a file that fails to load empties its slot and the project still loads. Limits and the decoder: [Drums, samples](#samples).
- **Sidechain keys (#175, API 9):** a component entry may name `"key": {"layer": id, "tap": "pre" | "post", "level": gain}`: that layer's sound (after its effects, before or after its fader) feeds the component's sidechain input (a post key is the pre tap times the fader's own volume and pan, automated ones included, whether the layer is muted, soloed out or its fader still ramping, so muting or unmuting a kick never drops or doubles a duck), and the notes it plays feed the component's key-notes input (the ducker's Notes mode). Only a component whose descriptor has a stereo `"sidechain"` audio input takes one (the ducker, the compressor and the gate; `keyedEffectDescriptor`); a key on any other is ignored with a warning. A layer keying a component keeps running while muted or soloed out, so its key still arrives (a muted kick can drive a ducker). Keys are not delayed by latency compensation: they arrive as early as they can, and the ducker's look-ahead covers a late one. A layer may key any number of components: each slot's route table (its sends, then the keys it feeds) and the mix graph's routing room are sized from the project at `setProject` (owner, 2026-11-19; `Engine::kDefaultRoutes`, 24, until a layer feeds more, when the graph is rebuilt off the audio thread for the most any one layer feeds, within the graph memory budget, or the update is refused with the budget's error).
- **Routing loops are refused:** the project is a graph of routes (a layer to its output bus, each send that is not a feedback send, each key from its source to the keyed layer), and an update whose routes make a loop is refused, naming it ("routing makes a loop: 'Verb' -> 'Group' -> 'Verb' (a layer can't hear itself, except through a send marked "feedback"; keys never feed back)", `validateRouting` in `project.cpp`); so is a send to a missing layer or to an organization-only folder, a send to its own layer that is not a feedback send, a send from a folder without a bus, a bad `output` (above), and a key from the component's own layer or a bus-less folder. A key is never a feedback route: a key that closes a loop is refused even when a feedback send is on the way back. A key may come from any layer that plays, a track that receives sends included (its pre tap then carries them). The app never sends one: it leaves such routes out of the engine's copy (`RoutingPlan`, [app](app.md)).
- **Latency compensation across the routing:** a layer's own latency is its instrument's and inserts'; a bus's input arrives at the latest of what feeds it (the layers whose output it is and the sends into it), and every route into a bus or the master is delayed by how much earlier it arrives, so everything that meets is in time, through any depth of buses, outputs and sends (`Engine::updateCompensation`). A send into a track meets that track's instrument: it is delayed to the instrument's latency when it arrives earlier, and when it arrives later it stays late, with a warning naming the frames (the track's instrument is never delayed for it). Keys and feedback sends are not compensated: a key arrives as early as it can (a ducker's look-ahead covers a late one), and a feedback send is one chunk late by design. A parameter that changes a component's latency (the ducker's look-ahead turned on or off) recomputes it at once.
- A layer's `automation` array supports `volume` and `pan` lanes, each with any number of ordered `{step, value}` points. The audio callback evaluates them per sample against absolute song position; no UI timer or per-block parsing is involved.
- **Curved segments (#230, owner 2026-11-17):** any automation point (track, project or graph lane) may carry `curve`, the shape of its segment to the next point: absent or `"linear"` (straight), `"slow"` (eases in), `"fast"` (eases out), `"s"` (S-curve) or `"step"` (holds the point's value until the next one), and `tension` −1..1 bending Slow, Fast and S-curve. Bounded on load: an unknown name reads linear, a tension outside −1..1 is clamped, one that is not a finite number reads 0, and Linear and Step drop it (`readPointCurve`, `project.cpp`; `Graph::setAutomationLanes` refuses a curve past Step or a tension outside −1..1). One rule evaluates every lane (`automationLaneValue` and `automationCurveShape`, `params.h`), a closed form of `+ − × ÷` only, so with the engine's strict floating point every platform gives the same bits and nothing allocates or locks: with b = 0.4375 (1 + tension) (0 to 0.875, dyadic), Slow(t) = t(1−b) / (t(1−b) + (1−t)(1+b)), Fast(t) = t(1+b) / (t(1+b) + (1−t)(1−b)), S-curve = Slow(2t)/2 below t = ½ and ½ + Fast(2t−1)/2 from it, Step = 0; the value is `v0 + (v1 − v0) × shape(t)`. Tension −1 is a straight line, 0 (the default) puts a Slow segment's middle at 9/32 of the way, 1 at 1/16. A linear segment keeps the original expression `v0 + (v1 − v0) × t`, so every project without curves plays bit for bit as before (no reference re-recorded). Tests: `automation_curve_shapes_hit_exact_references` (dyadic values and recorded bits), `automation_lane_curves_follow_the_earlier_point_and_linear_stays_bit_identical` (`params_test.cpp`), `project_parser_reads_point_curves_bounded` and `track_volume_automation_follows_curved_segments` (`engine_test.cpp`). Curves need no C API change: they travel in the project JSON. Volume values are gains from 0 to +12 dB (`kMaxMixGain`, more is held there); pan values are -1–1.
- If a clip references a missing or broken pattern, that clip contributes no notes; legacy broken patterns keep their last good version playing.
- `audition` is the Builder's preview, with an optional pattern: a C++ `instrument` and native `components` written as a layer's (API 17). With `audition_only` (it needs an instrument), only the preview is heard. The app's Solo preview of a pattern used by a C++ instrument track plays it through that track's instrument and effects.
- Values outside their range are clamped:
  - BPM 20–400
- Any number of layers: the Engine grows to fit, up to what its memory budget allows (`maxLayers()`), and refuses a project with more by name (F7.3).
- `loop_range` (default false) with `loop_start_steps` and `loop_end_steps` turns the loop range on (see Playback); while it is on, both must be whole steps with the end after the start, and the song lengthens to reach the end. Off, the two are not read.
- Song positions are refused with a message naming the field, never moved or shortened (F7.2): `loop_bars`, layer `length_steps`, clip `start_steps`/`length_steps`/`source_offset_steps`, automation point steps and the audition length must be whole numbers (points may be fractional) that fit the 32-bit step count, so a song holds whole bars up to 2^31 − 1 steps (`maxLoopBars`/`maxArrangementSteps` in `engine/src/project.h`). Project automation lengthens the loop to reach its last point.
- Clips are expanded off the audio thread. A layer whose clips would expand past the project-state budget left (an upper bound, checked before expanding) is not installed, with a warning. Building takes about three times the result, so that peak must fit the budget left after the project so far.
- **Audio tracks (API 18):** a layer's `audio_clips` and `input`, and the project's `assets`: see [Audio clips](#audio-clips).
- **Per-note FX (#127, API 22):** a layer's `noteComponents` (component entries; stereo effects that declare the per-note domain, at most one event input each, no sidechain key) run once per note between its instrument's voices and the instrument's own effects, in `noteVoices` slots (1–16, default 8); the instrument must have a voice bus. Automation lanes and modulation links name them with the kind `noteComponent` (`layer`, `component`/`index`, `target`/`param`). See [Per-note FX host](#per-note-fx-host-127-steps-46).
- **MIDI effects (1.1.0, API 41):** a layer's optional `midiComponents` (component entries, at most 16; absent in older projects, which play as before) run in order on its notes before its instrument ([MIDI effects](#midi-effects-110)). Their lanes are `{"target_type":"midiComponent","layer","component","target","points"}`.
- Fields the app adds for itself, such as colours, are ignored.

## Exports

| Kind | What it renders |
| --- | --- |
| Loop | One exact project loop, or the loop range while it is on (refused before rendering when it is longer than a WAV file holds, about 3.1 hours of stereo float at 48 kHz). The engine renders two loops and keeps the second (with a loop range: from the start to the range's end, then the range again), so the file starts with the tails it has when it repeats. Includes a `smpl` loop chunk over the whole file. Bit-identical to the second pass of live playback |
| Free | Any length from 0.1 to 600 seconds, from the start |
| One-shot | One pass of one layer alone (its own, its pattern's or its clips' length, to the end of the last clip, or the project loop if it follows it), at most 30 s, without its folder or sends. A C++ instrument track with no pattern, clips or length plays one note (`ExportOptions` `note` 60, `velocity` 1, `noteSeconds` 0.5; `caw render --note --velocity --note-seconds`; the C API uses the defaults) through its effects until it has stayed below −80 dB for 0.25 s after the note. The end is trimmed to 5 ms after the last sound above −80 dB |
| Wavetable | 1–256 frames of 2048 samples. From a C++ instrument (the target is a layer with one, played with its values, or a built-in instrument's id): each frame is a fresh instance holding A0 (MIDI 21, 27.5 Hz) at a sample rate of 27.5 × 2048 = 56,320 Hz, so one cycle is one frame, and the frame is the 4th cycle (after the note's attack), the mid of its output; the swept parameter goes from its minimum to its maximum (one frame: its default), constrained as the host constrains it (choices step). A component's saved state (a user wavetable, drum samples) is not loaded. Each frame's DC is removed, and the table is normalized to peak 1. Written as 32-bit float mono |

- Files are WAV at 44.1 or 48 kHz, as 24-bit PCM or 32-bit float.
- An export refuses to run if a pattern a layer plays has an error.

### Song renders

The Export sheet's renders (`engine/src/render_stems.*`, API version 15, roadmap 0.3, 6.4 and 6.5): the mix and stems over a range, in one engine pass per range.

- **Request** (`caw_render_song(project_json, request_json, progress, cancel)`, or as a worker-pool job with `caw_worker_pool_submit_song`; unknown keys are refused): `out_dir` (an existing folder; every file goes straight into it), `project` (the `{project}` name), `pattern` (default `{project}_{region}_{stem}`; also `{kind}` and `{index}`; characters other than letters, digits and `. _ - # +` become `_`, the separators an empty token leaves collapse, a repeated name gets `_2`, `_3`), `mix` (default true), `tracks`, `buses` (a stem per track that plays and per folder bus) or `layers` (these ids instead), `bounds` (`song`, `loop` (the loop range, else the song), `selection` with `start_steps`/`end_steps`, or `regions`: `[{"name", "start_steps", "end_steps"}]`, up to 1,024, a set of files each), `sample_rate` (8,000..192,000), `bit_depth` (16, 24 or 32 for float), `dither` (TPDF, default on, used at 16-bit only), `normalize` (`none`, `peak` with `target` dBFS, default −1, or `lufs` with `target` LUFS, default −14, and `ceiling` dBTP, default −1, above 0 off), `normalize_each` (stems each to the target instead of by the mix's gain), `tail` (`none`, `seconds` with `tail_seconds`, or `silence`: until every file has been below `silence_db`, default −90, for half a second, plus 10 ms, at most `tail_seconds`, default 30, up to 600) `stems_through_master` (default true) and `stem_tap` (`post`, the default, or `pre`: freeze and bounce, 1.0.5, below).
- **Result:** `{"ok", "error", "files": [{"path", "name", "kind" (mix, track or bus), "layer", "region", "frames", "gain_db", "peak_db", "lufs"}]}`; peak and loudness are as written, −200 for silence or not measured. On failure or cancel no file of the request is left.
- **One pass:** the range plays once, straight through (the loop range is turned off for it), started with `Engine::play(start)` as Play from that step is. `Engine::setOutputTap` copies each stem's layer output into a buffer as it plays: a track's or bus's signal after its effects, volume, pan and latency compensation, where it meets the others. So every file of a range starts on the same frame and has the same length. The song is lengthened past the range and its tail when they reach beyond it, so playback never goes back to the start inside them; clips and patterns end where they end, so a tail is the notes' releases and the effects ringing out. Each file streams to a `.raw.part` float file beside it and is then encoded with its gain (`WavFileWriter`, through `.part`), so memory doesn't grow with the length and normalising needs no second render.
- **Stems through the master:** each stem then gets the master's linear stage as `runMasterNode` applies it to the mix (its volume, the 5 Hz DC filter and the 10 ms fade in when playback starts after the song's first frame), not the master's effects or the limiter. The mix's gain goes on every file unless `normalize_each`.
- **Parity [Fact, tests in `engine/tests/render_stems_test.cpp`, 2026-11-11]:** the mix is bit for bit what playback from the range's start plays, at any device buffer size (callbacks of 441, 64, 37, 512, 1 and 192 frames): the song, a selection, the loop range and a region. A tail leaves the range's own frames unchanged. The stems that reach the master (top-level tracks and buses; a bus carries its children and the sends into it) add up to the mix within 1e-5 when the master has no effects and its limiter is idle; a soloed track's mix equals its stem within 1e-6; a stem without the master stage equals that track's output at the master's input bit for bit. **Exceptions:** master effects and the safety limiter act on the mix only, so stems don't add up to a mix they change; a track inside a bus is in that bus's stem as well as its own, and a send's return is in the bus's stem, not the sender's, so add only the stems that reach the master; an instrument that sounds without notes (the test signal's Free mode) keeps sounding through a tail, and with Until silent it runs to the cap.
- **Fixed with this (2026-11-11):** the master's 10 ms fade in when playback starts mid-song left the rest of the chunk it ended in at 1 − 1/480 of full level, so playback from a step depended on the device's buffer size (`runMasterNode`'s `finishSample`).
- **Pre-fader stems (`stem_tap: "pre"`, freeze and bounce, 1.0.5, #209):** `Engine::setOutputTap(layer, l, r, preFader)` copies a track's signal after its instrument, per-note FX and effects (a bus's after its effects), before its volume, pan, sends and latency compensation. The render then drops each such stem's first `Engine::layerLatency` frames (its instrument's, per-note FX's and effects' declared latency, as compensation counts it) and runs that much longer, so frame 0 is the range's first frame. A pre-fader render's silent tail is judged on its stems only when the mix isn't written. No C API change (a new request key; an engine without it refuses the request by name). **[Fact, `engine/tests/render_stems_test.cpp`, 2026-11-14]:** a track frozen as the app sends it (its pre-fader stem as a fixed audio clip from step 0, no instrument or effects, its fader, pan, notes and a post-fader send kept) renders bit for bit the same mix as the live track, its delay's tail past the song's end included, at one tempo and on a 120→90 ramp; a 240-frame-latency insert's pre-fader stem lines up with the plain one; frozen, the engine holds no insert of the track. CPU on this container (Linux x64 VM, GCC 13 Release, best of 3 × 2 s, 2026-11-14): one busy Flagship with a reverb 21.7 ms per second of audio live, 3.4 ms frozen (the whole engine: the clip player, fader and master).
- **Dither and 16-bit:** `WavSpec.pcmBits` 16 or 24 and `dither`: ±1 LSB triangular noise from a seeded xorshift32 added before rounding, as the sample-pack maker's (Lipshitz, Wannamaker and Vanderkooy, JAES 40(5), 1992), so the same request gives the same bytes; exact silence stays silent.
- **Loudness:** the integrated loudness and true peak come from `LoudnessMeter` (BS.1770-5) run on the file's samples as they render.
- **[Assumption]** Serum may want an extra `clm ` chunk to recognise 2048-sample frames. This isn't written yet; check before relying on Serum.

## Hardware profile and execution policy

The native hardware probe is a one-time, non-real-time snapshot. It uses active/online logical CPUs, process affinity where exposed, and Linux/Android cgroup CPU and memory limits where readable. Windows uses available physical memory; Linux/Android read system-available memory as `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo` (free memory plus reclaimable cache; Android keeps `MemFree` low on purpose, and budgeting from free pages alone refused First loop on a Galaxy S22+, 2026-11-11), falling back to free pages, and combine it with a finite cgroup limit minus current usage. An exhausted finite cgroup limit is represented by a 1 MiB scheduler budget; a zero field means the signal is unavailable. Positive measurements are budgeted at one quarter, capped at 8192 MiB. macOS/iOS currently use half of reported physical memory as a conservative fallback, not live available memory. The scheduler falls back to one worker for unknown or very low memory. On Android, the host reads thermal status (Android 10+) and battery-saver state once, then listens for changes through event-driven platform channels; foreground/background and audio-playing transitions also refresh the worker budget. Unsupported platforms retain an explicit `Unknown` pressure state; background/offline work reserves one host core where possible and GPU work is disabled. Physical-device behavior still requires validation.

## C API (`engine/include/caw.h`)

- **Engine:** `caw_engine_create(sample_rate)` creates one.
  - `caw_engine_set_project(json)` returns `{"ok", "modules": {id: entry}, "warnings": {"project": [...]}}`, one entry per pattern module ([Project file](#project-file-json)).
  - Live controls: `caw_engine_set_layer_mix`, `caw_engine_set_master` (a 5 ms audio-thread gain ramp), `caw_engine_play` (API 40: Play, Play at, the count-ins and Stop return 1 when the command reached the audio thread's queue, 0 when it stayed full, as when audio stalled, and nothing changed; callers may ignore it. `Engine::send` retries a full queue for about 2 s, then drops the command, and every caller now says so: the master, metronome, touch release and overload reset return 1 or 0 too, the note previews 1, 0 or −1 for nothing to send, and a project update names each refused command in its warnings), `caw_engine_play_at` (play from a position in sixteenth steps, converted with the tempo in effect when the play arrives, so a pause resumes on the same beat; it fades in over 10 ms, as does a play that interrupts a stop's fade; outside the loop it starts at 0; notes that began before it are not heard), `caw_engine_stop`, `caw_engine_clear_overload`. API version 7 (2026-11-10, REAPER flow gaps 3 and 4): `caw_engine_play_count_in(engine, step, beats)` clicks `beats` (0..64) beats at the tempo, accented on each bar, then plays from `step` as `play_at` does, on the sample after the last beat (previews play over it; a Stop cancels it; while playing it is `play_at`); `caw_engine_set_metronome(engine, on)` clicks every beat while the song plays, accented on the bar. The click is built in (`Engine::startClick`): 30 ms of a decaying sine, 1760 Hz at 0.5 on the bar and 1320 Hz at 0.32 on other beats, mixed into the master before the safety limiter while playing; the count-in, which runs with the song stopped, adds it to the output directly, held to ±1 (its peak is under the limiter's ceiling). Off by default; offline renders never click. It was chosen over a hidden click track as simpler and real-time safe: no allocation, a few floats of state. `caw_engine_release_touches(engine)` ends every knob touch as a Play does, without moving the transport, so lanes rule again at once (the app's automation Touch mode). Tests: `count_in_clicks_each_beat_then_plays_on_the_sample_after_the_last`, `metronome_clicks_on_every_beat_while_playing_and_not_when_off`, `released_touches_let_the_lanes_rule_again_while_playing`, and `count_in_metronome_and_released_touches_never_allocate_on_the_audio_thread`.
  - **Tempo map (API 31, #207):** `caw_engine_set_tempo_map(engine, CawTempoPoint[] {beat, bpm, ramp}, n)`, `caw_engine_set_time_signatures(engine, CawTimeSig[] {beat, num, den}, n)` (each on a bar line of the one before; the bars are worked out from the beats) and `caw_engine_set_bpm(engine, bpm)` (one point) return 0, or 1 with nothing changed; `caw_engine_position(engine, CawPosition*)` (`struct_size` first) reports the sample, beat, seconds, tempo, bar, beat in bar and signature. Count-in and metronome beats are the signature's ticks. See [Tempo map and time signatures](#playback).
  - Note previews (#103): `caw_engine_note_on(layer_id, id, pitch, velocity)`, `caw_engine_note_pitch`, `caw_engine_note_off`, `caw_engine_notes_off`; heard through the layer's effects and mix while playing or stopped, without moving the transport (detail: [engine build status](engine-build-status.md)). The on-screen keys and pads play through these; MIDI devices through the device input below.
  - **MIDI devices (API 15, `engine/src/midi_device.*`):** `caw_engine_midi_bytes(layer_id, bytes, count, time_ns)` takes a MIDI 1.0 stream (running status within a call; real-time, system and SysEx bytes skipped) and converts each Channel Voice message to a MIDI 2.0 UMP at the edge with the UMP specification's min-centre-max upscaling; `caw_engine_midi_ump(layer_id, words, count, time_ns)` takes UMP words (type 2 upscaled, type 4 as is; utility such as JR timestamps, system, SysEx and data packets skipped by their length). Both push into the engine's `Midi2InputQueue` (`engine/src/midi2.h`; owner, 2026-11-19): one 1,024-slot ring whose last 128 slots only releases may take (note offs, sustain off, All Sound Off CC 120, All Notes Off CC 123, the host's release), so a flood never loses a note off. Each message gets a producer sequence number, and the callback plays its messages by frame, then sequence. When the 896 shared slots are full, continuous values (controllers, bend, channel and poly pressure, per-note pitch) merge on the producer side, under its mutex, by (track, group, channel, note or none, kind, index), so MPE member channels and per-note values stay apart, into a 256-entry table that later pushes and the UI's meter reads move into the ring, oldest first, as space returns; a sustain off removes a waiting sustain on. Note ons, program changes, RPN/NRPN (CC 6, 38, 96–101) and bank select never merge: when full they are dropped and counted by `caw_engine_midi_dropped`, as is a release when the whole ring is full. The callback never drops: a full pending list leaves the rest in the queue from any thread but the audio callback's. `time_ns` is on `caw_midi_clock_ns()` (steady_clock: CLOCK_MONOTONIC, Android's `System.nanoTime`, so `MidiReceiver` timestamps go in unchanged; 0: now). At each callback the audio thread works out every message's frame: a callback stands for the frames just before it started, so a message plays one callback (plus `caw_engine_set_midi_options`' delay, 0–200 ms) after it was played, with the spacing kept; one later than that plays at frame 0; one for a later callback waits. Nothing on that path allocates (`engine_rt_test`). On a native instrument a Note On holds a device note (a preview with its own id range, the device's key and channel; Stop's fade and mute end it, `caw_engine_notes_off` does not); Note Off ends it unless the channel's sustain pedal (CC 64) holds it; Poly Pressure and MIDI 2.0 per-note pitch reach that note (the drums' pad pressure and note learn read them); Pitch Bend repitches the channel's notes over the bend range, Channel Pressure sets their pressure; CC 120 and 123 end them; every CC, Program Change and Channel Pressure also reaches the instrument as its own MIDI event (MIDI 2.0 words, note id 0). With `CAW_MIDI_MPE` channel 1 is the master and channels 2–16 one note each (their bend over the per-note range, 48 by default, and their CC 74 the note's timbre). A `.caw` voice chain takes only the notes, at the block's start. `caw_engine_midi_release(layer_id or NULL)` ends a track's (or every track's) device notes at the next callback; `caw_engine_position_at(time_ns)` is the transport position (samples, unwrapped) the audio thread rendered at that time, -1 while stopped, which recording uses.
- **Meters:** `caw_engine_meters` (master sample peaks since the last read, left and right), `caw_engine_layer_meter` (a track's or folder's sample peak since the last read, after volume and pan; `caw_engine_layer_meter_stereo`, API version 5, also gives each side's peak, and either call resets both; `caw_engine_layer_meters`, API version 25, reads every project layer's stereo meter under one lock into a caller's `CawLayerMeterStereo` array, the first min(count, capacity), resets them the same way, allocates nothing and returns the layer count, `all_track_meters_read_in_one_call`) and `caw_engine_scope` (1024 samples of a track's signal before pan, per layer, or for the master and the audition by `CAW_SCOPE_MASTER`/`CAW_SCOPE_AUDITION`). Loudness and correlation (API version 12, `engine/src/meters.*`): `caw_engine_loudness` fills a `CawLoudness` with the master's momentary (400 ms), short-term (3 s) and integrated loudness in LUFS, their maxima, each side's true peak since the last read and the maximum since the reset (dBTP), the master's stereo correlation and the seconds the integrated value has measured; `caw_engine_loudness_reset` restarts the integrated value and maxima (Play does too); `caw_engine_layer_correlation` gives a track's or folder's correlation (of what it sends on, about 300 ms). A value with no reading yet is `CAW_METER_NONE` (-200). **[Fact]** The definitions follow ITU-R BS.1770-5 (11/2023: K-weighting, the 400 ms gated blocks with 75 % overlap, the -70 LUFS absolute and -10 LU relative gates, Annex 2's 4x true-peak filter) and EBU Tech 3341 V4 (11/2023: the momentary, short-term and integrated windows); `meters_test.cpp` checks the filter against BS.1770-5's printed 48 kHz coefficients and the meter against Tech 3341's minimum test signals. The audio thread K-weights and squares each sample and publishes at each 100 ms sub-block; the integrated value is gated from a fixed 0.01 LU histogram when read, so memory stays fixed however long it plays. The meter measures what the device plays, after the safety limiter, while something reads it: from the engine's start until 30 s pass with no `caw_engine_loudness` read, and again from the next read, which starts it over as a reset does (nothing in the app reads it yet; it cost about 6 % of a three-track project's audio work, [performance](performance.md)). **[Assumption]** the 300 ms correlation window is a usual meter's, not a standard.
- **Hosts and devices:** `caw_engine_process(left, right, frames)` lets hosts such as a plugin drive the audio. `caw_audio_start` and `caw_audio_stop` open the device through miniaudio: AAudio or OpenSL ES on Android, WASAPI on Windows, Core Audio, and ALSA or PulseAudio.
- **Live output safety (API 35, #244):** `caw_engine_set_output_safety(engine, flags)` with `CAW_OUTPUT_LIMITER` (1) and `CAW_OUTPUT_MONO` (2), ORed (other bits are dropped), and `caw_engine_output_safety(engine)` reading them back (a new engine starts with `CAW_OUTPUT_LIMITER`; 0 for a null engine). Any thread; the device callback reads the flags once per render. `caw_engine_process` and every render leave them out ([Playback](#playback)).
- **General mixer routing (API 36):** no new function: outputs, sends into tracks and feedback sends ride the project JSON ([project file](#project-file-json)). `caw_engine_set_send` changes a send into a track or a feedback send while it plays, as any send; a feedback send's `limiter` changes by a project update (its route table is rebuilt and its ring carries on). An older project plays as before.
- **Exports:** `caw_render(project_json, options, path, progress, cancel, …)` writes a WAV. Progress counts 0–1000, and a non-zero cancel stops it. `caw_render_song` and `caw_worker_pool_submit_song` (API 15) make [song renders](#song-renders): the mix and stems.
- **MIDI files:** `caw_pattern_from_midi`, `caw_pattern_export` and `caw_pattern_export_song` (API 31; see [MIDI files](#midi-files)).
- **Function library:** `caw_library()` returns JSON: `functions` (name, category, signature, doc, example and args with name, doc and optional) and `constants`. The app's Library sheet reads it.
- **Native C++ components:** `caw_engine_load_component(engine, library_path, expected_component_id, trust_level)` loads a built shared library into the Engine's `ModuleRegistry` and returns JSON with `ok`, component ID, error text and error code, and (API 37) the file's `sha256`, its `scan_verdict` and `grace_applied`.
