Every file is scanned before it is opened ([pre-load scan](#pre-load-scan)); a blocked verdict refuses the load with error code 11. `caw_engine_load_component_ex(engine, path, id, trust, flags, legacy_sha256)` takes `CAW_LOAD_LEGACY_GRACE` with the SHA-256 of the file the user trusted before 1.0.7; `caw_engine_scan_component` returns the scan as JSON. The caller must pass `CAW_NATIVE_TRUST_BLOCKED`, `CAW_NATIVE_TRUST_ISOLATED` or `CAW_NATIVE_TRUST_TRUSTED`; there is no default. Blocked refuses before opening the library, Isolated is rejected until a separate-process host exists, and Trusted loads in-process. `caw_engine_unload_component` returns 1 only when the library can be unloaded (no live processor wrapper retains it). A build does not call either API. The component exports one C symbol, `caw_get_component_v3` (`engine/include/caw_component.h`); the Engine activates each master-chain instance (`activate(sample rate, kBlock)`) before it plays, and creation, destruction and memory stay inside the component. `caw_engine_components` returns every loaded component's descriptor as JSON (identity, role, timing, ports with layout and channel count, parameters with choices and capability flags, UI controls). The Dart FFI wrapper calls load, unload and the listing on a background isolate. Components see the song's transport in each block (`CawProcessBlock.transport`, `caw::Transport`): the tempo and beats per bar always, and the position in beats with `playing` set while the song plays. A project places loaded stereo effects (one stereo audio input, one stereo audio output, any parameters; a built-in may add a stereo `sidechain` input and a key-notes event input, fed by its `key`) in a layer's `components` (after its .caw chain, before volume and pan) and in `master_components` (before the safety limiter); each entry is an id or `{"id": ..., "params": {name: value}, "bypass": bool, "auto_gain": bool, "state": base64, "state_version": n}` (`auto_gain`, API 39: [per-slot auto gain](#per-slot-auto-gain-and-output-meters-api-39)). A bypassed component crossfades to its input over 5 ms and is then not run. A component that says it has fallen silent (`ModuleProcessor::quiet()`, the C interface's `caw.quiet.v1` extension) sleeps (is not run, and its output is silence) until an event or input that is not silent arrives. A declared tail alone never puts one to sleep: it would fall asleep on a block boundary with its timed state frozen, so the output would depend on the device's buffer size (second audio review, 2026-11-10; test `a_sleeping_component_renders_the_same_at_any_block_size`). The built-in drums and Flagship say when they are quiet (Flagship once no voice sounds and its effects have fallen below −94 dB), as do the effects suite's effects after silence; the others keep running. A saved state loads when the chain is built (a component may refuse it, which refuses the update), or while playing through `caw_engine_set_component_state` (API 14; the fx-space bullet below). Project automation lanes reach component parameters with `{"target_type": "component", "layer": id (absent: the master), "component": index, "target": param}`: a lane rules while playing, a knob move overrides it until the next Play, and parameters the descriptor marks not automatable ignore lanes. A component that is not loaded, has other ports or names an unknown parameter refuses the whole update. An update with the same components in the same order keeps each chain's running state. `caw_engine_set_component_param(engine, layer_id, index, param_id, value)` changes a value while playing (NULL or empty layer id: the master), ramping over the parameter's smoothing. `caw_engine_set_component_bypass` bypasses one while playing, and `caw_engine_component_states` returns each component's state as JSON (base64) for the project; the app stores it on every save (`ProjectDoc.storeComponentStates`). API version 4 (instruments, below). After Stop, effects that declare a tail (`tail_frames`) ring out on silence until their output stays below −80 dB for 0.25 s (at most the declared tail, and 30 s); effects without one stop with the transport and start the next play from silence. Tracks, buses and sends are kept in time across the whole routing (each layer's latency is the sum of its components' `latency_frames`, up to 32,768 frames; see Latency compensation under [Project file](#project-file-json)). Every Engine also has the Library's built-in stereo tools, which need no trust choice and can't be unloaded. `org.caw.stereo.balance` (`pan` −1..1: the side being turned down falls linearly, the other stays at unity) and `org.caw.stereo.width` (`width` 0..2, mid/side: 0 is mono, 1 unchanged; a mono signal is unchanged at every width) both live in `engine/src/builtin_components.cpp`, with kernels in `caw/library/stereo.hpp`. `org.caw.distortion` (`shape`: Drive, Hard clip, Fold or Crush; `drive` 1..20, `bits` 1..24, `mix`, `output` 0..2) distorts each channel separately. Its kernels in `caw/library/distortion.hpp` are the same code the .caw `fx.drive`, `fx.crush` and `fx.fold` call, so both sound the same (their renders unchanged). `org.caw.delay.pingpong` (`sync`: Free or a note division from 1/16 to a bar, `time` in ms when free, `feedback` 0..0.95, `cross` 0..1 for ping-pong: toward 1 the left line takes the mono average of both inputs and the right line none, so even a centred sound bounces left, right, left, its first echo at the dry level (before the second audio review, 2026-11-10, the left line took the sum, 6 dB over); `mix`) has up to 4 s of delay, set aside at activate (a longer division is held at 4 s), and glides to a new time; it syncs to the song's tempo and rings out after Stop. `org.caw.chorus` (`rate` 0.05..5 Hz, `depth`, `mix`) sweeps each side a quarter cycle apart, so a mono signal comes out wide. `caw_engine_components` lists them first, with `"builtin": true`. `caw_engine_reload_component` loads a rebuild (a new file) of a loaded component: every chain using it switches to the new build, keeping each instance's values, bypass and (if the new build accepts it) state, and a chain swap of any kind (a rebuild, an effect added or removed) crossfades over 20 ms while it sounds. The per-component trust store remains outstanding.
- **Native instruments (#156, API version 4):** a component with one event input and one stereo audio output (no other ports; `ComponentChain::isInstrument`) can be a layer's `instrument`; an instrument in `components`, or an effect as `instrument`, refuses the update. It is a one-component `ComponentChain` (`buildInstrument`) on the track's slot, so values, bypass (a crossfade to silence), state, automation, transport, latency (added to the track's), rebuilds and the project-component cap work as for effects; `CAW_INSTRUMENT_INDEX` (0xFFFFFFFF) addresses it in `caw_engine_set_component_param`, `_bypass` and `_component_states`, and lanes use `{"target_type": "instrument", "layer": id, "target": param}`. The engine sends it the track's notes as MIDI 2.0 UMP note events with host note ids (the encoding in [the SDK](native-module-sdk.md#writing-an-instrument)), queued per block in a `NoteEvents` list set aside when the instrument's chain is built (4,096 events a block and 1,024 held notes, 360 KB per instrument track, about 23 MB at 64 tracks, counted in the project memory budget; the host sets no practical polyphony cap, owner 2026-11-10). A thousand notes at once is far past what a track plays, and 4,096 events leave room for every held note to end and start again in one block; the earlier 16,384 events and 4,096 notes took 1.5 MB a track, about 94 MB at 64 tracks and twice that for a track during a rebuild (second audio review, 2026-11-10). Room for every held note's off is kept back from note-ons and per-note motion, so a full block drops a pitch or pressure update, never an off; drops are counted (`overflow()`), and a 1,025th note ends the oldest. The block's events are put in frame order by a natural merge sort of the runs they arrive in, frame-accurate in every block: pattern and clip notes with the same rules .caw voices follow (chance per repeat, ratchets, clip loops, loop restarts ending held notes, a slide as a pitch glide on the held note without a new on, the note's bend, vibrato, pressure and timbre every 16 frames, and held notes following a pattern edit, below in [Patterns](#patterns)), note previews (`caw_engine_note_on/_pitch/_off/_notes_off`: piano roll and MIDI keyboards), and offs for every held note at Stop, at a tempo change and at Play. After Stop it rings out (its release, then the track's effects) until the track is quiet for 0.25 s, at most 30 s; at Play it is reset unless a preview is held, so an export (the same path, `setProjectOffline`) starts from silence; one still ringing out after Stop first fades out over the new pass's first 5 ms, rendered at Play inside that callback, so the instruments ringing share a budget of 480 frames of that work (`Engine::kPlayTailBudgetFrames`: two fade over 5 ms, 16 over 0.6 ms each), and Play never costs more than about two 5 ms tails (second audio review, 2026-11-10). A track muted long enough to stop is reset before it plays again. A new or rebuilt instrument takes over the held notes (a new on for each, same ids) while the old one releases them and crossfades out over 20 ms. The track then runs its .caw effects (they are mono: they take the instrument's mid, and without any the instrument stays stereo), its native inserts, volume and pan.
- **Built-in instruments (`engine/src/builtin_instruments.cpp`):** Drums and Flagship, built on the Library kernels (`caw/library/`, [SDK](native-module-sdk.md#the-library)), deterministic, allocation-free while processing, read their knobs every 16 frames, and each has reference renders (`builtin_instruments_test.cpp`, `drums_test.cpp`). They are listed with the loaded components (`caw_engine_components`, marked `"builtin": true`, with their `"presets"`), so the app can build a knob page for each from its descriptor.
  - `org.caw.drums` ("Drums", 2026-11-11): 64 pads of layered drum synthesis and samples, in Kit or Single mode; see [Drums](#drums-orgcawdrums). The old `org.caw.drum.kick`, `.snare`, `.hats` and `.kit` load only through migration to it.
  - `org.caw.synth.flagship` ("Flagship", #171, `engine/src/builtin_flagship.cpp`): the "synth that can do all the things", in original code (no Vital code, presets or wavetables; [decisions](decisions.md)). 64 voices; 230 parameters, grouped by id prefix so a layout can make sections (1.0's 215 keep their indices; 1.1.0's 15, below, are appended after them; of the 215 the last 24, `voice_mode`, `glide`, `mod1_via`..`mod16_via`, `osc1_fm_ratio`..`osc3_fm_ratio` and `osc1_fold_mode`..`osc3_fold_mode`, were added after the slots so a slot's destination number stays what saved projects hold):
    - **`osc1_`..`osc3_` (three wavetable oscillators):** `on` (osc 1 on by default), `table` (Basic: sine, saw, square, 25 % pulse; Analog: triangle, warm saw, shark fin, soft square, soft pulse; Vocal: A, E, I, O, U formants; Harmonic: a band of harmonics sweeping up over 16 frames; Classic: sine, triangle, saw and square, the retired Morph synth's shapes and phases, so its frame is that synth's `shape`; generated by code at the first activate, 1,024-sample mip-mapped frames shared by every instance; User: the imported table, below, or a sine with none), `frame` 0..1 (between frames, linearly), `level`, `pan`, `octave` ±4, `semi` ±12, `fine` ±100 ct, `phase`, `phase_rand` (0 every copy starts at `phase`, 1 at random), `warp` (None, Bend, Sync up to 8×, Mirror, Quantize 256..2 steps, FM: phase modulation by a sine at `fm_ratio` 1..16 times the note, up to 0.75 cycles deep, Fold: a wavefolder on the table's value (sin(x × drive × π/2)), drive 1..8, blended in over the first quarter, with `fold_mode` Per copy (the default, and what a project or preset saved without it plays: each unison copy folded, then panned) or Whole stack (#197, owner 2026-11-13: the copies summed at their pan gains, before the level, and each side's sum folded once, one fold a sample when every copy is centred; then the level, so the level never changes the fold in either mode; spread copies fold side by side because only a side's own copies reach it; Osc FM hears the mean of the two sides' folds; a rougher sound, as the stack's beating is folded as one waveform, and the cheaper one: two folds a voice instead of one a copy), Osc FM: phase modulation by the oscillator before it round the ring (osc 1 hears osc 3, 2 hears 1, 3 hears 2), its mono output before its level, so a modulator at level 0 is heard only as FM, up to one cycle per unit, held to ±2 units; osc 1 hears osc 3 one 16-frame control period late, the same at any block size; #171). Against aliasing: every warp reads a mip level chosen for how fast it runs the table (FM and Osc FM: 1 + 2π × depth × ratio; Fold: half the drive); FM renders at twice the rate (the table read exactly between samples) and comes down through a polyphase IIR half-band decimator (`caw/library/oversample.hpp`'s design, 8 coefficients), which puts what it makes up to 1.5 × the sample rate in the stop band. Fold is a hybrid (#197, owner 2026-11-13; [decisions](decisions.md)): each oscillator of a note folds either at the sample rate with first-order antiderivative anti-aliasing (ADAA) or on the 2x path FM uses (the table read between samples too, each value folded, the decimator after), in both fold modes; Eco folds every note with ADAA. A note takes the 2x path when f × (drive + 2) > 10 kHz, where f is the highest frequency it may play (the note, the oscillator's tuning, its stack's top copy and detune, and `bend_range` up; as at 48 kHz) and drive the most it may reach (8 when a mod slot moves `warp_amt`): at full drive from about A#5 with the default bend range, at 0.4 (drive 3.8) from about G6. The choice is made when the voice starts from silence and holds until it falls silent, so a bend, glide or moved knob never switches a sounding note's path and never clicks; a note bent or glided far above where it started keeps ADAA and aliases as Eco would there. ADAA: each output is the fold's mean over the straight line from the previous value p to this one v, (G(v) − G(p)) / (v − p) with G its antiderivative; for the sine this is exactly sin(k m) × sin(k d/2) / (k d/2) (m the midpoint, d the step, k = drive × π/2), so the 0/0 where v ≈ p never arises (the second factor is a series near 0, exactly 1 at d = 0, where the output is the plain fold of v) and the blend's straight part averages to m. Both paths fold with the same fine sine series (about 1e-7; the FM warp's cheaper parabola, 0.001 off with kinks, left partials of its own at about −65 dB). ADAA delays the fold by half a sample and rolls off its top octave a little (the mean of two samples: −2 dB at 10 kHz, −5 dB at 15 kHz). [Fact, `flagship_tables_test.cpp`, 2026-11-13: a sine at A6 (1760 Hz) leaves its aliases about 80 dB down with FM at full depth and ratio 2, and 51 dB down with Osc FM from a sine an octave up; Fold at full drive 69 dB down (2x; ADAA alone 39 dB, the naive fold 33 dB, the 2x path before #197 63 dB), an octave lower 67 dB (ADAA; before #197: 62 dB), at 0.6 of the drive 80 dB (2x; before: 73 dB), A7 at full drive 56 dB (before: 55 dB); the crossover's sweep is in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a6-flagship-fold-and-drive-197)] and `warp_amt`, `unison` 1..16 (osc 1 seven), `detune` 0..100 ct (where the outermost copies sit), `detune_power` −1..1 (0 even spacing, +1 gathers copies near the note as \|x\|⁴, −1 pushes them out as \|x\|^0.25), `spread` (pan of each copy: \|x\| × spread, mirror pairs on opposite sides alternating pair by pair, so spread 0 is exactly mono), `blend` (0 only the centre copy or two, 0.5 all equal, 1 only the others; levels power-normalised), `stack` (Off, Octave, 2 octaves, Fifth, Major chord, Minor chord, Harmonics: intervals cycled across the copies in pitch order), `dest` (Filter 1, Filter 2, Both, Bypass). The layout is `caw/library/unison.hpp`.
    - **`flt1_`, `flt2_` (two filters per voice):** `on`, `type` (Ladder LP/HP/BP 24/12 dB, SVF LP/HP/BP/notch, Comb: peaks at the cutoff and its multiples, Formant: `morph` sweeps A, E, I, O, U and the cutoff over 1 kHz shifts the formants), `cutoff` 20..20000 Hz, `resonance`, `drive` 1..10 (a level-matched tanh before the filter, in float four samples at a time since #197: `Saturator::processApproxFloat`, within 1e-6 of the double form), `keytrack` (0..1 of an octave per octave from C4), `morph` (formant vowel, comb damping), `mix`. `flt_routing`: Series (filter 1 into filter 2; Both feeds filter 1) or Parallel (summed).
    - **`env1_`..`env3_`:** ADSR (`attack`, `decay`, `release` 0.001..10 s, `sustain`); env 1 is the level. **`lfo1_`..`lfo3_`:** `shape` (lfo.hpp's seven), `rate` 0.01..40 Hz, `sync` (Off, 1/32 .. 8 bars: locked to the song's position while it plays), `mode` (Free: one for the instrument; Retrigger: per voice, from each note; Running: one for the instrument at the synced rate, running on from Play rather than locked to the song's position, so a sweep longer than the loop carries on across loop restarts). **`rnd_`:** `rate`, `mode` (sample and hold or smooth), per voice.
    - **`mod1_`..`mod16_` (the mod matrix; edited in the flagship's device editor, [app](app.md)):** `src` (None, Env 1..3, LFO 1..3, Random, Velocity, Note, Pitch bend (the note's per-note pitch from where it started, over `bend_range`), Pressure, Timbre, Mod wheel (controller 1 when the host sends it), Accent (a 303's: 0 up to velocity 0.7, 1 at velocity 1)), `via` (a second source the slot's amount is multiplied by, 0..1; None: 1, so "env 2 by accent" opens the filter further only on accented notes), `dst` (None or any parameter before the slots: value k + 1 is parameter k, labelled by name), `amt` −1..1 and `bipolar` (a source is 0..1; bipolar makes it −1..1). A slot adds amount × source in the destination's knob travel (its curve: an exponential knob moves in octaves), slots on one destination add up, and the result is clamped to the range. The three choice lists 1.1.0 appended to (`table`, `warp` and the filters' `type`) are the exception (1.1.1; they carry `legacyChoiceCount`, below): on a 1.0 choice a slot moves the knob in the 1.0 list's steps and within it (table 0..5, warp 0..7, type 0..8), as 1.0.9 did, and on a 1.1.0 choice over the whole list. In 1.1.0 a slot moved them by a fraction of the longer lists, so an old patch with an LFO or envelope on its filter type swept into the new comb, phaser and diode models (the owner's report: an old project playing echoes with no delay on the track); `a_slot_moves_an_old_patch_s_tables_warps_and_filter_types_as_1_0_9_did`. A project modulator's link (#161) on one of them follows the same rule (`a_project_link_moves_an_old_choice_within_the_old_list`). Applied every 16 frames per voice; a voice source moves an instrument-wide parameter (below) from the newest voice. Slot 1 starts as env 2 opening filter 1 by 1.5 octaves.
    - **Voice mode:** `voice_mode` Poly (a voice per note), Mono (one voice, last-note priority: releasing the newest note returns to the one held before; every note restarts the envelopes) or Legato (one voice; a note played while another is held glides there without restarting them, as a 303's slide; a pattern `slide` arrives as the host's pitch glide). `glide` 0.001..2 s: how long a mono or legato voice takes to reach a new note (to 40 dB of the way), from the pitch it is sounding, even when it is only releasing.
    - **Instrument-wide:** `volume`, `velocity` (how much a soft note is quieter), `bend_range` 1..48 st; **`fx_` (effects, in this order):** distortion (`fx_dist_on`, `_type` Drive/Hard clip/Fold/Crush, `_drive` 1..20, `_mix`), a three-band EQ (`fx_eq_on`, `_low`/`_mid`/`_high` ±18 dB, `_mid_freq` 100..8000 Hz, `_mix`; shelves at 250 Hz and 4 kHz), chorus (`fx_chorus_on`, `_rate`, `_depth`, `_mix`), ping-pong delay (`fx_delay_on`, `_sync` Off or 1/16 .. a bar, `_time` 0.01..4 s, `_feedback` 0..0.95, `_mix`) and reverb (`fx_reverb_on`, off by default: a reverb per track costs about two voices; `_size`, `_decay` 0.1..20 s, `_damping`, `_mix`). An effect switched on starts from silence.
    - **User wavetable (the component state, #171):** `caw_engine_set_component_state` with the instrument index, state version 1, in one of two forms: an import, `CWTI` + a little-endian u32 split (0: the file is 2048-sample frames, the usual single-cycle table export, and a file shorter than 2048 samples is one cycle; 1..256: that many equal frames, each resampled linearly to 2048) + a WAV file's bytes (PCM 8/16/24/32-bit or float, any channels, mixed to mono, at its own rate; at most 8 MiB); or the saved form `saveState` writes, `CWT1` + u32 frame count (1..256) + u32 frame size (2048) + every frame's samples as 16-bit little-endian PCM, the table normalised to a peak of full scale. Anything else is refused whole and the table playing is kept: another version, a short or unknown header, a size that does not match, 0 or more than 256 frames, another frame size, a split over 256, frames under 16 samples, a WAV the decoder refuses, a silent table. An empty state is no table. Each frame is measured with a 2,048-point FFT, its first 512 harmonics (DC dropped) become a 1,024-sample mip-mapped frame like the built-in tables', and the table is scaled so its loudest frame has a full-scale saw's RMS. Building runs on the control thread when the chain is built (a state change rebuilds the chain and crossfades it in): about 2 ms a frame on a desktop core (256 frames: about 0.55 s; the same bytes again: 6 ms, since instances holding the same table share one copy) [Fact: measured 2026-11-11, Release, x86-64]. The `table` choice User plays it; the app's Import wavetable… sends the import form and stores the saved form in the project.
    - **Presets** (`engine/src/presets.cpp`, listed by `caw_engine_components` as `"presets"`: name, description and the values they set; the others keep their defaults): Init (the defaults), Morph pad, Mono bass, Acid 303, Basic, Pluck and FM bell. Morph pad, Mono bass, Acid 303 and Basic replace the retired synths (owner, 2026-11-10: "every instrument that can will be replaced by the better synth"; [decisions](decisions.md)): Morph pad is the Classic table at 0.45 with seven copies 18 ct out, spread 0.7, a 12 dB SVF at 1.8 kHz, LFO 1 Running over 16 beats sweeping the frame by 0.25 and timbre opening the filter 4 octaves; Mono bass is legato with 40 ms glide, a saw and a sine an octave down, a 12 dB SVF at 600 Hz that env 2 opens 2.5 octaves, drive 1.2; Acid 303 is legato with 60 ms glide, a saw into the ladder at 500 Hz, resonance 0.7, env 2 opening it 3 octaves and accent (via) 1.25 more, accent shortening env 2 and raising the volume, drive 2; Basic is a saw and a square 7 ct apart into a 12 dB SVF that env 1 (the level) opens 2.5 octaves. How close each is to the synth it replaces: [performance](performance.md#one-synth-2026-11-10). Pluck and FM bell are the .caw `synth.pluck` and `synth.fm` (#122): Pluck is a saw into a 12 dB SVF at 300 Hz that env 2 opens to about 6.3 kHz and closes over 0.2 s, the level falling over 0.4 s; FM bell is a sine through the FM warp at ratio 3, env 2 opening the warp amount (the index) by 0.75 and letting it fall over 0.6 s, the level over 1.5 s, no filter.
    - The default is a warm, wide saw stack (seven copies 25 ct out, spread 0.8, a ladder at 2.4 kHz opened by env 2). Poly voices whose filter 1 is set alike (a linear SVF without drive that no slot moves for that voice, no key tracking, filter 2 off) share one filter with their level before it, as the Morph synth did; a voice whose filter is moved apart takes its own to the note's end. Voices whose own filters are set to the same ladder or SVF low-pass run them side by side (four voices at a time on x86-64, two on Arm64; two centred voices share a two-wide vector), each voice's output summed in the voices' order, so the output is the same as one voice at a time within the tolerance tests and bit-identical on the builds checked (the engine's fast floating-point flags may fuse the paired and single paths differently, about 1 ulp; [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a2-flagship-voices-side-by-side)). Oscillator phases are 32-bit fixed point. Each block size renders the same sound, bit-identical on the builds checked (the control grid runs on across blocks and restarts at a note-on; a whole period's fixed-length loops and a shorter piece's may be fused differently under the fast flags); with no voice and the effects below −94 dB for 0.1 s (plus the delay time) it clears the effects and says it is quiet. Cost: [performance](performance.md#flagship-synth-2026-11-10).
    - **1.1.0 synthesis (Raven, 2026-11-19; ideas only from Vital, which is GPLv3: none of its code or presets):** every addition is appended (parameters after the 215, choices at the ends of their lists, and a slot moves an old choice in its 1.0 list's steps: the mod matrix above), so old presets and projects render as before: `engine/tests/references/flagship.cawref` holds the seven 1.0 presets and two old warp patches recorded with the code before Raven and still compared, and the stress suite's golden hashes (`flagship_heavy16`) did not move.
      - **Oscillator interaction:** `osc1_mod_src`..`osc3_mod_src` (Prev: round the ring, Osc FM's old source and the default; Osc 1..3, itself counting as Prev; Noise: white noise drawn per voice on the control grid) choose what Osc FM and the new warps hear. Warps 8..10: **Osc RM** (the unwarped stack times the source's mono copy: 1 − a + a·m), **Osc AM** (1 − a/2 + a·m/2, the carrier kept), **Osc sync** (each copy's phase is the source's matching copy's phase times its speed over the source's, times 1 + 7·amount: stateless, so any block size gives the same; each restart's jump band-limited by a two-sample polyBLEP, none at Eco). The FM ratio of Osc FM is the source oscillator's own tuning. Oscillators render 1, 2, 3: a later source is heard one control period (16 frames) late, as Osc FM's ring always was; sync reads the source's phase, never late.
      - **Spectral and additive warps** (11..18: Stretch, Shift, Odd/even, Formant, Smear, Disperse, Low/high cut, Comb; `engine/src/flagship_banks.*`): each table and mode is resynthesised once (an FFT of every frame's top mip level, the warp on its harmonics, the loudness kept) at 8 amounts into an ordinary mipmapped table, its frames resampled to at most 16. An oscillator reads it as any table: two frames on one of the 8 amounts (the cost of an unwarped read), four between them (about 1.5x). Banks are shared by every instance (a cache of 32 slots keyed by table and mode; about 0.7 MB for a 4-frame table, 2.75 MB for 16 frames) and built off the audio thread by one worker thread (started at the first activation; it wakes every 50 ms and frees a bank nobody held for about 10 s). Live, an oscillator plays unwarped for the few tens of milliseconds until its bank is ready; an offline render (`BuildingOffline`: exports, the tests) builds it on the spot, so a render never depends on the worker's timing. `table` gains **Additive** (index 6, after User): eight rule-made spectra (saw, square, odd-heavy, even-heavy, every third missing, drawbars 1-2-3-4-6-8, a soft bright one, a 20 % pulse).
      - **Filters** (types 9..13, `engine/src/flagship_filters.h`, zero-delay-feedback TPT after Zavalishin 2012): **Diode LP** (a diode-ladder-style 24 dB low-pass: four solved one-poles, asymmetric and first-stage clips; a character model, not a circuit's), **Sallen-Key** (12 dB, the state-variable form with damping 3 − K and a clipped band state: aggressive), **Phaser** (4 all-passes, 8 with `morph` ≥ 0.5, feedback from the resonance), **Neg comb** (the comb with negative feedback: odd harmonics an octave below the cutoff) and **Vowel** (the formant peaks over the dry signal). Comb and Formant were already there. The diode, Sallen-Key and phaser run both sides in one loop.
      - **Sources:** **LFO 4** (`lfo4_shape`, `_rate`, `_sync`, `_mode`, as the others) and **Macro 1..8** (`macro1`..`macro8`, 0..1, the instrument's knobs) appended to the source list (values 15..23, after Accent). The 15 appended parameters are not mod destinations (as `voice_mode` and the FM ratios).
      - **Presets:** 18 after the seven (`engine/src/builtin_flagship_presets.cpp`): Sync lead, Ring bell, AM sub bass, Noise breath pad, FM wobble bass, Stretched bell, Odd/even morph pad, Formant vox lead, Smeared air pad, Disperse zap pluck, Comb choir, Additive organ, Diode acid, Sallen-Key scream, Phaser strings, Vowel talk bass, Hollow comb pluck, Macro sweep keys.
      - **Cost** (`caw_bench SECONDS 320`, `engine/bench/raven_cost.cpp`, also in mode 300 and the `caw_raven_cost` ctest; µs per second of audio for 8 held notes against the reference workload, x86, 2026-11-19, a busy machine): default patch 11,900 (1.0.9: 12,100 by mode 200: unchanged), two oscillators of one copy 3,300; Osc RM 4,900, Osc AM 4,500, Osc sync 5,200; Stretch on a level 3,450, Formant between levels 4,800 (seven copies 9,500); Diode 15,600, Sallen-Key 9,900, Phaser (8 stages) 21,500, Neg comb 11,600, Vowel 9,100; LFO 4 and a macro 5,800. `flagship_heavy16` as it is 127,000 and with Raven's modes 292,000 per second of audio through the engine. Each has a ceiling about twice its figure (the default patch's at its 1.0.9 cost) so a regression fails. Tests: `engine/tests/flagship_raven_test.cpp` (each mode's behaviour, block sizes 1/37/64/512, bounded and finite, the banks' resynthesis within −80 dB and their sharing, live and offline, reference renders in `flagship_raven.cawref`) and `flagship_raven_rt_test.cpp` (no allocation on the audio thread).

- **The internal test signal (`org.caw.test.osc`, `engine/src/builtin_test_signal.*`):** an instrument for tests: every engine test plays it (or a built-in) where it once played a .caw source (#160); `engine/tests/test_signal.h` builds its tracks. Registered with `ModuleRegistry::addInternal`, so it plays by id (a layer's `instrument`, the component test suite, a wavetable target) but `builtinComponents()` and `caw_engine_components` never list it, and no browser shows it. Parameters, unsmoothed: `shape` (Sine, Saw, Square, Noise: xorshift32 seeded by `seed`, DC: outputs `level`, Impulse: every `period` s or once, Sweep: exponential to `sweep_to` over `sweep_time` s), `mode` (Free: one signal from Play, ignoring notes; Notes: one per held note, 64 voices, pitch from the note, level times velocity, cut on its off), `freq` or `note` (the Free pitch), `level`, `left`, `right`, `phase`, `seed`, `sweep_to`, `sweep_time`, `period`. Tests build tracks with it through `engine/tests/test_signal.h` (`testSignal`, `testSignalLayer`, `testSignalNotesLayer`, `testSignalProject`).
- **Retired drums (one drum instrument, 2026-11-11):** `org.caw.drum.kick`, `.snare`, `.hats` and `.kit` are gone from the engine; a project, a sample-pack request or `caw_migrate_instrument` turns one into `org.caw.drums` with its values mapped (no preset; [Drums, migration](#migration-of-the-old-drums)).
- **Retired synths (one synth, 2026-11-10):** `org.caw.synth.morph`, `org.caw.bass`, `org.caw.synth.acid` and `org.caw.synth.basic` are gone from the engine; a project (JSON or `ProjectSpec`) or a sample-pack request naming one plays Flagship with the matching preset, its old knobs mapped onto Flagship's (`migrateLegacyInstrument`, `engine/src/presets.cpp`: e.g. the bass's `env` becomes slot 1's amount, 5 × env octaves over the cutoff knob's 9.97; the basic synth's decay and release take its 5-time-constant fall to Flagship's 60 dB one, × 1.38; its resonance becomes the SVF q it had), and its instrument lanes move the Flagship knob an old knob maps to in the same units (cutoff, resonance, attack..release, glide or slide, shape, unison, spread, drive, volume, the basic synth's detune); a lane on any other old knob is dropped, and a sample-pack step on one is refused. `caw_migrate_instrument(entry_json)` (API 8) gives the app the same entry, the preset's name and the lane map; given an entry naming a `"preset"`, it fills the preset's values under the entry's own. A component entry in a project may name `"preset"` too: its values go under the entry's `params`.
- **Per-slot auto gain and output meters (API 39, 1.0.8):** `caw_engine_set_component_auto_gain`, `caw_engine_component_auto_gain` and `caw_engine_component_meter` (`CawSlotMeter`); the Duck variant (1.0.8 the Compressor's fifth, 1.1.0 the Ducker's first). [Per-slot auto gain](#per-slot-auto-gain-and-output-meters-api-39), [Duck](#the-duckers-duck-variant).
- **Merged effect devices (API 38, FX consolidation for 1.0.8):** `caw_engine_components` lists each merged device (the table in [Merged devices](#merged-devices-fx-consolidation)) with its first variant's descriptor, a `"variants"` array (every variant's own descriptor, in `variant` order) and its old effects' presets, migrated; the old ids it replaces are retired (not listed). `caw_migrate_component(entry_json)` turns an effect entry `{"id", "params", "preset"}` into its merged form: `{"ok", "migrated", "id", "params", "renamed": {old: new}, "dropped": [old...], "keeps_state"}` (a rack's old children too: `<slot>_fx`, `<slot>.variant`, `<slot>.<param>` and the routes' `modN_dst`); the app moves a lane or link on a renamed parameter, drops one on a dropped parameter and leaves the rest. `caw_engine_set_component_param` refuses a different `variant` (send it in the project). [Merged devices](#merged-devices-fx-consolidation).
- **At least 8 presets on every effect (1.1.0, 2026-11-19; `engine/src/builtin_more_presets.cpp`, rack chains in `builtin_fx_racks_presets.cpp`):** the devices that had fewer gained job-named presets, appended after their own so every saved name keeps its meaning (no existing preset or reference render changed). Each description starts with its purpose (Guitar, Vocals, Drums, Bass, Mixing, Mastering, Sound design, Lo-fi), and each device's last new preset has a playful name (the sound plan's "hidden preset" at the end of the list, unmarked, as Mimic's "Talking crow"). Now 8 each: the Limiter, Transient and Phaser (from none; `fx::appendedVariantPresets`, after a merged device's other presets), the Compressor's Down (8, after Up's and Bus's: 31 in all), the De-esser, Reverser, Stutter, Comb, Disperser, Multiband dynamics, Tape stop, Trance gate, Resonator, Formant and Ring mod (appended to their own lists in `ownBuiltinPresets`), the FX rack and Multiband rack (useful chains: a vocal chain, drum bus crunch, a dub-ish echo chamber; band polish, vocal de-harsh, a different effect per band), and the Sampler (`org.caw.sampler`, from none: playback, envelope and filter only, never a sample path). Tests: `every_effect_has_at_least_eight_job_named_presets_that_play` (`fx_dynsplit_test.cpp`: 8 or more per device and Down, unique names, the purpose first on the new ones, real parameters in range, music played at 120 BPM finite, below 4 and audible), `rack_factory_presets_build_and_sound` (8 or more, every child found strictly, every value a knob of the configured rack), `sampler_presets_set_playback_envelope_and_filter_and_play`, and the merged devices' preset-count tests counting the appended ones last.
- **Quality (API 8):** `caw_engine_set_quality(engine, CAW_QUALITY_ECO | _NORMAL | _HIGH)` (Normal by default; `quality.h`): the built-ins keep a pointer to their Engine's setting when they are built and read it while they play (one relaxed atomic load a control). Eco: Flagship caps each oscillator at 4 unison copies (laid out again for 4, so the detune and spread stay as wide) and renders its FM warp at the sample rate instead of 2x oversampled and folds every note with ADAA, where Normal and High fold high notes 2x oversampled (#197's hybrid) (both alias more; each voice keeps the path it started with until it falls silent, so a quality change never clicks a held note), the saturator turns its oversampling off, the reverbs (`org.caw.reverb.pro` and Flagship's) run two input all-passes a side instead of four and no line modulation (`lib::Reverb::setLight`), and the effects publish display data 30 times a second instead of 60. High: the saturator oversamples 4x (two half-band stages) where its knob says 2x. The clipper's Auto oversampling follows the setting (off in Eco, 2x, 4x in High) and Eco runs a chosen factor one stage lower, at most 2x (2x off; 4x and 8x at 2x); phase distortion reads its delay with linear interpolation in Eco (Hermite otherwise). The other oversamplers (EQ & Filter group, ring modulator, the rotary's drive) already run only in High; the Drums' are in the Drums section below. Eco's FFT work is the convolver's shorter response (2 s, against 3.5 s Normal and 5 s High: fewer partitions); its partition sizes set its declared latency, so no quality changes them, and no other built-in runs an FFT while playing. An export's own engine renders at Normal.
- **Silence costs little (speed phase 1, 2026-11-12):** besides sleeping components (above) and effects (`fx::EffectBase`: asleep after their idle time of silence in and out), the Engine's own silent work is skipped where the result is bit-for-bit what the full loop gives: a track whose instrument and inserts give all +0 writes zeros without its gain, pan and level sums; a settled send or key from a silent block adds nothing; a bus with settled volume and pan and no lanes runs one gain per side; the master loudness meter skips the K-weighting and true peak for silence once its filters and history hold 0, and the correlation meters skip their sums. The master DC blocker's decay, with silence coming in, settles at exactly 0 once below −240 dB (per sample, so on the same frame at any block size), so the master chain's effects, which sleep only on exact silence in, fall asleep within a second of a song's tail instead of about 3 s later ([performance](engine-performance.md#1-free-wins)).
- **The device buffer (API 8, `engine/platform/audio_miniaudio.cpp`):** `caw_audio_set_buffer_mode(engine, CAW_BUFFER_AUTO | _LOW_LATENCY | _BATTERY, recording)`. Auto opens the device with a 1024-frame period (about 21 ms at 48 kHz) while not recording and asks no size while recording; Low latency never asks a size; Battery always 1024 frames and miniaudio's conservative profile. A running device reopens (a short gap) when the size changes. With no size asked, AAudio's output takes the stream's own callback sizes (miniaudio's `noFixedSizedCallback`; `takesDeviceSizes` in `audio_miniaudio.cpp`), its burst on a low-latency stream, and so does any AAudio output on Android 11 and older, where the size never reaches the stream (below). **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, read 2026-11-13]** without that, miniaudio's fixed callback falls back to the stream's whole buffer capacity in one period (`ma_device_init_by_type__aaudio`: `periodSizeInFrames = bufferCapacityInFrames`, `periodCount = 1` when `getFramesPerDataCallback` is unspecified; `ma_device_init` sizes its intermediary buffer from it): on the Galaxy S22+ about 1950 frames (40 ms) rendered inside one burst's callback, so Low latency dropped out well under 100 % load (the Benchmark, 2026-11-13). AAudio's documentation recommends its own sizes for the lowest latency (`AAudioStreamBuilder_setFramesPerDataCallback`). WASAPI, Core Audio, ALSA and PulseAudio are not affected: miniaudio's period there is the backend's (the size asked, or its default) and the backend calls back with it. **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, read 2026-11-17]** the defaults with no size asked: WASAPI and ALSA 10 ms (`MA_DEFAULT_PERIOD_SIZE_IN_MILLISECONDS_LOW_LATENCY`), PulseAudio 25 ms (`ma_calculate_period_size_in_frames_from_descriptor__pulse`: "buffers of < ~20ms result glitches when running through PipeWire"), JACK the server's own period (`jack_get_buffer_size`). So on WASAPI Low latency asks for the endpoint's smallest period itself (below); elsewhere off Android it is that default unless a size is chosen. `caw_audio_buffer_frames` reports the callback size: the burst (`AAudioStream_getFramesPerBurst`) where the output takes AAudio's sizes, else miniaudio's fixed size. **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, `ma_open_stream__aaudio`, read 2026-11-10]** the low-latency profile asks AAudio for `AAUDIO_PERFORMANCE_MODE_LOW_LATENCY` (its MMAP fast path) and the conservative one for `PERFORMANCE_MODE_NONE` (miniaudio never asks for `POWER_SAVING`); a period is passed to `setBufferCapacityInFrames`/`setFramesPerDataCallback` only with `aaudio.allowSetBufferCapacity`, which the larger buffer sets. **Usage (#236, 2026-11-17):** every AAudio output opens with `AAUDIO_USAGE_GAME` (`cfg.aaudio.usage`; **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source]** `ma_create_and_configure_AAudioStreamBuilder__aaudio` passes it to `AAudioStreamBuilder_setUsage` where that exists, and `ma_device_reinit__aaudio` keeps it over a reroute). **[Fact, developer.android.com/games/sdk/oboe/low-latency-audio, read 2026-11-17]** "games should indicate usage `AAUDIO_USAGE_GAME` to take full advantage of latency optimizations, especially when connected to Bluetooth headsets". Side effects checked: **[Fact, AOSP `AudioAttributes.java` main, read 2026-11-17]** its volume stream is `STREAM_MUSIC`, the media volume, as `USAGE_MEDIA`'s; **[Fact, NDK r30 `aaudio/AAudio.h`]** `setUsage` is API 28 (older systems keep the default, media) and MEDIA and GAME are the only usages other apps' playback capture may take. The app requests no audio focus of its own and its background playback is the `mediaPlayback` foreground service, neither tied to the stream's usage. **[Assumption]** audio policy routes and ducks GAME as it does MEDIA (one product strategy); to check on the phone with another app playing. The Bluetooth codec is not chosen: `BluetoothA2dp.setCodecConfigPreference` needs `BLUETOOTH_PRIVILEGED` (#236). **Automatic buffer size (API 34, #236, off by default):** `caw_audio_tune_buffer(engine, start_frames)`, which the app calls about once a second while playing when `Settings.bufferTuning` is on (no Settings row yet), tunes only where the output has no size of its own, Low latency or Auto while recording (`tunesBuffer`; else it returns 0 and leaves the fixed 1024 frames, so the app keeps no size; `the_buffer_tuner_runs_only_in_low_latency_or_while_recording`), and sizes the AAudio output's buffer as Oboe's LatencyTuner does (`src/buffer_tuner.h`: two bursts up to the capacity, a burst more on each rise of `AAudioStream_getXRunCount`, a burst less after 60 s without one, never back to a size that underran on this stream; **[Fact, Oboe main `LatencyTuner.cpp`/`.h`, read 2026-11-17]** Oboe starts at two bursts, adds one per underrun rise and never shrinks; the low-latency guide above asks for two bursts). `AAudioStream_setBufferSizeInFrames` runs on the calling (control) thread under the reroute lock, never on the audio thread; a new stream starts again, told by a count of opens and reroutes (not the stream's address, which an allocator may hand back); a new playback starts the quiet time from then (the engine's Play count), so a pause is not counted as 60 s without underruns; behind the app (`caw_audio_set_background`) it waits. The app keeps the size per output kind (`Settings.tunedBufferFrames`) and starts the next stream on that kind there (`the_buffer_tuner_grows_on_underruns_and_shrinks_only_to_sizes_that_held`). **[Assumption]** AAudio runs a low-latency stream's callback on a high-priority (SCHED_FIFO) thread; a shared or Bluetooth stream's is time-sharing. Either way the callback gets an Android performance hint session, its system calls on a thread of its own ("Audio threads" below).
- **Each platform's low-latency path (#237, 2026-11-17, branch `platform-audio`):**
  - **Windows, Low latency (`CAW_BUFFER_LOW_LATENCY`, no size chosen):** before the open, `wasapiPeriods` (`audio_miniaudio.cpp`) reads the endpoint's mix rate, its shared engine's periods (`IAudioClient3::GetSharedModeEnginePeriod`, Windows 10+, through a vtable declared as miniaudio's) and its exclusive minimum (`IAudioClient::GetDevicePeriod`), on a client of its own that is never initialised; the open then asks the smallest legal period of at least 128 frames (`src/device_period.h`: a multiple of the fundamental from min to max; exclusive: the minimum device period rounded up to frames). **[Fact, Microsoft Learn "Low Latency Audio", updated 2025-03-26, read 2026-11-17]** by default every app gets 10 ms buffers and small ones need `IAudioClient3`; when one app asks a small period the engine runs every app on that endpoint at it; the inbox HDAudio driver takes 128 to 480 frames, and small buffers cost power. **[Fact, Microsoft Learn IAudioClient3::InitializeSharedAudioStream, read 2026-11-17]** the period must be a multiple of the fundamental between min and max (Windows 10+). **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, `ma_device_init_internal__wasapi`, read 2026-11-17]** shared mode uses `InitializeSharedAudioStream` only when WASAPI need not convert the rate (the engine's rate is the mix format's: `AUDCLNT_STREAMFLAGS_AUTOCONVERTPCM` makes it fail), else `IAudioClient::Initialize` at the asked period times 3, which Windows raises to its minimum buffer (**[Fact, Microsoft Learn IAudioClient::Initialize]**); so a rate other than the device's keeps Low latency at 10 ms (`wasapi.noAutoConvertSRC` would keep the small period with miniaudio's own resampler, a sound change, not taken). A size that does not open retries at miniaudio's default before any other fallback, and so does one that opens at another period (miniaudio's quiet fallback to `IAudioClient::Initialize`, about 3 × 144 frames of buffer, some 9 ms, instead of the engine period; the audio review): the device is closed and opened at the default, and the same check after a WASAPI reroute reopens it at its settings for the new endpoint. `caw_audio_status` gains `smallest_period` (API 34, with the buffer tuner; `frames` asked, 0 none; `source`: `shared_engine`, `device_period`, `refused` (asked, opened at another period, so opened again at the default) or `none`; all cleared at every open; the engine's `engine_default`, `engine_fundamental`, `engine_min`, `engine_max`, `mix_rate`, `device_period_hns`; zeros off WASAPI). Auto, Battery and a chosen size are unchanged. **[Assumption]** under 128 frames each callback's fixed costs dominate (the floor). Compiled by MinGW here; not yet run on Windows.
  - **Windows exclusive mode** is the existing off-by-default `CAW_AUDIO_EXCLUSIVE` (Settings › Sound › Exclusive mode), shared when refused; in Low latency it now asks the device's minimum period. **Hybrid CPUs:** the callback is not pinned: it is miniaudio's thread in MMCSS "Pro Audio" and opted out of power throttling (above). **[Fact, Microsoft Learn "Quality of Service", updated 2025-12-09, read 2026-11-17]** threads MMCSS tags for audio get the Deadline QoS ("high performance to meet media deadlines"), audible processes are High QoS, and "on platforms with heterogeneous processors, the QoS of a thread may restrict scheduling to a subset of processors"; the workers keep their E-core exclusion (`SetThreadSelectedCpuSets`). **[Assumption]** the scheduler keeps a Deadline thread on the performance cores; `caw_audio_threads`' `core_kind` shows where it ran.
  - **ASIO (research only, not added):** **[Fact, Steinberg press release 2025-10-15, read 2026-11-17]** ASIO moved "from a sole proprietary license option to a dual licensing model": Steinberg's proprietary licence or GPLv3 (**[Assumption]** from secondary reports of the SDK's FAQ: a GPLv3 product releases its own source under GPLv3). Neither is permissive (C.A.W is Apache-2.0 and takes no GPL), so ASIO stays out unless the owner signs Steinberg's proprietary agreement.
  - **Android:** nothing in the stream setup keeps LE Audio away: 48 kHz (the app's default, `Settings.sampleRate`), stereo f32, `AAUDIO_USAGE_GAME` (#236). **[Fact, developer.android.com BLE audio overview, read 2026-11-17]** LE Audio is in Android 13 (API 33); `TYPE_BLE_HEADSET` "describes the audio device type as a LEA device"; dual-mode headsets are the rule, and the system, not the app, picks the transport. The app's route model says which link the output is (`AudioEndpoint.bluetoothProfile` in `app/lib/platform/audio_route.dart`: `le_audio` for `TYPE_BLE_HEADSET` 26, `TYPE_BLE_SPEAKER` 27, `TYPE_BLE_BROADCAST` 30, `TYPE_BLE_HEARING_AID` 33; `a2dp` 8, `sco` 7, `asha` 23; **[Fact, developer.android.com AudioDeviceInfo, read 2026-11-17]** values and API levels 31, 33 and 37); the Latency page already shows the kind ("Bluetooth LE"), and a separate LE Audio label waits for the owner's look. **Exclusive (MMAP):** the existing `CAW_AUDIO_EXCLUSIVE` (off by default, `docs/decisions.md`) asks `AAUDIO_SHARING_MODE_EXCLUSIVE`; **[Fact, AOSP frameworks/av main `AudioStreamBuilder.cpp`, read 2026-11-17]** AAudio turns an exclusive request into shared when the device's MMAP exclusive policy is NEVER, uses MMAP only with `AAUDIO_PERFORMANCE_MODE_LOW_LATENCY` (so Battery never gets it), and opens the legacy path when MMAP fails; **[Fact, developer.android.com AAudio guide, read 2026-11-17]** exclusive streams "are likely to have lower latency, but they are also more likely to get disconnected"; **[Fact, developer.android.com/games/sdk/oboe/low-latency-audio]** OboeTester measured 20 ms round trip with every recommendation and 26 ms shared. The engine reads back what it got (`modes_read_back`). **BLE MIDI:** **[Fact, AOSP frameworks/base main `BluetoothMidiDevice.java`, read 2026-11-17]** `MidiManager.openBluetoothDevice` hands the device to the system's BluetoothMidiService, which opens the GATT itself and asks `requestConnectionPriority(CONNECTION_PRIORITY_HIGH)` and an MTU of 517; the app holds no `BluetoothGatt`, so there is nothing more to ask (`BluetoothMidi.kt`). Jitter is not measured yet.
  - **Linux:** **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25]** no native PipeWire backend; the default order tries PulseAudio (PipeWire's `pipewire-pulse` on most current distributions), then ALSA, then JACK. **[Fact, PipeWire 1.6.11 docs, `libpipewire-module-protocol-pulse`, read 2026-11-17]** `pulse.min.req` and `pulse.min.quantum` 256/48000 (5.3 ms) are the floor for a Pulse client, `pulse.default.req` 960/48000; **[Fact, `pipewire.conf`]** `default.clock.quantum` 1024, `min-quantum` 32. So Low latency on Pulse is miniaudio's 25 ms; a chosen size (Settings or `caw_audio_configure`) goes lower, down to that 5.3 ms floor. `CAW_AUDIO_BACKEND` (environment, unset by default; any name `ma_get_backend_from_name` knows: `jack`, `alsa`, `pulseaudio`, `wasapi`…) puts one backend first for the CLI and test runs (if that backend opens but its device doesn't, the default order is tried); on `jack` (JACK, or PipeWire's MIT-licensed libjack) the callback runs on the server's real-time thread at its period. **rtkit (research only, not added):** **[Fact, rtkit README]** the daemon is GPLv3+, its client reference BSD/MIT-style, over D-Bus; **[Fact, dbus `COPYING`, main, read 2026-11-17]** libdbus is "the Academic Free License version 2.1, or the GNU General Public License version 2 (or ... any later version)". AFL-2.1 is OSI-approved but not a plain permissive licence (it ends on a patent claim and is GPL-incompatible), so it is not "clearly permissive": the **[Proposal]** stays open for the owner (a hand-written D-Bus call to rtkit needs no library at all).
  - **Verify:** the Benchmark's live mode on each platform in Low latency (Windows: `smallest_period` and `period_frames` in the status, the dropouts; the S22+ with an LE Audio headset: the route's `bluetoothProfile`, with and without Exclusive mode); CI's `engine.yml` builds Windows with clang-cl, which compiles `wasapiPeriods`.
- **The device's choice, a click sound and a test tone (API 10, `engine/platform/audio_miniaudio.cpp`):** `caw_audio_devices()` returns JSON with the backend's name, whether it takes an exclusive request, and each playback device's name, default flag and native rates (shared and exclusive); it opens a miniaudio context, so the app calls it off the UI isolate. Each device has an `id`: the backend's device id as text (WASAPI's endpoint id string, ALSA/PulseAudio/Core Audio names; empty for AAudio and the null backend). **[Fact, Microsoft Learn "Endpoint ID Strings", read 2026-11-11]** two identical audio adapters have identical friendly names but unique endpoint ids, and an endpoint id survives restarts and a USB replug but changes when the driver is upgraded or reinstalled. `caw_audio_configure(engine, device_id, device_name, period_frames, flags)` keeps a device by id, else by name (both empty: the default; one not found opens the default), a callback size of 16 to 8192 frames (0: the buffer mode's) and `CAW_AUDIO_EXCLUSIVE`, and reopens a running device under the device lock (never on the audio thread; the app calls it on a background isolate, one call at a time). It lists devices only for a named choice and never on AAudio. When the choice does not open (at `caw_audio_start` or on a reopen, including a buffer-mode reopen), the system default opens shared at its own size instead, so Play is never silent: it returns 2 and the status has `"fallback": true` and `fallback_reason`; 1 means nothing opened and the device is closed. With an explicit size the buffer mode only chooses the performance profile. **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, `ma_create_and_configure_AAudioStreamBuilder__aaudio` (miniaudio.h line 39416), read 2026-11-11]** AAudio gets the size only when `aaudio.enableCompatibilityWorkarounds` is off or `ma_android_sdk_version() > 30`; the workarounds are on by default, so on Android 11 and older the chosen size is ignored and the output takes AAudio's own callback sizes (above; Settings › Sound shows what it gave). `caw_audio_status(engine)` returns what the device gave (backend, device and its id, internal sample rate, `period_frames` the callback size as `caw_audio_buffer_frames` has it, `periods` the buffer's periods at that size, `device_sizes` whether the output takes AAudio's own sizes, exclusive, low latency, `modes_read_back`, the fallback) and the request. Where the output takes AAudio's sizes: `DeviceRender::openVarying` chooses its path per callback (the FIFO for a size of whole blocks or `kFifoFrom` and over, or while it still holds frames; straight only once it is empty, so a switch drops or repeats nothing) and declares a block short of one as its most held back; the buffer is the stream's `AAudioStream_getBufferSizeInFrames` (read at open, after a reroute and behind the app), which `periods`, the output's latency without a timestamp and the input's round trip use (miniaudio's period count is then the whole capacity, which put recordings about 30 ms late); the input bridge's fill counts the stream's whole capacity as the output's callback, which also covers a rerouted stream (the bridge keeps the fill it opened with). A reroute (headphones, Bluetooth) rebuilds the stream with `noFixedSizedCallback` kept (**[Fact]** miniaudio 0.11.25 `ma_device_reinit__aaudio`, read 2026-11-13); miniaudio's rerouted notification sets a flag on its job thread, and the next status, latency or size call reads the new stream's modes, burst and buffer under its reroute lock (`refreshAfterReroute`). **[Fact]** (same source) the rebuilt stream is never given a size (`allowSetBufferCapacity` is not carried over), so an output opened at a fixed size (Auto's 1024) would get AAudio's own callbacks under miniaudio's fixed 1024, a whole 1024-frame render inside one burst's callback. So the notification also marks such an output for a reopen, and the next status, latency or size call reopens it at its settings, as a buffer-mode change does (`reopenAfterReroute`: `reopenLocked` on the same Engine, so the transport, project and position carry on; the route change was a gap already). That call takes `gOpLock` only with `try_lock`, so a poll never waits for another open, and no reopen runs on the audio thread or miniaudio's job thread. Not testable under the stub (`caw_tests` links `audio_none`): a device run confirms it. **[Fact, miniaudio 0.11.25 source, read 2026-11-11]** AAudio has no enumeration API and miniaudio lists only the default device (`ma_context_enumerate_devices__aaudio`); so on AAudio a device is chosen by its Android id instead, `aaudio:<id>` (`src/device_choice.h`, #242; the app reads the ids from `AudioManager`), for the output (`caw_audio_configure`) and the input (`caw_audio_configure_input`). It is passed as `ma_device_id.aaudio` to `AAudioStreamBuilder_setDeviceId` (**[Fact]** miniaudio 0.11.25 `ma_create_and_configure_AAudioStreamBuilder__aaudio`). Text that is not such an id (untrusted, from the settings file) opens the default. A chosen input that does not open falls back to the default, as the output does, with `fallback_reason`. **[Fact]** (same source, `ma_device_reinit__aaudio`: "Only doing rerouting with default devices") a reroute after a disconnect reopens the default. So the status reports each stream's own `routed_device_id` (`AAudioStream_getDeviceId`). An output, or an input, on another device than the one chosen is a fallback with its reason: Android routed it elsewhere, or it disconnected. Tests: `an_aaudio_device_is_chosen_by_its_id_and_junk_opens_the_default`. an exclusive request becomes `AAUDIO_SHARING_MODE_EXCLUSIVE` (`ma_create_and_configure_AAudioStreamBuilder__aaudio`) and WASAPI's exclusive mode returns `MA_SHARE_MODE_NOT_SUPPORTED` when refused; the engine then opens shared. AAudio may grant a shared stream or the legacy path for what was asked, and miniaudio does not read the modes back, so the engine reads `AAudioStream_getSharingMode` and `AAudioStream_getPerformanceMode` (NDK API 26, looked up in the `libaaudio.so` miniaudio opened) once at open, before the stream starts and on the opening thread, so no reroute can replace the stream meanwhile; `modes_read_back` says they were read, and a later reroute may change them. On WASAPI `exclusive` is what opened; elsewhere both are what was asked. `caw_engine_set_metronome_sound(engine, volume 0..1, CAW_CLICK_CLASSIC | _WOOD | _TICK)` sets the click (Classic 30 ms at 1760/1320 Hz, Wood 40 ms at 880/660 Hz, Tick 8 ms at 3520/2640 Hz) through atomics read at each click. `caw_engine_test_tone(engine, seconds)` mixes up to 10 s of 440 Hz at −12 dBFS after the master volume and the transport's fades and before the safety limiter (which follows the louder of the song and the sum), playing or not, with 10 ms fades (0 fades it out); where the master stage does not run (stopped, a count-in without previews) the tone goes through the same limiter state. `caw_engine_test_tone_sounding` is a start request the audio thread has not taken or what it published at its last callback, so a tone ended before any callback or too short to be a sample never reads as sounding; opening or closing the device drops it. The engine's sample rate is still fixed at `caw_engine_create`; the app's rate change applies at the next start (recreating the engine live would also mean reloading every native component and display that holds it). **[Assumption]** on Android a rate other than the device's own is resampled and may cost AAudio's low-latency (MMAP) path; Settings says so when the device lists its rates. Tests: `quality_test.cpp`, `the_test_tone_goes_through_the_safety_limiter`.
- **The built-in effects suite (#173, `engine/src/builtin_effects*.cpp`, and the ducker, #175):** thirteen stereo effects in original code on the Library's kernels (plus the Dynamics and Drive group's five, `engine/src/builtin_fx_*.cpp`, below), listed with the other built-ins. They share `fx::EffectBase`: knobs read every 16 frames on a grid that runs across blocks (any block size gives the same bits, tested at 1, 37, 64 and 500 frames), ramps between grid points for gains and mixes, and sleep: after silent input and output (below −110 dB) for their own time (a delay's time, a reverb's pre-delay plus 0.2 s) they clear their state at a grid point and say `quiet()`, and they wake on the first input sample that is not 0, where the grid restarts. Each publishes [display data](#display-data) about 60 times a second. An effect whose control has nothing to do while its knobs hold still can ask `EffectBase::knobsUnchanged` whether every knob is what it was at its last control (a block whose values are all constants is compared once, at its first control); the EQ uses it. Times, frequencies and rates taper (exponential knobs). Reference renders: `engine/tests/references/effects.cawref` and `stereo_tools.cawref`; costs: [performance](performance.md#effects-suite-2026-11-10).
  - `org.caw.eq` ("EQ"): 8 bands `b1_`..`b8_`, each `on`, `type` (Bell, Low shelf, High shelf, Low cut 12/24/48, High cut 12/24/48, Notch, Band-pass, Tilt, Low cut 96, High cut 96; the 96s appended 2026-11-11 for Slice EQ's slopes), `freq` 20..20000 Hz, `gain` ±24 dB, `q` 0.1..18, `channel` (Stereo, Mid, Side, Left, Right); `output` ±24 dB. Defaults: a 30 Hz low cut and an 18 kHz high cut (off), a 100 Hz low shelf, bells at 250, 800, 2500 and 6000 Hz and a 10 kHz high shelf, all at 0 dB, so the default passes its input bit for bit. Bands are trapezoidal SVF stages (`lib::EqFilter`): minimum phase, zero latency; cuts are Butterworth stages whose last Q follows `q`; a band switched on, off or to another type or channel fades over 5 ms. A control with every knob as before and no band fading does nothing (its bands and coefficients would come out as they are), and the output gain at rest is one multiply a sample.
    - **Per-band dynamics (1.1.0, appended after `output`):** each band's `dyn` (Off), `thresh` −60..0 dB (−24), `range` ±24 dB (−6: cut when loud; positive lifts), `ratio` 1..20 (3), `attack` 0.1..200 ms (5), `release` 5..2000 ms (120), `key` (Self, Key, Band 1..8). Only a bell, shelf or tilt is dynamic. Its detector is the band's listening filter (`eqListenFilter`, `eq_bands.h`: a band-pass at its frequency and Q; for a low shelf or low cut a 12 dB low-pass at its frequency, a high one a high-pass) on its channel of the EQ's input (Stereo and Mid hear the mid), of the optional input `key` (Key; unconnected: silence), or at another band's place (Band n: that band's type, frequency and Q); then a peak that falls over six periods of the frequency (1..100 ms). At each 16-frame control the gain offset moves toward `sign(range) × min(over × (1 − 1/ratio), |range|)` (`over` the level's dB above `thresh`) with the attack while deepening and the release while recovering, and the band's coefficients follow when it moves by 0.005 dB (`EqFilter::setGainFast`: the gain's powers only, the frequency's tan kept; back at 0 a full `set()`). Off by default, and while off every older path runs as before (1.0.9's renders unchanged, `effects.cawref`). Dynamic bands run even at 0 dB; the skip of unchanged controls (`knobsUnchanged`) still holds: only the dynamic bands' detectors and gains are worked at each control. About 15 operations a dynamic band-frame. The `key` input is an optional port (`optionalPorts`, connected by a patch), so the EQ stays a plain stereo effect that can run per note; the track sidechain picker does not reach it yet.
    - **Band solo (1.1.0):** `solo_band` (Off, Band 1..8; not automatable, modulatable or MIDI-mappable) plays the output through that band's listening filter. A component built for an offline render (`Engine::setProjectOffline`: exports and stems, `BuildingOffline` in `quality.h`, read as `EffectBase::offlineRender()`) ignores it, so solo never reaches an export.
  - `org.caw.compressor` ("Compressor"): `threshold` −60..0 dB, `ratio` 1..20, `knee` 0..24 dB (the soft-knee curve of Giannoulis, Massberg and Reiss 2012), `attack` 0.05..200 ms, `release` 5..2000 ms, `auto_release` (0.3× the release after a transient, up to 2× under held compression), `makeup` −12..24 dB, `auto_makeup` (on: adds half the reduction a 0 dBFS signal would get), `mix`, `lookahead` 0..5 ms, `detect` (Peak, RMS over 10 ms), `link` 0..1 (stereo link), `sc_hpf` 20..1000 Hz (the detector's high-pass; 20 is off), `sc_listen`. Latency: a fixed 240 frames (5 ms at 48 kHz), whatever the look-ahead, because a descriptor declares one latency; the look-ahead moves where the detector reads inside that delay. `sidechain` (Internal, External): External makes the detector hear the component's key (another track, see Sidechain keys above) instead of its own input; with no key it hears silence. It runs each 16-frame control period in passes (the rings, the levels, the static curve two samples at a time with its log as two-wide vectors and no log for a run below the knee, the envelope, the output), each sample's arithmetic as frame by frame.
  - `org.caw.limiter` ("Limiter"): `gain` 0..24 dB in, `ceiling` −24..0 dB, `release` 1..1000 ms, `lookahead` 0.1..4.9 ms, `true_peak` (a 4× interpolated peak estimate, `lib::TruePeak`). A sliding minimum of the gain each sample needs, an instant-attack release and a moving average as long as the look-ahead, so the gain is down when the peak leaves the 240-frame delay; then a hard clamp at the ceiling, so sample peaks never pass it. Latency 240 frames. The merged Limiter (1.1.0) appends `target_on` and `target`, a loudness target it only reports against ("Around the effects").
  - `org.caw.reverb.pro` ("Reverb"): the Library's FDN run wet only: `size`, `decay` 0.1..30 s, `predelay` 0..500 ms or `predelay_sync` (Free, 1/64 .. 1/4; up to 1 s), `damping`, `low_cut` 20..2000 Hz and `high_cut` 1..20 kHz on the wet, `width` 0..2, `mod` (line modulation) and `mod_rate` 0.05..5 Hz, `freeze`, `duck` (the input turns the wet down by up to 30 dB × duck: fully at 0 dBFS, not at all below −50 dBFS), `mix`. Tail: infinite (freeze); it sleeps once silent.
  - `org.caw.delay.pro` ("Delay"): `mode` (Stereo, Ping-pong, Dual), `sync` (Free, 1/32, then 1/16 .. 1/2 straight, T triplet and D dotted, 1 and 2 bars) and `time` 1..4000 ms, `sync_r`/`time_r` (the right side in Dual), `offset` ±50 % (the right side's time in Stereo), `feedback` 0..1, `low_cut`, `high_cut` (in the loop, on each echo), `drive` (soft saturation in the loop; 0 is clean), `mod_depth` (up to ±3 ms) and `mod_rate`, `duck`, `freeze` (the loop repeats exactly and takes no input), `mix`. Up to 4 s (a longer division is held there); 4-point Hermite reads; a new time glides over ~50 ms. Tail: infinite.
  - `org.caw.saturator` ("Saturator"): `mode` (Tape: tanh and a 15 kHz roll-off; Tube: asymmetric, even harmonics; Soft clip: cubic; Hard clip; Fold: a sine fold; Crush: `bits` 1..16 and `rate` 200..48000 Hz sample-and-hold, never oversampled; and since fx-dynamics, 2026-11-11, appended so saved values keep their meaning: Fuzz: tanh(x + x³), the negative side flattening at −0.7; Rectify: full wave, the octave up; Tri fold: a triangle wavefolder; Bit shape: `bits` amplitude steps at the oversampled rate, no sample-rate drop; Fat: tanh plus half its square, a strong second harmonic, Faturator-style), `drive` 0..36 dB, `bias` (asymmetry; its DC is removed), `tone` (a ±9 dB tilt at 1 kHz), `oversample` (Off, 2× by the Library's polyphase IIR half-band: no latency), `auto_gain` (−drive/2 dB), `output`, `mix`, `spread` 0..1 (the bias ±0.25 apart left and right: a wider, uneven drive, as Kilohearts Distortion's Spread), `dynamics` 0..1 (gives the output back the input's envelope: linked 5/80 ms followers before and after, their ratio in dB times `dynamics`, ±24 dB, at control rate; as Distortion's Dynamics). A mode or oversampling change dips the wet path to dry over 2 ms, switches with clean filters and comes back over 2 ms (no click). Factory presets: Warm tape, Tube glow, Fuzz box, Octave up, Wavefolder, Bit shaper, Faturate.
  - `org.caw.dynamics` ("Dynamics", `builtin_fx_dynamics.cpp`): one curve with two thresholds. `low_threshold` −80..0 dB with `low_ratio` 0.1..10 (above 1 lifts sound below it toward it, upward compression; below 1 pushes it down, expansion), `high_threshold` −80..0 dB with `high_ratio` 0.1..10 (above 1 compresses, below 1 is upward expansion), `knee` 0..24 dB (each threshold's quadratic knee), `attack` 0.05..200 ms, `release` 5..2000 ms, `range` 0..48 dB (the most it lifts or cuts), `detect` (Peak, RMS 10 ms), `in_gain` and `out_gain` ±24 dB, `mix`. The detector works in dB (linked peak, smoothed with attack and release); the upward lift is also never more than an instant-attack detector allows, so a hit after a quiet passage isn't spiked by the whole boost. Presets: Gentle glue, Upward lift, Squash both ways, Noise expander, Punch expander, Parallel smash.
  - `org.caw.multiband` ("Multiband dynamics"): three bands split by Linkwitz-Riley 4th-order crossovers `xover_low` 40..1000 Hz and `xover_high` 1..12 kHz (kept an octave apart; the low band and the dry path go through the matching all-passes, so with no compression the bands sum flat: tested ±0.05 dB), each compressed downward above `bN_down_thr` at `bN_down_ratio` 1..50 (50: ∞) and upward below `bN_up_thr` at `bN_up_ratio` 1..50 (lifts held to 36 dB and by the instant detector as above), then `bN_gain` ±24 dB; `bN_attack`, `bN_release`. `mode`: Multiband uses the band knobs; **OTT** (the default) uses fixed per-band thresholds, ratios, times and makeup in the style of the well-known OTT preset [Assumption: the values usually quoted for Ableton Multiband Dynamics' OTT preset; not confirmed from a primary source, 2026-11-11] and adds only the band gains. `depth` (dry/wet against the all-passed dry), `time` 0.1..10× (scales every attack and release), `upward` and `downward` 0..1 (scale each side's gain change; OTT's makeup follows the larger), `in_gain`, `out_gain`. Presets: OTT, OTT gentle, Upward only, Fast and crispy, Mix bus, Tame lows.
  - `org.caw.deesser` ("De-esser"): a Linkwitz-Riley 4th-order split at `freq` 2..16 kHz; the high part's linked peak over `threshold` −60..0 dB is held down at 6:1 (0.5 ms attack, `release` 10..500 ms), no more than `range` 0..24 dB. `mode`: Split turns down only the high part, Wideband everything (5 ms cross-fade); `listen` plays the high part. Presets: Vocal, Bright voice, Low esses, Hats tamer.
  - `org.caw.clipper` ("Clipper", `builtin_fx_drive.cpp`): `mode` (Hard; Soft: a quadratic knee `softness` 0..1 wide that meets the ceiling with zero slope; Analog: an asymmetric tanh), `drive` 0..24 dB, `ceiling` −24..0 dB, `oversample` (Auto: off in Eco, 2× Normal, 4× High; Off, 2×, 4×, 8× by cascaded half-bands, Eco running them one stage lower, at most 2×; a factor change cross-fades two chains over 256 frames), `master` "Clip to 0 dB" (ceiling 0 dBFS, output and mix ignored, and a last clip at the base rate so no sample passes full scale), `output`, `mix`. A mode change cross-fades the curves over 5 ms. Without Clip to 0 dB the oversampled output can overshoot the ceiling (the half-bands' phase: up to about 5 dB on clipped noise, measured 2026-11-11); clipping again at the base rate would bring the aliases back (a 7 kHz sine: aliases −15 dB at 1×, −48 dB at 2×, −53 dB at 4× and 8×, −18 dB if clipped again). Presets: Master clip, Drum smash, Soft round, Analog warmth, 808 grit.
  - `org.caw.phasedist` ("Phase distortion"): each side read from a delay line around a fixed 48-frame centre (its declared latency; the dry path is delayed as much), moved up to ±46 frames by the side's own signal: low-passed by `tone` 100..20000 Hz, divided by its own envelope as far as `normalize` 0..1 says (at most 40 dB of lift), plus `bias` ±1 and ±`spread` 0..1 (left up, right down), clamped to ±1, times `drive`² × 46 frames. A 4-point Hermite read (linear in Eco). `mix`. Presets: Subtle grit, Metallic, Warm bend, Wide phase, Dynamic.
  - `org.caw.gate` ("Gate"): `threshold`, `range` −80..0 dB, `ratio` 1..50 (an expander below about 4, a gate above), `attack`, `hold`, `release`, `knee`, `sc_hpf`, `sidechain` (Internal, External: keyed by another track, as the compressor). `org.caw.transient` ("Transient shaper"): `attack` and `sustain` ±1 (up to ±24 dB from two pairs of level followers), `output`, `mix`.
  - `org.caw.ducker` ("Ducker", `engine/src/builtin_ducker.cpp`, #175): plays a drawn duck shape, gain 1 − `depth` × `mix` × shape. Triggers (`mode`): **Audio** follows its key's audio (a key is required) through `key_hpf` 20..2000 Hz and `key_lpf` 200..20000 Hz (Simper's SVF, 20 Hz and 20 kHz are off) and a peak follower (0.05 ms attack, 30 ms release, per side): with `response` Trigger, the key crossing `threshold` −60..0 dB plays the whole shape once and it re-arms when the key falls 4 dB under the threshold after the attack has played (one kick, one duck, however long it rings); with Follow, the duck follows how far the key is over the threshold (full depth 12 dB over it), smoothed by the attack and release, held by the hold, per side and linked by `link` (Follow only: Trigger plays one shape for both sides, from the louder one). A NaN or Inf in the key reads as 0. **Notes** plays the shape on every note-on of the key's track, on the note's own frame (no detection, no latency); `velocity` 0..1 scales the depth by the note's velocity. **Tempo** (the default; no key needed) plays it on every `rate` division of the song's beat (1/16, 1/8 T, 1/8, 1/8 D, 1/4 T, 1/4, 1/4 D, 1/2, 1 bar, 2 bars; a bar is 4 beats) shifted by `offset` 0..1 of a division, only while the song plays; Play from inside a division, or a wake from sleep (its first non-silent input frame), ducks first on the next one (only a start less than a frame after a division's start ducks at once; a start exactly a frame after does not, whatever the block's start rounds the beat to, 2026-11-12). The shape: `attack` 0.1..500 ms down to full depth (a fixed concave bend), `hold` 0..1000 ms, `release` 1..4000 ms back up, bent by `tension` −1..1 (0 straight; > 0 stays low longer and swells back, the classic pump; < 0 springs back and settles), the bend (e^(6·tension·x) − 1)/(e^(6·tension) − 1). A retrigger starts the attack from wherever the duck is, so it never jumps; a new depth from `velocity` jumps only while nothing is ducked, and during a duck glides in over the attack. `lookahead` (Off, 1, 3, 5, 10 ms; Audio only) delays the main signal so the duck leads the key; its latency is reported only while it is on (`VariableLatency`, so the chain's latency follows the knob). `mode` and `lookahead` are not automatable or modulatable, since the reported latency reads their held values; a live change of either crossfades the old read point to the new one over 5 ms. `bands` (Full, Lows, Highs) ducks only below or above `crossover` 40..2000 Hz, split by a Linkwitz-Riley crossover (two Butterworth sections a side, so the bands sum flat in magnitude; Linkwitz, JAES 24(1), 1976); the split always runs, so a change of `bands` crossfades the two paths over 5 ms from current filter state. The key and split filters settle at 0 instead of decaying through denormals. `listen` plays the filtered key. Built-in presets (shapes; the trigger is left alone): Pump, Quick, Smooth, Gate. Original code; the follower and filters are the Library's. **[Assumption]** no free ducker does all three triggers with a drawn shape and a lows-only option; the owner's view (2026-11-11), not a survey.
  - `org.caw.filter` ("Filter"): `type` (Low-pass and High-pass 12/24 dB SVF, Band-pass, Notch, Ladder low-, high- and band-pass), `cutoff`, `resonance`, `drive` 0..24 dB, an LFO (`lfo_amount` ±4 octaves at 1, `lfo_rate`, `lfo_sync` Off .. 4 bars, locked to the song while it plays, `lfo_shape` the Library's seven, `lfo_stereo` up to half a cycle between the sides) and an envelope follower (`env_amount` ±4 octaves, `env_attack`, `env_release`), `mix`. `org.caw.phaser` ("Phaser"): `stages` 2..12 first-order all-passes, `rate`, `sync`, `depth` (±3 octaves), `center`, `feedback`, `stereo`, `mix`. `org.caw.flanger` ("Flanger"): `delay` 0.2..10 ms swept from 5 % to 195 % at full `depth`, `rate`, `sync`, `feedback`, `stereo`, `mix`. `org.caw.utility` ("Utility"): `gain` −60..24 dB, `invert_l`, `invert_r`, `channels` (Stereo, Mono, Left, Right, Swap), `width`, `pan` (balance), `bass_mono` with `bass_freq` 40..500 Hz (the side below it removed), `dc_block`.
- **Drums (API 11):** `caw_drums_info()`, `caw_drums_randomize(type, seed, amount, pad, current_json)` and `caw_drums_explode(entry_json)`, pure functions returning JSON ([Drums, C API](#c-api-for-the-drums)). Parameters in `caw_engine_components` may carry `"group"` (`ParameterDescriptor::group`, built-ins only).
- **The Creative and Pitch & Time effects (fx-creative, `engine/src/builtin_fx_creative*.cpp`):** eight more `EffectBase` effects in original code, registered by `fx::registerCreative`, with factory presets (`builtin_fx_creative_presets.cpp`, listed by `builtinPresets`), tests and reference renders in `engine/tests/fx_creative_test.cpp` (`references/fx_creative.cawref`). Synced timing locks to the song's frame while it plays (the beat rounded to a frame, so any block split gives the same steps) and runs free at the song's tempo while stopped; randomness is seeded. CPU, measured with callgrind on Linux x64 (`-O2`, stereo, 48 kHz, defaults, playing; instructions inside `EffectBase::process` per second of audio, 2026-11-11): tape stop 9 M, reverser 11 M, stutter 6 M, pitch shifter 26 M, frequency shifter 35 M, trance gate 8 M, bitcrush 7 M, ring mod 15 M (about twice that at High). **[Fact, kilohearts.com/products/kilohearts_essentials and kilohearts.com/docs/snapins, read 2026-11-11]** Kilohearts Essentials has 34 snapins; the Creative and Pitch & Time ones are Tape Stop, Reverser, Trance Gate, Bitcrush, Pitch Shifter, Frequency Shifter and Ring Mod, each covered below; the stutter is not a Kilohearts snapin (a beat repeat, after Ableton's Beat Repeat). The DSP sources are named from the books and papers themselves, cited from knowledge and not re-read this session **[Assumption: chapter numbers]**.
  - `org.caw.tapestop` ("Tape stop", Pitch & Time): `stop` 0..1 (the trigger: at or above 0.5 the tape stops, below it plays; a knob so a lane or modulation can trigger it; the UI's hold-to-stop pad sets it while held), `stop_time` 20..8000 ms or `stop_sync` (Free, 1/32 .. 2 bars), `start_time` 1..8000 ms or `start_sync`, `curve` −1..1, `mix`. A recording buffer (8 s plus room, set aside at activate) read at a speed v: stopping, v = (1 − x)^p over the stop, p = 4^curve (0 a straight ramp, > 0 drops fast and drags, < 0 holds and falls late); the last 5 % of the speed fades the level out; stopped, silence; on release the motor starts on the tape under the head, v = 1 − (1 − x)^p, and once at speed the lagging tape crossfades back to the live input over 12 ms. A stop pressed during a start (or the reverse) carries on from the speed it has. A stopped tape stays stopped through sleep. Playing, it passes its input bit for bit. *Kilohearts' Tape Stop:* Play (the motor state), Stop Time, Start Time, Curve; what makes it good is the one big switch with musical curves. Technique: varispeed playback, a read pointer moving at speed v through a delay line (DAFX, 2nd ed., Zölzer (ed.), Wiley 2011, time-segment processing), cubic between samples (Niemitalo, "Polynomial Interpolators for High-Quality Resampling of Oversampled Audio", 2001).
