  - `org.caw.reverser` ("Reverser", Pitch & Time): `sync` (Free, 1/32 .. 2 bars; default 1/4), `time` 20..2000 ms when free, `crossfade` 0..50 % of a chunk, `mix`. The input is cut into chunks (at most 2 s); at each boundary the chunk just recorded starts playing backwards, so the output is the input reversed one chunk late (the effect, not a latency it reports). Each backwards read is a grain with equal-power fades in and out (sin and cos) over the crossfade, the old one fading out past its chunk's start as the new one fades in. *Kilohearts' Reverser:* Delay time with Sync, Crossfade (in percent of the section) and Mix; good because one knob gives the swell-into-the-beat sound. Technique: the reverse delay of time-segment processing (DAFX ch. 6) with granular overlap-add fades (Roads, "Microsound", MIT Press 2001).
  - `org.caw.stutter` ("Stutter", Pitch & Time): `interval` (1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 4 bars: how often a repeat may start), `offset` 0..15 sixteenths into it, `division` (1/64 .. 1/2: the slice), `length` (1/16 .. 2 bars: how long a repeat lasts), `chance` 0..1, `seed` 0..9999, `decay` 0..1 (repeat n is (1 − decay)^n as loud), `pitch` −12..12 semitones per repeat (repeat n reads the slice at 2^(n·pitch/12), cubic), `gate` 0.1..1 of each slice sounding, `hold` (at or above 0.5: repeat now, for as long as held), `mix`. The roll for interval i is a hash of (seed, i), so a song repeats in the same places every time, wherever playback starts. The first slice plays through and is recorded (3 s set aside), so going in needs no fade; repeats have 1.5 ms edges and going out crossfades back to the input. Technique: a beat-repeat sampler with a resampled read.
  - `org.caw.pitchshift` ("Pitch shifter", Pitch & Time): `semitones` −24..24 (whole), `cents` ±100, `grain` 10..200 ms (not automatable or modulatable: it sets the latency), `jitter` 0..1 (random detune up to ±35 cents, a new target each half grain), `mix`. Two read taps sweep a grain-long delay at speed 1 − ratio, half a grain apart, each faded by sin² so the two always sum to one; with no shift and no jitter it settles on one tap half a grain back (no comb). **Latency:** half a grain plus one frame (60 ms: 1441 frames at 48 kHz), reported per instance (`VariableLatency`), and the dry signal is delayed the same so the mix doesn't comb; a new grain size fades out and in over 5 ms. Eco reads linearly, Normal and High cubically. A formant-preserving mode is not built: it needs a spectral-envelope correction (LPC or cepstrum in an FFT shifter) or PSOLA with pitch detection, more latency and CPU than this two-tap design; a choice for the owner (`decisions.md`). *Kilohearts' Pitch Shifter:* Pitch (semitones), Jitter, Grain Size, Mix; good because it is cheap and sounds musical on chords at small shifts. Technique: the delay-line-modulation pitch shifter (DAFX ch. 6–7).
  - `org.caw.freqshift` ("Frequency shifter", Pitch & Time): `shift` ±2000 Hz, `direction` (Up: the upper sideband; Down: the lower; Both: up on the left, down on the right), `spread` 0..200 Hz (left shift − spread/2, right + spread/2), `feedback` 0..0.95 through `delay` 1..1000 ms (the loop soft-limited by tanh: each pass shifts again, the barber-pole), `mix`. Single-sideband modulation: I cos − Q sin (up) or I cos + Q sin (down), with I, Q from a 90° all-pass pair made from the Library's polyphase half-band (Valenzuela and Constantinides, IEE Proc. G, 1983) moved by a quarter of the sample rate (each section's coefficient negated, one branch a frame late; Harris, "Multirate Signal Processing for Communication Systems", 2004): 12 sections, transition 20 Hz, so the phase difference stays within 0.1° from 20 Hz to 22 kHz at 48 kHz (measured) and the other sideband is at least 50 dB down from 40 Hz to 15 kHz (tested). IIR: no latency. *Kilohearts' Frequency Shifter:* one Shift knob; good for detuned, inharmonic and barber-pole sounds no pitch shifter makes. Technique: Bode's frequency shifter (DAFX ch. 3, modulators).
  - `org.caw.trancegate` ("Trance gate", Creative): `steps` (8, 16, 32), `rate` (1/32, 1/16 T, 1/16, 1/8 T, 1/8, 1/4 per step), `s1`..`s32` step levels 0..1 (0 off; the drawable pattern), `attack` 0.1..200 ms, `decay` 1..2000 ms (to about −26 dB of the way to the sustain), `sustain`, `release` 1..1000 ms, `length` 0.05..1 (the gate's share of a step before its release), `swing` 0..1 (every second step up to half a step late), `legato` ("Tie steps": a step following a sounding one doesn't retrigger, and the gate carries on into a next step that is on), `mix` (how far the closed gate cuts). Each step that is on starts an ADSR from where the envelope is, its peak the step's level; the gain is 1 − mix + mix·envelope. 42 parameters. *Kilohearts' Trance Gate:* eight pattern slots, a step editor (toggle, drag to tie), Length, ADSR, Mix and Resolution; good because the pattern is drawn, not typed. Technique: a step sequencer driving an amplitude envelope.
  - `org.caw.bitcrush` ("Bitcrush", Creative): `bits` 1..24 (fractional: 2^(bits − 1) levels each side of 0), `rate` 200..48000 Hz (sample-and-hold), `jitter` 0..1 (each hold period stretched or squeezed by up to 90 %, seeded), `dither` 0..1 (TPDF of up to one step; Lipshitz, Wannamaker and Vanderkooy, JAES 40(5), 1992), `adc` ("Anti-alias") and `dac` ("Anti-image") 0..1 (4-pole Butterworth low-passes from the host's top down to just under the crushed Nyquist, out of the path at 0), `output` −24..12 dB, `mix`. At 24 bits and 48 kHz it is transparent (within 1e-6). Clearly different from the saturator's Crush mode (a plain bits-and-rate stage after drive): it models a converter pair; quality does not change it (the aliasing is the sound). *Kilohearts' Bitcrush:* Rate (down to 200 Hz), Bits, ADC Q and DAC Q (the conversion quality: low ADC Q gives aliasing in the lows, low DAC Q in the highs), Dither, Mix; good because the two Qs choose which grit you keep.
  - `org.caw.ringmod` ("Ring mod", Creative, keyed: a stereo `sidechain` input): `source` (Sine, Triangle, Square, Saw, Noise, Sidechain), `freq` 0.1..8000 Hz (noise: its low-pass cutoff), `spread` 0..1 (left down and right up by up to a semitone), `bias` 0..1 (carrier b + (1 − b)·c: toward AM, at 1 the input passes), `rectify` −1..1 (folds the carrier's negative half up, or its positive half down), `mix`. Square and saw are band-limited by PolyBLEP (Välimäki and Huovilainen, IEEE Signal Processing Magazine 24(2), 2007). Sidechain multiplies by the key another track sends (silence without one; 1.1.0: through the shared `sc_hpf`, `sc_lpf` and `sc_listen`, appended); in the app a key turns `source` to Sidechain, and removing it back to Sine. At High quality the product runs at twice the rate (the Library's half-band, no latency), so sums above Nyquist fold back far less (tested). *Kilohearts' Ring Mod:* Bias, Rectify, Mix, Frequency, Spread and an internal oscillator or noise generator, or its sidechain input; good for metallic, robotic and tremolo sounds from one device.
- **The EQ & Filter group (fx-filters, 2026-11-11; `engine/src/builtin_fx_filters*.cpp`, tests `engine/tests/fx_filters_test.cpp`, references `references/fx_filters.cawref`):** nine more stereo effects on `fx::EffectBase`, for the effect browser's "EQ & Filter" tab (owner, 2026-11-11: "any effect you would reasonably want is already there", modelled on Kilohearts' snapins). Original code; the techniques are described below in our words. Every one is tested at 1, 37, 64 and 500-frame blocks (same bits), with random and extreme knobs on loud music, a full-scale square and DC (finite, under +46 dB), for sleep and wake, its display layout, its presets (3 to 8, each value in range) and its response; subnormal tails are flushed on the 16-frame control grid so the flush, too, is block-size independent. Kilohearts research: [Fact] the snapin list and descriptions on [kilohearts.com Essentials](https://kilohearts.com/products/kilohearts_essentials) and the [snapin manual](https://kilohearts.com/docs/snapins), read 2026-11-11 (Comb Filter, Filter, Formant Filter, Ladder Filter, Nonlinear Filter, Resonator and 3-Band EQ are free Essentials; Disperser, Slice EQ, Carve EQ and Filter Table are paid). Costs below are callgrind instructions per second of stereo audio at 48 kHz, in `EffectBase::process` only, with the test's busy settings (2026-11-11, x86-64 GCC Release; `org.caw.filter` at its defaults for scale: 14.4 M).
  - `org.caw.ladder` ("Ladder filter"). *Kilohearts' Ladder Filter:* cutoff, resonance ("high values will make the filter resonate at the cutoff frequency"), topology (transistor or diode ladder; the diode rolls off more gently), Saturate with Drive and Bias; its appeal is vintage warmth and self-oscillation. *Ours:* `mode` (Low-pass, Band-pass, High-pass), `slope` (12 dB, 24 dB), `cutoff` 20..20000 Hz, `resonance` 0..1 (feedback 0..4.4; it self-oscillates from about 0.91 and stays bounded), `drive` 0..36 dB, `bias` −1..1 (tilts the saturation: even harmonics), `comp` "Bass comp" 0..1 (makes up the passband the feedback takes away, as the input gain 1 + comp·k), `mix`, `output`. *Technique:* Zavalishin's zero-delay-feedback (TPT) ladder ([The Art of VA Filter Design, rev. 2.1.0, ch. 5](https://www.native-instruments.com/fileadmin/ni_media/downloads/pdf/VAFilterDesign_2.1.0.pdf), checked 2026-11-11): four one-pole TPT stages, the feedback loop solved for the current sample. The first stage's input is w = T(a − k·y4) with T a tanh shifted by the bias; since y4 is linear in w, the equation is solved each sample by the linear answer and two Newton steps (the slope is ≥ 1, so it converges), with filter.hpp's rational `softClip` standing in for tanh (no exp per sample). Outputs by pole mixing (12 dB: y2, 2w·H(1−H), w(1−H)²; 24 dB: y4, 4w·H²(1−H)², w(1−H)⁴), each unity mid-passband; the feedback always comes from y4, so every mode peaks at the cutoff. A mode or slope change crossfades between the two taps over 5 ms (both come from the same state). High quality runs it 2x oversampled (the Library's half-band). Distinct from `org.caw.filter`'s ladder types (input-stage soft clip only, no drive into the loop, no 12 dB or bias, no oversampling). Tests: −60..−80 dB three octaves above at 24 dB, −30..−45 at 12 dB; a tap at full resonance rings on at 220, 1000 and 3000 Hz within 4 % and under 4.0, and dies away at 0.85; drive 24 dB adds over 10 % distortion where 0 dB adds under 5 %. Display (9): see the table. Presets: Acid squelch, Warm low-pass, Self-oscillation, Fat drive, Thin band, Airy high-pass. Cost 22.5 M (Normal), 60.4 M (High, 2x).
  - `org.caw.nlfilter` ("Nonlinear filter"). *Kilohearts' Nonlinear Filter:* type (all-pass, low-, band-, high-pass, notch), cutoff, Q, drive ("overdrives the filter, which makes the effect of the nonlinear behaviour more prominent"), mode (clean "does not color the signal"; the others' names are not given on the page) [Fact, read 2026-11-11]. *Ours:* `type` (Low-pass, Band-pass, High-pass, Notch, All-pass), `cutoff`, `q` 0.5..25, `drive` 0..36 dB, `shape` (Clean, Warm: tanh, Diode: soft one way and saturating at 0.6 the other, Hard: clip, Fold: a sine fold, Rectify: a lopsided bent curve), `mix`, `output`. *Technique:* a TPT state-variable filter (Zavalishin, ch. 4) whose band-pass integrator output passes the shape before it updates its state and feeds the low-pass, the place an OTA integrator saturates; the band is shaped at √k times its level (k = 1/Q), so a high Q saturates sooner but not Q times sooner. Every shape has slope 1 at 0, so quiet signals hear the clean filter. With a shape the driven input meets a soft ceiling at 4 and half the drive's dB is taken back (Clean takes it all back, being linear). A type or shape change fades to a freshly cleared second filter over 5 ms. High quality oversamples 2x. Tests: Clean is the analog SVF (−3 dB at the cutoff with Q 0.707, the all-pass flat within 0.2 dB); every shape stays bounded and differs from Clean at drive 30 dB and Q 20. Presets: Analog warmth, Screaming band, Diode growl, Folded metal, Hard notch, Clean all-pass. Cost 23.1 M, 53.9 M at High.
  - `org.caw.comb` ("Comb filter"). *Kilohearts' Comb Filter:* cutoff (the peaks' spacing), polarity Plus (a peak at 0 Hz) or Minus (a trough), Stereo (the right side's polarity flipped: wide yet it folds back to the original in mono), mix; feed-forward, "hollow out the sound". *Ours* adds the feedback comb: `tune` (Hz, Note), `freq` 20..5000 Hz or `note` 12..108 with `fine` ±100 ct, `feedback` −0.99..0.99 (negative: peaks at the odd multiples of f/2, hollow, an octave down), `damping` (a one-pole low-pass in the loop), `polarity` (Plus, Minus: flips the delayed path and the loop together, so peaks and troughs trade places), `stereo` (Same, Flip right), `spread` (the right side up to 30 ct sharper), `mix`, `output`. *Technique:* the textbook comb (Smith, [Physical Audio Signal Processing, "Feedback Comb Filters"](https://ccrma.stanford.edu/~jos/pasp/Feedback_Comb_Filters.html), CCRMA; Zölzer, DAFX, ch. 3): out = ½(x + p·d), d the line one period back, the line fed x + p·fb·LP(d); a 4-point Hermite fractional read, the delay gliding over about 20 ms (a retune sweeps, never clicks), the loop low-pass's own lag ((1 − a)/a samples) taken off the line so it stays in tune, and the output scaled by peak^−0.7 with peak = (2 − |fb|)/(2(1 − |fb|)) (high feedback rings about 4.5 dB over the dry level at 0.9). Tests: the first echo one period on (in Hz and by note), Plus/Minus swapping peaks and troughs by more than 15 dB, negative feedback ringing at f/2, the damped comb's period exact to a sample. Presets: Metallic, Hollow, Flange static, Tuned A, Wide comb. Cost 15.8 M.
  - `org.caw.resonator` ("Resonator"). *Kilohearts' Resonator:* pitch, decay, intensity, timbre (all harmonics like a saw, or odd like a square), mix. *Ours:* `mode` (String: harmonics 1..16; Odd string; Modal: a struck free-free bar's modes, ratios (x_n/x_1)² from the roots of cos x·cosh x = 1, 1, 2.756, 5.404, 8.933…; Chord: four chord tones with four harmonics each; Scale: sixteen notes of a scale), `chord` (Major, Minor, Sus4, Major 7, Minor 7, Power, Octaves), `scale` (Major, Minor, Major penta, Minor penta, Dorian, Harmonic minor), `note` 24..96, `fine`, `decay` 0.05..10 s (T60 at the root), `brightness` (higher modes ring as long and as loud at 1; at 0 they fall off as rank^−1.4 and decay as (f1/f)^0.9), `spread` (odd and even modes panned apart, the right bank up to 8 ct sharp), `mix`, `output`. *Technique:* modal synthesis (Smith, PASP, ["Modal Synthesis"](https://ccrma.stanford.edu/~jos/pasp/Modal_Representation.html)): sixteen TPT band-passes in parallel per side, Q = π·f·T60/ln 1000 (a two-pole resonance's envelope falls 60 dB in T60), each mode's band output × √k so a broad input gives each about the same power whatever its decay; the pitch glides over 30 ms; a safety ceiling on the bank (instant down, 150 ms back) keeps a held note at a resonance under 0 dBFS. Both sides run as one two-wide vector (`Pair`, `src/stereo_pair.h`). [Assumption] the bar ratios are beam theory, computed here, not checked against Fletcher & Rossing's table. Tests: modal at A3 rings at 220 Hz and falls 17..32 dB in 0.4 s at a 1 s decay; every mode stays under the ceiling on a full-scale tone at its root. Presets: Plucked string, Hollow square, Glass bell, Minor chord, Pentatonic wash, Short body. Cost 58.3 M.
  - `org.caw.formant` ("Formant filter"). *Kilohearts' Formant Filter:* a vowel selector pane (an XY pane of vowel symbols whose axes take modulation), Q, Lows and Highs (let the lows or highs through); "boost two frequencies to mimic the sounds of different vowels". *Ours:* `vowel_x`, `vowel_y` (the pad: A at 0.5/0.85, E 0.15/0.55, I 0.1/0.1, O 0.85/0.55, U 0.9/0.1, front to the left as on the IPA chart), `shift` "Gender / shift" ±12 st (all formants), `q` 1..30, `lows`, `highs` (the dry signal low-passed under F1 or high-passed over F3), `mix`, `output`. *Technique:* the source-filter model (Fant, Acoustic Theory of Speech Production, 1960): three band-passes in parallel at the first three formants (the Library's `kVowelFormantsHz`, [Assumption] rounded textbook values), levels 2, 1.4 and 0.9 (F1 +6 dB, so a vowel keeps about the level that went in); the pad morphs by inverse-distance weights (1/(d² + 10⁻⁵)²) averaged in log frequency, landing exactly on a vowel at its point. Tests: A peaks at F1 and is 10 dB over at 2290 Hz, I the other way round; +12 st moves the peak an octave; Lows adds 10 dB at 80 Hz. Presets: Ahh, Ooh, Eee, Deep voice, Small voice, Talk box. Cost 24.9 M.
  - `org.caw.disperser` ("Disperser"). *Kilohearts' Disperser:* "magic all-pass filter": frequency, amount ("increasing the order of the all-pass filter"), pinch ("the Q… concentrating the delay around the cutoff"); spreads transients over time without changing the gain. *Ours:* `amount` 0..1 (0..64 second-order all-pass stages, fractional amounts a blend of the chain's outputs either side, so it glides), `freq`, `pinch` (Q 0.3..9), `spread` (the right side's frequency up to an octave higher), `mix` (against the dry: notches). *Technique:* a cascade of TPT all-passes (x − 2k·band; Smith, [Introduction to Digital Filters, "Allpass Filters"](https://ccrma.stanford.edu/~jos/filters/Allpass_Filters.html)); stages leaving the chain are cleared, so they come back clean; both sides as one vector. Tests: flat within 0.05 dB at 100 Hz, 1 kHz and 10 kHz with 64 stages; an impulse's energy centre moves later with more stages. Presets: Laser zap, Boomy kick, Soft snap, Wet bass, Phasey. Cost 77.5 M at 45 stages (about 1.7 M a stage).
  - `org.caw.eq.dj` ("3-band EQ"). *Kilohearts' 3-Band EQ:* splits (two crossovers) and the low, mid and high gains. *Ours* adds DJ kills: `low`, `mid`, `high` −24..+12 dB, `kill_low`, `kill_mid`, `kill_high`, `low_x` 40..1000 Hz, `high_x` 1..12 kHz (held at least 1.5× the low split), `output`. *Technique:* Linkwitz-Riley 4th-order crossovers (two Butterworth TPT stages each; Linkwitz, "Active crossover networks for noncoincident drivers", JAES 24(1), 1976 [Assumption: citation details not re-checked]), the low band through the high split's all-pass (the LR4 sum is a 2nd-order all-pass, Q 0.707), so with every gain at 0 dB the bands sum flat; gains and kills glide over about 4 ms. Tests: flat within 0.05 dB at 40 Hz..12 kHz; kills −40 dB (low, high) and −25 dB (mid, at 800 Hz between splits). Presets: Bass kill, Mid scoop, Highs only, Warm boost, Telephone. Cost 25.2 M.
  - `org.caw.tilt` ("Tilt EQ"; no Kilohearts counterpart): `tilt` ±12 dB from the lows to the highs, `freq` "Pivot" 100..10000 Hz, `q` "Steepness" 0.3..1.5, `channel` (Stereo, Mid, Side; a change fades out, clears and back over 5 ms), `output`. The parametric EQ's Tilt band (`lib::EqFilter`): −tilt/2 below, +tilt/2 above. Presets: Brighter, Darker, Air lift, Weight, Wide sparkle. Cost 7.3 M.
  - `org.caw.geq` ("Graphic EQ"; Kilohearts' Carve EQ is "a 31 band graphic EQ", whose other tools the page does not list): `g1`..`g31` ±12 dB at the ISO third-octave centres 20 Hz..20 kHz, `range` 0..1.5 (scales every band), `output` −24..+12 dB. *Technique:* cascaded bells half an octave wide (Q 2.871) whose gains are corrected for their overlap by an interaction matrix, the method of Välimäki and Liski, "Accurate cascade graphic equalizer" (IEEE Signal Processing Letters, 2017; [Fact] cited as the origin of the closed-form cascade design by later Aalto papers, e.g. [Liski, "The Quest for the Best Graphic Equalizer", DAFx 2017](https://dafx.de/paper-archive/details/Ycir51R86FWcfPNqrjV2kQ), checked 2026-11-11): B[i][j] is band j's dB at centre i per dB of its gain (probed at 12 dB), LU-factored at activate, and each knob change solves B·g = t (about a thousand flops, only when a gain moved); one linear step, not the paper's second iteration. Bands above 0.45 of the rate are dropped; bells with no gain are not run; both sides as one vector. A post-EQ spectrum (64 bins) and the real curve are published while a UI watches. Tests: +6 dB at 1 kHz within 0.6 dB, all bands +6 flat at +6 within 0.6 dB, a ±6 zig-zag landing on the right side at 800 Hz and 1 kHz. Presets: Smile, Vocal presence, Radio, Less mud, Sub boost. Cost 53.3 M with every band moved.
  - **Slice EQ** (Kilohearts: "razor sharp digital filters up to 96 dB/oct", mid/side, an analyser) is covered by extending `org.caw.eq` (above), which already had mid/side per band and an analyser: Low cut 96 and High cut 96 (eight Butterworth stages) are appended to its types, so saved projects keep their numbers. **Not built:** Filter Table (a wavetable-driven filter; it needs a table editor and its own design) and the Ladder Filter's diode topology (its coupled stages need their own derivation; Zavalishin's ch. 5 covers it).
- **The Space, Modulation and Utility effects (fx-space, 2026-11-11; `engine/src/builtin_fx_space*.cpp`, `caw/library/convolver.hpp`, tests `engine/tests/fx_space_test.cpp` and `fx_space_rt_test.cpp`, references `references/fx_space.cawref`):** eleven stereo effects on `fx::EffectBase` in original code, registered by `fx::registerFxSpace`, with 4 to 8 factory presets each (`builtin_fx_space_presets.cpp`). Kilohearts' snapins are read from [kilohearts.com/docs/snapins](https://kilohearts.com/docs/snapins) (2026-11-11); of this group's, Ensemble, Haas, Stereo, Channel Mixer, Chorus, Reverb and Gain are snapins, while convolution, shimmer, tremolo, vibrato, auto-pan, rotary speaker and doubling are not in that list (they are in the brief). Cost is callgrind instructions per second of 48 kHz audio at the defaults (pink noise, 128-frame blocks, x86-64 Release, Normal quality; the `caw test-component` harness's own cost, measured as the channel mixer's, subtracted; 2026-11-11): about 142 M for the convolver, 109 M the shimmer, 53 M the doubler, 31 M the ensemble and the imager, 25 M the rotary, 10 M the vibrato, 8 M Haas, 2 M or less the tremolo, auto-pan and channel mixer (for scale, `org.caw.reverb.pro` 71 M). Every effect is tested for block-size independence through sleep and wake, finite bounded output at extreme knobs, falling quiet, its display layout, a reference render and no allocation on the audio thread (`caw_rt_tests`).
  - `org.caw.convolver` ("Convolver", Space): `source` (Room, Hall, Plate, Spring, Chamber, Cathedral, Gated, Metal tank, Cloud, Loaded file), `decay` 0.2..10 s, `size` 0..1 (stretch: a file plays 0.5x..2x), `damping`, `length` 0.05..1 (trim, faded over its last tenth), `reverse`, `predelay` 0..500 ms, `low_cut` 20..2000 Hz and `high_cut` 1..20 kHz on the wet, `width` 0..2, `mix`. *Impulse responses are generated by the engine* (nothing to license): decorrelated seeded noise under an exponential decay (−60 dB at `decay`) low-passed ever darker by `damping`, after each type's seeded early reflections (the noise-tail model of Moorer, "About This Reverberation Business", Computer Music Journal 3(2), 1979); Spring is repeated rising chirps (dispersion), Gated a burst cut short, Metal tank a burst ringing through four combs, Cloud a shower of sine grains on a pentatonic scale. *Or a WAV the user loads:* PCM 16/24/32-bit or float 32/64, any channel count (the first two kept), at most 10 s and 960,000 frames and a 16 MB file, every size checked as untrusted (`fx::decodeIrWav`); it is decoded when the chain is built, on the control thread, never on the audio thread. The saved state is the decoded response (16-byte header and floats); the state also takes the WAV's own bytes. *Technique:* partitioned FFT convolution in two sizes (the non-uniform partitioning of W. G. Gardner, "Efficient Convolution without Input-Output Delay", JAES 43(3), 1995, in its simplest form): the first 3,584 frames in 512-frame partitions (latency 512 frames, declared, the dry delayed to match), the rest in 4,096-frame partitions whose latency the head covers exactly, so the long tail costs an eighth of the multiply-adds; each partition size a uniformly partitioned overlap-save convolver with a frequency-domain delay line (F. Wefers, *Partitioned Convolution Algorithms for Real-Time Auralization*, PhD thesis, RWTH Aachen, 2015). The FFT is the Library's own deterministic, allocation-free radix-2 (`lib::Fft` in `convolver.hpp`): the engine's other FFTs (`analysis.cpp`, offline, on `std::complex` vectors; `tables.cpp`, internal to table building) allocate per call. A new response is built in slices on the audio thread's spare budget (about a quarter of a second for 5 s) and crossfaded in over one partition, so a knob never clicks or blocks; the response is up to 5 s at High, 3.5 s at Normal, 2 s at Eco. Display (73): see the table. Presets: Small room, Concert hall, Vocal plate, Surf spring, Stone cathedral, 80s gated, Reverse swell, Metal tank. **Loading a WAV while playing (API 14):** `caw_engine_set_component_state(engine, layer_id, index, data, size, version)` gives a playing component a new saved state: the chain is rebuilt on the control thread with every component's current values and states and this one's replaced (as `caw_engine_reload_component` does), then crossfaded in; 0 and nothing changed when there is no such component or it refuses the state. Other components in that chain restart their running state (a delay's echoes) with the swap. Tests: a response of taps comes out exactly after 512 frames in both partition sizes; the partitioned convolver matches direct convolution; every generated type sounds and decays; a WAV through the C API into a playing chain; bad files refused.
  - `org.caw.shimmer` ("Shimmer", Space): `size`, `decay` 0.5..30 s, `predelay` 0..500 ms, `damping`, `shift` (+12, +7, +5, +19, +24, −12 semitones), `amount` 0..1, `tone` 1..16 kHz, `low_cut` 50..2000 Hz, `mod`, `width` 0..2, `mix`. Built on the existing reverb kernel (the Library's 8-line FDN, as `org.caw.reverb.pro`; J.-M. Jot and A. Chaigne, "Digital Delay Networks for Designing Artificial Reverberators", AES 90th Convention, 1991) with its wet fed back into its input through a two-tap delay-line pitch shifter (70 ms window; Zölzer (ed.), *DAFX*, 2nd ed., Wiley 2011, ch. 6), band-limited and gently saturated, so each round climbs by the shift; `amount` sets the loop gain (at most 0.6, less for tails past 4 s, inside a tanh: it cannot run away). No Kilohearts snapin; distinct from `org.caw.reverb.pro` by the pitched loop. Display (7). Presets: Octave heaven, Fifths, Subtle sparkle, Infinite, Dark descent.
  - `org.caw.haas` ("Haas", Space). *Kilohearts' Haas:* Channel (which side to delay) and Delay. *Ours adds* a bass guard, level and a mono check: `side` (Left, Right), `delay` 0..40 ms, `guard` 20..1000 Hz (below it the delayed side stays undelayed: a complementary split that sums flat, so the bass stays centred), `level` −12..+6 dB on the delayed side, `mono_check` (sums the output to listen as a phone speaker would), `mix`. *Technique:* the precedence effect (H. Haas, "Über den Einfluss eines Einfachechos auf die Hörsamkeit von Sprache", Acustica 1, 1951): the earlier side places the sound, the delay reads as width. In mono it is a comb with notches at odd multiples of 1/(2 × delay); the display meters the correlation, the level lost in mono and the first notch (500/delay ms Hz). Display (7). Presets: Wide, Subtle, Extra wide, Mono check.
  - `org.caw.ensemble` ("Ensemble", Modulation). *Kilohearts' Ensemble:* Voices, Detune, Spread, Mix, Motion (patterns for the voices' modulation). *Ours:* `voices` 2..8, `delay` 3..40 ms, `detune` 0..50 cents, `rate` 0.05..8 Hz, `spread` 0..1, `motion` (Sine, Triangle, Random, Vintage: a fast shallow sine on the slow one, as bucket-brigade string ensembles had [Assumption: the circuits are not checked here]), `tone`, `mix`. Each voice a delay swept by its own LFO (phases spread around the cycle), read with 4-point Hermite interpolation, panned across `spread`; the depth is set from the cents and the rate (a sine of depth D samples at f Hz bends the pitch by at most 2πfD/sample rate), so the detune stays as set at any rate (J. Dattorro, "Effect Design, Part 2: Delay-Line Modulation and Chorus", JAES 45(10), 1997). The bigger, richer unit beside `org.caw.chorus` (rate, depth, mix only), which stays as it is; Kilohearts' Chorus (delay, rate, depth, spread, taps) is covered by the ensemble's controls. Display (13). Presets: Strings, Wide unison, Subtle thicken, Seasick, Shimmer pad.
  - `org.caw.doubler` ("Doubler", Modulation): `voices` (2, 4), `delay` 5..60 ms, `detune` 0..30 cents, `drift` 0..1 (each copy's timing wanders up to 3 ms on a seeded smooth-random LFO), `width`, `low_cut`, `high_cut`, `mix`. Double-tracking: copies of the mono sum, each late by its own time, pitched a few cents off by a two-tap shifter (a steady detune without a chorus's wobble) and panned out, the low end cut so they don't muddy the centre. No Kilohearts snapin. Display (7). Presets: Vocal double, Guitar wall, Tight, Sloppy.
  - `org.caw.vibrato` ("Vibrato", Modulation): `rate` 0.1..14 Hz or `sync` (Free, 1/32 .. 2 bars, locked to the song while it plays), `depth` 0..100 cents, `shape` (Sine, Triangle, Smooth random), `stereo` 0..1 (the right LFO up to half a cycle behind), `mix` (1: no dry). A delay swept by an LFO, its depth from cents and rate as the ensemble's, held to 12 ms (DAFX ch. 2, vibrato). No Kilohearts snapin. Display (6). Presets: Singer, Tape wow, Warble, Synced chorus.
  - `org.caw.tremolo` ("Tremolo", Modulation): `rate` 0.1..20 Hz or `sync`, `depth`, `shape` (the Library LFO's seven: Sine, Triangle, Saw up, Saw down, Square, Sample & hold, Smooth random), `smooth` (a one-pole of up to 15 ms on the gain, so a square's steps don't click), `stereo`, `mode` (Classic: the level; Harmonic: split at 800 Hz, the lows and highs moved in opposite directions, after 1960s amplifiers [Assumption: the 800 Hz split is ours, not measured from one]). Display (7). Presets: Amp tremolo, Harmonic, Chopper, Stereo pulse, Random flutter.
  - `org.caw.autopan` ("Auto-pan", Modulation): `rate` 0.05..20 Hz or `sync` (default 1/2), `depth`, `shape` (the seven), `smooth`, `offset` (the right side's phase), `width` (the source's own width folded to this first). Synced to the song's beat while it plays. Display (7). Presets: Gentle sway, Ping-pong, Triplet swirl, Wander, Dotted bounce.
  - `org.caw.rotary` ("Rotary", Modulation): `speed` (Slow, Fast, Brake), `accel` 0.25..4 (inertia scale), `balance` horn/drum, `drive` 0..24 dB (a tanh, 2x oversampled at High), `width` (mic angle), `doppler`, `mix`. A Leslie-style speaker after J. O. Smith, S. Serafin, J. Abel and D. Berners, "Doppler Simulation and the Leslie", DAFx-02, 2002: the mono sum split at 800 Hz by a 4th-order Linkwitz-Riley crossover; horn and drum spin at their own speeds (chorale 0.83 / 0.67 Hz, tremolo 6.75 / 5.8 Hz [Assumption: commonly quoted figures, not measured here]) and ease between them with light (≈0.7 s) and heavy (≈4 s) inertia; two virtual mics hear each rotor through a delay shortest when it faces them (Doppler, up to 0.5 ms horn, 0.2 ms drum) and loudest then. No Kilohearts snapin. Display (6). Presets: Chorale, Tremolo, Gritty organ, Brake, Guitar cab.
  - `org.caw.imager` ("Imager", Utility). *Kilohearts' Stereo:* Mid, Width, Pan and a stereo meter (balance and correlation; red where negative). *Ours is multiband:* `low_freq` 40..1000 Hz and `high_freq` 1..12 kHz (4th-order Linkwitz-Riley crossovers, the low band through the upper crossover's all-pass so the bands sum in phase; S. Linkwitz, "Active Crossover Networks for Noncoincident Drivers", JAES 24(1), 1976), `low_width`, `mid_width`, `high_width` 0..2 (each band's side scaled), `mid_gain` and `side_gain` −24..+12 dB, `output`. The display meters the correlation overall and per band, the balance, the level lost in mono and a 128-point mid/side vectorscope. At unity it is flat (tested). Beside `org.caw.utility` (gain, width, pan) and `org.caw.stereo.width`, which stay. Display (265). Presets: Master widen, Mono bass, Narrow, Air, Sides only.
  - `org.caw.channelmixer` ("Channel mixer", Utility). *Kilohearts' Channel Mixer:* Left to Left, Right to Left, Left to Right, Right to Right, each −1 (inverted) .. 0 .. 1. *Ours is the same matrix* (`left_to_left`, `right_to_left`, `left_to_right`, `right_to_right`), smoothed, with a correlation and balance meter. Display (4). Presets: Swap, Mono, Left only, Invert right, Side to mono.
- **Display data (#173, API version 6):** `caw_engine_component_display(engine, layer_id, index, out, max)` copies the latest floats a playing component published for its UI (addressed as in `caw_engine_set_component_param`; NULL or empty layer id: the master; `CAW_INSTRUMENT_INDEX`: the instrument) and returns the count: 0 when it publishes nothing yet or the engine is busy with a project update (it never waits for the control lock: `try_lock`), −1 when there is no such component. Components publish about 60 times a second through a lock-free double buffer (`caw/display.hpp`, [SDK](native-module-sdk.md#display-data)); reading allocates nothing and never blocks the audio thread (tests `display_test.cpp`, `publishing_and_reading_display_data_never_allocates`). Dart: `Engine.componentDisplay(layerId, index, Float32List)`. Each built-in's layout is under [Display data](#display-data).
- **Component testing:** `caw_component_test(request_json, trust_level, output_dir, input_dir)` renders one C++ effect (a test signal) or instrument (notes) offline and returns its measurements as JSON (see [Component testing](#component-testing)). The host gives a library's trust (the user's own choice for it); a request naming a `trust` is refused. Written WAVs are plain file names inside `output_dir` and a WAV test signal a plain name inside `input_dir` (NULL: none), so a request handed on from project data can't load code or touch files on its own. A test WAV is read only from a regular file of at most 200 MB. The CLI keeps `--trust` and paths, since its user types them. It blocks while it renders, so hosts call it from a worker, never the UI or audio thread.
- **The Benchmark (API 29, 30):** `caw_stress_scenarios`, `caw_worker_pool_submit_stress` and its readers, and the plug-in benchmark `caw_worker_pool_submit_component_bench` (API 30; see [The Benchmark](#the-benchmark)).
- **Sample packs:** `caw_sample_pack_render(request_json, trust_level, progress, cancel)` renders an instrument over notes, velocities and parameter steps to named WAV files in one directory and returns the result as JSON (see [Sample packs](#sample-packs)). It blocks while it renders, so hosts call it from a worker, never the UI or audio thread.
- Strings the engine returns are freed with `caw_free`. Only `caw_*` symbols are exported.
- Nothing throws across the API: running out of memory comes back as an error.

### Merged devices (FX consolidation)

Owner, 2026-11-17 (the merge table is in the private plans): "one really good device instead of five okay ones", for 1.0.8. Built 2026-11-18: the framework and every family's merges below, each variant the old class unchanged, so a migrated project renders the same bits; then (part F) the old ids retired. New DSP comes as further variants appended so saved indices never move: the first was the Compressor's Duck (variant 4, built 2026-11-19, [below](#the-duckers-duck-variant)), then 1.1.0's [new modes](#new-modes-110) of the Delay, Reverb, Drive, Bit distortion and Convolver (built 2026-11-19). 1.1.0 also re-cut the Compressor by the owner's rule (only true siblings merge): [the Talon/Hatch/Duck split](#the-talonhatchduck-split-110) moved Gate, Shape and Duck out behind a `talon_layout` marker and added upward compression, Bus, the expander, the pedal gate, Level and Carve; the EQ gained Bands' dynamics and solo, Linear and Match and the Filter its Wah (the table below); M/S, auto-filter and the other 1.1.0 modes come after.

| Device (id) | Variants (old id) | Optional ports |
|---|---|---|
| Compressor (`org.caw.compressor`, reused) | Down, Dynamics (`dynamics`), Up, Bus (1.1.0: Gate, Shape and Duck left; [split](#the-talonhatchduck-split-110)) | none; a real `sidechain` on Down, Up and Bus |
| Gate (`org.caw.gate`, reused again in 1.1.0) | Gate, Expand, Pedal gate | none; a real `sidechain` on each |
| Ducker (`org.caw.duck`, 1.1.0) | Duck (1.0.8's Compressor variant 4), Level, Carve | none; a real `sidechain` on Duck and Carve |
| Limiter (`org.caw.limiter`, reused) | Limiter | none |
| Transient (`org.caw.transient`, reused) | Transient | `transient`, `sustain` (the output split by its onset follower; they sum to it) |
| Drive (`org.caw.drive`) | Distortion (`distortion`), Saturate (`saturator`, its own `mode` kept), Clip (`clipper`), Phase (`phasedist`), Amp, Pedal, Bands (1.1.0, new DSP, [below](#new-modes-110)) | none |
| Bit distortion (`org.caw.bits`) | Crush (`bitcrush`), Bitwise (1.1.0, new DSP; its glitch ops 1.1.1, [below](#new-modes-110)) | none |
| EQ (`org.caw.eq`, reused) | Bands, Graphic (`geq`), DJ (`eq.dj`), Tilt (`tilt`), Linear and Match (1.1.0, new DSP, below) | `key` (input) on Bands: its dynamic bands' key; a real `sidechain` on Linear and Match (Match's reference) |
| Filter (`org.caw.filter`, reused) | Multimode, Ladder (`ladder`), Nonlinear (`nlfilter`), Wah (1.1.0, new DSP, below) | none (the old classes compute only the response they play) |
| Utility/Stereo (`org.caw.utility`, reused) | Utility, Width (`stereo.width`), Balance (`stereo.balance`), Imager (`imager`), Haas (`haas`), Mixer (`channelmixer`) | `mid`, `side` on Utility (they sum to it) |
| Chorus/Flanger (`org.caw.chorus`, reused) | Chorus, Ensemble (`ensemble`), Doubler (`doubler`), Flanger (`flanger`), Vibrato (`vibrato`), Rotary (`rotary`) | none |
| Phaser (`org.caw.phaser`, reused) | Phaser | none |
| Tremolo/Pan (`org.caw.tremolo`, reused) | Tremolo, Pan (`autopan`), Shape (`ducker`, the drawn duck; 1.1.0, 1.0.8–1.0.9 the Compressor's variant 3) | none; Shape's real `sidechain` and `key_notes` |
| Delay (`org.caw.delay`) | Digital (`delay.pro`), Ping-pong (`delay.pingpong`), Tape, Reverse, Multi-tap (1.1.0, new DSP, [below](#new-modes-110)) | none |
| Reverb (`org.caw.reverb`) | Hall (`reverb.pro`), Shimmer (`shimmer`), Room, Plate, Spring, Gated, Reverse (1.1.0, new DSP, [below](#new-modes-110)) | none |
| Pitch/Shift, Soar (`org.caw.shift`) | Pitch (`pitchshift`), Frequency (`freqshift`), Harmonizer, Octaver (1.1.0, new DSP, [below](#voice-devices-glint-soars-harmonizer-and-octaver-mimic-110)) | none |
| Exciter, Glint (`org.caw.exciter`, 1.1.0, new) | Exciter, Bass (new DSP, [below](#voice-devices-glint-soars-harmonizer-and-octaver-mimic-110)) | none |
| Convolver (`org.caw.convolver`, reused; merged in 1.1.0) | Convolver (as it was, the user's IRs), Cab (new DSP, [below](#new-modes-110)) | none |

**The EQ's and Filter's 1.1.0 modes** (`builtin_fx_eq_plume.cpp`, `eq_design.*`, `builtin_fx_filters_wah.cpp`; tests `fx_eq_plume_test.cpp`, `fx_eq_plume_rt_test.cpp`, references `plume.cawref`). Original code. Each is a new variant appended (EQ 4, 5; Filter 3), so saved values keep their meaning; its presets come from `fx::newEqFilterVariantPresets`. Costs are `caw_bench SECONDS 300` on the x86 dev box (µs of CPU per second of stereo audio, 64-frame blocks, 2026-11-19), each with a ceiling there (the phone budget / 2.5 [Assumption: the S22+ about 2.5× slower]; mode 300 exits 1 over one). Bands' dynamics and solo are above, in the EQ's entry.
- **Linear (EQ variant 4):** the bands' knobs (same ids as Bands, so they carry over), `output`, `lin_quality` (Eco, Normal, High; fixed by hand: it sets the latency), `solo_band`, and Match's knobs (below; `match` off). The static bands' summed magnitude (each band's `EqFilter::magnitude2` on the design grid) becomes a zero-phase FIR by frequency sampling, centred and Kaiser-windowed (β 6), played by `lib::PartitionedConvolver` in 256-frame partitions. Taps 2,048 / 4,096 / 8,192; latency taps/2 + 256 = 1,280 / 2,304 / 4,352 frames (27 / 48 / 91 ms at 48 kHz), reported per instance (`VariableLatency`, with the descriptor at Normal's). The lanes are mid and side: Stereo bands act on both, Mid and Side on one, and **Left and Right bands act as Stereo** (a pair of linear-phase lanes can't cross the sides). No dynamics (a FIR can't follow per-period gain changes). A moved knob designs the new FIR a slice per control step (`eqd::StagedFft`: about 60–150 steps, at most about 40 k operations each), then the convolver crossfades over one partition; the first design runs at once, so a render starts right. Match's curve, when on, is multiplied into the magnitude (linear-phase Match). Pre-ringing before transients is expected. Tests: the impulse symmetric about the latency and within 0.1 dB of the bands' minimum-phase curve from 50 Hz (Normal); the reported latency equal to the measured one at each tier; a band moved mid-stream with no gap. Cost: Eco 3,700, Normal 4,000, High 4,900 (medium; ceilings 8,000); RAM about 0.7 MB an instance (the High-sized convolver, about 400 KB, the design and learner buffers). While its track is armed it plays the same bands minimum phase, latency 0 ("Around the effects").
- **Match (EQ variant 5):** `match` (On), `match_amount` 0..1 (1), `match_smooth` 0..1 (0.3), `match_low` / `match_high` (20 Hz..20 kHz), `match_learn` (Off, Reference, Input, Both; an action: not automatable), `output`. Learning (`eqd::SpectrumLearner`, on the audio thread): the mid of the key (the reference: a clip, a file or a track through the sidechain) and of the input, 4,096-point Hann frames one after another (frames under −80 dBFS RMS skipped), the FFT a slice per control step and each bin's power summed; a take that starts forgets its side. The curve (`eqd::matchCurve`, a 1/24-octave grid from 20 Hz): reference minus input in dB, each 1/6-octave smoothed, then ±`smooth` × 1 octave, clamped to ±12 dB, × amount, faded to 0 over 1/3 octave outside the limits; it is designed (once a take ends) as a 2,048-tap **minimum-phase** FIR (the real cepstrum folded onto positive times and exponentiated, on a 4,096-point grid; the last 256 taps faded) in 256-frame partitions: 256 frames of latency, declared. A flat curve plays one tap. The learned spectra are the state (`saveState`: "CAWM", the bins, both frame counts and each bin's mean power; 16 KB; checked on load), so a stored snapshot is a reference too. Its display carries a fit of up to 8 bells to the full curve (greedy: the largest deviation and its half-height width) for the app's "Fit bands", which writes them into Bands. Tests: a signal matched to itself gives a flat curve and plays the input; an input at half the reference's level is lifted 6.02 dB; the snapshot saved, loaded (the same sound) and damaged ones refused. Cost 3,400 playing, 4,600 while learning both (medium; ceilings 8,000); RAM about 0.3 MB. [Assumption] Not patent-checked: check before it ships.
- **Wah (Filter variant 3):** `mode` (Envelope, Pedal), `curve` (Classic 400 Hz..2.2 kHz Q 4..7; Vintage 450 Hz..1.7 kHz Q 6..9; Bass 150 Hz..1.1 kHz Q 3..5 with more low end; Wide 250 Hz..4 kHz Q 2.5..5: C.A.W's own curves, not measured from a pedal), `pedal` 0..1 (heel to toe; automatable, modulatable and MIDI-mappable, so a knob, a lane, an expression pedal or React to… moves it), `sensitivity` (0..+40 dB before the sweep), `attack`, `release` (the level's follower, per control step on the louder side's peak), `range`, `direction` (Up, Down), `resonance` (Q × 0.5..1.5), `mix`, `output`. One TPT SVF a side: its band-pass (unity at the peak) plus the curve's share of low-pass; position glided over 5 ms, frequency and Q at each control from `detTan`; a parked pedal skips its controls. Tests: heel peaks low and toe high (12 dB apart), the envelope opening on a loud note and less on a quiet one, Down reversing it. Cost 1,100 envelope, 700 pedal (light; ceilings 2,000).
- **All four** (and Bands with dynamics and solo): the same bits at 1, 37, 64 and 512-frame blocks with the knobs held (constant values, so `knobsUnchanged` skips controls); finite after a NaN and an Inf (the convolvers and the Wah take one as 0; a dynamic detector starts again clean) and tiny after denormal input; displays finite; no allocation on the audio thread while playing, redesigning, learning and moving knobs (`caw_rt_tests`); at least 8 presets each (Bands' dynamics 8, Linear 8, Match 8, Wah 10), every value a knob in range. Bands with 4 dynamic bands costs 4,400 (medium, ceiling 8,000), with all 8 and two 48 dB cuts 8,600 (its static bands alone 5,800; heavy ceiling 24,000).

Old ids are `org.caw.` plus the name shown. `org.caw.downmix` (stereo to mono) stays its own device; `org.caw.convolver` was too until 1.1.0 merged it under its own id (variant 0 the convolver unchanged, so an entry without `variant` plays it and needs no migration; variant 1 Cab). Every migration keeps each parameter's id and value (no renames were needed) and the state. Where variants share a parameter id in different units (Drive's `drive` and `output`, Delay's `sync` divisions), a value left over from another variant is ignored by the engine; the app resets them on a variant change.

- **One id, a `variant`** (`engine/src/fx_merged.*`): a merged device has one component id and a reserved choice parameter **`variant`** ("mode" is taken: the saturator has one). Each variant is its own `EffectBase` class with its own descriptor: the merged id and name, its own parameters (`variant` among them), latency, tail, ports and cost. `createComponent(registry, id, params)` makes the class the values choose, for a chain entry and for a rack slot (`<slot>.variant`), so a variant runs only its own path: no per-sample branch, no extra virtual call. `variant` is structural (`componentStructure`, generalising the rack's `rackStructure`): another value rebuilds the chain on the control thread and crossfades it in, as a rack's slot change does, and `ComponentChain::setValue` refuses it. It is set by hand only (owner, 2026-11-18): not automatable, modulatable or MIDI-mappable, no smoothing. A value of another variant's parameter left in an entry (after a variant change) is ignored, as a rack's left-over slot values are; an unknown parameter is still refused. The registry lists a merged id with its first variant's descriptor (the default: an entry without `variant` runs it).
- **A legacy class as a variant:** `legacyVariantDescriptor` makes a variant's descriptor from the old class's own (the old parameters in their order, so the class's indices hold, and `variant` appended; latency, tail, ports, state and cost kept), and `AsVariant<OldClass>` runs the unchanged class under it (the class only stops being `final`). A variant with new DSP builds its own table with `variantParameter`. Pitch/Shift: variant 0 "Pitch" is the pitch shifter (latency half a grain, per instance), 1 "Frequency" the frequency shifter (no latency); old parameter ids are kept verbatim (they don't collide).
- **Migration** (`engine/src/fx_migrate.*`): each family's table has one `LegacyEffect` per old id: the merged id, its `variant`, the parameters that are renamed, value-mapped or dropped (`ParamMove`; a parameter not listed keeps its id and value; `lanes = false` drops lanes and links on one whose units or curve differ), fixed values that keep the old sound (`ParamSet`), whether the old state carries over, and the old descriptor (so a rack's routes can be remapped once the old id is retired). `Engine::setProjectImpl` migrates every component list (the master's, each layer's `components` and `noteComponents`, the audition's) and every rack holding an old child (`<slot>_fx` to the merged id's hash, `<slot>.variant` added, `<slot>.<param>` moved, each route's `modN_dst` index re-pointed at the same knob in the new flattened list), then moves the automation lanes and modulation links on those components to the renamed parameters, dropping those renamed to nothing, as the retired synths' lanes are (`migratedLegacyParameter`). `caw_migrate_component` gives the app the same for its document. A merged device's presets are its old effects' presets, migrated, under their old names (`mergedPresets`, reached through `builtinPresets`).
- **Optional ports** (`EffectBase::optionalPorts`, `builtin_effects.h`): extra stereo outputs (a filter's low and high, a utility's mid and side, a transient designer's transient and sustain, dry and wet) and inputs a built-in may offer, at most 4. They are not in its `ModuleDescriptor` (hosts, the SDK and the native ABI never see them): a host connects one with `connectOptionalPort(index, left, right)` (a block-sized buffer, between blocks), and the effect reads `optionalOutLeft(k)` once per `run()`, null while unconnected; an output reads silence while the effect sleeps. Only the [patch model](#patches-built-not-yet-used) connects them (a wire naming one), and nothing uses patches yet. A real `"sidechain"` input stays a descriptor port where one exists today (compressor, gate, ducker).
- **Retired old ids** (part F, 2026-11-18): `registerMergedDevices` runs last in `registerBuiltinEffects` and sets every old id's registration aside (`ModuleRegistry::retireBuiltin`), so `caw_engine_components`, the registry's `find`, `create` and `builtinComponents` know only the merged devices; nothing makes an old id as a device of its own. Everything that may still name one migrates it first and plays the merged device's variant, the same bits: a project (`Engine::setProjectImpl`), a preset or rack (`caw_migrate_component`), `Engine::createNativeComponent`, the component testing suite (the component and `compare`), the plug-in benchmark, the sample-pack maker's instrument and effects and the wavetable export (`fx::migrateLegacyId`; each looks up the variant's descriptor with `findComponentDescriptor`). Each old class and its descriptor function stay (the variants wrap them; the migration reads the old parameter list from them), and an old preset name still resolves under the old id and under the merged id. `ModuleRegistry::restoreRetired` registers them again for tests only (the family tests compare each variant with the old effect as it ran). The built-in rack presets name the merged ids with each child's `<slot>.variant` (the same values and routes as the old presets migrated, checked 2026-11-18), so no shipped preset needs migration.
- **A reused id** (a merged device keeping an old id, its old effect as variant 0): `registerMergedDevices` replaces the old registration in place (`ModuleRegistry::replaceBuiltin`, never a loaded or internal component), so the registry lists the merged descriptor with `variant`; its presets are its own (variant 0) followed by its old effects' (`ownBuiltinPresets`, `mergedPresets`). An entry under a reused id with no `variant` plays variant 0 and needs no migration.
- **Port shape per variant:** the chain, the engine's key routing and the per-note check read a merged device's ports from the chosen variant's descriptor (as a rack slot does), so the Dynamics variant runs per note and isn't keyed while Compressor is.
- **Racks carry a layout version:** `rack_layout` (= 4 since 1.1.0's "Around the effects", 3 from 1.1.0's split, 2 from 1.0.8; the rack's last parameter, after the routes, not automatable). A rack under 4 lists its Duck, Carve, Gate, Pedal gate, Ring mod, Mimic and Limiter children without the parameters 1.1.0 appended to them (the sidechain filter, the loudness target), so its routes are re-pointed past them. A rack under 3 is older than the split: its Compressor children's variants are read with 1.0.9's numbering and moved, each merged child's list read as ending at `variant`, and its routes re-pointed. A rack without it predates the merge: each child that is a merged id with no `<slot>.variant` is read as variant 0 without `variant`, gets `<slot>.variant` = 0, its routes are re-pointed past the new parameter, and the version is added, once (a marked rack is never migrated again). The app must always save `rack_layout`.
- **Cost:** a merged variant costs what the old id did (`caw_bench SECONDS 300`, 2026-11-18, x86-64 GCC Release, µs of CPU per second of audio, within each run's noise), e.g. pitch shifter 3145 vs Pitch 3120, saturator 2267 vs Saturate 2244, clipper 4x 10760 vs Clip 10020, ducker (tempo) 1687 vs Shape 1683, graphic EQ 1601 vs Graphic 1603, ladder 2919 vs 2968, doubler 5591 vs 5588, shimmer 10269 vs 10187. The Benchmark's `every_effect` scenario names the merged ids with each variant, one entry per old effect in the old order (the same coverage and sound: its `stress.cawref` render is unchanged; its project hash is new), and costs what it did: **[Fact]** (x86-64 VM, GCC 13 Release, `stress::run` 10 s of 256-frame callbacks, eight alternating runs each, 2026-11-18) profile mean 1086 µs before and 1085 µs after, plain (mix pool) 1013 µs and 1019 µs, within the runs' spread (min 1030/1037 and 981/976 µs).
- **Tests:** `fx_merged_test.cpp`: the variants' descriptors (old parameters in order, `variant` structural, latency, tail and cost kept); each variant's render bit-identical to its old id's over every preset and 12 random value sets; the chain's latency per variant equal to the old id's at four grains; every old preset resolving under `org.caw.shift` with equal values and `variant`; a saved project (JSON) with both old ids on a track and the master and lanes on them rendering bit-identically to its merged form; a rack with both old children and two routes migrating (routes re-pointed) and rendering the same bits; a variant change rebuilding the chain (the display layout changes), `set_component_param` refusing a new `variant`, left-over values ignored; `caw_engine_components`' `"variants"` and presets; `caw_migrate_component` for an entry, a preset, a rack and another id; an optional port declared and unconnected rendering bit-identically, and connected receiving its signal without changing the output. `fx_merged_rt_test.cpp` (`caw_rt_tests`): variant changes by project updates while playing, on a track, the master (old ids, migrated) and a rack, with no allocation on the audio thread. Valgrind clean (2026-11-18). Each family has the same checks in `fx_merged_{dynamics,drive,eqfilter,stereomod,space}_test.cpp` (and an `_rt_test.cpp` in `caw_rt_tests`), plus: a migrated Shape in Notes mode ducking on its key's notes, the Dynamics variant per note and unkeyed, Transient's and Utility's optional outputs summing to the output, an old rack with a reused-id child routed to a later slot keeping its routes on the same knobs, every rack preset needing no migration (and settling after one when given an older layout), and (`fx_merged_test.cpp`) every old id retired: not found, made or listed, `createNativeComponent` making its variant, its presets resolving under both ids, and no listed device's preset needing migration.

#### The Ducker's Duck variant

`engine/src/builtin_fx_duck.cpp` (owner, 2026-11-18: a Compactor-style ducker and leveller, C.A.W's own design and code, with the controls the owner listed for Kilohearts Compactor). `variant` 0 of `org.caw.duck` since 1.1.0 (1.0.8–1.0.9: `variant` 4 of `org.caw.compressor`, migrated), its own parameter table (`legacyVariantDescriptor` appends `variant` to it) and a real stereo `sidechain` input; no latency, no tail.

- **Parameters:** `threshold` −60..0 dB (−20), `amount` 0..1 (0.5), `attack` 0.05..200 ms (5), `hold` 0..500 ms (30), `release` 5..2000 ms (150), `detection` (RMS, Peak, ISP), `output` ±24 dB, `mix`, `sidechain` (Internal, External). Ids that another Compressor variant uses mean the same there, except that Duck's detector choice is `detection` (the Compressor's `detect` has other indices).
- **Curve:** above the threshold the gain falls by `amount` × the level's excess in dB: 1 holds the level at the threshold (infinite ratio), 0.5 halves the excess (2:1), 0 does nothing; nothing happens below it. The reduction follows its target with the attack while it deepens, waits `hold` once it stops deepening (and while it is held down at its target), then recovers with the release. The output is the input × output gain × (1 + (reduction − 1) × mix); the output gain and the mix ramp in a straight line over each 16-frame control step (`Ramp`), so a moving knob or lane doesn't zipper.
- **Sleep:** as every keyed effect of the suite (`EffectBase`), its key counts as input: it doesn't fall asleep (which clears its reduction to 0 dB) while the key sounds through a gap in its input, and one asleep wakes when its key sounds, also in a chain, which runs a sleeping keyed effect whose key sounds (2026-11-19 review: before, a note starting mid-duck after a gap popped in at full level).
- **Detector:** linked across both sides. External hears the key (another track, [sidechain keys](#project-file-json)); Internal, or a block with no key port at all, hears its own input, which makes it a leveller. In a chain an unkeyed External hears silence and ducks nothing, as the Compressor variant; the app's `setComponentKey` turns `sidechain` to External with a key and back without one, as it does for the Compressor and Gate ([app](app.md)). RMS is a 10 ms mean square; Peak the louder side's |x|; ISP the samples and three points between each pair (4x), interpolated by an 8-tap Hann-windowed sinc per point normalised to unity at DC (within 0.1 dB up to 12 kHz and 0.55 dB at 16 kHz, at 48 kHz; the detector hears ISP about 4 frames late). ISP is worked out for each run of at most 16 frames, always all 16 lanes, in float, vectorised across frames, so the bits don't depend on the block size.
- **Presets** (variant 0 of `org.caw.duck`, first in its list; `fx::newVariantPresets`; a 1.0.9 entry naming one under the Compressor still opens, `fx::applyMovedPreset`): Kick duck bass, Voice over music, Pad pump, Snare makes room, Parallel duck (keyed: `sidechain` External), Gentle leveller, Vocal rider, Peak catcher, Bus glue (its own input).
- **Display** (10 floats): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 detector level peak dB (the key or its own input, as detected), 3 the key's own peak dB (−120 unless External), 4 reduction now, 5 most reduction, 6 threshold, 7 amount, 8 detection, 9 sidechain (0: internal).
- **Cost:** **[Fact]** `caw_bench 3 300` (x86-64 VM, GCC 13 Release, its own input, threshold −30, amount 1, 2026-11-19, three runs, µs of CPU per second of audio): RMS 1196–1305, Peak 1119–1234, ISP 1360–1489, against the Compressor variant's default 1766–1915 (and 3105–3370 RMS with its sidechain high-pass).
- **Tests** (`fx_duck_test.cpp`): the descriptor; the static curve in every detection mode at four amounts and two thresholds, the output and mix; hold against no hold after a key burst; ISP seeing a 12 kHz sine's peaks between samples that Peak and RMS miss; key against Internal against no key port, and an unkeyed chain; the same bits at blocks of 1, 37, 64 and 500; still ducked when the input comes back after a 500 ms gap while the key sounds, and woken by a key that began while it slept (alone and in a chain); output and mix moving by about as much every frame; every preset reducing something; the display; three reference renders (`references/fx_duck.cawref`, stable). `slot_tools_rt_test.cpp`: it plays keyed in ISP with no allocation on the audio thread.

#### New modes (1.1.0)

New DSP appended to merged devices (the sound plan's "New modes in existing effects", owner 2026-11-19), each its own class and parameter table with `variant` appended (`legacyVariantDescriptor` of its own table), original code. Each runs only its own path, sleeps on silence and is built for the lowest CPU and RAM (owner, 2026-11-19): coefficients at the 16-frame control points with ramps, buffers sized at `prepare` to the mode's longest time at the real rate. Presets: `fx::newVariantPresets` (8 or more per mode, job-named and tagged by purpose in the description). Shared parts: `engine/src/fx_modes_kit.h` (a power-of-two delay line with Hermite and linear reads, Schroeder and first-order all-passes, a seeded noise, Hann and equal-power pan).

**Cost** (`caw_bench 2 300`, x86-64 VM, GCC 13 Release, 2026-11-19, two runs, µs of CPU per second of stereo audio; 10000 = 1 % of a core), RAM per instance at 48 kHz (its buffers). Each has a ceiling in `caw_bench SECONDS 300` (`modesCostOver`: the run exits 1 when one is over): its plan budget on one phone core (light 0.5 %, medium 2 %, heavy 6 %) over 2.5: 2000, 8000 and 24000 µs/s. The box is shared and noisy (the same build measured 30–50 % apart between runs while other work ran), so the costs below are ranges. **[Assumption]** the Galaxy S22+ is 2–3× slower than this x86 box, not yet measured per mode on the phone.

| Mode | µs/s | RAM | Ceiling (budget) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Delay: Tape (default; worn: wow, flutter, wear 1) | 3010 (3010) | 1 MiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Delay: Reverse | 2350–3640 | 2 MiB (a 2 s window needs 4 s) | 8000 (medium) |
| Delay: Multi-tap (unpitched; 4 pitched taps) | 2070–3140 (3780–5640) | 1 MiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Reverb: Room | 5450–5620 | about 450 KiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Reverb: Plate | 6320–6560 | about 265 KiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Reverb: Spring | 2390–2560 | 64 KiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Reverb: Gated | 4850 | about 260 KiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Reverb: Reverse | 5910 | 1 MiB | 8000 (medium) |
| Drive: Amp (Crunch, 1x; High gain, Auto 2x) | 2790–3390 (6750–7480) | none (filters only) | 8000 (medium) |
| Drive: Pedal (Overdrive, 1x; Fuzz, Auto 2x) | 1790–1830 (4560–4850) | none | 8000 (medium) |
| Drive: Bands (1 band; 3; 4) | 1680–1780 (3340–3880; 4650–4690) | none | 8000 (medium) |
| Bit distortion: Bitwise (default; shift, XOR, AND; 1.1.1: echo ring; every glitch op) | 860 (870; 1330–1490; 1770–1900) | about 31 KiB at 48 kHz (a 50 ms echo and an 80 ms grain of 16-bit words a side) | 2000 (light) |
| Convolver: Cab (close; far mic) | 3280 (3380) | about 6 KiB (its rings and reflection lines; the responses shared) | 8000 (medium) |
| (for scale) Reverb: Hall, Shimmer | 6860–7060, 13980–14460 | | none (old) |

RAM is the buffers each instance allocates at 48 kHz, power-of-two lines rounded up (twice as much at 96 kHz). Every mode also plays in one chain, its knobs moving and its display read, with no allocation on the audio thread (`fx_modes_rt_test.cpp` in `caw_rt_tests`), and the new tests pass under AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (Debug, `CAW_SANITIZE=ON`, 2026-11-19).

**Delay** (`engine/src/builtin_fx_delay_modes.cpp`; latency 0, infinite tail as the Digital delay):
- **Tape** (variant 2): `sync` (divisions, 1/8 dotted), `time` 20..2000 ms (375), `feedback` 0..1 (0.45), `wow` (0.25), `flutter` (0.15), `saturation` (0.3), `loss` (0.35), `wear` (0.15), `spread` (0.3), `mix` (0.3). One head per side; the time glides (80 ms), so a moved time bends the pitch as tape does. Wow is a 0.55 Hz sine and a seeded drift (a new target every 250 ms), up to 6 ms; flutter two fast sines (7.3 and 11.9 Hz) up to 0.4 ms; both worked out at the control points and followed in a straight line. What the head reads goes through the loss (a low-pass from 16 kHz down to 1.2 kHz and a high-pass from 30 up to 200 Hz), the saturation (tanh up to 6× in, unity for quiet signals so the loop can't grow past `feedback`) and wear (a seeded hiss up to −54 dBFS riding on the tape's signal, silent on a silent tape, and seeded dropouts up to −12 dB), and that is both the wet sound and what goes back on the tape: every repeat is darker, softer and more worn than the last. `spread` lengthens the right side up to 8 % and moves its wow a quarter turn. Display (13): 0 input peak, 1 wet peak, 2 output peak, 3 left time s, 4 right time s (gliding), 5 feedback, 6 the left head's wow and flutter now ms, 7 wow, 8 flutter, 9 saturation amount, 10 loss, 11 wear, 12 the left dropout gain now (1: none).
- **Reverse** (variant 3): `sync` (1/4), `time` (the window) 30..2000 ms (500), `feedback` 0..0.95 (0.3), `tone` 500..20000 Hz (12000), `spread` 0..0.5 (0), `mix` (0.5). Two heads per side half a window apart each play the last window backwards (frame k of a segment is the one written 2k + 1 frames before) under a Hann window, so the two sum to one with no click at a turn; a new window length is taken at a head's next segment. Feedback goes through the tone low-pass back on the line. `spread` starts the right side's heads that fraction of a window later. Display (8): 0 input peak, 1 wet peak, 2 output peak, 3 window s, 4 and 5 the left heads' places in their windows (0..1), 6 feedback, 7 spread.
- **Multi-tap** (variant 4): `sync` (1/8), `time` (the step) 10..1000 ms (250), `pattern` (Straight 1-2-3-4, Dotted 1.5-3-4.5-6, Triplet, Swing 1-1⅔-3-3⅔, Gallop 1-1.5-2-3, Cascade ¼-½-1-2 steps), `feedback` 0..0.95 (0.25), `tone` (9000 Hz), per tap `tN_level`, `tN_pan` (equal power), `tN_pitch` ±12 semitones, `mix` (0.35). A mono line (the input's mid), the longest tap 5 s. An unpitched tap is one Hermite read; a pitched one a two-head delay-line shifter (60 ms grains, Hann). Taps run one after another over each run (a silent tap costs nothing; every read is at least 18 frames back, so none reads what the run writes). The taps' sum, normalised by their levels, goes through the tone low-pass back on the line. Display (25): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 step s, 3 pattern, 4 feedback, then per tap from 5 (5 floats): time s, level, pan (−1..1), pitch, peak dB.
- **Tests** (`fx_delay_modes_test.cpp`, with `fx_modes_harness.h`): appended after Digital and Ping-pong; Tape's repeats at its time and halving with feedback 0.5, darker each repeat with loss, a hot input saturated, wow moving the repeat in time, wear's hiss silent and asleep after silence; Reverse playing a ramp backwards and a steady sine without dips or clicks; Multi-tap's taps at the pattern's steps, panned hard and equal-power centred, an octave-up tap doubling a sine, feedback; the same bits at blocks of 1, 37, 64 and 512; NaN, infinities and subnormals not latching (finite after, silent and asleep after silence); 8 presets each, sounding; the display; three reference renders (`references/fx_delay_modes.cawref`, stable).

**Reverb** (`engine/src/builtin_fx_reverb_modes.cpp`; latency 0, infinite tail as Hall's). Every mode ends in the same output stage: `low_cut`, `high_cut`, `width` (mid/side, 0..2) and `mix`, its filters and width run on both sides as one two-wide vector. The display's first three floats are input peak, wet peak, output peak.
- **Room** (variant 2): `size` (0.4), `decay` 0.1..5 s (0.8), `early` (0.6), `damping` (0.5), `predelay` 0..200 ms (5); low cut 100, high cut 12000, mix 0.25. Eight early reflections per side (a tapped line; C.A.W's own times 4.3–36.7 ms and signs, scaled 0.3–2× by size, each 0.8 of the last) and the Library's 8-line network at a small size in its light form (two diffusers a side, no sweep) for the tail, which starts among the reflections. Display (15): 3 decay s, 4 pre-delay s, 5 size, 6 early, 7–14 the left reflections' times s.
- **Plate** (variant 3): `predelay` (10 ms), `decay` 0.2..10 s (2), `damping` (0.4), `diffusion` (0.75), `mod` (0.3); low cut 80, high cut 14000, mix 0.25. A figure-eight tank of the plate family in the literature (Dattorro, "Effect Design Part 1", JAES 1997; no code of anyone's): a 12 kHz input low-pass, four input all-passes, then two halves, each a modulated all-pass (swept up to 16 frames at 29.761 kHz, the halves in quadrature at 0.9 Hz), a delay, a damping low-pass, an all-pass and a delay, feeding the other half; lengths from that paper's 29.761 kHz scaled to the rate; the decay gain per half sets 60 dB per RT60; six output taps per side are C.A.W's own. Display (8): 3 decay s, 4 pre-delay s, 5 diffusion, 6 modulation, 7 the first all-pass's sweep now ms.
- **Spring** (variant 4): `decay` 0.3..6 s (1.8), `tension` (0.5), `length` (0.5), `tone` 1..9 kHz (4500); low cut 150, high cut 9000, width 0.7, mix 0.3. Per side a loop: a delay of 25–80 ms (the right 13 % longer), 12 first-order all-passes (coefficient −0.45 down to −0.85 with tension: the dispersion that delays the lows behind the highs, a spring's chirp) and the tone low-pass, decaying by its RT60; both springs run as one two-wide vector. Display (7): 3 decay s, 4 tension, 5 length, 6 the left loop s.
- **Gated** (variant 5): `size` (0.6), `decay` 0.3..10 s (2.5), `threshold` −60..0 dB (−30), `hold` 10..1000 ms (250), `release` 2..500 ms (40), `predelay` 0..100 ms; low cut 150, high cut 10000, mix 0.35. The Library's network (light form) through a gate: it opens (1 ms) while the input's louder side is over the threshold, holds `hold` after it falls under, then closes with `release`. Display (8): 3 gate now (0..1), 4 most open since the last publish, 5 threshold, 6 hold s, 7 decay s.
- **Reverse** (variant 6): `length` 100..2000 ms (600), `curve` (0.5), `diffusion` (0.7); low cut 100, high cut 9000, mix 0.35. A swell: the input through two all-passes, 24 taps per side over the length (each at a seeded place in its slot, the sides different), gains rising as (k/24)^(1 + 4 curve), normalised to equal energy, signs alternating, then two more all-passes; it builds and stops at the length. The taps run one after another over each run (every tap at least 18 frames back). Display (30): 3 length s, 4 curve, 5 taps (24), 6–29 the left taps' gains, earliest first.
- **Tests** (`fx_reverb_modes_test.cpp`): appended after Hall and Shimmer; a longer decay keeping more tail (Room, Plate, Spring) and every mode's tail ending; Room's first reflection at 4.3 ms × its size scale and none with early 0; Plate dense and wide; Spring's lows arriving after its highs, more with tension; Gated open through its hold and shut after, shut under its threshold; Reverse rising to its length and stopping, a gentler curve starting louder; the same bits at blocks of 1, 37, 64 and 512; bad input; 8 presets each; the display; five reference renders (`references/fx_reverb_modes.cawref`, stable).

**Drive** (`engine/src/builtin_fx_drive_modes.cpp`; latency 0, no tail; original code, nothing from Fire (AGPL) or any other drive). The filters run on both sides as one two-wide vector (`modes::PairSvf`, `modes::PairEq` in `fx_modes_kit.h`); each ends in a one-pole DC block at 8 Hz (the asymmetric shapes make an offset). **Oversampling** only where measured: `oversample` (Auto, Off, 2x) on Amp and Pedal; Auto is 2x for Amp's Lead and High gain and Pedal's Distortion and Fuzz (off at the Eco tier), else 1x. **[Fact]** measured 2026-11-19 (`fx_drive_modes_test.cpp`, a 2.9 kHz sine at 0.3, the aliases' energy against the fundamental, 1x / 2x): Clean −101/−116 dB, Crunch −45/−54, Lead −21/−40, High gain −21/−40, Overdrive −44/−87, Distortion −20/−56, Fuzz −22/−59. The 2x is the Library's half-band all-pass pair (6 coefficients, transition 0.06: the same aliasing as 8 at less cost; no latency), its design built once at start-up and shared.
- **Amp** (variant 4): `voicing` (Clean, Crunch, Lead, High gain; Crunch), `gain` (0.5), `bass`, `mid`, `treble` (0..1, 0.5 flat, ±12 dB), `presence` (0..1 = 0..+9 dB, 0.3), `output` −24..12 dB, `mix`, `oversample`. A tightening high-pass (60/90/120/150 Hz by voicing), the gain (0–18 dB up to 18–52 dB), one asymmetric soft-clip stage (Clean, Crunch) or two with a 5.5 kHz low-pass and 6× between (Lead, High gain; both stages and that low-pass inside one 2x region when oversampled), then C.A.W's own tone stack (a low shelf at 120 Hz, a bell at the voicing's 800/650/700/500 Hz, Q 0.8, a high shelf at 2.8 kHz, presence a high shelf at 5 kHz), a gentle power-stage clip and the voicing's roll-off (9/7.5/7/6.5 kHz), level-trimmed per voicing. No cabinet: that is the Convolver's [Cab](#new-modes-110). Display (11): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 voicing, 3 input gain dB, 4 the gain stages' peak dB, 5–8 bass, mid, treble, presence dB, 9 the mid's Hz, 10 oversampling factor.
- **Pedal** (variant 5): `type` (Overdrive, Distortion, Fuzz), `drive` (0.5), `tone` (0..1: a low-pass 600 Hz..11 kHz), `level` −24..12 dB, `mix`, `oversample`. Overdrive: a 720 Hz high-passed drive (0..40 dB) soft-clipped (x / (1 + |x|)) over half the clean signal (a mid hump); Distortion: 80 Hz up, +10..56 dB into a hard knee held at ±1; Fuzz: +12..48 dB into a biased, asymmetric clip that closes at low levels (a fuzz's gating). Display (7): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 type, 3 drive, 4 gain dB, 5 tone Hz, 6 oversampling factor.
- **Bands** (variant 6): `bands` (1–4; 3), `x1`, `x2`, `x3` (120, 1000, 5000 Hz; each held a third of an octave above the last), per band `bN_type` (Clean, Soft, Hard, Tube, Fuzz, Fold), `bN_drive` (0..36 dB, half of it taken back off), `bN_tone` (−1..1, a tilt about the band's geometric centre), `bN_mix`, `bN_solo`, `bN_mute`; `output`, `mix`. Linkwitz-Riley 24 dB/octave splits (one state-variable stage gives a crossover's low and high, each through a second), every band below a crossover through that crossover's own 2nd-order Butterworth all-pass, so the bands sum to an all-pass: flat within 0.1 dB with every band Clean. Only the crossovers in use run; a muted (or un-soloed) band's shaper is skipped; each band's shaper is picked once per run. Display (26): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 bands, 3–5 crossovers Hz, then per band from 6 (5 floats): in peak dB, out peak dB, type, drive, active (1, or 0 when muted, un-soloed or unused).
- **Tests** (`fx_drive_modes_test.cpp`): appended after Phase; harmonics rising from Clean to High gain; bass and treble moving their own ends; Distortion harder than Overdrive, Fuzz's even harmonics, Overdrive at drive 0 nearly clean; the aliasing above, 2x cutting it by 15 dB or more where Auto picks it and under −40 dB at 1x where it doesn't, and the display's factor; Bands flat at 1–4 bands, solo and mute, a driven band distorting only its range; the same bits at blocks of 1, 37, 64 and 512; bad input; 8 presets each; the display; four reference renders (`references/fx_drive_modes.cawref`, stable).

**Bit distortion: Bitwise** (`engine/src/builtin_fx_bits_bitwise.cpp`, variant 1 of `org.caw.bits`; light; keyed since 1.1.1: its `sidechain` input is the Combine's key; latency 0, no tail): `bits` 2..16 (12), `shift` −8..8 (0), `xor` and `and` (16-bit masks 0..65535; 0 and 65535), `output`, `mix`, `variant`, then 1.1.1's glitch ops appended after `variant` (so 1.1.0's indices hold), each off by default: `rotate` −15..15 (0), `reverse` 0..16 lanes (0), `or` (mask, 0), `combine` Off/Key/Echo, `combine_op` XOR/AND/OR, `combine_mask` (65535), `echo` 0.1–50 ms (4), `rate` (the step: 1/32, 1/16 T, 1/16, 1/8 T, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1 bar; 1/16), `pattern` (8 bits, one a step; 255), `freeze` (mask, 0), `grain` 1–80 ms (12), `hold` 0..1 (0.5), `flips` 0..1 (0), `seed` 0..9999 (1), `tone` 500 Hz–20 kHz (20 kHz: off), `gate` −80..−10 dB (−80: off), `level` Off/Auto (Auto), and the shared sidechain filter (`sc_hpf`, `sc_lpf`, `sc_listen`). Each sample, held to ±1, is rounded to a signed word of `bits` bits, shifted (left: louder, wrapping round past full scale; right: coarser steps) and wrapped back to its bits. With every 1.1.1 op off, 1.1.0's loop runs unchanged: the word XORed and ANDed with the masks' top `bits` bits (so a mask means the same at every word size) and read back, bit for bit as in 1.1.0 (its presets and reference render unchanged). With any on, both sides' words go through, in order: freeze (the `freeze` lanes loop the step's first `grain` ms for `hold` of each step: a stutter in those lanes), combine (the `combine_mask` lanes XOR/AND/OR the key's word, quantised the same way, or the word's own `echo` ms ago: a comb, a metallic ring; with no key routed the Key combine waits and the word passes), rotate (round the word), reverse (its lowest `reverse` lanes), the XOR, OR and AND masks, and flips (each lane flips for the whole step with probability `flips`, drawn from `seed` and the step's number: a render repeats, and a song position always flips the same lanes). The ops act only on the steps `pattern` names (bit 0 the first of 8); a step is `rate` at the song's tempo, its position while the song plays and a free clock from the last wake while stopped; the bits and the shift always act. Then the wet goes through `tone` (a one-pole low-pass), `gate` (the wet only while the input's peak, falling over 80 ms, is above it; about 3 ms to open or shut) and, with `level` Auto while an op acts, an 8 Hz offset filter and a gain that keeps the wet's RMS (about 10 ms) at or under the input's; then Output and Mix. In every case a word that rounds to 0 stays 0, so silence stays silence (a mask would otherwise make it a constant) and it sleeps. Integer arithmetic for the word (both sides packed in one 64-bit word, the masks repeated in each half), the same on every platform; only the chosen ops' loop runs (by tone or level, the combine's source, and whether a lane op is on), the knobs are decoded only when one moves, the followers at control rate. Display (45): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 bits, 3 shift, 4 XOR mask, 5 AND mask, 6–21 how often each of the left side's 16 lanes (as a 16-bit word, lane 0 the least significant) flipped since the last publish (0..1 a frame: the lanes "drawn flipping"), 22–37 the latest word's bits; 1.1.1: 38 the step in the pattern (0..7; −1 while no op acts), 39 whether the ops act on it, 40 the lanes frozen now, 41 the lanes flipped now, 42 the combined word's latest bits (each as 16 lanes), 43 the gate (0..1), 44 the level's gain (dB). Presets: 19 (1.1.0's 9; 1.1.1's Metal ring, Tin can echo, Key XOR, Key carve, Beat masks, Stutter freeze, Full freeze, Dice flips, Bit rotor, Mirror grit and Glitch bass, each tagged by purpose). Tests: `fx_bits_bitwise_test.cpp` (every frame bit-exact against the arithmetic written out independently at seven settings, the defaults a plain 12-bit quantiser, the lanes, block sizes, bad input, presets, the 1.1.0 reference render `references/fx_bits_bitwise.cawref`) and `fx_bits_glitch_test.cpp` (the appended table and the key port; neutral 1.1.1 knobs bit for bit 1.1.0's; rotate, reverse and OR, the key's three combines (block sizes 1/37/64/512 through the key), the echo and the pattern's steps bit-exact against the arithmetic written out lane by lane; the freeze's grain loop; flips seeded and repeating; block sizes and bad input at three glitch settings; the glitch presets never louder than the input (within 0.42 dB of RMS on music), not white noise (spectral flatness under 0.25 and within 0.15 of the input's) and still moving in every loud quarter second; two reference renders, `references/fx_bits_glitch.cawref`). caw_bench 300 times the default, the 1.1.0 masks, the echo ring and every glitch op at once against the light ceiling.

**Convolver: Cab** (`engine/src/builtin_fx_cab.cpp`, variant 1 of `org.caw.convolver`; medium; latency 0, a 1024-frame tail): `cab` (1x12 open, 2x12 closed, 4x12 closed, 1x10 small, 4x10 bass, 1x15 bass; 4x12), `position` (0 the cone's centre .. 1 its edge; 0.3), `distance` (0 close .. 1 far; 0.2), `output`, `mix`. Each cabinet is C.A.W's own design, not measured from any product: its voice (four peaks and dips, a 4th-order roll-off at 3.6–9.5 kHz, an inverted reflection inside the box at 0.18–0.45 ms) is a short impulse response, **128 taps at 48 kHz (2.7 ms; 120 at 44.1 kHz, 256 at 96 kHz; at most 1 KiB of floats per cabinet)**, rendered once at start-up for 44.1, 48, 88.2 and 96 kHz (filters run on an impulse, the last quarter faded) and shared read-only by every instance (about 36 KiB for all six cabinets at four rates); another rate renders its own at prepare.
