The cabinet's lows (a high-pass at 35–110 Hz and a box resonance bell) are filters after it, as is the mic: position a high shelf at 3 kHz (+2 .. −8 dB) and a presence bell at 4.5 kHz (+3 .. −3 dB), distance a proximity low shelf at 150 Hz (+6 dB close), a low-pass from 20 kHz down to 9 kHz and a floor reflection 0.3–3 ms late (up to 0.35). The response runs direct (both sides interleaved in one doubled ring, a four-wide vector dot product, fixed summing order): at this length cheaper than an FFT, and no latency. A choice of cabinet starts on a clear history. The user's IRs stay in variant 0, as today; Cab keeps a loaded IR's state as it came (`saveState` gives it back), so switching back to the Convolver still has it. Display (72): 0 input peak, 1 output peak, 2 cab, 3 position, 4 distance, 5 taps, 6 the response's length ms, 7 points (64), 8–71 the response in 64 groups (0 when nobody watched). Tests (`fx_cab_test.cpp`): the Convolver merged with its old self as variant 0 (latency, state limit, cost and parameters kept, an entry without `variant` playing it); every cabinet passing the mids, cutting the deep lows and the top, no two alike; the edge darker than the centre, close lifting the lows; the response sizes per rate; the state kept; block sizes; bad input; 10 presets; two reference renders (`references/fx_cab.cawref`). `fx_merged_space_test.cpp` and `fx_merged_test.cpp` were updated for the merge.

#### The Talon/Hatch/Duck split (1.1.0)

Owner's rule, 2026-11-19 (private sound plan: complete, not just merged; merge only true siblings). The 1.0.8 Compressor's five variants become three devices, and Shape (periodic volume, not dynamics) goes to Tremolo/Pan. Each moved variant is its class unchanged; the new ones are their own classes (`builtin_fx_talon.cpp`, `builtin_fx_hatch.cpp`, `builtin_fx_duck_level_carve.cpp`, shared parts in `fx_dyn_common.h` and `fx_bandbank.h`, presets in `builtin_fx_dynsplit_presets.cpp`).

| Device (id) | Variants |
|---|---|
| Compressor (`org.caw.compressor`) | Down (0, the old compressor class), Dynamics (1, unchanged), Up (2), Bus (3) |
| Gate (`org.caw.gate`) | Gate (0, the old gate class), Expand (1), Pedal gate (2) |
| Ducker (`org.caw.duck`) | Duck (0, 1.0.8's Duck class), Level (1), Carve (2) |
| Tremolo/Pan (`org.caw.tremolo`) | Tremolo (0), Pan (1), Shape (2, the old ducker class) |

- **The `talon_layout` marker:** every Compressor variant's list ends with a hidden structural `talon_layout` (= 1; not automatable, modulatable or MIDI-mappable), after `variant` (and after Down's extras). An `org.caw.compressor` entry without it is from 1.0.8 or 1.0.9: its `variant` is read among the old five, and 2 (Gate) becomes `org.caw.gate` 0, 3 (Shape) `org.caw.tremolo` 2, 4 (Duck) `org.caw.duck` 0; 0 and 1 stay and get the marker. Parameters keep their ids and values, so lanes, links and keys follow unchanged (`fx::LayoutDevice`, `fx::LayoutMove` in `fx_migrate_dynamics.cpp`; `migrateLegacyEffect` handles it in the same pass as the id rows, so projects, racks, presets, `caw_migrate_component` and `migrateLegacyId` share it). A rack under `rack_layout` 3 moves its Compressor children the same way and re-points its routes (Down's list grew by 4). `org.caw.gate` is a device's own id again (its retired row went: a pre-1.0.8 gate loads as Gate 0); `org.caw.ducker` now migrates to Tremolo/Pan 2; `org.caw.dynamics` to Compressor 1 with the marker. Every Compressor preset carries the marker. A 1.0.9 entry naming a preset that moved (a Duck preset under the Compressor) still opens (`fx::applyMovedPreset`: its values with its 1.0.9 `variant`, then the migration). The app writes `talon_layout` on every Compressor entry and rack layout 3 (`app/lib/model/merged_devices.dart`, `project.dart`). **[Proposal, not built]** backing the project up before its first conversion (the plan's safety net) is the app's job and not done here.
- **Down:** the old class with three parameters after `variant`: `sc_lpf` (500 Hz–20 kHz, 20 kHz off), `character` (Clean, Warm: tanh on the compressed signal before the mix) and `output` (−24..+12 dB, ramped); `detect` gains ISP (lib::TruePeak, 4x). At their defaults the old code path runs, bit for bit. Its look-ahead stays 0–5 ms (a fixed 240-frame latency, as 1.0.8 reported).
- **Up** (upward compression): below the threshold the gain rises (thr − level)(1 − 1/ratio), the compressor's soft knee mirrored, capped by `range` (Max lift, 0–36 dB); below `floor` the lift falls away 1 dB per dB, so silence and noise are not raised (silence stays exactly silent). Loud parts pass at unity, so it has no makeup. Parameters: `threshold`, `ratio` 1–10, `range`, `floor`, `knee`, `attack`, `release`, `lookahead` 0–10 ms (its delay reported as latency per instance, `VariableLatency`; not automatable), `detect` (Peak, RMS, ISP), `link`, `sc_hpf`, `sc_lpf`, `sc_listen`, `sidechain`, `character`, `mix`, `output`. The look-ahead line is sized at activation for 10 ms at the rate.
- **Bus** (glue): a feedback compressor: the detector hears the output (the input's level plus the gain change in dB, so no per-sample log: exact for Peak, close for RMS, ISP and the key filters with a slowly moving gain), through a curve whose slope is 1 − ratio, so the settled input-to-output ratio is the chosen one. `ratio` is a choice of 1.5, 2, 4 and 10:1; `attack` 0.1–30 ms (held so the loop gain per sample stays ≤ 0.9: about 0.2 ms at 10:1); `release` 0.1/0.3/0.6/1.2 s or Auto (a fast follower, the attack in and 100 ms back, and a slow one, 300 ms in and 1.2 s back; the deeper applies, so a transient recovers fast and a held level slowly); `knee`, `makeup`, `auto_makeup`, `detect`, `link`, key filters, Listen, `sidechain` (External is fed forward from the key), `character`, `mix`, `output`. The per-sample gain follows the dB value by a cubic series (`dyn::GainTracker`, resynced every 16 frames).
- **Expand:** a downward expander: `threshold`, `ratio` 1.5–4, `range`, `knee` (lib::expanderGainDb), `attack`, `hold`, `release`, `detect`, key `sc_hpf`/`sc_lpf`, Listen, `sidechain`, `output`. No latency.
- **Pedal gate:** no look-ahead, no latency. A fast peak follower (0.1 ms in, 20 ms out) on the key opens above `threshold` and closes only below `threshold − hysteresis`, after `hold`. While open it measures the level's trend (dB per second, smoothed over 50 ms); `tracking` blends the release from `release` toward the time that decay needs to fall 20 dB, worked out every 16 frames. `range`, `attack`, `sc_hpf`, `sidechain`. Keyed by its own input (before a Drive, that is the clean signal) or the sidechain. **[Proposal, not built]** the plan's key "Track input (pre-FX)", for a gate placed after the Drive, needs the chain to expose its input buffer to keyed effects; decided when the sidechain picker is built.
- **Level** (a slow rider): the momentary loudness (BS.1770 K-weighting designed at the rate from the standard's analogue parameters, which give its tabled 48 kHz coefficients; both sides' mean square over 400 ms as 16-frame block sums) moves the gain toward `target` (−40..0 LUFS) within `max_boost` and `max_cut`, with the `speed` time constant (0.1–10 s); under `gate` it holds still. `lookahead` Off/50/100/200 ms delays the audio (latency per instance; buffer sized at activation). `output`.
- **Carve** (Trackspacer-style, keyed): the key's spectrum in 16 or 24 (`bands`, the High setting) log-spaced bands, 40 Hz–16 kHz (`fx_bandbank.h`'s `Analyzer`: two TPT SVF band-pass stages per band, unity at the centre, Q 3, followers with `attack`/`release`), sets a cascade of SVF bells (`BellCascade`, Q 2.5) on the same centres: each band dips by `range` × `amount` × how far the key's band is above `threshold` (full at 30 dB above), in 0.05 dB steps, inside the window `low`–`high` (half-octave edges). The bells overlap, so a key spanning neighbouring bands would add their dips (a 1 kHz key at range 9 dipped about 12 dB there before 2026-11-19): each band's summed dip (every bell's response at its centre, worked out exactly from the bilinear bell at 3, 6, 12 and 24 dB and interpolated) is held to `range`, a few passes scaling a band over it and its two neighbours each side. `stereo`: Linked (the key's mid dips both sides), Stereo (each side by its own), Mid/Side. `listen` plays what the dips take out; `output`. No latency. Only the bands in the window are measured and only bands with a dip run; with no key it passes the input bit for bit. The bank is shared for Mimic's vocoder.
- **Presets:** at least 8 per new mode, job-named, the purpose first in the description (Up 9, Bus 8, Expand 8, Pedal gate 8, Level 8, Carve 8; Duck's 9 now under `org.caw.duck`; Gate's and the ducker's follow their devices).
- **Cost** (`caw_bench 0.5 302`, also printed by mode 300; x86-64 VM shared with other builds, GCC 13 Release, 2026-11-19, µs of CPU per second of stereo audio, bytes per instance; CI ceilings: light 2000, medium 8000, heavy 24000, the owner's 0.5/2/6 % of an S22+ core over an **[Assumption]** 2.5× slower phone core): Down 1520 (ISP, key low-pass, warm, output 3060), Up 1080 (RMS, 5 ms look-ahead, warm 1340; ISP 1780) 17.8 kB, Bus 1640 (ISP and key filters 3100; keyed 1620; best of five runs after 2026-11-19's work: knobs read only when one moves, the output loop free of mode tests; 1750 before) 13.9 kB, Expand 620 (ISP 2090) 13.6 kB, Pedal gate 1070 12.9 kB, Level 770 99.6 kB at 48 kHz (77 kB of it the 200 ms look-ahead line), Carve 16 bands 6600 (medium), 24 bands Mid/Side 13900 (High: the heavy budget; 10820 before the Range clamp) 25 kB. `ctest` runs `caw_dynsplit_cost` (Release only) and fails over a ceiling.
- **Tests** (`fx_dynsplit_test.cpp`): each moved variant in a 1.0.9 project (keyed, with a lane) rendering its new device's bits, the ids and values after migration, once; Down and Dynamics unchanged with the marker; a marked entry never moved; a 1.0.9 rack with Down, Duck, Gate and a transient shaper and two routes migrating (routes re-pointed by 4) and rendering the 1.1.0 rack's bits; a moved preset opening; `caw_migrate_component` and `migrateLegacyId`; Up's lift at four levels (in the curve, capped, under the floor), silence; Bus's four ratios through the loop and auto release; Expand's ratio and range; Pedal gate's hysteresis both ways, no latency and its tracked release; Level's K-weighting against the standard's table, its ride, boost limit and gate; Carve's dip in the key's band only, the window, Listen and no key; latency reported equals measured; every variant at blocks of 1, 37, 64 and 512 and after a NaN, an infinity and subnormals; presets (≥ 8 a mode, unique names, playing finite); display counts; reference renders (`fx_dynsplit.cawref`). `fx_merged_dynamics_test.cpp`, `fx_merged_stereomod_test.cpp` and `fx_duck_test.cpp` hold the moved variants against their old classes.

### Per-slot auto gain and output meters (API 39)

Owner-approved device UI plan, built 2026-11-19 in `ComponentChain` (host level: every effect slot of a track's or the master's chain, built-in or third-party; not the instrument or per-note FX).

- **Auto gain** (`engine/src/auto_gain.*`, a listening aid, off by default): a trim after the slot's effect, before its bypass crossfade, matching the short-term loudness of what the effect gives out to what it was given, so it can be compared with its bypass by ear. Both are K-weighted (BS.1770's pre-filter, `LoudnessMeter::kWeighting`), both sides' energies summed and integrated by a one-pole of 1.5 s (its equivalent rectangular window is 3 s, BS.1770's short-term; chosen 2026-11-19). Every 16 frames, counted across blocks, the target is 10 log10(input / output energy), clamped to ±24 dB; the trim follows it with a 200 ms one-pole and the gain ramps linearly over the next 16 frames. While either energy is below −70 LUFS (BS.1770's absolute gate) the target holds, so silence never drifts and a device that falls silent is not lifted. Turning it on, and every Play, starts its analysis from 0 dB, so a render from Play repeats; the gain heard ramps from where it was to that fresh trim over 10 ms on its grid (480 frames at 48 kHz; `AutoGainMatcher::fadeFrames`), so a Play while tails sound under a trim doesn't step, and the render is a fresh chain's from there. Turned off while playing, the trim ramps to unity over the same 10 ms before the matcher stops (no click; from then on the same bits as never on), and turned on again mid-ramp it starts from the gain it had reached. While its effect sleeps and the chain skips it, the matcher advances its grid and decay over those frames as if it had heard their silence (only the grid once everything has settled at 0), so the trim is the same at any host block size, live or in an export. A bypassed slot plays its input untrimmed. It plays in a render exactly when the project says so. Cost **[Fact]** (a scratch bench of `AutoGainMatcher`, x86-64 VM, GCC 13 `-O3`, 2026-11-19): 590–850 µs per second of audio per slot with it on; off it costs one relaxed load a block.
- **Project file:** a component entry's optional `"auto_gain": true`; absent is off, so the app writes it only when on and older projects read and save unchanged. A project update sets it again from the entry; `currentSpecs` keeps it through a rebuild. It is read on any entry, but only an effect chain (`ComponentChain::build`: a track's inserts, the master's) has it: an instrument entry and per-note FX (`buildNoteEffects`, built by the same `make`) ignore it, and their `setAutoGain` returns false.
- **C API:** `caw_engine_set_component_auto_gain(engine, layer_id, index, on)` (1, or 0 for no such effect) and `caw_engine_component_auto_gain(engine, layer_id, index, &trim_db)` (1 with the trim, 0 while off reads 0 dB; 0 when busy, −1 none; never waits). Dart: `Engine.setComponentAutoGain(layerId, index, on)` and `Engine.componentAutoGain(layerId, index)` (a `double?`).
- **Slot meter:** each slot's output (after its trim and bypass crossfade: what the next slot hears) raises two lock-free peaks and a clip flag (a sample over full scale, |x| > 1) once a block; `caw_engine_component_meter(engine, layer_id, index, CawSlotMeter* out)` reads and resets them (`peak_l`, `peak_r` linear, `clipped` sticky until read; `CAW_INSTRUMENT_INDEX` the instrument; 1, 0 busy, −1 none). Dart: `Engine.componentMeter(layerId, index)` returns a `SlotMeter` (`peakL`, `peakR`, `clipped`) or null.
- **Tests** (`slot_tools_test.cpp`): a +6 dB and a −9 dB utility trimmed to −6 and +9 dB with the output's RMS within 0.2 dB of the input's, the ±24 dB clamp; silence exactly 0 dB, the trim holding through 20 s of silence, no lift below the floor; off bit-identical (and the same bits once its 10 ms off-ramp ends), the flag kept by `currentSpecs`; turned off on a 1 kHz peak with no step between samples, on again mid-ramp likewise; a new Play under a −6 dB trim with no step, then a fresh chain's bits; the same bits at blocks of 64 and 37 and from a new Play as a fresh chain (after the ramp); the same bits and trim at blocks of 64, 37, 16 and 1 over a utility that sleeps in 300 ms gaps; per-note FX and an instrument ignoring it; bypassed plays the input; the meter's peaks, clip, reset and sticky flag; the C API and the project JSON. `slot_tools_rt_test.cpp` (`caw_rt_tests`): toggling, a new Play and reading trims, meters and display while playing allocate nothing. App: `test/slot_tools_engine_test.dart` (with `CAW_LIB`).

### Around the effects (API 44, 1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 from the private sound plan's "Around the effects" (the engine side; the app's headers, key filter, loudness area and smart freeze are in [app](app.md)).

- **The armed signal:** an audio track whose `"input"` is `armed` with `monitor` `on` or `auto` plays its inserts' near-zero-delay versions; playback of other tracks and every export (`setProjectOffline`) play the full ones. One generic path, engine-internal (`component_chain.h`): a built-in implements `ArmedVersion` (`setArmed`, and `armedDiffers` for the badge; most keep an `ArmedFlag`, one relaxed load at their control step); the Engine sets it on the track's chain in each project update (`ComponentChain::setArmed`, on the control thread, before `updateCompensation`), and each effect's `VariableLatency::latencyFor` reports its armed latency, so the chain compensates. A rack passes it to its children. **[Decision, this session]** armed means armed with monitoring not `off` (whether stopped, playing or recording), so the latency changes only when the project does, never with the transport. The versions:
  - Linear EQ (variant 4): the same bands as minimum-phase filters (`lib::EqFilter`, mid and side lanes as the FIR's; Left and Right bands act as Stereo), latency 0; Match's curve is left out (it is a FIR). Switching starts the other path from silence (a short gap at arm time). Its display shows latency 0 and taps 0 while armed.
  - Preen Denoise and De-reverb: the 512 monitoring window (11 ms), as `monitor` On; Pitch fix (Lark): its 10 ms monitoring mode.
  - Ducker (`org.caw.ducker`, look-ahead) and Duck's Level (`org.caw.duck` variant 1, 50–200 ms look-ahead): look-ahead off (latency 0).
  - The Compressor's Up (`org.caw.compressor` variant 2, 0–10 ms look-ahead): look-ahead off (latency 0); no badge at look-ahead 0.
  - Pitch/Shift's Pitch (`org.caw.shift` variant 0): the grain at most 20 ms (latency 481 frames at 48 kHz instead of half the knob's grain).
  - Iris: Eco's window (1,024, the shortest it has tables for: 21 ms).
  - Not changed: Hush's look-aheads (De-click 5 ms, De-plosive 3 ms, Breath 20 ms; fixed lines), the Harmonizer (1.5 periods of 70 Hz: inherent to its pitch detection), and the Limiter (5 ms).
  - The badge (`slotArmed`): the chain is armed, the slot is not bypassed and `armedDiffers` for its held values (Preen already on `monitor`, a ducker with no look-ahead: no badge).
- **The sidechain filter on every keyed effect:** `sc_hpf` (20–2,000 Hz, 20 off), `sc_lpf` (500 Hz–20 kHz, 20 kHz off) and `sc_listen` (Off, On: the output is what the detector hears), one implementation (`fx_sidechain.h`: `SidechainFilter` over `dyn::KeyFilter`, 12 dB/octave TPT SVFs per side, each skipped at its end stop, bound to the descriptor's ids at prepare, coefficients worked out only when a value moves). Appended after `variant` (merged devices) or at the end of the table, so saved values, presets and references keep their meaning: Duck (`org.caw.duck` 0) and Carve (2), Gate (`org.caw.gate` 0) and Pedal gate (2) (both had `sc_hpf`: they keep their own and add `sc_lpf` and `sc_listen`), Ring mod (`org.caw.ringmod`: the key carrier) and Mimic (`org.caw.vocoder`: the Sidechain carrier). Talon (Down, Up, Bus), Hatch's Expand and the old Ducker had them already. Not added: the EQ's Linear and Match (their key is Match's reference spectrum), Phase align (its key is the alignment reference: a filter would move its phase) and Pitch fix (its key is notes only); there is no keyed envelope on the Filter. The filter applies to the detector's source (the key, or the input when the effect listens to itself), as Talon's does.
- **The Limiter's loudness target:** `target_on` (Off) and `target` (−30..−5 LUFS, −14), after `variant`, not automatable. Only a readout, never gain (a ceiling, not auto gain): while on, the Limiter's output runs through a `LoudnessMeter` (true peak off: its own detector has the peaks), integrated from when the target turns on and again at each Play. Display floats 7–12 below. RAM: one meter, about 100 KB an instance (none in an export); cost about the K-weighting's two biquads per side per sample while on.
- **CPU and latency per slot:** `caw_engine_component_cost(engine, layer_id, index, CawSlotCost* out)` (`Engine::componentCost`, `ComponentChain::readSlotCost`): `cpu_share` (the slot's process time over the audio time, 0.01 = 1 % of a core; smoothed a quarter of the way at each read; −1 until a measured block has passed), `latency_frames` and `latency_ms` (what it reports now, 0 bypassed), `armed` (the badge). Versioned: the caller sets `struct_size` and the engine writes only what it covers; fields are only appended. A read bumps a counter; the audio thread then times every slot of that chain (a `steady_clock` pair per slot, one block in four, single-writer relaxed atomics, no allocation; a clock read costs about 30 ns on the x86 test machine; measured with five slots: about 75–90 µs per second of audio more while watched, so about 15–18 µs/s a slot, noisy) until 2 s pass with no read, else costs one relaxed load a block. 1, 0 busy, −1 none.
- **Tests** (`fx_extras_test.cpp`, `fx_extras_rt_test.cpp`): an armed track's latency 0 + 512 + 0 + 481 against the full 2,304 + 2,048 + 2,400 + 1,441, back again disarmed or with monitoring off, never in an export, and the badges; no badge where the short setting is on already; armed Linear within −40 dB of Bands with the same band, no delay, the same bits at blocks of 1/37/64/512, and the FIR's delay again disarmed; Preen and Pitch fix's armed latencies; the Compressor's Up at 5 ms (240 frames, 0 armed, a click out where the reported latency says at blocks of 1/37/64/512, the chain's latency and badge through the Engine); every keyed effect has the three parameters, off by default and after `variant`; Listen plays the key, the low-pass at 500 Hz takes a 6 kHz key down by more than 30 dB and the high-pass at 1 kHz an 80 Hz key likewise; block sizes 1/37/64/512 the same bits with the filter on; a NaN in the key never sticks; explicit defaults the same bits as none; the Limiter's short-term and integrated loudness of a 1 kHz sine within 0.3 LU of −20 LUFS, the distances, the same bits with the target on as off; the cost unmeasured before a read, a share between 0 and 1 after, the utility's next to nothing, its own overhead printed; the C API's short-struct contract; and no allocation on the audio thread while arming toggles, the filter moves, Listen flips, the target meters and every slot is timed.

<!-- fx-racks begin -->
### Racks (containers, fx-racks)

Two built-ins hold other built-in effects inside one device (`engine/src/builtin_fx_racks*.cpp`): `org.caw.rack.fx` (a serial chain of up to 8 effects with its own modulators, after Kilohearts' Snap Heap) and `org.caw.rack.multiband` (up to 5 bands split by Linkwitz-Riley crossovers, each band a chain of up to 4 effects, after Multipass). **[Fact]** for what is below once the rack tests pass; the design was written first (2026-11-11).

- **Children live in the rack's own parameters.** Each slot has `<slot>_fx`, the child's identity: 0 for an empty slot, else the FNV-1a hash of the child's component id held to 24 bits (`rackChildHash`; exact as a double, never 0), and `<slot>_on` (Off/On, a 5 ms crossfade to the slot's input). Slots are `s1`..`s8` on the FX rack and `b1s1`..`b5s4` (band, slot) on the multiband rack. A child's parameters follow the rack's own as `<slot>.<param>` (`s2.cutoff`, `b3s1.threshold`), in slot order, each as its child declares it. So the project JSON needs nothing new: a rack is an ordinary component entry, `{"id": "org.caw.rack.fx", "params": {"s1_fx": 1234567, "s1.cutoff": 800, ...}}`, and presets (engine and user), undo, A/B compare and the app's value model carry the whole rack as one set of values. A hash rather than an index into a list means any built-in added later can go in a rack without a shared table; a test checks no two built-ins collide.
- **Per-instance descriptor.** The registry's descriptor of a rack lists only its own parameters (empty slots). `ComponentChain` builds a rack (an engine-internal `fx::RackContainer`) by calling `configure(registry, params)` right after `create`, before reading `descriptor()`: the rack creates its children from the `_fx` values and returns a descriptor with their parameters appended. Every value, ramp, automation lane (`"target": "s2.cutoff"` on the rack's component index), knob move (`caw_engine_set_component_param(..., "s2.cutoff", v)`) and MIDI mapping then works as for any parameter; the rack hands each child its slice of the resolved values. A slot naming an effect that isn't a built-in stereo effect, or a rack, refuses the update (a rebuild drops it instead). A change of any `_fx` value is a structural change: `sameComponents` compares the slots, so the chain is rebuilt (and crossfaded) like an effect added or removed; every other value keeps the running chain.
- **Modulation (FX rack).** The rack's modulators are 4 LFOs, 2 envelope followers, 2 ADSR envelopes, a random source and 8 macros. Its 16 routes use the Flagship's layout, `modN_src`, `modN_dst` (parameter index + 1 among every parameter before `mod1_src`: the rack's own and its children's), `modN_amt` (−1..1 of the knob's normalized travel) and `modN_bipolar`, so several routes may reach one knob and the app's mod matrix widgets work unchanged. The routes come after the children's parameters, so a structural change can move a destination's index: the app re-points routes by parameter id when it changes a slot. Modulation is worked out every 16 frames on the rack's grid and moves a child's value only between that child's own control points, never the base value the host holds.
- **Envelopes from notes.** The FX rack is a keyed effect (a `sidechain` input and a `key_notes` input): its ADSR envelopes are gated by the notes of the layer named as its key (their ons and, since 2026-11-19, their offs, so a note's release starts at its end), and a follower may follow the key's audio instead of the rack's input. A child that is itself keyed (the compressor, the gate, the ducker) hears the rack's key.
- **Latency.** The FX rack reports the sum of its switched-on children's latency (`VariableLatency`, so a child's own variable latency counts). The multiband rack delays every band to the longest band's latency and reports that; its crossovers are minimum-phase IIR and add none. The dry path of `mix` is delayed by the same amount, so dry and wet stay aligned.
- **Display data.** The rack publishes its own block (below). A child's block is read through `caw_engine_component_display` with `index` = the rack's index + (child + 1) × 65536, child counting the slots from 0 in the order above (the FX rack's `s1` is 0; the multiband rack's `b2s1` is 4). No new C function.
- **Nesting.** A rack never holds a rack (refused at configure): no depth to bound, and each rack's cost stays one level of children.
- **Real time.** Children are created, activated and their buffers set aside on the control thread when the chain is built; a rack change is a new chain swapped in lock-free like any other. The audio thread never allocates (tested in `caw_rt_tests`).
- **The modulation model is device-neutral (the base for #161).** A device that offers modulation declares, as ordinary parameters after the knobs it lets be modulated, N route slots of four values: `modN_src` (a choice; its *labels* name the sources, e.g. "LFO 1", "Macro 3"), `modN_dst` (0 none, else parameter index + 1 among the parameters before `mod1_src`), `modN_amt` (−1..1 of the target's normalized travel) and `modN_bipolar` (Unipolar/Bipolar: a source's 0..1 becomes −1..1). The device evaluates its sources on its control grid, sums every route's offset per target (several routes reach one knob), and applies `base normalized + offset`, clamped, to the processor's own copy of that knob's value; the host's base value, automation and undo never see modulation. The Flagship and the FX rack share this contract; the app's `ModMatrix` (`app/lib/model/mod_matrix.dart`) reads any device that declares it, mapping sources by their labels (so a device lists only the sources it has, in its own order) and, with `prefix`, presents a rack child's knobs (`s2.`) as the child's own. The project-level version of the same record is built (#161): see [Modulation](#modulation-project-modulators-and-links-161) below. The rack keeps its own modulators and evaluates its routes inline (`FxRack::control`); the arithmetic both use (`modRouteOffset`, `modulatedValue`, the shared LFO shapes and random hash) is one header, `engine/src/mod_route.h`, so the rules cannot drift. A choice list a later release appended to carries `legacyChoiceCount` (`ParameterDescriptor`, engine-internal like `group`, not in the C interface): on one of its old choices a route, slot or link moves the value in the old list's steps and within it (`modulationTop` in `params.h`, used by `modulatedValue` and `denormalizeOffsets`, and by Flagship's own matrix), on an appended choice over the whole list; set on Flagship's tables, warps and filter types (1.1.1). It costs a field test per modulated value, and a descriptor copy on the stack only for an old choice.
- **CPU** (callgrind instructions in `ComponentChain::process` per second of stereo noise at 48 kHz, 64-frame blocks, x86-64 GCC `-O2`, 2026-11-11; scratch bench, the chain's own small cost included): an empty FX rack 26 M (the modulators and the dry path); FX rack with a filter and a delay and one LFO route 78 M, against 16 M + 29 M for those two effects alone (so the container's cost is about 33 M with children); the multiband rack with 3 empty bands 47 M, with 5 empty bands 87 M (crossovers, all-pass compensation and band delays); 3 bands each with a compressor 104 M, against 17 M for one compressor alone. [Assumption] a phone core retires a few billion instructions a second, so a 5-band rack is a few percent of one core.

#### Research (Kilohearts, read 2026-11-11)

- **Multipass** ([kilohearts.com/docs/multipass](https://kilohearts.com/docs/multipass)): a band splitter into "up to five different frequency bands", Linkwitz–Riley filters so "a flat frequency response is achieved when the bands are mixed back together", split bars dragged on the display; per lane enable, mute ("the band splitter will still send audio to the lane, but it will simply be discarded"), solo, gain, pan and a per-lane mix; Pre FX and Post FX lanes around the split; eight macros; a modulation lane. **Ours:** the same split and per-band mute, solo, gain and pan, a bypass per band instead of the per-lane mix, an overall mix and output. Not covered: the Pre/Post FX lanes (a chain before or after the rack does that), per-lane mix, the auxiliary outputs, and modulators inside the multiband rack (its knobs take automation; a rack can't go inside the FX rack, so modulating them waits for #161).
- **Snap Heap** ([kilohearts.com/docs/snap_heap](https://kilohearts.com/docs/snap_heap)): "seven lanes" of snapins run left to right, adjacent lanes linked to run in parallel, each with enable, mute, solo, gain, pan and mix; eight macros routed to "several parameters"; a modulation lane; presets with a browser. **Ours:** one serial lane of up to 8 effects, each with its own on switch, 8 macros, and fixed modulators. Not covered: parallel lanes (the multiband rack with one band is not a substitute; a follow-up if wanted).
- **Modulators** ([kilohearts.com/docs/modulation](https://kilohearts.com/docs/modulation)): a lane of up to 32 modulators, among them Envelope (with a "Seamless" restart), LFO with custom shapes, LFO Table, Curve, Random (frequency, jitter, smooth, chaos), Audio Follower (gain, attack, release), Pitch Tracker, Note, Velocity, MIDI CC, Remap, limits, Scale and Sample & Hold; outputs unipolar, bipolar or inverted; several modulations per knob, each with its amount. **Ours:** a fixed set (4 LFOs with 8 shapes including a drawn one, sync, phase and free/retrigger/song modes; 2 followers on the input or the key; 2 ADSR envelopes from the key's notes, restarting from where they are as Seamless does; random with smoothing and S&H; 8 macros) and 16 routes with amount and polarity. Not covered: pitch tracker, curve, LFO table, remap and the modulator-of-modulator tools.
- **Technique.** Linkwitz–Riley 4th-order crossovers whose low and high outputs sum to an all-pass (S. Linkwitz, "Active Crossover Networks for Noncoincident Drivers", JAES 24(1), 1976); for more than two bands, each band passes the other splits' all-passes so all bands share one phase response and sum flat (the standard compensation, e.g. Zölzer (ed.), *DAFX*, 2nd ed., Wiley 2011, ch. 2 on filters and crossovers). LFOs are phase accumulators read through shape functions; the follower is a one-pole attack/release envelope on the rectified signal (*DAFX* ch. 4).
<!-- fx-racks end -->

### Modulation (project modulators and links, #161)

Any modulator output can move any parameter of any instrument or component on any track, bus or the master, another modulator's parameter, and a track's or bus's volume, pan and send levels. **[Fact]** for what is below: built and tested 2026-11-11 (`engine/src/modulation_links.*`, `builtin_modulators.*`, `mod_route.h`; tests `engine/tests/modulation_test.cpp`, `modulation_rt_test.cpp`).

- **One model.** A link is the racks' and Flagship's route record at project level: `amount` (−1..1 of the target's normalized travel), `bipolar` (a source's 0..1 becomes −1..1) and a `curve` (−1..1, 0 straight: `u^(1+3c)` above 0, `1 − (1−u)^(1+3|c|)` below; `detPow`). Links into one target add up (in link-id order), the sum moves the resolved base value's normalized position, clamped, and the base value (what the project stores, automation writes and undo restores) never changes. A device's own matrix (`modN_*`) keeps working inside it; a knob can have both.
- **Project JSON.** `"modulators": [{"id", "name", "layer" (where the app shows it), "input" (the layer it hears), "hears": "notes" | "audio" | "both", "tap": "pre" | "post", "component": {component entry}}]` and `"modulation": [{"id" (whole number ≥ 1), "source" (a modulator id), "output" (0..7), "target": {"type", "layer", "index", "param"}, "amount", "bipolar", "curve"}]`. Target types: `component` (`layer` absent or "" is the master chain; `index` the component; `param`), `instrument` (`layer`, `param`), `modulator` (`layer` is the modulator's id; `param`), `volume`, `pan` and `send` (`layer`; a send's `index`). A parameter whose descriptor says `modulatable: false`, or a choice in the app, takes none. Volume and send links move the fader's travel (the app's taper: 0 dB three quarters up, +12 dB at the top), pan links pan's −1..1 travel; a muted or soloed-out track (fader gain 0) stays silent whatever its links do.
- **Modulators** are components whose outputs are `CAW_PORT_MODULATION` ports (one float per frame), with at most one event input (the notes of `input`) and one audio input (its audio after its effects, or with `tap: post` after its volume and pan): `ComponentChain::isModulator`. A track's chain refuses one ("add it to the project's modulators"). Outputs are held to 0..1 (NaN → 0). They run as one `ComponentChain` built with `buildModulators`, so their knobs ramp, take values and keep state as any component's (`caw_engine_set_modulator_param`).
- **Built-ins** (`org.caw.mod.*`, written on the public `caw/modulator.hpp`): `lfo` (Sine, Triangle, Saw up, Saw down, Square, Sample & hold, Smooth random, Custom: 16 drawn points `p1..p16`; `rate` 0.01..40 Hz or `sync` (Free, 1/32 .. 2 bars); `phase`; `mode` Free, Retrigger (on each note of its input), Song (synced: locked to the song position)); `envelope` (ADSR `attack`, `decay`, `sustain`, `release` from its input's notes; `mode` Retrigger or Legato); `follower` (stereo audio input; `attack` 0.1..500 ms, `release` 5..5000 ms, `gain` ±24 dB); `steps` (16 steps `s1..s16`, `steps` 1..16 long, `sync` (default 1/16) or `rate`, `glide`; locked to the song while it plays); `random` (`rate`/`sync`, `smooth`, `seed`); `macros` (`macro1..8`, eight outputs `m1..m8`); `midi` (MIDI to CV: outputs `pitch` (`low`..`high` note to 0..1, `glide`), `velocity`, `gate`, `pressure` (channel or poly); `priority` Last, Highest, Lowest). The LFO shapes and the random hash are the FX rack's (`mod_route.h`).
- **User modulators**: `caw/modulator.hpp` gives port helpers (`mod::output`, `mod::notesInput`, `mod::audioInput`), a `mod::Modulator` base with `render(const mod::Block&)`, `mod::decode` for MIDI 2.0 note, pressure, controller and bend messages, `mod::HeldNotes` (last, highest, lowest) and `mod::Ramp` (control-rate output drawn straight between blocks). `examples/cpp/modulator` is a Euclidean gate (outputs `gate` and `ramp`); the engine tests load it as a trusted native component and route it to a track.
- **Timing.** Modulators run in blocks of 16 frames on a grid counted from Play, each block hearing what its input played in those 16 frames, and its output applies 96 frames (2 ms at 48 kHz) later, given the transport of the time it applies. So every input is known whatever order the mix graph runs tracks in, and the result is the same for every device buffer size and for an export (tested: one render in 64-frame blocks and one in blocks of 1..512 frames give the same bits). At each Play every modulator restarts from 0 (state reset, grid at 0), as an export starts. A target's offset is worked out on the same grid and drawn straight between grid points (control rate with smoothing); a component sees per-frame values only while the offset moves. While stopped the modulators run on (free LFOs move, previews are modulated).
- **Loops.** Notes never make one (a track's notes don't depend on its sound), so an envelope from a track's notes may move that track. A modulator that hears audio makes its input layer feed every layer its links reach (also through modulators it moves): `modulationEdges` adds those edges to `validateRouting`, which refuses a loop through folder buses, sends, keys and modulation alike ("a layer can't hear itself"). Modulators moving each other in a loop are refused too ("modulation makes a loop"); otherwise each runs after the modulators that move it, whatever their order in the list.
- **Sizes** (owner, 2026-11-19). Modulators, links, links per target and note-source links per layer are project-sized: the plan is built off the audio thread for what the project holds. Past 64 modulators, 256 links, 32 links into one target or 64 note-source links on a layer it still plays, with a warning that it costs much CPU (`modulationCostWarnings`); only past 4,096 of any (`kModSafetyLimit`, a safety bound against untrusted files) is a project refused, with an error naming the bound. 8 outputs per modulator (`modulation_links.h`); a per-note chain still runs at most 64 moved parameters (`kNoteFxMaxBindings`). The resolver's own 8 links (F6.3) don't apply: the host sums the links. A component with more than 8192 parameters, or more than 32 parameters with per-frame values in one block, plays the rest unmodulated.
- **Real time.** The control thread builds a plan (the modulators chain, rings of 256 frames of each output and each input heard, every target's links) and hands it over lock-free; the audio thread swaps it in at the next chunk and hands the old one back to be freed. A project update that keeps the same modulators, components and inputs keeps them running (phase, envelopes) and only sets values. Nothing on the audio thread allocates (`caw_rt_tests`: plan swaps, Plays, notes, knob and amount moves).
- **C API (version 19; 18 is the audio-engine branch's):** `caw_engine_set_modulator_param`, `caw_engine_set_modulator_bypass`, `caw_engine_set_modulation_amount` (a ring drag), `caw_engine_modulator_outputs` (each output at the last chunk, for a modulator's UI) and `caw_engine_modulation_offset` (a link's target's summed offset, for the knob's live dot); the readers never wait.
- **CPU** (wall time per second of audio at 48 kHz in 64-frame blocks, x86-64 GCC `-O2`, 2026-11-11, a scratch bench, two runs; two tracks of noise and saw with a filter each, about 7–9 ms alone): 1 modulator with 1 link adds about 1 ms; 4 modulators (LFO, random, follower) with 8 links into cutoff, resonance, volume and pan about 7–8 ms; 16 with 32 links about 11–13 ms; 64 with 128 links about 40 ms (4% of a core). Much of it is the targets themselves: a filter whose cutoff moves works its coefficients out per frame. [Assumption] a phone core is 3–5 times slower than this desktop core, so a handful of modulators costs a few percent of one core; measure on the S22+.

### Display data

What each built-in publishes through `caw_engine_component_display`, float by float (levels are peaks since the last publish in dBFS, floored at −120; gain reduction in dB as a positive number; a "now" value is the latest sample's). A built-in not listed publishes nothing. The app decodes these in `app/lib/model/device_display.dart` (`decodeDisplay`).

### Dynamics and drive: research and technique (fx-dynamics, 2026-11-11)

What the Dynamics and Drive effects are modelled on, and how. Kilohearts' current snapins are listed in its documentation ([kilohearts.com/docs/snapins](https://kilohearts.com/docs/snapins), read 2026-11-11: 3-Band EQ, Bitcrush, Channel Mixer, Chorus, Comb Filter, Compactor, Compressor, Delay, Distortion, Dynamics, Ensemble, Filter, Flanger, Formant Filter, Frequency Shifter, Gain, Gate, Haas, Ladder Filter, Limiter, Nonlinear Filter, Phase Distortion, Phaser, Pitch Shifter, Resonator, Reverb, Reverser, Ring Mod, Stereo, Shaper, Tape Stop, Trance Gate, Transient Shaper), with Faturator documented separately ([kilohearts.com/docs/faturator](https://kilohearts.com/docs/faturator), read 2026-11-11). Our code is original; the techniques below are textbook ones, in our own words.

- **Dynamics** (Kilohearts Dynamics: low and high thresholds and ratios dragged on the visualizer, attack, release, knee, in and out gain, mix; [Fact], their docs, 2026-11-11). What makes it good: one picture holds four processors (upward and downward compression, both expansions). Technique: a log-domain level detector and a static curve with a quadratic soft knee per threshold (Giannoulis, Massberg and Reiss, "Digital Dynamic Range Compressor Design — A Tutorial and Analysis", JAES 60(6), 2012; Zölzer (ed.), *DAFX*, 2nd ed., Wiley 2011, ch. 4). The instant-attack limit on the upward lift is our choice (a lifted transient otherwise spikes by the whole boost: +21 dB measured on a pluck before it, 2026-11-11).
- **Multiband with OTT** (OTT is Xfer Records' free plugin, after the "OTT" preset of Ableton's Multiband Dynamics, not a Kilohearts snapin; Kilohearts covers multiband through Multipass, a container). What makes it good: upward and downward compression per band at once makes quiet detail and loud parts meet, with a single depth knob. Technique: Linkwitz-Riley 4th-order crossovers (Linkwitz, "Active Crossover Networks for Noncoincident Drivers", JAES 24(1), 1976), whose low and high outputs sum to an all-pass, so the bands are phase-matched with all-passes; one detector and curve per band as above. OTT's per-band numbers are an [Assumption] (see the effect above).
- **De-esser** (no Kilohearts snapin). Technique: a compressor whose detector hears only the band above a frequency, applied to that band or to everything (Zölzer, *DAFX* ch. 4, de-essing as a sidechain-filtered compressor).
- **Clipper** (no Kilohearts snapin; Compactor covers clipping as a limiter and ducker, Limiter and Transient Shaper's Clip switch the rest). What makes one good: a transparent knee, oversampling so the harmonics don't fold back, and a guaranteed ceiling for mastering. Technique: a memoryless curve at 2^k times the rate between half-band filters (the Library's polyphase IIR half-band: Valenzuela and Constantinides, IEE Proc. G, 1983).
- **Phase distortion** (Kilohearts Phase Distortion: drive, normalize, tone, bias, spread, mix; "similar to feedback FM"; [Fact], their docs, 2026-11-11). Technique: a delay line read at a position the signal itself moves (self phase modulation), with fractional-delay interpolation (J. O. Smith III, *Physical Audio Signal Processing*, CCRMA online book, "Delay-Line Interpolation"). The fixed centre makes the latency constant and declarable. [Assumption] Kilohearts' exact mapping of drive to phase is not published; ours swings the delay up to ±46 frames.
- **Distortion types** (Kilohearts Distortion: drive, bias, spread, type overdrive / saturate / foldback / sine / hard clip / quantize, dynamics, mix; Bitcrush: rate, bits, ADC and DAC quality, dither, mix; Faturator: drive, fuzz, color, mix, stereo; Shaper: a drawn curve with drive, overflow and a DC filter; [Fact], their docs, 2026-11-11). Covered by the saturator's eleven modes, spread and dynamics; aliasing is held down by oversampling (2×, 4× at High) as Välimäki and others recommend for virtual-analog nonlinearities (e.g. Esqueda, Pöntynen, Parker and Bilbao, "Virtual Analog Models of the Lockhart and Serge Wavefolders", Applied Sciences 7(12), 2017, on folders' aliasing). Not covered yet (follow-ups): Bitcrush's ADC/DAC quality and dither, Faturator's color pre-emphasis and stereo turbo as their own knobs, and Shaper's drawn curve.
- **Transient or envelope follower to modulation** (no Kilohearts snapin; Phase Plant's envelope follower modulator). Not built: the graph executor has modulation outputs and links (F6.3), but projects and the app don't route them yet, so a follower's output would go nowhere. A follow-up once modulation links reach projects.

### MIDI effects (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (owner's sound plan: "on a track before its instrument", all in the same device kit).

- **Design:** a MIDI effect is a component of role `event_processor` with one event input and one event output and nothing else (`midifx::isMidiEffect`, `engine/src/midi_fx.h`), so a loaded one can be one too. A layer's `midiComponents` become entries 1.. of its instrument's chain (`ComponentChain::buildInstrument(..., midi)`; the instrument is entry 0), so building, crossfaded rebuilds, values, bypass, state, display and automation work as for any chain entry, and a change to the list rebuilds the instrument's chain (held notes move over, as on any rebuild). `ComponentChain::runMidiEffects` runs them in order on the block's events in `NoteEvents` (ping-pong between its storage and one scratch list of `NoteEvents::kCapacity`, made only with MIDI effects) and leaves the last one's output there; the Engine calls it after the pattern, device MIDI and note motion, before the modulation taps, the per-note FX and the instrument (`Engine::runInstrument`), and `process()` runs them itself when nothing did that block (a fading instrument, a Play tail), so note-offs always pass through them. A bypassed one passes its input; one that fails a block or writes events unsorted or past the block passes its input for that block. Keys for a ducker's `key_notes` still come from the pattern, not the MIDI effects.
- **Note ids:** a built-in MIDI effect at position p gives its own notes ids `0x80000000 + (p << 24) + n` (24-bit n), so they never meet the Engine's (which count from 1) or another MIDI effect's.
- **Timing and randomness:** each keeps a clock of frames since its reset and a queue of later events (sized to its job: none for Scale, 256 for the Arpeggiator and Chords, 512 for Humanize, 2048 for Note echo; a quarter kept for note-offs), and reads each knob at the frame of the event or step it shapes, so the output is the same at any block size (tested at 1, 37, 64 and 512). Random choices hash the seed with the note's song sample while playing (a running count while stopped), so every Play gives the same notes. Every note-on gets its note-off (tested over long runs), a strummed or echoed note released before it starts never starts, and non-note MIDI (controllers, bend) passes through.
- **Devices** (presets: 9 each, job-named, the description starting with its purpose):
  - Arpeggiator `org.caw.midi.arp`: `rate` (1/32 .. 2 bars, triplets and dotted), `pattern` (Up, Down, Up-down, Down-up, As played, Random, Chord), `octaves` 1–4, `gate` 0.05–1 of a step, `swing` 0–0.5 of a step on odd steps, `hold` (latch: a new chord after all keys are up replaces it), `vel_mode` (As played, Fixed) and `velocity`, `seed`. While playing, step n falls on song sample round((n + swing on odd n) × step × frames per beat) at the block's tempo, so a pass repeats and a key pressed between steps waits for the next; while stopped, the grid starts at the first key.
  - Chords `org.caw.midi.chords`: `root`, `scale` (the Library's 14 scales), `type` (Triad, Seventh, Ninth, Sus2, Sus4, Sixth built from scale thirds; Power and Octaves fixed), `inversion` 0–3, `voicing` (Close, Open: the second tone up an octave, Wide: the fourth too), `strum` 0–200 ms (low to high), `added` velocity. A key outside the scale is moved to its nearest scale note (a tie goes down); scales without seven notes use fixed major shapes. The played note keeps its id; bends move every tone.
  - Scale `org.caw.midi.scale`: `root`, `scale`, `mode` (Nearest, Down, Up, Filter: left out), `transpose` −7..7 scale degrees. Microtonal fractions and bends follow the note.
  - Note echo `org.caw.midi.echo`: `sync` (Free or a division) and `time` 10–2000 ms when Free, `repeats` 1–16, `decay` (velocity per repeat; stops under 0.02), `pitch` −24..24 semitones per repeat, `dry`. A repeat lasts as long as the played note.
  - Humanize `org.caw.midi.humanize`: `timing` 0–50 ms (late only: live notes can't come early), `velocity` ± 0–0.5, `seed`; the note's motion and off move with it.
- **C API 41:** `CAW_MIDI_COMPONENT` (0x20000000) | k addresses MIDI effect k in `caw_engine_set_component_param`, `_bypass`, `_display` and `_set_component_state`; `caw_engine_component_states` lists them with that index. Dart: `cawApiVersion` 40 and `Engine.midiComponentFlag` (no UI). Modulation links to a MIDI effect's knobs are not built (open).
- **C API 42 (1.1.0):** the clip pitch editor's engine: `caw_pitch_clip_analyse` (audio in, notes JSON out, a `CawPitchClip` handle), `caw_pitch_clip_curve`, `caw_pitch_clip_render` (edits JSON in, audio out) and `caw_pitch_clip_free` ([Lark](#lark-pitch-correction-and-the-clip-pitch-editor-110)). Dart: `cawApiVersion` 42 and `Bindings.pitchClipAnalyse`, `pitchClipCurve`, `pitchClipRender`, `pitchClipFree` (the clip pitch editor uses them, `lib/engine/pitch_clip_engine.dart`).
- **Cost** (`caw_bench 2 301`, x86-64 VM, GCC 13 Release, a four-note chord a beat and a 16th-note line at 120 BPM, µs of CPU per second of audio; bytes per instance as allocated): Arpeggiator 136 µs/s (13 KB), Chords 123 (24 KB), Scale 111 (7 KB), Note echo 16 repeats 564 (96 KB), Humanize 114 (25 KB): all light. A track with MIDI effects also holds a 115 KB event scratch list.
- **Tests:** `engine/tests/midi_fx_test.cpp` (each effect's notes, the arpeggiator's grid and swing, block sizes and NaN knobs, note-ons matched by offs, the chain live, uneven and offline giving the same bits and the same again after a second Play, a reference render `midi_fx.cawref`, the index flag, refusals, the project file); `midi_fx_rt_test.cpp` (no allocation while they play, while knobs move and while the list changes).

### Tools: Meters, Lo-fi, Phase align (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19. Each passes NaN through without keeping it (states that go non-finite start again) and gives the same output at any block size (tested at 1, 37, 64 and 512).

- **Meters (`org.caw.meters`)**, a merged device, `variant` 0 Analyzer, 1 Tuner, 2 Loudness (only the chosen one runs); the output is the input bit for bit; whole-signal only (not per note); 24 presets (8 a mode).
  - Analyzer: `source` (Left/right, Mid/side, Mono), `tilt` 0–6 dB/oct (default 4.5). The Library's display analyzer, fed and run only while a page reads it, 30 times a second. 1354 µs/s on screen, 116 KB.
  - Tuner: `reference` A4 415–466 Hz, `range` (Wide 30–1500 Hz, Bass 28–450, Guitar 70–1300, Voice 70–1500), `threshold` (YIN's, 0.15). YIN (de Cheveigné and Kawahara, JASA 111(4), 2002) written in-house from the paper (aubio is GPL): mono, low-passed at 2.5 kHz and decimated to about 12 kHz, a 384-sample window, 15 analyses a second, only on screen. Tested within 3 cents from E1 to E4 and at A415. 758 µs/s on screen, 20 KB.
  - Loudness: `target` −36..−5 LUFS (−14), `reset_on_play`, `reset` (Off to On starts over). The engine's `LoudnessMeter` (ITU-R BS.1770-5 K-weighting, gating and 4x true peak; EBU Tech 3341 V4 windows, the meter EBU R 128 specifies), always measuring, its integrated value worked out 10 times a second; tested on Tech 3341's −23 dBFS 1 kHz tone (−23.0 LUFS ± 0.1). 2492 µs/s (medium: the true peak), 113 KB. Loudness range (Tech 3342) is not built.
- **Lo-fi (`org.caw.lofi`):** `age` (macro: crackle +0.6, wear +1, hiss +0.5, wobble +0.5, grit from 0.6), `crackle`, `wear` (low-pass 20 → 2.5 kHz, low cut 20 → 90 Hz, soft saturation), `hiss`, `wobble` (wow 0.55 Hz, flutter 6.3 Hz, seeded drift, up to 3 ms), `band` (Off, Radio 200–4500 Hz, Phone 300–3400 at 24 dB/oct, Cassette 40–12000), `grit` (16 → 2 kHz hold, 16 → 6 bits), `seed`, `mix`, `output`. Each section runs only when above 0; the noise follows the input (0.25 s release, off below −80 dBFS), so it sleeps after its input. 10 presets. 1200 µs/s (band and hiss) to 3404 (every section, medium), 15 KB.
- **Phase align (`org.caw.phasealign`):** keyed (its `sidechain` is the reference track), 1 frame of latency (the host compensates). `mode` (Manual, Auto), `delay` 0–20 ms (sub-sample: 4-point Lagrange, smoothed over 20 ms), `invert`, `learn` (Off to On learns again), `window` 0.5–10 s. Auto accumulates the cross-power spectrum of frames where both sound and takes GCC-PHAT's peak (Knapp and Carter, 1976) within ±20 ms, refined by a parabola: the lag, the polarity (its sign) and a confidence; then it stops. A track that is late can't be moved earlier: the display gives the lag so the key's track can be delayed. Tested: 37 frames early and flipped found within 0.1 frame. 10 presets. 602 µs/s manual, 2141 while learning, 87 KB (FFT tables shared).
- **Cost ceilings:** `caw_bench SECONDS 301` (and the end of `300`) times every MIDI effect and tool against a ceiling and fails over it: 2000 µs/s for light, 8000 for medium (the owner's 0.5 % and 2 % of an S22+ core, **[Assumption]** a phone core 2.5x slower than this x86 one); `caw_tools_cost` runs it in Release ctest. Every cost mode (300–310) times each case against a reference workload in the same run, so a busy machine does not fail it ([performance](performance.md#cost-ceilings-against-a-reference-2026-11-19)).
- **Tests:** `engine/tests/fx_tools_test.cpp` (pass-through in every mode and block size, the analyzer's peak, tuner notes and cents, the EBU tone, Lo-fi off passing through and each section changing the sound, sleeping after its input, delays, polarity and learning, NaN, references in `fx_tools.cawref`).

### Voice devices: Glint, Soar's Harmonizer and Octaver, Mimic (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`builtin_fx_voice.*`; tests `fx_voice_test.cpp`, `fx_voice_rt_test.cpp`, references `fx_voice.cawref`; costs `caw_bench SECONDS 303`, also at the end of `300`, in Release ctest as `caw_voice_cost`). Original code. Each passes NaN and infinities without keeping them, settles subnormals to 0, sleeps after silence and gives the same bits at any block size (tested at 1, 37, 64 and 512). Costs are x86 µs per second of audio (this box, 2026-11-19); ceilings 2000 light, 8000 medium, 24000 heavy (**[Assumption]** an S22+ core 2.5x slower).

- **Glint (`org.caw.exciter`)**, a merged device, `variant` 0 Exciter, 1 Bass (`builtin_fx_exciter.cpp`). Both make harmonics the same way: a band of the input divided by its own peak envelope (instant attack) swings within −1..1 at any level, Chebyshev polynomials of it (T2 = 2u² − 1 is exactly a sine's 2nd harmonic, T3 its 3rd, T4, T5) are weighted and multiplied back by the envelope, so the harmonics follow the band's level in a straight line (tested: 20 dB quieter in, 20 dB quieter harmonics) and for a sine their levels are the weights, which the harmonics bars show. 19 presets (10 Exciter, 9 Bass).
  - Exciter: `freq` 800 Hz–12 kHz (3000; a 12 dB/oct high-pass gives the band), `amount` 0..1 (0.35; the harmonics at 2 × amount), `character` 0 even (warm) .. 1 odd (bright), `tone` 2–20 kHz low-pass on the harmonics (20 kHz: off, the default), `listen` (the harmonics alone), `output`. T2 and T3 only, shaped at 2x (the Library's polyphase IIR half-band, no latency) at Normal and High quality, which keeps a cubic alias-free below the old Nyquist; Eco shapes at the base rate. The harmonics are high-passed at `freq` again (the even part's DC and slow envelope) before they are added. 4178 µs/s at 2x (medium), 14 KB.
  - Bass: `freq` 40–250 Hz (100; the mid's lows below it, 24 dB/oct, make the harmonics), `amount`, `character`, `density` 0..1 (adds T4 and T5), `cut` 0..1 (crossfades the dry signal to itself high-passed at `freq`, 24 dB/oct, per side; it runs only while `cut` is up: headroom on small speakers), `listen`, `output`. The harmonics are band-passed `freq`..6 × `freq` (one SVF band-pass) and added to both sides. The missing-fundamental effect it relies on is textbook psychoacoustics; no product's design is copied. 1554 µs/s (light).
- **Soar's Harmonizer (`org.caw.shift` variant 2)** (`builtin_fx_shift_voices.cpp`): `voices` 1–4 (2), per voice `vN_degree` −14..14 scale steps (+2, +4, −3, +7: a third and a fifth above, a fourth below, an octave above in a seven-note scale; a semitone a step in Chromatic), `vN_level` 0..1, `vN_pan`, `key` C..B, `scale` (Major, Minor, Harmonic minor, Melodic minor, Dorian, Phrygian, Lydian, Mixolydian, Major pentatonic, Minor pentatonic, Blues, Chromatic), `formant` 0..1 (1 keeps the formants), `detune` 0–30 ct (voices ±, ±½), `glide` 1–300 ms, `mix` (0.5: dry and voices both full; 0 dry only, 1 voices only), `output`. The mid is tracked by YIN (`fx_pitchtrack.h`: written from the paper, decimated to about 8 kHz, 70 Hz–1.2 kHz, its lags spread four per control step, an analysis about every 10 ms, so the cost is even and block-size independent); its nearest scale note (0.3-semitone hysteresis) gives each voice its target, so the voice keeps the singer's bends and vibrato; unvoiced input keeps the last shifts. Each voice is pitch-synchronous overlap-add (the PSOLA principle, Moulines and Charpentier 1990): Hann grains two periods long at marks one period apart (a shared train, not epoch-picked), laid one new period apart, read at the input's speed (formants kept) or at the ratio (moved), loudness held by 1/√(grain density). Latency 1.5 periods of 70 Hz (1033 frames at 48 kHz, about 21 ms), the dry delayed to match, reported per instance. Tested: A3 and E4 in C major give C4 and G4, E4 in E major G#4, −7 an octave below, mix 0 is the dry delayed exactly. 3813 µs/s with 2 voices, 5932 with 4 (heavy class), 55 KB.
- **Soar's Octaver (variant 3):** `tracking` (Auto, Mono, Poly), `range` (Guitar, Bass: the tracking low-pass 900 or 350 Hz and Poly's window 30 or 50 ms), `sub` 0..1 (0.7), `up` 0..1, `dry` 0..1, `tone` 80 Hz–4 kHz (the sub's 12 dB/oct low-pass), `output`. Mono: a flip-flop toggled by each rising crossing of the tracked mid (hysteresis a tenth of its level) times the tracked signal is the sub-octave; the octave up is the tracked signal full-wave rectified less its DC; no latency. Poly: two delay-line shifters (ratios 0.5 and 2, two Hann-faded taps) that take chords but blur (their phase restarts every window) and lag by up to a window. Auto plays Mono while the crossing periods are steady (jitter under about 8 %: confidence over 0.6, back under 0.4) and fades to Poly over 30 ms otherwise. Mono 1377 µs/s, Poly 1678, Auto on noise (both) 2796 (medium class), 30 KB.
- **Soar's presets:** 19 (10 Harmonizer, 9 Octaver), after the old effects' (`fx::newVoiceVariantPresets`).
- **Mimic (`org.caw.vocoder`)** (`builtin_fx_vocoder.cpp`), a keyed effect (its `sidechain` and `key_notes` are the key track's audio and notes), no latency, so it is safe on an armed track; medium class. `carrier` (Built-in, Sidechain), `wave` (Saw, Pulse 35 %, Noise), `note` 24–96 (48: the note it plays with none held), `octave` −2..2, `unison` 1–3, `detune` 0–50 ct, `glide` 1–500 ms, `bands` (Auto: Eco 12, Normal 20, High 32; or 12, 20, 32), `low` 50–400 Hz (80), `high` 3–16 kHz (12000), `sharpness` 0.5–2 (each stage's Q from the band spacing, 0.7/(spacing × ln 2) at 1), `attack` 0.5–50 ms (4), `release` 5–500 ms (40), `formant` −12..12 semitones, `sibilance` 0..1 (0.3), `width` 0..1 (0.3), `freeze`, `mix`, `output`. The shared filterbank (`fx_bandbank.h`, Carve's; `bands::Synthesis` added for the carrier side): the modulator (the input's mid) through log-spaced 4th-order band-passes, followed; the carrier through the same bands, each weighted by the modulator's level `formant` semitones lower (interpolated, held at the ends) over the carrier's own level there (2 ms / 30 ms, at most +40 dB: any carrier speaks at the modulator's level), at control rate with ramps; the bands alternate left and right by `width`; the modulator high-passed at 5 kHz (24 dB/oct) is added by `sibilance`. The built-in carrier (the Library's polyBLEP saw and pulse, or white noise) plays the key's notes: note-ons within 10 ms make a chord of up to 8, a later one starts a new chord, a note-off lets its note go; with none held it plays `note`. **[Fact, engine.cpp `keyNotes`, 2026-11-19]** the key path carries the key pattern's note-ons and note-offs (an off for each on sent, with its id and key; a slide sends its first note's off at its end; at one frame offs come before ons, so a ratchet's next repeat is kept): the carrier follows the key track's MIDI, not the vocoder's own track (an effect hears no notes of its own track). Tested: a 1 kHz modulator speaks the drone's 8th harmonic over its 23rd (>20 dB), a 3 kHz one the reverse (>12 dB: overlapping bands let a saw's stronger low harmonics through), formant ±12 moves the spectrum's centre, the output within 6 dB of the input's level, the key's notes and chords and offs, a key track's note let go at its end through the engine (`mimic_lets_the_key_tracks_note_go_at_its_end`), the key's audio as carrier, silence asleep. 14 presets (the last, "Talking crow", is the plan's hidden-preset touch; hiding it is the page's work). 5169 µs/s at 20 bands, 6777 at 32, 17 KB.
### Clean-up: Preen and Hush (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`engine/src/builtin_fx_preen*.cpp`, `builtin_fx_hush.cpp`, `fx_clean*.h`). Two merged devices, each variant only its own path, whole-signal only. Original code from published methods (named in the files); no WPE (NTT's linear-prediction dereverberation patents, e.g. US8271277B2) and no RNNoise (an open owner question: Denoise's gain stage is one function, `DenoiseCore::rawGains`, that a voice model would replace as another mode).

- **The shared STFT** (`fx_clean_dsp.h`): both sides in one complex FFT (the Library's `Fft`), sqrt-Hann analysis and synthesis, a quarter-window hop; FFT tables and windows shared by every instance (built at prepare). **[Fact, tested]** the latency is exactly one window (the plan's assumption that it can be cut to window − hop does not hold with this bookkeeping): `quality` Eco 1,024 (21 ms), Normal 2,048 (43 ms), High 4,096 (85 ms) at 44.1/48 kHz (doubled above 70 kHz, again above 140 kHz, halved below 32 kHz). `quality` and `monitor` are set by hand only (no lane, modulation or MIDI); each variant reports its latency from them (`VariableLatency`), so the chain compensates. **Monitoring:** `monitor` On switches to a 512 window (11 ms) whatever the tier; an armed track gets the same window without it (the armed signal, "Around the effects").
- **Preen (`org.caw.clean`)**, `variant` 0 Denoise, 1 De-hum, 2 De-reverb:
  - Denoise: `reduction` 0–40 dB (12; 0 is the input back to rounding, null below −90 dB), `sensitivity` ±12 dB (the noise estimate's offset), `smoothing` (gains across bins, weighted by how far down they are, and falling gains held over time), `low`/`high` protect (at 20 Hz and 20 kHz: off), `transient` (an onset lets the gains up), `adaptive` (minimum statistics over 1.5 s, Martin 2001, fixed bias 2), `learn` (set by hand: On averages the noise played and passes it unchanged; Off keeps the profile, saved as the component's state, ≤ 65,560 bytes, checked on load), `listen` (what is removed), `quality`, `monitor`. Gains: decision-directed a-priori SNR (Ephraim and Malah 1984, a = 0.95), Wiener, floored at −Reduction. Adaptive uses the tracked noise; Adaptive off uses the learned profile (none: unity). The profile is used only with Adaptive off.
  - Denoise's **clip pass** `fx::denoiseClip(inL, inR, outL, outR, frames, rate, DenoiseClipSettings, error)` (`fx_clean.h`): offline at the High window, aligned (no latency), the gains a triangle-weighted mean of the frames 3 either side; noise from a given profile (Denoise's state bytes) or the clip's own tracked estimate averaged. Plain arguments so a C API function can wrap it; no C API or app call yet.
  - De-hum: `frequency` (Auto, 50, 60, Custom), `custom` 20–400 Hz, `harmonics` 1–16 (8), `width` 0.5–20 Hz, `depth`, `balance` (odd −1 … even +1), `follow` (drift; off: held after 6 s), `listen`. Goertzel filters on the mono sum decimated to about 4 kHz pick the 50 or 60 Hz comb over 1.5 s windows and refine the fundamental from each harmonic's log-power parabola; TPT SVF notches at the harmonics; no latency. Tested: 50.3 and 59.8 Hz found within 0.2 Hz, harmonics 1–3 down by more than 20 dB, a 440 Hz tone within 0.5 dB; depth 0 is the input bit for bit.
  - De-reverb: `amount`, `decay_mode` (Auto, Set), `decay` 0.1–10 s, `early` 20–100 ms (T_l), `floor` −40–0 dB, `smoothing`, `low`/`high`, `listen`, `quality`, `monitor`. Lebart, Boucher and Denbigh (2001): late power = the power (50 ms smoothing) T_l earlier × 10^(−6 T_l / T60), removed by a floored power-subtraction gain; Auto estimates T60 from steady 15 dB falls of the frame energy over 250 ms. Tested: a 1.2 s noise reverb's tail down by more than 4 dB, the bursts within 3 dB.
- **Hush (`org.caw.vocalclean`)**, `variant` 0 De-click, 1 De-plosive, 2 Breath, on one event detector (2.7 ms hop: band energies below 150 Hz, 150 Hz–5 kHz and above 5 kHz, zero crossings, spectral flatness of a 256-point FFT, voicing from a normalised autocorrelation at about 12 kHz only when asked; each variant turns on only what it reads):
  - De-click (5 ms latency): `sensitivity`, `length` max 0.1–2 ms, `mode` (Click, Crackle: lower threshold, at most 0.5 ms), `listen`. Order-16 LPC per side (refitted every 5.3 ms on repaired audio); a click is a residual above k × its running RMS (k 10 → 4); the gap is rebuilt by least-squares AR interpolation (Janssen et al. 1986), the Toeplitz system solved by Levinson. Tested: 32 two-sample clicks on tones all found, error down by more than 20 dB; clean tones untouched.
  - De-plosive (3 ms): `sensitivity`, `frequency` 60–300 Hz, `amount` 0–24 dB, `length` 10–80 ms, `listen`. A low-band burst rising over its recent level (15 → 6 dB) and 4 times more than the mid band sets a low shelf to −Amount for that event (bypassed otherwise, so the voice passes exactly). A sustained bass note's start can trigger it for up to Length (it is for voices).
  - Breath (20 ms): `sensitivity`, `range` 0–40 dB, `fade` 2–30 ms, `keep` (between phrases), `listen`. A hop is breath-like when it is 12–55 dB under the phrase's level, flat, noisy (zero crossings), not mostly above 5 kHz (not a sibilant) and unvoiced; 20 ms of them start the fade down (so it lands at the breath's start in the delayed audio); over 600 ms is not a breath. **[Assumption]** `keep` lowers a breath that follows a pause of 250 ms or more by half the Range (the plan's "keep between phrases" is not defined further: an owner question). The plan's exact clip mode is not built.
- **Presets:** 50, at least 8 a variant (`fx::cleanPresets`, through `fx::newVariantPresets`).
- **Display data:** see the table above (`org.caw.clean` and `org.caw.vocalclean` rows).
- **Cost** (`caw_bench SECONDS 310`, also at the end of `300`; `caw_clean_cost` in Release ctest; this x86 box, 2026-11-19, µs per second of audio at 48 kHz; bytes per instance from the allocations): Denoise 9,400 (Normal), 9,000 (Eco), 10,300 (High), 8,500 (monitoring), 451 KB (sized for High, so a tier change never allocates); De-hum 2,700 (8 notches) to 5,000 (16), no buffers; De-reverb 8,600 (Normal), 9,600 (High), 336 KB; De-click 4,400 with 10 clicks a second, 24 KB; De-plosive 1,000, 2 KB; Breath 2,400, 15 KB. Ceilings: 24,000 (heavy: Denoise, De-reverb), 8,000 (medium: De-hum, De-click, Breath), 2,000 (light: De-plosive). **[Assumption]** a phone core 2–3× slower: Denoise and De-reverb about 2–3 % of an S22+ core.
- **Tests:** `engine/tests/fx_clean_test.cpp` (variants, latency per tier measured against the reported one, transparency, SNR on notes in noise, a learned profile saved and restored, musical noise lowered by smoothing, the clip pass aligned and undoable, hum found, reverb tail, clicks, pops, breaths, block sizes 1/37/64/512, NaN, presets, references in `fx_clean.cawref`: Preen stable, Hush chaotic), `fx_clean_rt_test.cpp` (no allocation while knobs, tiers, monitoring and Learn move).

### The AutoStacc beta pack: Murmur, Peck, Bend, Iris (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`engine/src/builtin_pack*.cpp`, `engine/include/caw/library/grain.hpp`). Four new devices of the AutoStacc beta pack: built into the engine for now (an AutoStacc download from 1.2.0, the private plan's decision), each marked in `caw_engine_components` with `"pack":"autostacc-beta"` beside `"builtin":true` (`fx::packOf`, `builtin_pack.h`). Each runs only its own path, sleeps on silence (except while frozen or held), allocates only at prepare and is original code. Costs are x86 µs per second of audio against the reference workload (`caw_bench SECONDS 307`, also part of 300; ctest `caw_pack_cost`); the phone is assumed about 2.5× slower **[Assumption]**. Murmur's densest case at High's cap of 64 grains is a stress case with its own ceiling of 32,000 µs/s, as Flock's and Timber's at-cap cases are: about 21,000 here, and **[Fact, PR #258 CI, 2026-11-19]** 24,836 on GitHub's x64 runners, which measure the pack 10–20 % above this machine after normalisation.

- **The grain engine** (`caw/library/grain.hpp`, header-only, shared by Murmur and later Flock): a source (`Ring`: a stereo recording ring padded so a cubic read never wraps an index; or a padded read-only sample), a control-rate scheduler (`schedule`: grains start on their own frame by the interval with `jitter`, so timing is block-size independent) and a fixed pool of `kMaxGrains` (128) capped per quality tier (`murmurCap` 16/32/64, `flockCap` 64/128/128; a grain spawned while full is dropped and counted, its random draws still made so later grains don't shift). A grain holds a read position, rate (pitch; negative backwards), window phase, gains and pan; per grain-sample one interpolated stereo read (Catmull-Rom at Normal and High, linear at Eco) and one window-table lookup (Hann, Tukey, Gaussian, Expodec, Rexpodec: 1,025-point tables and their means, built at registration). A ring grain's start age is kept so every frame it reads is recorded and not overwritten (its age moves by 1 − rate a frame while recording, −rate frozen); a grain too long for the ring is shortened. Pitch spread may be quantised (`quantise`: Chromatic, Major, Minor, Pentatonic, Octaves, Fifths). Seeded (`seed`). Flock's additions, unused by Murmur (whose renders are unchanged): a scheduler clock per caller (`schedule(…, next, voice)`: each Flock voice schedules into the one shared pool, its grains tagged with the voice), `renderVoices` (each grain into its voice's buffer, reading the mip copy its `level` names; position and rate in 32.32 fixed point and window phase in 0.32, exact integer sums, so any split into chunks gives the same frames; finished grains stay until `retire()` at the period's end, so the summing order never depends on the split), `endVoice`, and in `Settings` the mip `levels` and `fit` (a sample grain starts where its whole read fits, else sounds only while inside the sample).
- **Murmur (`org.caw.pack.granular`)**, granular texture; no latency; medium (heavy at a dense cap). `buffer` 0.5–4 s (by hand; a new length starts the ring silent), `position` 0..1 back from the newest frame, `spray` 0–500 ms, `size` 1–500 ms, `density` 0.5–200 Hz or `sync` (a division), `jitter`, `pitch` ±24 st, `spread` 0–24 st and `scale`, `reverse` chance, `window`, `pan` spread (balance law, unity in the centre), `glide` 0–2 s (the pitch knob's smoothing), `width` 0–2 (the wet's side), `feedback` 0–0.95 (the wet soft-clipped back into the recording 32 frames late), `freeze` (holds the ring once it has been filled, so a project or preset that starts frozen records first), `scan` −2..2 (frozen: moves through the ring at that rate), `seed`, `mix`, `output`. Each grain's level is 1/(overlap × window mean), eased toward 1/√ of it as spray, spread and jitter decorrelate the grains. Seeded at reset and when the song starts (from `seed` and the start beat to a 16th). Tested: with size 10 ms, density 200 Hz, Hann and no spray the output is the input 24 frames late within −60 dB (Normal and High), the cap per tier, freeze holds 440 Hz over 1 kHz input and plays on over silence, an octave up, seeds, reverse, block sizes, bad input. 12 presets. 2445 µs/s at the default, 12402 dense at Normal's 32, 4506 at Eco's 16, 21066 at High's 64 (heavy); 1.56 MB.
- **Peck (`org.caw.pack.glitch`)**, glitch on the song's grid; no latency; light. `grid` 1/32–1/2 (at most 2 s), `chaos` (the chance a slice is glitched), the weights `repeat` (the previous slice again), `stutter` (the slice's first 1/N repeated N times; N 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 up to `stutters`), `reverse` (the previous slice backwards), `gate` (open for the first quarter, half or three quarters), `pitch` (the previous slice resampled by whole semitones up to `range`, looped), `seed`, `hold` (repeats the slice before it while held; nothing is recorded meanwhile), `smooth` 0.5–10 ms (each action fades from and back to the dry sound at its edges), `mix`, `output`. The rolls are pure functions of (seed, slice), like Stutter's, so a song glitches the same way wherever playback starts and the display shows a bar's plans before they play. Tested: chaos 0 is the input bit for bit; each action on a ramp; plans repeat per seed and differ across seeds; hold; block sizes; bad input. 11 presets. 447–969 µs/s, 0.78 MB.
- **Bend (`org.caw.pack.warp`)**, warp; 3 frames of latency (the newest a cubic read takes; the dry path is delayed to match); light. `mode` Time or Speed, `cycle` 1/4–4 bars (locked to the song, free when stopped), `depth` 0–1000 ms (Time), `range` 0–2 octaves (Speed), `points` 4/8/16 of `p0`–`p15` (−1..1; default a sine of 8: a gentle wow), `shape` Linear, Smooth (cosine) or Step (cyclic, the last point back to the first), `smooth` 0–200 ms (a one-pole on the delay or speed at control rate, then a per-frame ramp), `resync` (Speed: back to the newest frames at each cycle's start, crossfaded 10 ms), `mix`, `output`. Time: the delay is 4 frames + `depth` × (value + 1)/2, so a rising curve slows and lowers the sound and a falling one speeds it up (faster than real time: backwards). Speed: 2^(value × range); the read is held inside the 2 s ring (at an end it moves at 1×). Tested: depth 0 is the input 3 frames late at mix 1, 0.5 and 0; a delay rising by a third of real time plays 1 kHz at 667 Hz; half speed plays it at 500 Hz; block sizes; bad input. 10 presets. 1001–1239 µs/s, 0.78 MB.
- **Iris (`org.caw.pack.spectral`)**, spectral effects on Preen's STFT (`fx_clean_dsp.h`); heavy. `quality` (by hand) Eco 1,024, Normal 2,048, High 4,096 points at 44.1/48 kHz: the latency is exactly that window, reported per setting (`VariableLatency`). Per side and bin, each stage costing nothing at its neutral value: `gate` −100 (off)..0 dB of a full-scale sine's bin, dropping a bin under it by `gate_range` 0–80 dB; `blur` 0..1 (each bin's magnitude through a one-pole of 20 ms–3 s, its phase the input's, run on at the bin's centre frequency where the input goes quiet); `freeze` (captures the average of the next 6 frames over −80 dB that each follow another (about 64 ms at Normal, 48 kHz; owner's ask 2026-11-19, smoother than one frame): each bin's magnitude their mean, its frequency, measured from its phase advance over the hop, their magnitude-weighted mean, its phase the last frame's; a new window restarts the average; the frozen layer is that spectrum times the painted curve, advanced at those frequencies, its phase dithered by `drift`, faded in and out over `fade` 10–4000 ms); `paint` 0..1 (how much of the painted curve applies); `shift` ±2000 Hz in whole bins, each moved bin's phase turned to match; `mix` in the bin domain (so the dry sound is aligned) and `output`. **The paintable frozen spectrum:** the painted curve (64 log bands, 20 Hz–20 kHz, −60..+12 dB, interpolated across bins) and the captured spectrum are the component's state, version 1: `IRIS`, 64, bins, window, rate (float), 64 paint floats, then each side's magnitudes and frequencies in bins (20 + 256 + 16 × bins bytes; nothing when unpainted and uncaptured), checked on load (magic, bands, a power-of-two window 256–65,536 whose bins it holds, the exact size, finite values held to their ranges) and taken by the audio thread at its next frame through a seqlock, resampled by frequency to another window. A UI paints by writing the state (`caw_engine_set_component_state`) and reads the frozen spectrum and paint back from the display; a state loaded while frozen plays without a new capture, and a reset keeps it. Tested: with every stage off the output is the input one window late within −90 dB at each tier; freeze holds 440 Hz over 1 kHz input and over silence and lets go; a freeze over 440 Hz turning into 1 kHz two hops in holds both (the average) and reports nothing captured until its frames are in; the state saves, reloads, paints the 440 Hz bands down 40 dB (the tone drops 30–45 dB), refuses bad states and plays at another window; shift moves 1 kHz to 1210.9 Hz; the gate removes −50 dB noise and keeps a loud tone within 1 dB; blur spreads a click; block sizes; bad input. 11 presets. 6845 µs/s with every stage off at Normal, 10342 all on, 7879 Eco frozen, 11693 High all on; 0.35 MB at 48 kHz.
- **Open questions (owner):** whether the pack mark should also reach the native descriptor (it is only in the components listing now); Murmur's dense High cap sits near the heavy budget (21066 of 24000).

### Lark: pitch correction and the clip pitch editor (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`engine/src/lark_dsp.h`, `builtin_fx_lark.*`, `lark_editor.*`, `capi_pitch_clip.cpp`; tests `lark_test.cpp`, `lark_rt_test.cpp`, references `lark.cawref`; costs `caw_bench SECONDS 306`, also at the end of `300`, in Release ctest as `caw_lark_cost`). Original code from the papers named in `lark_dsp.h` (YIN, de Cheveigné and Kawahara 2002; pYIN's candidate idea, Mauch and Dixon 2014, ideas only; TD-PSOLA, Moulines and Charpentier 1990). Monophonic and time-domain throughout: no STFT, no spectral separation, no event-to-note association (the design's line against Celemony's US8022286B2, whose claim 1 needs all three on a polyphonic recording). Keep vibrato ships (owner, 2026-11-19); Antares' pending vibrato claims are to be re-checked when published.
