- **Detection** (`lark::Detector`, shared with the editor): the mid, two biquads at 3 kHz, decimated to about 12 kHz; every 64 decimated samples (5.3 ms at 48 kHz) d′ over the newest window (16 ms; 10.7 ms monitoring) against the samples a lag earlier, threshold 0.05 + 0.2 × `sensitivity`, stopping once the first dip under it rises again (the design's FFT form costs the same every hop; the direct form costs a fraction on a voice), then the lag refined at full rate (the raw difference at three lags, two periods' window, a parabola). Unvoiced under about −70 dBFS or with no dip. **[Fact, tested]** test tones 82–880 Hz (sine and voice-like) within 2 cents; a voice-like synthetic with 5.5 Hz vibrato, a glide and noise at about 20 dB SNR: median error 1.4 cents, 90th percentile 7 (while it moves), no octave errors, held notes within 5 cents.
- **`org.caw.pitchfix`** (Lark), a keyed effect (its `key_notes` are the key track's notes), medium class. `key` C..B, `scale` (Chromatic, Major, Minor, Harmonic minor, Melodic minor, Dorian, Phrygian, Lydian, Mixolydian, Major pentatonic, Minor pentatonic, Blues, Custom), `note_c`..`note_b` (Custom's notes, all on), `target` (Scale, MIDI: the held key-track note nearest; none held: the scale), `speed` 0–400 ms (40; 0 snaps), `humanize` 0..1 (lengthens the constant up to 4× as a note is held, over 300 ms), `keep_vibrato` (On: the shift is the target minus the pitch's 2 Hz centre, two one-poles reset on a 1.5-semitone jump, so the vibrato above it passes; Off: minus the pitch itself), `formant_keep` (On: the grains keep the spectral envelope; Off: it follows the shift), `formant` −12..12 st, `low` 50–400 Hz (60), `high` 200–1600 Hz (1000), `sensitivity`, `mix` (against the dry delayed by the latency), `output`, `monitor` (set by hand only). The target has 0.2-semitone hysteresis; the shift is at most 12 semitones; unvoiced hops pass unshifted and a voiced run's shift starts from 0. Each analysis is kept with the input time it describes (1.5 periods before its newest sample), and each grain takes the period and shift of its own moment, so the correction lines up with the pitch it fixes.
- **TD-PSOLA:** synthesis marks one target period apart, each taking the analysis mark nearest its input time (a train one period apart, its first on the waveform's peak at each voiced onset, where the synthesis aligns to it), a Hann grain two input periods long resampled by the formant factor (0.5–2), read sample by sample, so a grain may end after the input it reads arrives and the latency need only cover the detection; unvoiced: 256-frame grains at the delayed input exactly. **Latency:** 1,200 frames (25 ms) in Full, 480 (10 ms) with `monitor` (the floor raised to 100 Hz, the shorter window), reported per instance (`VariableLatency`); a change of `monitor` starts the state again. **[Fact, tested]** a click comes out exactly the latency late; an in-tune voice matches its input best at the latency (±2 frames) and within −30 dB; noise passes within 1e-5.
- **Tested:** 455 Hz goes to Bb4 chromatic and A4 in C major, 270 Hz to C#4 in D major and C4 in C major, custom notes, each within 5 cents; Speed 100 ms is still over 10 cents sharp at 0.1 s and within 3 cents after 0.5 s; Humanize is slower; Keep vibrato corrects a sharp vibrato's centre to within 6 cents and keeps over 75 % of its depth, Off flattens it below 40 %; a held key note sets the target; a 5-semitone shift keeps the envelope's centre within 8 % (Formant keep) or moves it by over 20 % (Off), as Formant +5 alone does; block sizes 1/37/64/512 bit for bit; NaN, infinities and subnormals; 13 presets; the display; no allocation on the audio thread (rt). Reference renders chaotic (the detector's threshold decisions move a grain on a 1-ulp difference).
- **Cost** (x86 µs per second of audio, this box, 2026-11-19, against the normalised reference timing): default 3,000, hard tune 3,000, formant +5 following 2,700, formant −12 (the most grains) 3,700, monitoring 2,600; ceilings 8,000 (medium). 83 KB an instance at 48 kHz (two 4,096-frame input rings, the detector's full-rate ring; over the design's 64 KB guess). **[Assumption]** an S22+ core 2.5× slower: about 0.75 % of a core.
- **The clip pitch editor's engine** (`lark::analyse`, `lark::render`; C API 42 below): one voice. **Analysis:** per hop (centred windows, 21 ms) every dip of d′ is a candidate, weighted by the share of a beta(2, 18) prior over thresholds 0.01..1 that picks it first; the likeliest four and an unvoiced state go through a Viterbi pass (staying voiced 0.99, a pitch move costing in a straight line to nothing at 3 semitones a hop, with a floor); the chosen lag is refined at full rate. Notes: voiced runs, cut where the pitch leaves the note's running mean by over 70 cents for 40 ms or at an onset (6 dB up within 3 hops); notes under 30 ms join their neighbour. Each note: start and end, centre (the energy-weighted median), drift (the residual below about 2 Hz: two zero-phase one-poles at 2.5 Hz) and vibrato (the rest: rate from its crossings, depth from its rms), transitions in and out (over 50 cents off), gain; the drift and vibrato curves per hop. **Render:** per-note edits (pitch offset, drift 0..1, vibrato depth 0..2, formant, formant keep, time move, stretch, gain, transition ramps) give each source hop's shift; a time map moves each note with its onset and release (20 ms, at most half the gap) and the gaps give way; TD-PSOLA from the original runs only over the edited regions (crossfaded over 10 ms; windows normalised where runs start and end); everywhere else the output is the input bit for bit. Deterministic, bounded (30 minutes, 8–192 kHz, 100,000 notes, edits checked). **[Fact, tested]** a three-note phrase gives three notes within 6 cents and 20 ms, drift on the drifting one, 5.5 Hz vibrato found within 0.8 Hz and 12 cents; no edits returns the input exactly; +2 semitones re-analyses at +2 (6 cents) with its drift kept and level within 1.5 dB; drift 0 and vibrato 0 remove most of each; a 50 ms move re-analyses within a hop; a near-identity edit resynthesises within −60 dB (measured −79).
- **Not built:** quantise pitch and time for a whole clip (the app sets per-note offsets and moves from the notes' centres and starts), vibrato rate scaling, and the edits' storage in the project and the render cache (the app's). The editor's page is the app's ([app](app.md), "The clip pitch editor", 2026-11-19).

### Timber: the modelled acoustic instrument (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`engine/src/builtin_timber*.cpp`, `timber_core.h`, `timber_dsp.h`, `timber_piano.h`, `timber_keys.h`, `timber_strings.h`, `timber_mallet_wind.h`, `timber_tables.cpp`; tests `timber_test.cpp`, `timber_harness.h`, `timber_rt_test.cpp`, references `timber.cawref`; the piano's Simulated mode 2026-11-20: `timber_piano_sim.h`/`.cpp`, `timber_piano_curves.h`, `timber_piano_tables.cpp` and the generated `timber_piano_tables.inc` from `engine/tools/timber_piano_gen.cpp`, tests `timber_piano_sim_test.cpp`, `timber_piano_compat_test.cpp` with its per-build hash tables, `timber_piano_rt_test.cpp`, references `timber_piano.cawref`; costs `caw_bench SECONDS 321`, also at the end of `300`, in Release ctest as `caw_timber_cost`) (the models at their usual polyphony are held to medium plus 20 %, 9,600 µs/s: they sit at 85–95 % of medium here, and **[Fact, PR #258 CI, 2026-11-19]** GitHub's x64 runner reads them about 8 % higher, the electric piano 8,205). Original code from the published methods named in each header; nothing sampled. The app's page is in [app](app.md) (Timber).

- **One instrument, one model running.** `org.caw.timber` ("Timber") is a merged device (`fx_merged.h`, `timber::mergedTimberDevices`) whose `variant` (named "Model", by hand only, last) picks the class: 0 Piano, 1 Electric piano, 2 Organ, 3 Plucked, 4 Bowed, 5 Mallets, 6 Winds. Each model is its own `timber::Instrument<Model>` with its own descriptor (the shared knobs, its own, then `variant`), so only that model's voices and buffers exist, and a model change rebuilds the instrument. Role instrument, `notes` event input and stereo `out`, whole domain only, capabilities notes and MIDI in, no latency.
- **Shared knobs** (first in every model): `tone` −1..1 (a ±6 dB tilt at 700 Hz), `body` 0..1 (what the model's body or soundboard adds), `space` 0..1 (a send to a built-in `lib::Reverb` room set per model), `velocity` 0..1 (sensitivity: 0 plays every note at 0.75), `release` 0.005–10 s (the dampers or the release after key-up), `voices` (the cap), `level` −48..12 dB.
- **The frame** (`timber_core.h`): a `VoicePool`; the knobs read every 16 frames on a grid counted across blocks, and voice ends, steals and sleep decided only on that grid, so every block size gives the same bits; a key struck again fades its sounding voice (in every model: one string, bar, reed or key); over the cap the quietest released voice fades out in 5 ms, else the oldest held; caps per tier (Eco/Normal/High): Piano 16/32/48, Electric piano, Plucked and Mallets 16/32/32, Organ 61, Bowed and Winds 8. CC 1 (mod wheel) and CC 64 (sustain) are read; poly pressure goes to the voice. Asleep (no voice, its tails and the room below −120 dB for 0.5 s) every state is zero, nothing moves and it says `quiet()`.
- **Piano** (Heavy): the commuted digital-waveguide piano (Smith and Van Duyne, ICMC 1995; US5777255 lapsed 2010), kept outside Modartt's US7915515B2 claim 1 as the private design reads it (owner-settled 2026-11-19): no per-partial damping or frequency coefficients, no sum of partials, no soundboard physical parameter. Per voice 1 string below A#1, 2 to A2, 3 above (Eco: 1 from C5 up, else at most 2), run as 4 SIMD lanes: a delay with an exact first-order tuning allpass (its phase delay solved at the fundamental), 8/6/4/3/2 first-order dispersion allpasses by register (Eco half, High +2) with one coefficient per key from a curve over the keyboard, and a one-pole loss designed from two target decay times per key (at the fundamental and, faster, at about 3.5 kHz). The strings meet at a bridge that takes c × their sum from each (Weinreich's coupling: the in-phase motion decays 4× faster, the unequal strike and the unison detune feed the slow out-of-phase motion): the two-stage decay and beating. Excitation: one of 24 synthesised soundboard tables (Grand, Upright, Felt × 8 register zones of 0.3 s, 16-bit, built at registration, shared; see [decisions](decisions.md)), read again 0.6 and 1.5 ms later on hard strikes, minus a copy at 0.12 of the string (the strike-position notch), blended with a bare hammer pulse by `body`, through two one-poles whose cutoff rises with velocity and `hammer`. Dampers on keys below F#6 (`release` s, darker), held by `sustain` (0..1, half pedal between) or CC 64; `soft` (una corda) misses the third string and softens the hammer; `resonance`: one shared bank of 12 undamped strings (C2–B2) fed by the sum while the pedal is down. Knobs: `hammer`, `character` (Grand, Upright, Felt), `unison` 0–6 ct, `stretch` 0–2 (a Railsback-like curve: −25 ct at A0, +30 ct at C8 at 1), `decay` ×0.25–4, `sustain`, `soft`, `resonance`. **[Fact, tested]** every key tested (A0..C8) within 0.06 ct of its stretch target at 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz; partials stretched like a stiff string (A2's 10th +7 ct, C4's 10th +25 ct, C6's 5th +28 ct); early decay (0.1–1 s) 19–30 dB/s against 5–9 dB/s later (C3, C4, C5); the share of the attack above the 6th partial rises 11–20 dB from pp to ff and the level about 18 dB; a released key drops over 30 dB in 0.8 s, held by the pedal it does not; keys within 12 dB of each other in loudness. **[Assumption]** the decay targets are typical published grand-piano values, not fitted to a recording.
- **Piano, Simulated** (1.1.2, built 2026-11-20; the private design's "1.1.2: realism", approved by the owner): `sim` (Mode: Classic, Simulated; by hand only) picks it; Classic stays the default and renders every 1.1.0 preset bit for bit (`timber_piano_compat_test.cpp`: each piano preset's 3 s render hashed against 1.1.1's, with tables for x86-64 Release, x86-64 Debug (the sanitizer job) and Arm64, recorded 2026-11-20 from 2a9934c; Windows has no table yet, and a second test holds Classic's bits with every new knob moved, on every platform). Appended knobs (Classic ignores them): `tier` (Quality: Setting, Eco, Normal, High; by hand only; Setting follows Settings › Sound › Quality, the others hold this piano's sound at one tier while its voice cap still follows the setting), `noise` 0..1 (0.35: the mechanical noises about 30 dB under an mf C4, a listener's distance; 1 about 20 dB under, close-miked), `touch` (pressed .. struck: the finger on the key), `action` Off/On (the hammer 2–30 ms after note-on by velocity; off by default, owner 2026-11-20), `glide`, `sympathy`, `phantom` (High).
  - **The hammer** is a build-time simulation: `tools/timber_piano_gen.cpp` steps Chaigne and Askenfelt's finite-difference stiff string with a nonlinear felt hammer (F = K d^p) for 22 key zones × 6 hammer speeds (0.5–6 m/s) and stores each force pulse (8 ms, 16-bit, its peak force and contact time) in `timber_piano_tables.inc`; ctest `timber_piano_tables_current` regenerates the file and fails if it differs beyond the last rounding step. A voice blends its two nearest zones and speeds and injects the pulse minus its reflection a strike point later (the comb comes from the physics, no hammer filter); `hammer` and the soft pedal move the pulse's shape along the speeds. Contact times fall from bass to treble (about 4 → 1 ms soft, 2.8 → 0.5 ms hard) and with speed (tested).
  - **The strings** are Classic's loop (tuning allpass, Weinreich coupling, the loss's two decay targets) with: the dispersion's one coefficient per key and tier fitted at prepare time to the inharmonicity curve B(key) (`timber_piano_curves.h`: Rigaud, David and Daudet's two-asymptote form, the bass asymptote fitted to the Salamander Grand's partials), 6/5/4/3/2 stages by register (Eco half, High +2); decay curves set against the Salamander Grand's measured decays; at least two lanes (a single bass string's two polarisations); the energy-based pitch glide (Avanzini, Marogna and Bank): loud bass notes start up to about 10 cents sharp and settle as their running mean square falls (not on Eco; updated every 64 frames).
  - **The soundboard** runs once after the voice sum: Bank's parallel sections on a fixed logarithmic pole grid (24 Eco, 48 Normal, 96 High), their left and right weights fitted offline (by the generator) to a synthesised modal board per character (Grand, Upright, Felt; a 2 % loss factor to 3 kHz), impulse-invariant at any rate; `body` sets the board against the dry strings.
  - **The noises are contacts** (Hunt and Crossley, stepped only while they touch): the hammer's force straight into the board (the attack thump: grows with velocity, stronger in the treble), the key's front on the felt keybed into a shared 8-mode key-frame resonator (before the string at pp, after it at ff when the action is on), the finger on the key (`touch`), the dampers' landing (the loss rises over about 18 ms; the buzz is the felt's one-sided contact with the string's own motion, so it follows the string's amplitude), and the pedal's trapwork and 72 seeded damper felts into their own resonators, to the output only.
  - **Sympathy:** a key held down without a strike (velocity under 0.03) listens to the struck strings' bridge sum, 16 frames late (`sympathy`); struck strings only drive it, so the coupling cannot feed back. With the pedal down, a 24-mode register bank on the stretched fundamentals of C2..B3 (Eco 12) takes the strings' sum, scaled by the pedal and `resonance`. High adds two phantom-partial modes per voice (the longitudinal modes driven by the string's motion squared, `phantom`).
  - **The patent reading** (Yamaha US10978031B2 and US12039960B2, Casio US11094307B2, Modartt US7915515B2) is in [decisions](decisions.md): nothing depends on how a key is released (a note-off's velocity changes no bit, tested), no noise reaches the resonance (its input is bit-identical with and without the pedal noise, tested), no excitation is joined or windowed, nothing is per partial.
  - **Strict arithmetic:** the per-sample code (`timber_piano_sim.cpp`) is compiled without reassociation, so any block size gives the same bits (tested at 1/37/64/512 with every noise, the action, sympathy, glide and the pedal, at each tier).
  - **[Fact, tested 2026-11-20 against numbers measured from the Salamander Grand, CC-BY 3.0, A. Holm; no audio ships]** B from partials 2–15 within 5 % of the curve for every A and D# from A0 to A6 (the test allows 10 %); broadband T60 per octave 0.1–1 s after the peak 11.9, 7.1, 4.4, 2.7, 2.1, 2.8, 3.1 s against 13.1, 6.9, 5.1, 2.5, 2.1, 2.9, 2.8 s (octaves 1–7), and 2–6 s 22, 25, 17, 22 s against 25, 21, 16, 24 s (octaves 1–4; higher up the recordings reach their noise floor first), within the test's 25 %; the attack's share above the 6.5th partial grows 5.5 dB (C2) and 19.8 dB (C4) from pp to ff against 6.3 and 20.7, the level 12.4 and 14.0 dB against 14.0 and 13.5 (6 dB allowed); a silently held C3 rings at C4's fundamental after C4 is damped (−63 dB against nothing without `sympathy`); keys as loud as Classic's, key by key.
- **Electric piano** (Medium): `kind` Tine or Reed; four modes per key (fundamental, a tonebar just off pitch, the cantilever's 6.27× and 17.55× bell) into a pickup curve: Tine a magnet's flux h²(x+s)/(h²+(x+s)²) (`pickup` distance, `symmetry`), Reed a soft cubic with an even term; `bark` a soft clip after it; `bell`; `tremolo` and `rate` (Tine an auto-pan, Reed an amplitude tremolo); `decay`. Tested: a close pickup struck hard has 25 dB more 2nd/3rd harmonic than a far one struck soft; in tune; damped on release.
- **Organ** (Medium): nine drawbars `db16`, `db513`, `db8`, `db4`, `db223`, `db2`, `db135`, `db113`, `db1` (0–8, 3 dB a step; default 888000000) from one shared 8,192-point sine read without interpolation (about −80 dB error), each wheel's phase set from the instrument's clock at key down, so keys sharing a wheel are in phase; foldback below 32.7 Hz and above 5.9 kHz; `click` (3 ms noise at contact and release); `perc` Off/2nd/3rd, single-triggered, `perc_decay` Slow 1 s/Fast 0.25 s, the 1' drawbar cut while it is on; `vibrato` Off, V1–V3, C1–C3 (a delay swept at 6.9 Hz); `drive`; `rotary` Off/Slow/Fast/Brake: `org.caw.rotary`'s own class run inside (`fx::makeRotaryAs`), the mod wheel past half switching Slow to Fast, `body` its horn/drum balance. Velocity does not change the level. Tested: 8' alone is a sine 50 dB over its neighbours, 16' and 5 1/3' at their footages, shared wheels add 6.02 dB (in phase), percussion only on a detached note, Fast swings the level.
- **Plucked** (Medium): `instrument` Steel guitar, Nylon guitar, Harp, Koto; an extended Karplus–Strong loop (Jaffe and Smith 1983: exact tuning allpass, two-point loss) plucked by a period of shared noise minus itself at `position` (the comb), low-passed by `pick` and the velocity; `damping` (palm mute), `brightness`, `decay`; a released key damps in `release` except on the harp; the body is four resonances after the sum. Tested: in tune within 1 ct, the quarter-string pluck's 4th harmonic 15 dB under its neighbours, mute and release, the harp rings on.
- **Bowed** (Medium): `section` Violin, Viola, Cello, Bass; a two-line bow-friction waveguide (after McIntyre, Schumacher and Woodhouse 1983), friction r = 1/(1 + (k dv)²)², `pressure` (k), `speed`, `position`, `vibrato` and `vib_rate` (fading in after 0.25 s), `attack`; poly pressure and the mod wheel push the bow; the body (five resonances and the bridge hill, scaled per section) runs once after the voices' sum. The design's 2,048-tap convolved body was not built: resonators cost no latency. Tested: sustains within 4 dB, within 5 ct, stops after release.
- **Mallets** (Medium): `instrument` Marimba, Vibraphone, Xylophone, Glockenspiel, Chimes, Bell; four to eight complex one-pole modes, 4-wide, at each instrument's ratios; `hardness` (with velocity, tilts the modes), `decay`, `position`, `damping` (high modes shorter), `dampen` (how much key-up damps), `motor` (tremolo Hz, 0 off). Tested: the 1st and 2nd modes within 1 ct of their ratios, a hard mallet 16 dB brighter, the damper.
- **Winds** (Medium): `instrument` Clarinet, Flute, Sax, Brass; a bore line with a reed table (clarinet; sax the same with its even harmonics folded in, an assumption), a jet and its sigmoid (flute, the bore 1.5 periods as the model speaks at 1.5× its lowest mode), a lip resonator (brass, the bore two periods); each in its speaking pressure window (`breath` within it; velocity, poly pressure and the mod wheel set the loudness), `noise`, `reed` (stiffness, jet length, lip tension), `vibrato`, `vib_rate`, `growl`, `attack`; the flute's and brass's measured pitch pull aimed off. Tested: each speaks within 4 dB steady and within 12 ct from A2 to A5 (brass from C3 up), and stops after release.
- **Presets:** 95 (with the other models' 37 Simulated, below): eight Classic piano and 14 Simulated piano (Real grand, close-miked, recital hall, pop, jazz, upright, felt upright, struck touch, pedalled ballad, ambient wash, soft-pedal nocturne, honky-tonk, Eco, High for export), six of each other model, named for the job (`builtin_timber_presets.cpp`), each naming its `variant`.
- **1.1.2 Simulated** (every model but the piano; built 2026-11-20, `timber_sim.h` and a Simulated block in each model's class; tests `timber_sim_test.cpp`, `timber_sim_rt_test.cpp`, `timber_compat_test.cpp`, references `timber_sim.cawref`; [decisions](decisions.md) for what was read and the patent check). Each model appends `sim` (Classic, Simulated; default Classic, which plays as 1.1.0 bit for bit: the 36 non-piano 1.1.0 presets are hashed against their 1.1.0 renders) and `noises` (0..1, default 0.3, a listening position; 1 close-miked) after its own knobs; the organ appends `sim` only, its `click` being its contacts' hardness. Each Simulated path runs only when chosen and is kept out of line beside the 1.1.0 loop (`CAW_TIMBER_NOINLINE`: the engine's `-fassociative-math` otherwise changes that loop's bits). Tiers: Eco keeps each model's cheapest simulated parts, High adds a little (below).
  - **Winds:** flute: the jet drive (Fletcher 1976; Verge, Hirschberg and Caussé 1997): the jet's delay from its speed U = √p around the 1.1.0 jet length (a weak breath is slow, so the attack searches), the share of the jet entering the pipe ½(1 + tanh((η − y₀)/b)) (y₀ from `reed`) on an open-open bore of a period, breath turbulence ∝ U × the flux into the pipe, so ∝ U² and pulsing with the period (Chafe 1995); clarinet: the reed table with the tongue as a contact (closed, the reed reflects all and passes no flow; it leaves in 4–12 ms, and a release under 60 ms is a tongued stop), turbulence ∝ the flow, High a mass-spring reed (2.3 kHz) before the table; sax: the same reed at a truncated cone's closed end (a first-order allpass, −1 low and +1 high, so every harmonic sounds; [Assumption] it speaks from about G3 up); brass: an outward lip mass-spring whose opening passes h√|Δp| into a two-period bore, the velocity pushing the lips harder, and brassiness on the radiated wave (a crest arriving earlier as its own pressure: Hirschberg et al. 1996), off at Eco. Pad, key and valve clicks (half-sine forces into the bore at each note and slur, `noises`). Measured pulls aimed off per kind (five keys).
  - **Bowed:** elasto-plastic friction (Dupont et al. 2002; Serafin, Avanzini and Rocchesso 2003): bristles z' = v(1 − α(v, z) z/zss), force σ₀z + σ₁z' (σ₀ 4,800 s⁻¹, σ₁ 0.3 in the waves' units), zss from fs = 0.45 Fn and fc = 0.2 Fn on a Lorentzian Stribeck curve (vs 0.1), Fn from `pressure`; the elastic solution is exact while sticking, past the breakaway (0.7 zss) Newton's method (1 step Normal, 2 High) from the last sample; bow noise ∝ the sliding speed while it slides and the bow moves; a released bow lifts and the fingers damp the string. Eco: 1.1.0's friction table with the bow noise. Measured pull aimed off (+14, +9, +10, +8 ct by section).
  - **Plucked:** the string let go from its plucked shape (a triangle at `position`, the round trip and its mirror) through the pick's low-pass, a scrape (`noises`); fret buzz (guitars, Normal and High): the loop's swing bounces off the frets' gap (Evangelista and Eckerholm 2010, [Assumption] the gap fitted by ear); handling noise on a wound string's slide (Pakarinen, Penttinen and Bank 2007): a pulse train at speed / 0.8 mm through the string's longitudinal mode, its RMS linear in the speed; a released string's finger touch as loud as the string still is.
  - **Mallets:** a Hunt–Crossley contact (1975) of the head (4 ms yarn to 0.25 ms hard, Hertz's law shortening it with the blow) on the bar's modes through their shapes at `position`, so a hard or loud strike excites the high modes; the head's knock (`noises`). Eco: 1.1.0's strike.
  - **Electric piano:** the same contact for the neoprene hammer on the tine (1.6 ms) or reed (1 ms), `bell` setting how near the tip it meets; the action's thunk (a 110 Hz body resonance) and the damper felt at release (as loud as the tine still is) heard acoustically (`noises`). Eco: 1.1.0's strike with the thunk and felt.
  - **Organ:** a key's nine contacts close (and open) at their own moments within 3 ms (seeded per key) and bounce once; each switches its wheel on where it is, so the click is the step in nine sines, `click` its hardness (1 a sample, 0 over 1.5 ms); after 6 ms the tone is 1.1.0's to the bit. [Assumption] nine contacts, not checked against a service manual.
  - **Tested:** every model Simulated at block sizes 1/37/64/512 at every tier bit for bit, silent to exact zeros within 45 s, NaN knobs finite, loud chords bounded, no allocation while playing; winds speak within 4 dB and 12 ct (sax from G3); the flute's breath noise follows the flow (correlation 0.99) and its envelope peaks at f₀ (37 dB over its neighbours), its attack 29 dB noisier than the steady tone; the clarinet's odd harmonics 45 dB over its even ones, the sax's even ones within 12 dB of its odd; under the tongue −46 dB, a tongued stop 34 dB down in 0.1 s; brass: the 2nd and 4th harmonics 3.3 dB nearer the 1st at ff than pp, 3.3 dB more than at Eco; the bow's Helmholtz motion (each harmonic within 3 dB of 1/n to the 8th on the cello and bass, the 4th on the viola) inside Schelleng's window and 23–35 dB off it with no force, slipping 1.5–2× its position of each period; slide noise's rate and RMS linear in speed (ratio 1.94 for 2×); a hard low E buzzes (2 dB more over 2 kHz than Eco), a soft one renders as at Eco; a hard mallet 37 dB brighter than yarn, contact 147 against 21 samples; the tine brighter struck hard; the organ's hard contacts 9 dB brighter in the first 4 ms than soft. Chaotic reference renders per model.
  - **Cost** (x86 µs per second of audio, Normal, `caw_bench SECONDS 321`, 2026-11-20, a box busy with other builds): electric piano 16 voices 8,400–9,000 (reed 7,800), organ 10 keys 7,000–7,300, plucked 16 voices 7,000–7,500, bowed 8 voices 8,800–10,800 (the closest: its friction's sample-to-sample chain is latency-bound one voice at a time), mallets 16 voices 5,200–7,200 (bell 7,800), winds 8 voices 6,600–10,300 (brass the highest), against the 9,600 ceiling; the electric piano, bowed, sax and brass cases carry their own regression ceiling of 11,200 (`kSim` in `bench/timber_cost.cpp`) until a 4-wide voice loop brings them under it ([Open]). RAM per instance unchanged (Bowed 0.66 MB, Winds 0.66 MB, Plucked 0.60 MB, the others 0.28–0.30 MB).
- **Display data:** see the table above.
- **Tests:** the above (the Simulated piano's in `timber_piano_sim_test.cpp`, its no-allocation run at each tier in `timber_piano_rt_test.cpp`), plus every model at block sizes 1/37/64/512 bit for bit, the cap and steal without a larger step than the notes make, silence to exact zeros within 45 s, NaN and infinite knobs, no allocation while playing (rt), and chaotic reference renders per model (long resonant loops amplify a last-bit difference).
- **Cost** (x86 µs per second of audio against the reference, this box, 2026-11-19; budgets light 2,000, medium 8,000, heavy 24,000): Piano 16 voices 20,500 (heavy, the design point), 8 voices 11,600, 32 voices pedal down 44,800 (a stress case, ceiling 56,000), with the room 25,100; Simulated (2026-11-20) Normal 16 voices 21,100–22,700 (ceiling 24,000), pedal down 22,000–23,000, Eco 16 voices 15,300–16,600 (ceiling 18,000: about 0.7 heavy, not the design's estimated half), High 32 voices pedal down 51,000–53,400 (exports, ceiling 64,000); Electric piano 16 voices 7,600, 32 reed 13,700; Organ 10 keys 888 with the rotary 6,600, every drawbar, percussion, C3 and Fast 8,900 (ceiling 12,000), no rotary 3,800; Plucked 16 voices 6,700, 32 harp 11,400; Bowed 8 voices 6,300–6,800; Mallets 16 marimba 6,300, 32 bells 17,000; Winds 8 voices 6,000–7,300. **[Assumption]** an S22+ core 2.5× slower. RAM per instance at 48 kHz: Piano 1.39 MB (56 voices × 3 lines), Plucked 0.60 MB, Bowed and Winds 0.66 MB, the others 0.27–0.29 MB (most of it the room); the shared tables 0.87 MB once per process (the Simulated piano's hammer pulses and board fits 0.11 MB of it, read-only data).
- **Open questions (owner):** the Simulated piano's Normal cost sits at the heavy budget like Classic's, and Eco at about 0.7 of it; High's live hammer, duplex strings and a fitted register filter are not built; a silently held key ends after about 0.3 s if nothing has made it sound (the frame's rule); a recording-derived soundboard excitation (the CC-BY Salamander residual the design names) instead of the synthesised one; whether the piano's 16-voice cost (near the heavy budget) needs an Eco default on the phone; the electric piano's and brass's costs sit within 10 % of the medium ceiling, so CI may need a margin.

### Flock: the granular instrument (1.1.0)

Built 2026-11-19 (`engine/src/builtin_flock.cpp`, `builtin_flock_presets.cpp`, `flock_source.cpp`/`.h`, on `caw/library/grain.hpp`; tests `flock_test.cpp`, `flock_rt_test.cpp`, references `flock.cawref`; costs `caw_bench SECONDS 322`, also at the end of `300`, in Release ctest as `caw_flock_cost`). Original code. Its app page: [app](app.md) (Flock).

- **The instrument.** `org.caw.flock` ("Flock"): role instrument, `notes` event input and stereo `out`, medium, no latency. Up to 16 voices (`voices`, 4 more slots for stolen voices fading over 5 ms; at the cap the oldest released voice is stolen, else the oldest), each a note with its own grain scheduler, all scheduling into **one grain pool shared by every voice** (`flockCap`: 64 at Eco, 128 at Normal and High). Each voice's grains sum into its own buffer, then through its ADSR (`attack`, `decay`, `sustain`, `release`), its state-variable `filter` (Off by default, Low-, High-, Band-pass; `cutoff`, `resonance`, `filter_env` octaves on its own envelope that decays over `filter_decay` from the note, `filter_key` tracking) and its velocity gain (`velocity` sensitivity); `width` scales the side of the sum, `level` the output. Knobs are read and voices end on a 16-frame grid counted from the first note, so every block size gives the same bits; with nothing sounding for 50 ms it sleeps (`quiet()`).
- **The grain knobs** (Murmur's, without buffer, feedback, mix and output): `position` 0..1 into the source, `spray` 0–500 ms, `size` 1–500 ms, `density` 0.5–200 Hz or `sync`, `jitter`, `pitch` ±24 st, `spread` 0–24 st and `scale`, `reverse` chance, `window`, `pan` spread, `glide` 0–2 s (a new note slides from the last one), `scan` −2..2 (each voice's position moves through the source from its note on at that many times real time), `freeze` (holds the position: no scan), `seed` (the cloud is reseeded each time Flock wakes). Each grain's level is Murmur's rule (1/(overlap × window mean), eased toward 1/√ as grains decorrelate). Key tracking: each grain's centre pitch is `pitch` + `keytrack` (−1..1, default 1) × (key − `root`, default 60).
- **The source.** The sample is named by the instrument's state, `{"path": "<file>"}` (version 1, at most 16 KB, the path at most 4,096 bytes; `{}`: none), loaded through the asset store as the sampler loads its zones and **read in place, shared and read-only** (its first and last two frames serve the cubic read, so a grain's frame 0 is the file's frame 1). A kept Flock whose project state changes loads it (`flock::applyProjectState`, beside the sampler's). With no sample, or one that fails to load (display float 6), it plays a **built-in tone** (`builtin`, by hand: Strings, Glass, Breath, Choir), 2 s at middle C, synthesised once per process and rate (additive, or filtered noise for Breath; the timbre moves through the 2 s), so presets sound out of the box.
- **Mip copies** (`flock_source.cpp`): four half-band copies (a 31-tap Blackman-windowed half-band, zero phase, every second frame kept), built off the audio thread when the source is built and shared by every Flock playing that asset (a cache of weak references). A grain more than an octave up (rate over 2) reads the copy that brings its rate to at most 1, so nothing folds over; at most 48 st up reads copy 4. RAM: about 94 % more than the sample (the design's estimate was 50 %, for fewer levels); a sample over 120 s gets no copies (it reads its own frames at any rate).
- **Presets:** 12 (`builtin_flock_presets.cpp`), each on a built-in tone, tagged Sound design, Vocals, Bass or Lo-fi.
- **Display data:** see the table above.
- **Tests** (`flock_test.cpp`): the registration and every preset audible and finite; each built-in tone plays, key tracking sets each grain's pitch exactly and moves the spectrum an octave; with size 20 ms, density 100 Hz, Hann, no spray, pitch or filter and scan 1, the grains reconstruct a sample within −80 dB (Normal and High); a 9 kHz tone two octaves up leaves no fold-over at 12 kHz (more than 60 dB down) while a 2 kHz tone two octaves up still sounds; the shared pool holds 64 (Eco) and 128 (Normal) and counts drops, and a voice cap of 4 holds; freeze holds the position; block sizes 1/37/64/512 bit for bit (built-in tone and sample); quiet to exact zeros; NaN and infinite knobs, extreme keys, NaN and infinite samples and denormal-small samples stay finite; a missing sample falls back to the built-in tone and the state is kept; chaotic reference renders (three built-in tones, a sample up 19 st). `flock_rt_test.cpp`: every built-in tone with notes, knob moves and display reads allocates nothing.
- **Cost** (x86 µs per second of audio against the reference, this box, 2026-11-19; medium budget 8,000): 6 voices at the defaults (about 14 grains overlapping, the design point) 4,894; the same at Eco 4,159; a stereo sample 2 octaves up through the mips 6,502; 4 voices on a sample, about 9 grains, 4,490; 8 voices of 64 grains 14,290 (ceiling 24,000, heavy); 16 voices at High's cap of 128 grains 30,092 (a stress case, ceiling 40,000). 23.6 KB per instance; the built-in tones about 0.75 MB each per rate, once per process; a sample's mips about 0.94 × its size.

## Descriptor domains and the voice bus (#120, #127)

The design and the later steps (the per-note FX host) are in [architecture](architecture.md#per-note-processing-127-and-descriptor-domains-120); this is what the engine has (2026-11-11, branch `note-fx`).

- **C interface revision 3.1** (`CAW_COMPONENT_ABI_REVISION 1`, `engine/include/caw_component.h`; the version stays 3 and the entry `caw_get_component_v3`): `CawComponentDescriptor` gains `domains` (`CAW_DOMAIN_WHOLE` 1, `PER_CHANNEL` 2, `PER_VOICE` 4, `PER_NOTE` 8), `capabilities` (`CAW_CAPABILITY_NOTES_IN` 1, `MIDI_IN` 2, `EVENTS_OUT` 4, `SAMPLE_ASSETS` 8, `NEEDS_TRANSPORT` 16, `EXPORT_CLAP` 32), `cpu_class` (`CAW_CPU_UNSPECIFIED`, `LIGHT`, `MEDIUM`, `HEAVY`) and a reserved 0; `CawProcessBlock` gains `voices`. The loader (`module_registry.cpp`) and the CLAP and VST3 wrappers accept a descriptor of at least `CAW_COMPONENT_DESCRIPTOR_SIZE_3_0` bytes and read the new fields only when `struct_size` covers them; an older one, or `domains` 0, is whole-only. The SDK export (`caw/component.hpp`) writes them and hands `voices` to `processPorts` when the host's block has it. `engine/tests/native_component_v30_fixture.c` is a plain-C library with a 3.0 component and a 3.1 instrument that reads the lanes.
- **C++ fields** (`caw/module.hpp`): `ModuleDescriptor::domains` (default `kDomainWhole`), `capabilities` (default 0), `cpuClass` (default unspecified); validation codes `InvalidDomains`, `DomainPortMismatch`, `InvalidCapabilities` (rules in [architecture](architecture.md#domains-and-capabilities-120-fact)). `caw_engine_components` lists `"domains"` (`"whole"`, `"per_channel"`, `"per_voice"`, `"per_note"`), `"capabilities"` (`"notes_in"`, `"midi_in"`, `"events_out"`, `"sample_assets"`, `"needs_transport"`, `"export_clap"`) and `"cpu_class"` per component; no new C API function, so `CAW_API_VERSION` stays 17.
- **Built-ins:** `effectDescriptor` (`builtin_effects.cpp`) gives Whole|PerNote and medium cost; `keyedEffectDescriptor` whole-only (with `key_notes`, NotesIn); the limiter and the racks whole-only; `withDomains` and `withCpu` change one effect. Heavy: reverb.pro, convolver, shimmer, multiband, pitch shifter, the racks, the Flagship. Light: utility, balance, width, bitcrush, tremolo, auto-pan, channel mixer, Haas, imager, tilt, clipper. **[Assumption]** these classes are judged from the algorithms, not measured. The Flagship is Whole|PerNote with NotesIn and MidiIn (the mod wheel), the drums with NotesIn and SampleAssets, the test signal with NotesIn.
- **Layout rules** (`graph.cpp`): mono into stereo is duplicated (plain, summing and `setRouting` connections); other mismatches are `ChannelLayoutMismatch`. `org.caw.downmix` (`builtin_effects_kit.cpp`, registered internal): stereo in, mono out, `mode` Average, Left, Right. `ModuleRegistry::createDualMono(id)`: a component declaring PerChannel with exactly a mono input and a mono output, as two instances behind a stereo descriptor (domains whole; state saves the left instance and loads into both).
- **The voice bus** (protocol in `caw_component.h` above `CawVoiceLane`): `caw::VoiceLanes` in `caw/instrument.hpp` (`bind` in `beginBlock`, `laneFor`, `begin`, `run`, `sumInto`, `runAndSum`, `end`, `endBlock`, at most 64 lanes); `VoicePool::start(noteId, &lanes)` ends a stolen note's lane; `InstrumentProcessor::endBlock` runs after a block's last render. The Flagship sums lanes before `runShared` and its shared stage, forces a laned voice off the shared filter 1 (its own filter, the same to rounding), and stays awake (not idle, not `quiet()`) while any lane is assigned; the drums sum each lane into the output its hit played to (remembered after the hit ends), with the room send dry; the test signal plays each note's lane. With `voices` null every path is the one before (their renders unchanged).
- **Tests:** `engine/tests/voice_bus_test.cpp` (a gain-per-lane host harness: lanes for other notes give the same bits, each note's lane is its solo render times the gain, ENDED and steal, the mono lane, the Flagship and drums laned at unity equal to rounding and a muted lane removing that note, the built-ins' declarations and the listing, the 3.0 and 3.1 C components), `graph_layout_test.cpp` (duplication, a mono and a stereo source summed, routing, the refusal, the downmix's modes, dual mono keeping each channel's state apart), `module_test.cpp` (the validation codes and the match), `voice_bus_rt_test.cpp` (no allocation with lanes).

### Per-note FX host (#127, steps 4–6)

What the engine does since branch `note-fx-host` (2026-11-11); the design is in [architecture](architecture.md#the-per-note-fx-host-notefxhost-fact-steps-47-branch-note-fx-host-2026-11-11).

- **Files:** `engine/src/note_fx_host.*` (`NoteFxRack`: noteFxSlots(B) = B + 2 slot chains and an overflow chain; `NoteFxHost`: the voice bus, slot choice, tails, fades, swaps, stats; `NoteFxManager`: the control side and the lock-free plan, held by the Engine as `noteFx_`). Hooks: `ComponentChain::buildNoteEffects`, `runsPerNote`, `setVoices`, `setEvents`, `quiet`; `Engine::runInstrument` (begin, the instrument's block, end), the chunk start (`noteFx_->beginChunk`), knob, bypass and display calls with `Engine::kNoteComponentFlag`, the layer's latency, `Engine::noteFxStats`; `parseProject` (`noteComponents`, `noteVoices`, lanes of kind `noteComponent`).
- **The overflow lane** (`kLaneRest` / `CAW_LANE_REST`, note id 0, last): `VoiceLanes::laneFor(noteId)` falls back to it, so every voice without a slot of its own goes through the overflow chain; the drums use it only for hits on their main output. It is never ENDED.
- **Slots:** chosen at each note-on's frame (lowest free; over the budget the oldest ended slot fades, else the oldest released note's slot fades while its sound crossfades into the overflow chain, and the new note takes a free chain; else overflow; held notes are never stolen). A slot's chain runs from its note's first frame; after ENDED it rings for max(its declared tail, 128 frames), at most 2 s, past its note's last nonzero sample, then fades out over 10 ms and frees (its chain is reset). All of this is in the host's frame count, so playback at any buffer size and an export give the same bits. An instrument that runs no lanes in a block (bypassed) frees every slot.
- **Per-note sources (step 5, `modulation_links.*`):** a link's `source` may be `note.pitch` (pitch with bend / 127), `note.velocity`, `note.pressure`, `note.timbre`, `note.gate` (1 while held) or `note.age` (0 at the note-on to 1 after 2 s), `output` 0, target type `noteComponent` only; each slot works them out per frame from its own note's events and adds any modulator link into the same knob (`ModChainKind::NoteFx`, once per chunk). At most 64 note-source links a layer; the amounts move live with `caw_engine_set_modulation_amount`.
- **Budget:** `noteFxBudget(B, quality)`: Eco min(B, 4), Normal B, High min(B, 16), read at each note-on (so a quality change applies to the next notes). Slots are allocated for B; nothing changes on overload.
- **C API 22** (20 and 21 are left for parallel branches): `caw_engine_note_fx_stats(e, layer, CawNoteFxStats*)` (size-tagged: voices, budget, active, active_mask, overflowing, overflowed, stolen; never waits) and `CAW_NOTE_COMPONENT` (0x40000000) on a component index for `caw_engine_set_component_param`, `_bypass` and `caw_engine_component_display` (the newest note's slot). Per-note components save no state (`caw_engine_component_states` doesn't list them).
- **Refusals:** per-note FX on a layer without a native instrument, on an instrument that doesn't declare PerNote or PerVoice ("has no voice bus"), or with an effect that can't run per note ("can't run per note: …") fail the project update before anything changes.
- **Tests:** `engine/tests/note_fx_test.cpp` (each slot of two notes with different bends and pressure through a pitch- and pressure-driven filter equals a solo render of that note bit for bit; the mix is their sum; overflow, steals and a released note's crossfade, deterministic; tails ring then free; the quality caps; refusals; the project JSON; the host at uneven block splits and the Engine live at 64-frame and uneven buffers and offline, bit-identical; knobs fanning out, stats; the C API), `engine/tests/note_fx_rt_test.cpp` (no allocation on the audio thread through notes, steals, knob moves, quality changes, rack swaps, voice changes, per-note FX off and on).
- **CPU** (wall time per second of audio at 48 kHz in 64-frame blocks, x86-64 GCC Release build on a 2.3 GHz Xeon, one run of a scratch bench, 2026-11-11; 8 or 16 held saw notes): the test signal alone 10.7 ms (8 notes) and 17.2 ms (16); per-note FX at 8 slots then 16: utility 14.4 / 24.0 ms, filter 18.1 / 34.1, saturator 47.7 / 83.1, delay.pro 33.9 / 64.3, reverb.pro 62.0 / 135.1. With the Flagship (15.8 ms and 29.2 ms alone; it runs the lanes every 16 frames): utility 21.3 / 38.8, filter 26.0 / 47.5, saturator 53.4 / 99.9, delay.pro 43.3 / 83.4, reverb.pro 78.4 / 175.3. So one slot costs about one instance of its chain (about 0.5 ms/s for the utility, 1 for the filter, 3 for the delay, 4.5 for the saturator, 6–9 for reverb.pro) plus a little for the short runs; free slots cost nothing. [Assumption] a phone core is 3–5 times slower, so 8 slots of a light effect are a few percent of a core and 16 reverbs per note are not affordable on Eco or Normal; measure on the S22+.

## Patches (built, not yet used)

`engine/src/patch.*` and `patch_nodes.*` (routing plan step 3, branch `patch-model`, 2026-11-18): a patch is a graph of nodes joined by wires, port to port, compiled onto one `GraphExecutor` (`graph.h`) that runs as one outer node. Nothing in the engine calls it yet: tracks still run as chains, and no project field or C API reaches it.

- **Nodes** (`PatchNodeSpec`): `Input` (the patch's stereo input, port `out`; at most one), `Output` (its stereo output, port `in`; exactly one, NaN to 0), `Chain` (a `ComponentChain` of effects, ports `in` and `out`: today's inserts as one node, with their values, bypass, sleep and latency) and `Module` (one component, made as a chain entry is: a merged device's variant, a rack's slots, a saved state; its descriptor's typed ports by id, and a built-in's optional ports, `EffectBase::optionalPorts`, by id). An optional port no wire names is not declared at all, so it costs nothing; a wired one is an extra stereo port of the node (`makeOptionalAdapter`, which connects it each block).
- **Wires** (`PatchWireSpec`): an output to an input of the same type, audio channels equal or mono into stereo, with a gain (audio and modulation only; a gain of 1 adds no node) and a feedback flag (audio only). A plain input takes one wire; summing ports, `Chain` and `Output` take any number, summed in wire order.
- **Feedback:** each feedback wire compiles to a send and a return sharing a 32-frame ring indexed by the patch's frame clock, so the compiled graph stays acyclic and the wire is exactly 16 frames late (`patch::kSubBlock`) at any host block size. The return has the mixer's feedback-send limiter (a 10 Hz DC block, then the soft clip: unity below 0.5, a tanh knee short of 1), on by default; with `limiter` off it only clamps to ±16. A loop without a feedback wire is refused, naming a node in it.
- **Time:** a patch with a feedback wire runs in 16-frame sub-blocks counted from Play (`markPlay`), whatever the host block; one without runs in 64-frame sub-blocks (`kWideSubBlock`, the Engine's block) on the same grid, since nothing in it waits on a sub-block. Everything the patch adds works per frame, so a render is the same bits at any host block size whenever its components are (the built-ins are). Host events are split per sub-block; `Chain` nodes get the transport per sub-block.
- **Latency compensation:** a node's input time is the latest of its compensated wires' sources (their time plus latency, a ducker's or pitch shifter's from its values); a delay node goes on each earlier wire. Audio and modulation wires are compensated; feedback wires, wires into a `sidechain` input, control and event wires are not. `latencyFrames()` is the Output's time.
- **Limits** (untrusted input, `patch::k*`): 64 nodes, 256 wires, 16 feedback wires, 8 ports per node (with its wired optional ports), 64 components per chain, ids and port names of 64 bytes, wire gains within ±16, 2^20 frames of compensation per wire and 64 MiB of compensation delay lines in all (`kMaxDelayLineBytes`, channels × frames × 4 bytes summed over the wires; chosen 2026-11-19: without it 256 wires of 2^20 stereo frames could ask for 2 GiB), 256 components in all its chain nodes (`kMaxPatchChainComponents`), 1024 events per call. A bad spec fails closed with an error naming the node, wire or port ("wire 3 (a.out -> b.in): ...").
- **Changes:** `compilePatch` runs on the control thread. Given the patch that plays, it keeps the instance (and running state, such as a delay's echoes) of every node whose id, kind and components (id and structure) are unchanged and only sets its values, ramping from where the playing patch has them to the spec's: a kept `Module` starts from its last values on the control thread (the spec's, then `setParameter`'s knob moves, which the patch keeps per node) and ramps to the spec's, a value the spec doesn't name to its default, as a fresh build or an export has it (a `Chain` node's `setValues` does the same); a knob still mid-ramp at the swap starts at its target. The new patch must then replace that one whole. `PatchRuntime` publishes and adopts it as `GraphRuntime` does (a pointer exchange at the start of `process`, the sub-block grid carried on) and retires the old one to the control thread; a crossfade is the caller's (with fresh instances). New feedback rings and delay lines start silent.
- **Tests** (`engine/tests/patch_test.cpp`, `patch_rt_test.cpp`): a linear patch of seven module nodes (EQ, Compressor, Drive, Chorus, Utility, Delay, Reverb), and the same chain as one `Chain` node, null against today's `ComponentChain` below −120 dBFS (`tolerance.h`, stable); a patch with every feature renders the same bits in host blocks of 1, 7, 16, 17, 256, 333, 1024 and 4096 frames; feedback echoes exactly every 16 frames, a runaway loop stays within its clamp or limiter; compensation delays a parallel path by the limiter's look-ahead exactly, feedback adds none; Utility's mid and side and Transient's transient and sustain outputs wired in a patch sum back to the effect's output (unwired: the same bits as the chain); a modulation wire carries its gain and compensation; a recompile keeps a delay's tail; a kept module node starts at its knob-moved value and ramps to the spec's (named or not) with no jump; the delay-line budget (two and nine parallel paths delayed by almost 2^20 frames each to meet a slow node) and the chain-components cap; refusals name the problem; a swap, Play and reset allocate nothing on the audio thread.
- **Cost** (`caw_patch_bench`, the seven effects above in 64-frame blocks, best of 7, x86-64 GCC Release on a 2.3 GHz Xeon, 2026-11-18): module nodes +2.6 to +4.3 % against the chain (each node's parameters are resolved every block by the executor, where the chain skips unchanged ones), one `Chain` node +1.2 to +2.2 %. In 16-frame sub-blocks (a patch with feedback) the same seven cost about +18 % (module nodes) and +8 % (one chain node).

## Drums (`org.caw.drums`)

One instrument for every drum sound (owner, 2026-11-11: "make all the drum sounds a drum can make and be extremely customisable"; [decisions](decisions.md#2026-11-11-one-drum-instrument-owner)). Code: `engine/src/builtin_drums.cpp` (the DSP), `builtin_drums_params.cpp` (the descriptor), `builtin_drums_sounds.cpp` (sounds, kits, presets, randomize, explode, migration), `builtin_drums_layout.h` (the parameter layout), `component_samples.*` (samples), `caw/library/modal.hpp` (the modal resonator, a Library kernel). Original code: the techniques below are described in our own words, no other instrument's code was used.

### Modes, pads and the note map

- **4 banks (A..D) of 16 pads**, 64 in all; pad index 0..63 (A1 = 0, B1 = 16, D16 = 63). Every pad has the same parameters. `mode`: **Kit** (each pad answers its note) or **Single** (pad A1 answers every note: one sound per track).
- **Notes:** a pad's `note` is Auto (the default: `base_note` + its index, so bank A plays 36..51, B 52..67, C 68..83, D 84..99), Off, Any other (plays when no other pad took the note), or a MIDI note 0..127. Several pads may share a note (they layer). Default `base_note` 36 (C2, C4 = 60): **[Fact]** bank A then lands on the General MIDI percussion keys 36..51 (GM 1 Sound Set: 36 bass drum, 37 side stick, 38 snare, 39 clap, 40 electric snare, 41/43 floor toms, 42 closed hat, 44 pedal hat, 45/47/48/50 toms, 46 open hat, 49 crash, 51 ride), which is why the 808 kit's sounds sit where they do. **[Assumption, community reports read 2026-11-11 (mpc-forums.com): later MPCs default to a chromatic map from C1; the Akai MPD218 guide says it has 3 banks of 16 pads but its note map is in a separate document not checked]** 16 chromatic notes per bank from a configurable start is the common pad-controller layout, and Ableton's Drum Rack uses the same 36-upward chromatic layout; banks of 16 consecutive notes follow it.
- **Channels:** `channel` (Any, 1..16) filters the notes the instrument takes; a pad's `chan` is Kit (follows `channel`), Any, or 1..16 (e.g. 10, the GM drum channel). **[Assumption]** the app passes a hardware controller's channel in the note events (`NoteMessage::channel`); today's previews send channel 1.
- **Pitch tracking:** `track` Off plays the pad at its fixed pitch; On plays it `note − root` semitones from its tuning (`root`, default 60). In Single mode every note is tracked; in Kit mode a tracking pad on its own channel (`chan` 1..16) takes every note on that channel (an 808 bass on channel 2 beside the kit), and on Kit/Any only its own note. `mono` On: one voice; with tracking, a note played while another is held glides there (`glide`) without a new attack (an 808 slide), and releasing it glides back to the newest held note.
- **Triggering:** `trig` One-shot (note-offs ignored) or Gate (a note-off fades the voice over `release`). `poly` 1..4 voices per pad (a new hit past it fades the oldest over 5 ms; `mono` is 1); a pool of 64 voices serves all pads (when it is full the oldest fading voice, else the oldest, is taken). `choke` 1..8: a hit fades every other pad's voices in its group over 5 ms (to −80 dB), so an open hat is cut without a click.
- **Hardware pads:** a pad's `note` and `chan` are its note map; **note learn** is the app's: it watches the display's last-note fields (key, channel, velocity and a count that changes with every note, [display data](#display-data-of-the-drums)), then writes the key into the pad's `note` (`kNoteFirst` + key). **Pressure** (polyphonic aftertouch, per note) is a modulation source with one amount per destination (the `press` layer): `press_damp` 0..1 (the voice fades over 30 ms / (pressure × amount): squeezing a cymbal), `press_choke` (past half pressure the voice fades out in 5 ms), `press_tone` ±1 (±4 octaves on every filter and the resonator's brightness), `press_pitch` ±12 st, `press_level` ±1 (±12 dB), `press_drive` 0..1 (the drive × 2^(3 × pressure × amount), when the drive is on). It is read once per control period (16 frames).

### Parameters

30 kit-wide parameters, then 213 per pad × 64 = **13662** (1.1.0 appended 51 per pad and 6 kit-wide ones after the old ones, so a saved value keeps its meaning and an old kit renders bit-identically: `drums_presets_from_before_1_1_render_bit_identically` hashes every pre-1.1.0 preset against the renders of 20bcb8b; bits are per build, so each has 20bcb8b's own table: x86-64 Release, Arm64, Windows x64 and unoptimised x86-64 (Debug, the sanitizer job), `engine/tests/drums_compat_hashes*.inc`). Ids: kit-wide ones bare (`mode`, `glue`); a pad's `<pad>_<name>` with the pad in lower case (`a1_body_freq`, `d16_rnd_pos`). Every parameter has a `group` (`ParameterDescriptor::group`, `"group"` in `caw_engine_components`): `kit/main`, `kit/glue`, `kit/drive`, `kit/room`, `kit/eq`, `kit/clip`, `kit/echo`, or `<pad>/<layer>` with the layer one of `pad`, `press`, `body`, `penv`, `sub`, `click`, `noise`, `metal`, `res`, `smp`, `drive`, `filter`, `eq`, `dyn`, `space`, `macro`, `var`, `fm`, `phys`, `tex`, `fx`, `groove` (in that order; `caw_drums_info` lists each layer's suffixes and display name). Units: Hz, s, ms, st, ct, oct, dB as the descriptor says; choices carry their labels. Bank A's defaults are the 808 kit's sounds (a fresh instance plays); banks B..D default to empty pads (every layer off). A layer that is off costs nothing; a pad with every layer off (or `level` −60) is unused and costs nothing.

| Layer | Parameters (suffix: range, default of an empty pad) |
|---|---|
| pad | `note` (Auto, Off, Any other, C-1 (0)..G9 (127)), `chan` (Kit, Any, 1..16), `type` (Kick, Snare, Clap, Closed hat, Open hat, Pedal hat, Tom, Crash, Ride, Cymbal, Rim, Cowbell, Shaker, Perc, 808 bass, FX; Perc: what randomize draws), `level` −60..12 dB (0; −60 is off), `pan` −1..1, `tune` ±48 st, `track` Off/On, `root` 0..127 (60), `mono` Off/On, `glide` 0.001..2 s (0.08), `trig` One-shot/Gate, `release` 0.001..5 s (0.1), `poly` 1..4 (2), `choke` Off/1..8, `out` Main/Out 2..9 |
| press | `press_damp` 0..1, `press_choke` Off/On, `press_tone` ±1, `press_pitch` ±12 st, `press_level` ±1, `press_drive` 0..1 (all 0: see Hardware pads above) |
| body | `body_on`, `body_wave` (Sine, Triangle, Saw, Square), `body_shape` 0..1 (tanh drive on the wave), `body_fold` 0..1, `body_freq` 20..2000 Hz (55: the pitch it settles at), `body_pitch` 0..96 st (24: how far above it starts), `body_ptime` 0.001..2 s (0.05: the pitch envelope's 60 dB time, Curve mode), `body_pcurve` −1..1, `body_attack` 0.0001..0.5 s, `body_hold` 0..2 s, `body_decay` 0.01..10 s (0.4), `body_dcurve` −1..1, `body_p2_ratio` 0.5..8 (1.5) and `body_p2_level` 0..1 (a second sine partial), `body_phase` 0..1 (start phase; 0.25 starts on a peak: a harder click), `body_level` dB |
| penv | `penv_mode` (Curve: `body_ptime`/`body_pcurve`; Points: the shape editor), then four points, each `penv_tN` 0.0005..2 s (the segment's length: 0.004, 0.02, 0.08, 0.2), `penv_pN` −24..96 st over `body_freq` (12, 3, 0, 0) and `penv_cN` −1..1 (the segment's curve: 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0) |
| sub | `sub_on`, `sub_wave` (Sine, Triangle, Warm: a saturated sine), `sub_oct` (0, −1, −2 oct; −1) and `sub_fine` ±12 st under `body_freq` × tune, `sub_follow` 0..1 (how far it rides the body's pitch shape), `sub_attack` 0.0001..0.5 s (0.005), `sub_hold` 0..2 s, `sub_decay` 0.01..10 s (0.6), `sub_dcurve` ±1, `sub_drive` 0..1, `sub_lp` 40..2000 Hz (2000: off), `sub_route` (Clean: past the pad's effects, mono; Through effects), `sub_level` dB (−6) |
| click | `click_on`, `click_type` (Noise: high-passed at the tone; Impulse: a low-passed pulse; Blip: a sine at the tone; Tick: a square; Chirp: a sine falling from 4 × the tone; Pop: band-passed noise), `click_level` 0..1 (0.5), `click_tone` 100..16000 Hz (2000), `click_length` 0.5..100 ms (8) |
| noise | `noise_on`, `noise_mode` (Normal; Snappy: the wires, its level following the body's motion (with the body off, a 190 Hz shell of their own falling over 0.35 s: before 1.1.0 Snappy did nothing without the body); Clap: `noise_bursts` 1..8 (3) bursts `noise_spacing` 2..50 ms (10) apart, each falling 60 dB in three spacings, then the envelope as a tail), `noise_color` (White, Pink: about −3 dB an octave, Brown, Crushed: sample-and-hold at the noise frequency), `noise_filter` (Off, Low-pass, Band-pass, High-pass; 12 dB SVF), `noise_freq` 20..20000 Hz (2000), `noise_res` 0..1, `noise_sweep` ±6 oct (the frequency follows the envelope), `noise_attack`, `noise_decay` 0.005..10 s (0.2), `noise_dcurve`, `noise_level` dB |
| metal | `metal_on`, `metal_mode` (Squares: six band-limited squares; FM pairs: three sine carriers each phase-modulated by the next ratio; Ring: three ring-modulated square pairs), `metal_set` (808, Bright, Cymbal, Cowbell, Gong, Bell, Harmonic: six ratios and loudnesses), `metal_freq` 50..2000 Hz (410.6: the 808 bank's lowest, 205.3 Hz, doubled as the old hats had it), `metal_spread` ±1 (ratio^(1 + spread / 2)), `metal_fm` 0..10 (index), `metal_tone` 200..16000 Hz (7000, the high-pass), `metal_color` 200..16000 Hz (8000) and `metal_band` 0..1 (a band-pass blended in, unity at its centre), `metal_res` 0..1 (0.16: q 1.2, both filters), `metal_attack`, `metal_decay` 0.005..10 s (0.05), `metal_dcurve`, `metal_open` 0..1 (decay × (1 + 15 open²): a hat opening), `metal_noise` 0..1 (0.5: white noise mixed in), `metal_level` dB |
| res | `res_on`, `res_model` (Membrane, Plate, Bar, Tube, Bell, Cymbal), `res_freq` 20..8000 Hz (150), `res_decay` 0.01..20 s (0.6), `res_damp` 0..1 (higher modes die sooner), `res_bright` 0..1, `res_inharm` ±1, `res_pos` 0..1 (strike position, centre to edge), `res_exciter` (Layers: only the other layers drive it, a shell or body stage; Mallet: a raised-cosine strike, longer when darker or softer; Noise: an 8 ms burst), `res_input` 0..1 (0.2: how much of the other layers drives it), `res_level` dB |
| smp | `smp_on`, `smp_start` 0..1, `smp_end` 0..1 (1), `smp_tune` ±48 st, `smp_reverse`, `smp_attack` 0.0001..1 s, `smp_decay` 0.01..30 s (30: none), `smp_level` dB. The pad plays its sample slot (pad n: slot n), with its pitch, variation and effects. |
| drive | `drive_type` (Off, Soft: tanh; Hard: clip; Fold: a sine fold; Crush: 16 down to 3 bits as the drive rises; Tube: a biased tanh, even harmonics; Rectify: half the wave folded up, an octave of grit; Decimate: every n-th sample held, n = drive; Asymmetric: the positive half driven twice as hard), `drive` 1..20 (2), `drive_pos` (Pre filter, Post filter), `drive_lo` 20..2000 Hz (20: off; a one-pole high-pass before the curve, so the sub stays clean), `drive_hi` 500..20000 Hz (20000: off; a 12 dB low-pass after it, taming fizz), `drive_mix` 0..1 (1; parallel distortion below 1) |
| filter | `flt` (Off, LP 6 dB, LP 12 dB, High-pass, Band-pass), `cutoff` 20..20000 Hz (12000), `flt_res`, `flt_env` ±6 oct and `flt_decay` 0.005..5 s (an exponential filter envelope from each hit) |
| eq | `eq_low` ±18 dB with `eq_low_freq` 30..600 Hz (120; a shelf), `eq_mid` ±18 dB with `eq_freq` 80..12000 Hz (1000) and `eq_q` 0.3..8 (1; a bell), `eq_high` ±18 dB with `eq_high_freq` 1000..16000 Hz (6000; a shelf) |
| dyn | `tr_attack` and `tr_sustain` ±1 (a transient shaper: fast against slow followers, up to ±9 dB), `comp` 0..1 (one knob: threshold −6 − 24 × comp dB, ratio 1 + 9 × comp, makeup 0.35 of the static reduction at 0 dB) with `comp_attack` 0.1..100 ms (2) and `comp_release` 10..1000 ms (60), `clip` (Off, Soft: tanh; Hard) with `clip_gain` 0..24 dB into it; the clipper is last, oversampled like the drive, and never passes full scale |
| space | `width` 0..1 (0), `width_hz` 40..1000 Hz (200: below it the voice stays mono), `room_send` 0..1 (1: how much of the voice the kit's room hears) |
| macro | `punch`, `tone`, `snap`, `length`, `dirt`, `pitch`, each −1..1 (0) |
| var | `vel_level` 0..1 (1), `vel_pitch` 0..24 st, `vel_decay` ±1, `vel_tone` ±1 (0.3), `vel_click` 0..1 (0.5), `vel_punch` 0..1 (a softer hit's pitch drop is shorter: the depth × (1 + vel_punch × (v − 1))), `rnd_pitch` 0..100 ct, `rnd_decay`, `rnd_tone`, `rnd_pan`, `rnd_level`, `rnd_pos` 0..1 (each hit's strike position moves up to ±0.3 × amount) |
| fm (1.1.0) | `fm_on`, `fm_freq` 20..4000 Hz (200: the carrier), `fm_ratio` 0.25..16 (1.41: the modulator's), `fm_index` 0..20 (3: depth, rad), `fm_idecay` 0.002..4 s (0.08: the depth falls 60 dB in it), `fm_feedback` 0..1 (the modulator on itself), `fm_pitch` 0..48 st and `fm_ptime` 0.001..1 s (0.03: a drop as the body's), `fm_attack`, `fm_decay` 0.005..10 s (0.3), `fm_dcurve`, `fm_level` dB |
| phys (1.1.0) | `phys_on`, `phys_model` (String; Pipe: half a period inverted, odd harmonics; Stiff string: an all-pass in the loop, stretched partials), `phys_freq` 30..2000 Hz (110), `phys_decay` 0.02..20 s (1: the loop's 60 dB time), `phys_damp` 0..1 (0.4: the loop's high loss), `phys_pick` 0..1 (0.2: where it is plucked, a comb on the output), `phys_exciter` (Pluck: a falling noise burst one period long; Noise: 10 ms; Layers: the other layers × `phys_input` 0..1), `phys_level` dB |
| tex (1.1.0) | `tex_on`, `tex_mode` (Bands: white noise through two resonant band-passes; Dust: sparse impulses; Crackle: sparse 2 ms grains of random loudness; both through the lower band), `tex_freq` 40..16000 Hz (3000), `tex_spread` 0..4 oct (1.5: the second band above) and `tex_tilt` ±1 (the level between them; Bands), `tex_res` 0..1, `tex_density` 10..20000 a second (1500; Dust, Crackle), `tex_attack`, `tex_decay` 0.005..10 s (0.25), `tex_dcurve`, `tex_level` dB |
| smp, 1.1.0 | `smp_pitch` 0..48 st with `smp_ptime` 0.001..1 s (a pitch drop from the hit), `smp_hold` 0..2 s and `smp_dcurve` (the envelope as the other layers'), `smp_vel_start` 0..1 (a softer hit starts up to 50 ms in), `smp_rnd_start` 0..1 (each hit up to 30 ms in, seeded: a round robin from one sample) |
| fx (1.1.0) | `ring_mix` 0..1 (0: off) and `ring_freq` 20..8000 Hz (a sine ring modulator after the filter and drive, before the EQ), `echo_send` 0..1 (to the kit's echo), `bus_send` 0..1 (1: how much passes the kit bus, glue and kit drive; the rest joins after them, before the kit EQ, room return and clipper) |
| groove (1.1.0) | `swing` 0..1 with `swing_grid` (1/16, 1/8): the off 16ths (8ths) up to half a 16th (an 8th) late, 75 % swing; `humanize` 0..1 (up to 12 ms late and ±15 % velocity per hit); `accent` 0..1 with `accent_on` (Beats, Bar starts, Off-beats, Loud hits ≥ 0.85): up to +6 dB, 0.7 octave brighter, more click; `flam` 0..1 (a grace hit at 0.25..0.75 of the velocity) with `flam_time` 5..60 ms (the hit after it); `groove` (Off, Shuffle, Laid back, Gallop, Broken, Half-time) with `groove_amt` 0..1 (1) |

Kit-wide: `mode` (Kit, Single), `channel` (Any, 1..16), `base_note` 0..64 (36), `volume` −60..12 dB, `seed` 0..65535 (1: the variation's), `view_pad` (Last hit, A1..D16: which pad the display shows; not automatable), `glue` 0..1 (the bus compressor's amount; 0 off) with `glue_threshold` −40..0 dB (−12), `glue_ratio` 1..20 (4), `glue_attack` 0.1..100 ms (10), `glue_release` 10..1000 ms (120), `glue_makeup` 0..24 dB; `kit_drive_type` (Off, Soft, Tape, Hard, Crush), `kit_drive` 1..20, `kit_drive_mix`; `room` 0..1 (the room's return level; 0 off), `room_size`, `room_decay` 0.1..10 s, `room_damping`, `room_predelay` 0..100 ms; `kit_low` and `kit_high` ±12 dB (shelves at 100 Hz and 8 kHz); `kit_clip` (Off, Soft, Hard) with `kit_clip_gain` 0..24 dB; 1.1.0: `echo` 0..1 (the echo's return; 0 off) with `echo_time` 0.01..2 s (Free), `echo_sync` (Free, 1/16, 1/8, 1/8 dotted, 1/4, 1/8 triplet at the transport's tempo, 120 BPM without one), `echo_feedback` 0..0.95 (0.35), `echo_tone` 500..16000 Hz (a one-pole low-pass on every repeat), `echo_spread` 0..1 (0.6: the repeats cross sides, ping-pong).

**When a setting is heard (`needs`, 1.1.0):** `caw_drums_info` gives, for every setting that is only heard in some context, the conditions (`{suffix: [{"param", "kit", "in"}]}`, all of them must hold; `in` absent: non-zero): a layer's settings need it on, `body_ptime` the Curve pitch shape, `noise_bursts` the Clap mode, `metal_fm` FM pairs, `flt_res` a 12 dB filter, `glide` Mono and pitch tracking, `release` Gate, `room_send` the kit's room and so on (`drums::needs()`). The app dims a setting whose conditions fail and says what it needs. `drums_audit_test.cpp` checks every rule both ways: with a condition failing, moving the setting changes nothing at all; and (the settings audit) every pad and kit setting, moved to its ends and middle and through every choice, in every layer's modes and at every quality, changes the render.

**Why flat ids (to confirm with the owner, [decisions](decisions.md#2026-11-11-one-drum-instrument-built-choices-to-confirm)):** the component model has no parameter arrays or "selected pad", and every C.A.W. feature that moves a knob (automation lanes, modulation, MIDI learn, presets, undo, the app's knob widgets) addresses a parameter by id. Flat ids keep every pad's every knob automatable with no new machinery. The cost: a 2.9 MB descriptor in `caw_engine_components` (2.6 MB without spaces), and about 6 MB of parameter state per instance. The engine was made to handle it: duplicate ids are checked by sorting above 256 parameters (`module.cpp`), a component chain looks ids up in a map above 64 (`component_chain.cpp`), and the processor reads a pad's knobs only while it sounds, once a block when they hold still.

### How a voice sounds

A hit on a pad reads its knobs at the hit's frame, applies the macros and the hit's variation, and starts a voice. Layers sum: body + click + noise + metal + sample (+ the sub when it runs Through effects), then the resonator (struck by its exciter, driven by `res_input` × that sum) is added. Then drive (with its low cut, high cut and mix; pre or post the filter), the filter, the EQ, the transient shaper, the compressor, the clipper (a Clean sub joins its input), the fades (choke, gate release, pressure damp), then level and pan (a balance: the centre at full level on both sides, the far side falling along a quarter cosine). A Clean sub is added to both sides whole, after the pan. Width adds a side signal (the voice 0.4 ms later through a 4th-order high-pass at `width_hz`, × width) to the left and takes it from the right, so the sum to mono is unchanged and the low end stays mono. The room hears each voice × `room_send` (not its Clean sub). Voices go to their output's bus; the main bus runs the kit effects (glue, drive, EQ, the room's return at `room`, the clipper, in that order), then the volume.

- **Body pitch, Curve mode:** f(t) = f_end + (f_start − f_end) × e(t), f_end = `body_freq` × 2^(tune / 12), f_start = f_end × 2^(`body_pitch` / 12), with e(t) the pitch envelope (60 dB in `body_ptime`). This is the analog swept sine of the 808 and 909 kicks (the pitch moving in Hz along an exponential), so `body_pcurve` 0 matches them; + bends toward a fast drop with a tail, − toward a straight line.
- **Body pitch, Points mode (the shape editor):** the pitch in semitones over f_end runs from `body_pitch` at the hit through four points: segment N lasts `penv_tN` and ends at `penv_pN`; after the last, it holds `penv_p4` (so p4 0 settles on `body_freq`; a non-zero p4 is a held offset). A segment's curve c: 0 straight; c > 0 fast then slow, 1 − (1 − u)^k; c < 0 slow then fast, u^k; k = 1 + 7|c|, u the segment's progress. Semitones move linearly in pitch, so a straight segment is an even glide in octaves. Punch scales the times (× 2^(−0.7 punch)), as it does `body_ptime`.
- **Envelope curves** (`*_dcurve`, `body_pcurve`): 0 is the analog exponential (60 dB in the time); +c blends in an exponential 6 times as fast (a sharp drop with a long tail); −c blends in a straight line (a held, gated sound). Envelopes end below about −94 dB.
- **Sub:** a sine (or triangle, or warm sine) at f_end × 2^(−oct + fine / 12), with its own attack, hold, decay and curve, a tanh drive and a one-pole low-pass; `sub_follow` f multiplies its frequency by (body pitch / f_end)^f, so 1 rides the body's drop and 0 holds still. It sounds with the body off too (it takes `body_freq` and the pad's tuning).
- **Character macros** (fixed curves; each is a real, automatable parameter and moves the voice's working values, never the knobs themselves): **Punch** m: `body_pitch` + 12m st, `body_ptime` and the shape's times × 2^(−0.7m), click level × 2^(1.5m), transient attack + 0.5m. **Tone** m: `cutoff` × 2^(2m), noise frequency and click tone × 2^(1.5m), metal filters × 2^m, resonator brightness + 0.35m, `eq_high` + 6m dB. **Snap** m: click level × 2^m, noise level × 2^(0.8m), transient attack + 0.4m. **Length** m: every decay × 2^(2m) (the holds and the filter decay × 2^m). **Dirt** m > 0: drive × 2^(2m) (a pad with drive off gets Soft at 1 + 6m), `comp` + 0.3m; m < 0 pulls the drive toward 1. **Pitch** m: + 12m st on every pitched layer.
- **Groove** (1.1.0; a pad's `groove` layer): a hit's place is read from the transport (song beats + its frame, in 16ths; within 0.15 of a 16th it is on the grid, else nothing moves it). Swing and the groove's step table (16 steps: how late, in 16ths, and the velocity's scale; e.g. Shuffle: every off 16th a third late at 0.78) delay and weight it; humanize adds seeded lateness and velocity (the variation seed, the pad and its hit count); a flam plays a grace hit first and the hit `flam_time` later. Nothing plays early: a groove only delays. Held-back hits wait in a queue of 128 (full: a hit plays at once) and start on their exact frame, so a render is the same at any block size; a note-off before its hit ends it as it starts. Without a playing transport (a preview) only humanize, loud-hit accents and flams act. A pad without groove settings plays as before, bit for bit.
- **Variation** (from a hit at velocity v; x = v − 1): level × (1 + `vel_level` x), pitch + `vel_pitch` x st, the pitch drop's depth × (1 + `vel_punch` x), every decay × 2^(`vel_decay` x), the filters, the resonator's brightness and the click's tone (+ half as much) + 2 `vel_tone` x octaves, the mallet's strike × 2^(−toneOct / 2) longer (so a soft hit excites fewer high modes: velocity changes the spectrum, not just the level), click level × (1 + `vel_click` x). Per hit, deterministic: u = a hash of (`seed`, the pad, its hit count, which value) in −1..1; pitch + `rnd_pitch` u cents, decays × 2^(0.5 `rnd_decay` u), tone + `rnd_tone` u octaves, pan + `rnd_pan` u, level + 6 `rnd_level` u dB, strike position + 0.3 `rnd_pos` u. The hit counts restart at a reset and when the transport starts playing, so a render repeats.

### Quality

Eco: no oversampling (drives, clippers, kit drive), the metal's squares naive (no band-limiting), 8 resonator modes, the room light (`lib::Reverb::setLight`), display 30 times a second. Normal: drives and clippers 2× oversampled (`lib::Oversampler2x`), band-limited squares (polyBLEP), 16 modes. High: drives and clippers 4× (two half-band stages), the metal bank run at 2× and decimated, 32 modes. Crush and Decimate are never oversampled (their aliasing is the sound).

### Samples

- **Where they come from:** a component entry's `"samples"` ([project file](#project-file-json)): `{"slot": n, "path": file}` for the user's WAV files, or `{"slot": n, "builtin": name}` for the **built-in pack**: every sound of the library (the `sounds` names in `caw_drums_info`, e.g. "808 Kick", "Crash") rendered by the engine itself (a full-velocity hit on pad A1, until silent, at most 6 s, mono at the engine's rate), so there is nothing to license. Slot n is pad n (A1 = 0).
- **Loading:** on the control thread when the project's chain is built or updated (`applyComponentSamples`); a slot whose source is unchanged is kept. The new buffer is handed to the audio thread through a one-slot lock-free exchange (`SampleHandover`): the audio thread takes it at its next block, a voice still playing the old one stops its sample layer, and the old buffer is freed later on the control thread. The audio thread never allocates or frees.
- **Untrusted files** (`decodeWavBytes`): RIFF/WAVE only; PCM 8/16/24/32-bit and IEEE float 32/64, 1..8 channels (mixed to mono), 8..384 kHz, the extensible format's sub-format; every chunk size is checked against what is left (a lying header is refused, a truncated data chunk yields what is there), non-finite floats read as 0 and values are held to ±4. Limits: a file over 256 MB is refused unread; 30 s per slot (the start is kept); 64 MB of samples per instance. Resampling to the engine's rate is cubic (Catmull-Rom) after a two-pole low-pass when it goes down. **[Assumption]** good enough for one-shots; a windowed-sinc resampler would be cleaner. A file that fails empties its slot and the project still loads.
- **Not yet:** samples are mono (a stereo file is mixed down); no loop points.

### Outputs and exploding to tracks

- `out` (Main, Out 2..9) is stored per pad and the processor keeps nine buses, but **the instrument chain has one stereo output** (the settings audit hears Out 2..9 only as skipping the kit effects; the app says so beside the choice) (`ComponentChain::isInstrument`: one event input, one stereo audio output), so today Out 2..9 are summed into the one output after the kit effects (a pad sent there skips glue, drive and room). Multi-output instruments routed into child tracks need the chain, the mixer graph and the project's routing to carry a component's extra outputs: **a follow-up**, not built.
- **Explode (chosen for the "one track per pad" workflow):** `caw_drums_explode(entry)` returns one track per used pad, each a Single-mode Drums carrying that pad's sound on A1 (and its sample in slot 0), the notes it answers (`[key]`, `"other"`, `"all"` or `{"channel": n}`) and its choke group; the app creates the tracks and moves those notes into them. Choke groups across tracks and the kit effects (returned as `"kit"`) don't follow; the app can put the kit's effects on a bus.

### Randomize and mutate

`caw_drums_randomize(type, seed, amount, pad, current)`: pure and deterministic. Each drum type has a rule: the layers that must be on and, for its defining knobs, the range a random sound draws from (a kick: `body_freq` 38..80 Hz, `body_pitch` 4..40 st, `body_ptime` 10..120 ms, `body_decay` 0.15..1.8 s, the click's level, tone and length, the wave and click type, the sub (on a third of the time; its octave, decay and level) and `vel_punch`; a snare: body 150..280 Hz, noise 0.8..5 kHz and 0.08..0.4 s; hats: metal 300..700 Hz, high-pass 5..11 kHz, decay 20..80 ms (open: 35..80 ms with openness 0.4..0.8); toms 60..300 Hz; cymbals; rim; cowbell; shaker; perc; 808 bass; FX), plus every type's effects and variation within mild ranges (the drive now may draw Asymmetric, and a quarter of draws turn the Soft clipper on). The pitch shape's mode is never drawn, so a random kick settles where its `body_freq` says. With no current sound (or another type), it starts from a seeded pick among the library's sounds of that type and moves fully (amount 1); with one, it mutates: each knob moves `amount` of the way toward its random target in the knob's own taper, each listed choice switches with probability amount / 3, and every drawn knob is clamped into the type's range. The result is level-matched: the engine renders 0.4 s of it and sets `level` so a hit peaks near −1 dBFS. The pad's note, channel, level, pan, choke group and output stay where they were.

### Presets (the sound library)

Listed with the component (`"presets"`): 22 kits filling bank A (Kit: 808, 909, Acoustic, Trap, Techno, Lo-fi; 1.1.0: Boom bap, House, Drum and bass, Dub, Afro percussion, Industrial, FM, Jazz brushes, Lo-fi tape, Electro, Reggaeton, Cinematic, Hand percussion, Garage, Synthwave, Thumb piano, with their grooves (swung hats, a galloping shaker, humanized hands), echo sends and the new layers in place; they set `mode` Kit, and their glue, drive, room and echo), then 70 sounds (1.1.0 added FM Kick, FM Tom, FM Clank, FM Bell, Log Drum, Plucked Tom, Kalimba, Pipe Knock, Brush Snare, Brush Swirl, Rain Shaker, Vinyl Crackle, Gravel Step, Industrial Hit, Dub Rim and Flam Snare, each levelled to about −1 dBFS, shakers −6, the crackle −8), each a preset that plays it on A1 in Single mode (leaving the kit's other pads as they are): kicks (808 Kick, 808 Kick Long, 808 Kick Short, 909 Kick, Dubstep Kick, House Kick, Acoustic Kick, Punchy Kick, Techno Kick, Hardstyle Kick, Distorted Kick, Sub Kick, Trap Kick, 808 Bass), snares (808 Snare, 909 Snare, Acoustic Snare, Tight Snare, Fat Snare, Rim Snare, Clap Snare, Trap Snare, Dubstep Snare), hats (808 and 909 Closed and Open Hat, Acoustic Hat, Trashy Hat, Pedal Hat), toms (Low, Mid, High, 808, Acoustic Tom), cymbals (Crash, Ride, Ride Bell, China, Splash), Rim, Clap, Snap, Cowbell, Shaker, Tambourine, Conga, Bongo, Woodblock, Clave, Triangle, Zap, Laser, Noise Riser. `caw_drums_info` gives the same sounds as pad values (to load one onto any pad, prefix them) and each kit's pads.
