When the engine is built with `CAW_SANITIZE` or `CAW_TSAN` (`CAW_SCAN_ALLOW_SANITIZER`) the sanitizer runtimes (`libasan`, `libubsan`, `libtsan`, `liblsan`, `libclang_rt.*`) count as system libraries, so the sanitizer CI jobs can scan their instrumented test components; a shipped build never sets it.
  - System calls in what the loader maps executable (each executable `PT_LOAD` rounded out to 64 KiB pages, so bytes beside the segment count; PE executable sections): AArch64 `svc` with any immediate (`(word & 0xFFE0001F) == 0xD4000001` at a 4-byte boundary, the A64 encoding from memory, **[Assumption]** not re-read from Arm's manual; the mask is safe either way): certain. x86-64 `syscall` (`0F 05`) and `int 0x80` (`CD 80`) right after a `mov` of a call number into eax/rax: certain; the bytes alone are a hint notice (a large library holds a few by chance: libcaw 33, caw_tests 36, libstdc++ 3, none after such a mov; glibc 492 after one; measured 2026-11-18).
  - Writable and executable sections or segments (certain), text relocations (hint), packer section names (UPX, ASPack, Themida, VMProtect, Enigma, MPRESS, …: certain) and the `UPX!` marker (hint); executable code above 7.2 bits per byte over at least 4 KiB (a hint warning) and data sections of at least 64 KiB above 7.9 (a hint notice).
  - Strings (ASCII and UTF-16LE, the first 64 MiB): URLs and IPv4 addresses (notices), `.onion` names, shells (`/system/bin/sh`, `/bin/sh`, `cmd.exe`, `powershell`) and wallet or browser-profile paths (warnings); all hints.
  - Load-time code: more than 16 ELF constructors, any PE TLS callback (MSVC's `thread_local` and MinGW use them): informational notices.
  - At most 256 findings, 24 per capability and kind, the first of each always kept, so many findings of one kind can't hide another.
- **Declarations:** a C.A.W component's capabilities are built into it ([native module SDK](native-module-sdk.md#declaring-capabilities)): the canonical JSON in an allocated section, `caw_caps` (ELF) or `.cawcap` (PE). The scan reads it (at most 16 KiB and 16 entries) and requires exactly the canonical form (`declarations.h`): bad JSON, an unknown or repeated id, a bad reason or site, two sections, or two declarations in one section make it **invalid**. No section is **absent** (a build from before 1.0.7, or one not built by C.A.W's tools). With an expected id, a `caw-component.json` beside the file whose `component.id` is that id must declare the same list (an absent section passes only if the manifest declares nothing); a difference, or invalid capabilities there, is **tampering**. A manifest for another id, or one that is not JSON, is ignored.
- **Policy** (`policy.h`, a pure function of the report): for a C.A.W component, a certain finding of a capability it does not declare blocks; so do malformed, unsupported, a dependency that is not a system library, invalid or tampered. A declared capability's findings become notices; hints keep their own weight and never block; load-time findings are notices. Verdicts: `clean`, `notice`, `warning`, `blocked`. For a CLAP/VST3 plug-in (not hosted yet; `CAW_SCAN_PLUGIN`, `caw scan --plugin`) every finding keeps its own weight and nothing blocks. **Legacy grace** (`CAW_LOAD_LEGACY_GRACE`, owner 2026-11-18: warn once, then block): for a file with no declaration section, undeclared capabilities warn instead of blocking; never for a malformed, unsupported, invalid or tampered file, a non-system dependency, or one that carries a section. It is tied to the exact file: `caw_engine_load_component_ex` takes the SHA-256 the app recorded when the user trusted it (errorCode 3 without it, 11 when the file's own hash differs), so a rebuild or another file gets none. A blocked load's message names each capability, its plain title and the first evidence.
- **Cache and the file:** the loader opens the file once, reads it (regular files only, never past 256 MiB + 1 byte, so a device or pipe can't hang it), hashes it (SHA-256, `sha256.h`) and loads that same open file: Linux `dlopen("/proc/self/fd/N")` with the descriptor kept open while the library is loaded (so the name is never reused meanwhile), Android `android_dlopen_ext` with `ANDROID_DLEXT_USE_LIBRARY_FD` (`android/dlext.h`, API 21; the app's minimum is 26; **[Assumption]** bionic's namespace allows an app-data file loaded this way, to check on the phone), Windows a handle that lets no one else write, rename or delete the file while `LoadLibraryExW` maps it. So the file can't be swapped between the scan and the load. Right after the load, on the control thread, the library is **warmed** (`engine/src/library_warm.h`, `engine/platform/library_warm_*.cpp`; owner, 2026-11-19): one byte read per page of its executable and read-only segments (from `dl_iterate_phdr` on Linux and Android: readable `PT_LOAD` segments that are executable or not writable, and `PT_GNU_RELRO`, the relocated `.data.rel.ro` and GOT; the PE sections on Windows), so the audio thread's first call into it does not wait on page faults. No `mlock`, and not `MADV_WILLNEED` alone (a hint). Over about 8 MiB in all, only the executable ones, up to 8 MiB. An execute-only segment is never read, and warming never fails a load; macOS touches nothing. The report is cached by hash in memory (256 entries, cleared when full), so a changed file is scanned again and the same bytes never are. A file past 256 MiB is malformed and not hashed. The manifest check and the policy run every time. `caw scan` and `caw_engine_scan_component` read the path themselves (regular files only).
- **What it is not:** a static look at imports and bytes stops lazy and obvious malware, nothing more. A component can reach the system without any import the scan reads: walk the process's loaded libraries in memory (the dynamic linker's list on Linux and Android, the PEB on Windows) and call `connect` or `execve` through pointers it finds there, decrypt or build its code at run time without an executable-memory import (`mmap` and `mprotect` are only hints), or behave only on some dates or devices. The hub's review of what it lists, its known-bad and known-good hashes and the user's own trust choice are the backstop; Trusted components still run in-process, unsandboxed.
- **JSON** (`caw_engine_scan_component`, `caw scan --json`; `scan_file.h`): `{"ok", "sha256", "size", "format": "elf64"|"pe32+"|"unknown", "arch", "host": "component"|"plugin", "verdict", "reason", "grace_applied", "cached", "manifest_checked", "declarations": "absent"|"valid"|"invalid", "declared": [{"id", "title", "reason", "sites"}], "blocked_by": [ids], "summary": {"imports", "constructors", "tls_callbacks", "component_entry"}, "findings": [{"capability", "title", "kind", "evidence", "strength", "severity" (as this host weighs it), "declared", "blocks"}]}`, or `{"ok": false, "error"}`.

## Component testing

The testing suite for C++ effects and instruments (#163; `engine/src/component_test.*`, measurements in `engine/src/analysis.*`). It loads one component, activates it at a sample rate and block size, feeds it a test signal or notes block by block on the calling thread (no audio device, never the audio thread), and measures the output. Both the C API (`caw_component_test`) and the CLI (`caw test-component`) take the same JSON request and return the same JSON result.

**Ports (2026-11-11).** A plain stereo effect runs through `process`; anything else runs through `processPorts` with every port it declares. The first audio input takes `signal`; later audio inputs (a sidechain) take their signal from `inputs` by port id, or silence. The first event input takes `notes` (MIDI 2.0 note on and off events with note ids, frame-accurate in each block); later event inputs get none. Modulation inputs read 0 and control inputs their port's default; modulation, control and event outputs are given room (1,024 events a block) and the events counted. The first audio output is measured as `output`; later ones are summarised in `ports.other_outputs`. Audio ports may be mono or stereo: a mono input gets the mid of its signal, a mono output is measured on both sides. A component with no audio input (an instrument) is fed only `notes` (one C4 at velocity 100 for 1 s when the request has none); the notes' end is the signal's end, the tail is measured after the last note off against the output's own peak, and `thd`, `latency` and the frequency response, which need a known input, are `null`. A component needs an audio output and an audio or event input; ports of more than two channels are refused.

**Request** (JSON object; an unknown key is an error, so a typo doesn't silently change the test):

| Key | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `component` | required | an id (`"org.caw.distortion"`) or `{"id", "library", "trust", "params": {id: value}}`. A built-in needs only its id. A built library needs `library` (the .so/.dll path) and `trust`: `"trusted"` loads it in-process, `"blocked"` and `"isolated"` are refused as in the Engine; there is no default. Unnamed parameters keep their defaults; values are clamped and stepped as in playback. |
| `signal` | sine | a type name or `{"type", ...}`. Types: `impulse` (one frame), `sine` (`frequency`, default 1000 Hz), `sweep` (exponential, `start_frequency` 20 to `end_frequency` 20000 Hz, held below 0.95 x Nyquist), `white`, `pink` (`seed`, default 1; `decorrelated`: independent left and right), `silence`, `dc`, `wav` (`path`: 16/24/32-bit PCM or 32-bit float, mono or stereo) and `buffer` (`left`, `right` arrays). `seconds` (default 1, at most 120) and `level_db` (dBFS: peak for impulse, sine, sweep and DC, RMS for noise; default 0 for an impulse, -6 otherwise). Signals other than `wav` and `buffer` are the same in both channels unless decorrelated, and are generated with the deterministic maths, so they are the same bits on every device. |
| `sample_rate` | 48000 | 8000 to 192000; a `wav` signal's own rate when left out (a mismatch is an error) |
| `block_size` | 64 | 1 to 8192: the `max_frames` passed to activate and the frames per process call (the last block may be shorter) |
| `tail_seconds` | from the descriptor | silence rendered after the signal: the declared tail and latency plus 0.25 s, or 5 s for an infinite tail; signal and tail together at most 120 s |
| `tempo` | 120 | the transport every block carries (playing, from beat 0) |
| `cpu` | true | time each process call |
| `fft_size` | 8192 | 256 to 65536, power of two, for the spectrum |
| `bands_per_octave` | 6 | resolution of the spectrum and frequency response |
| `compare` | none | a second component (same form as `component`) for a null test. It is loaded in its own registry, so the same id from another library file (another build) works |
| `bypass` | false | also null-test the output against the dry input (A/B against bypass) |
| `align` | `declared` | null-test alignment: `none`, `declared` (each side shifted by its declared latency) or `measured` (by cross-correlation, either side may be later) |
| `sweep` | none | `{"param", "steps", "from", "to"}`: render once more per value, `steps` (1 to 64; default 5, or one per choice of a choice parameter) evenly from `from` to `to` (default: the parameter's range) |
| `write_wav`, `write_input_wav` | none | write the output or the test signal as a 32-bit float stereo WAV, for listening |
| `notes` | none | for the first event input: up to 4,096 `{"pitch", "velocity", "start", "length"}` (MIDI pitch 0 to 127, fractions allowed; velocity 1 to 127, default 100; start and length in seconds, defaults 0 and 0.5). Refused for a component without an event input |
| `inputs` | none | `{port id: signal}` for audio inputs after the first (same form as `signal`, but not a WAV file); an unknown port is an error |
| `checks` | none | pass/fail thresholds: up to 256 `{"path", "min", "max", "equals"}`. `path` names a value of the result by dotted keys and array indices (`output.channels.0.peak_db`, `output.thd.left.thd_percent`, `bypass.depth_db`, `cpu.worst_percent`, `sweep.points.2.channels.1.rms_db`); a number must lie within `min`..`max` (either may be left out) and `equals` compares a boolean or number exactly. A missing or `null` value fails |

**Result** (keys are stable; `schema` is 1). Every result has `ok` and `error`; on failure that is all. A dB value of minus infinity (digital silence) is written as `null`; so is a value that doesn't apply.
- `component`: `id`, `name`, `version`, `role`, `source` (`builtin` or `library`), `latency_frames`, `tail_frames` (a number or `"infinite"`) and `params` (every value used).
- `settings`: `sample_rate`, `block_size`, `tempo`, `signal` (`notes` for an instrument), its level and frequencies, `notes` (how many), `signal_frames` and `render_frames` (signal plus tail).
- `output`:
  - `channels` (left, right): `peak_db`, `rms_db` (dBFS by amplitude: a full-scale sine has RMS -3.01 dB), `dc_offset`, `crest_db` and `gain_db` (output RMS minus input RMS), over the signal's span shifted by the declared latency (an impulse: the whole render); `nan`, `inf` and `denormal` (non-zero subnormal floats) are counted over the whole raw render, before the suite treats non-finite samples as 0 for the other measurements. `failed_blocks` counts process calls that returned false (the input passes through, as in the app).
  - `stereo`: `correlation` (1 identical, 0 unrelated, -1 inverted) and `width` (rms(side) / (rms(mid) + rms(side)): 0 mono, 1 fully out of phase).
  - `thd` (sine only), per channel: `fundamental_db`, `thd_percent`/`thd_db` (harmonics 2 to 10 below Nyquist) and `thd_n_percent`/`thd_n_db` (everything but the fundamental and DC). Measured by a least-squares fit of the known frequency over whole cycles of the steady state (past the declared latency and the signal's first 100 ms or quarter).
  - `impulse_response` (impulse only): `length_frames` and `length_ms`, until the output stays below -90 dB of the input's peak, and `truncated` (still above it at the end of the render).
  - `latency`: `declared`, `measured` (the lag of the largest cross-correlation between input and output, within the first 2^17 frames; `null` for a sine, DC or silence, whose every period matches) and `matches`.
  - `tail`: `declared`, `measured` (frames after the signal ends until the output stays below -90 dB of the input's peak), `measured_ms`, `truncated` and `within_declared` (measured at most declared tail plus declared latency; always true for an infinite tail).
  - `spectrum` (not for silence): `fft_size`, `window` (`hann`), `channel` (`mid`, (L + R) / 2) and `bands`, `[hz, db]` pairs from 20 Hz at `bands_per_octave`, each band's loudest bin, scaled so a full-scale sine on a bin reads 0 dB.
  - `frequency_response` (impulse, sweep and noise): `channel` and `bands`, `[hz, db]` = output power over input power per band, from the FFT of the whole render (first 2^20 frames); bands where the input has less than -60 dB of its loudest band's power are left out. It describes linear, time-invariant effects; for others it is an average.
- `cpu` (with `cpu`): `blocks`, `median_us`, `worst_us`, and both as `median_percent`/`worst_percent` of the block's real-time duration. The only part of a result that varies between runs: with `cpu` off, the same request gives the same JSON byte for byte.
- `bypass` and `compare` (when asked): `align`, `offset_frames` (output frames skipped on the tested side minus the other), `frames` compared, `exact` (every sample equal), `difference_peak_db`, `difference_rms_db` and `depth_db` (difference RMS relative to the tested output's RMS). `compare` also names the other `id`, `source` and `latency_frames`.
- `sweep`: `param` and `points`, each with `value` and the same `output` measurements except spectrum and frequency response, plus its own `warnings`.
- `ports`: how many audio, event, modulation and control inputs and outputs it has, `events_out` (events it emitted), and `other_outputs`: per later audio output, `port`, `channels`, `peak_db`, `rms_db`, `nan` and `inf`.
- `checks` and `passed` (with `checks`): each check with its `path`, the `value` found (`null` when missing), its bounds and `pass`; `passed` is true when every check passed.
- `warnings`: `non_finite`, `denormals`, `clipping` (a sample above 1.0), `failed_blocks`, `latency_mismatch`, `tail_exceeds_declared` and `tail_truncated` (the render ended while a finite tail still sounded).

**CLI:** `caw test-component org.caw.distortion --param drive=8 --report text`, or for a built library `caw test-component ./build/my-echo.so --id local.my-echo --trust trusted --signal impulse`. Options map onto the request (`caw` with no arguments lists them); `--request FILE.json` sends a request file as it is. It prints the JSON result (`--report text`: a few readable lines). It exits 1 when the test fails or a check fails, and with `--strict` also when the result has warnings, so CI can gate a component on it.

## Sample packs

The sample-pack maker (#164; `engine/src/sample_pack.*`) renders one instrument, optionally followed by a chain of stereo effects, offline on the calling thread (no audio device, never the audio thread) over a grid of notes x velocities x parameter steps (x round robins), and writes each cell as a WAV file into one output directory, with a manifest and optionally SFZ files. Each cell gets fresh instances, activated at the request's sample rate and block size; the instrument gets a MIDI 2.0 note on at frame 0 (velocity / 127, note id 1), held for `hold_seconds`, then a note off; the render goes on until, after the off and the chain's declared latency, the output has stayed below `tail_threshold_db` for 0.25 s (or `max_seconds` is reached: `tail_truncated`). The file then ends at the last frame above that threshold. Parameters keep one value per file (no smoothing), and the transport is playing at `tempo` from beat 0. The same request gives the same files byte for byte (the manifest records an FNV-1a hash of each).

**Trust and files.** Trust is never read from the request: the caller passes it (`trust_level` in the C API, `--trust` in the CLI) and it applies to every `library` the request names; only `CAW_NATIVE_TRUST_TRUSTED` loads one (blocked and isolated are refused, as in the Engine), and a `"trust"` key anywhere in the request is an error. Built-ins need no trust. Every file is written directly inside `output_dir` (created if missing, then made canonical): names are sanitised to letters, digits and `. _ - # +` (anything else becomes `_`, no `..`, no leading dot, at most 150 characters, Windows device names prefixed with `_`), a target that is a symbolic link or not a regular file is refused, and an existing file is refused unless `overwrite` is true. All names are checked (and must be unique, ignoring case) before anything renders. Each file is written to `NAME.part` and renamed, so a failure never leaves a half-written file; a cancelled or failed pack keeps the files finished before it and writes no manifest. The engine reads no WAV files here.

**Request** (JSON object; an unknown key is an error):

| Key | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `instrument` | required | an id (`"org.caw.synth.flagship"`) or `{"id", "library", "params": {id: value}}`; it must be an instrument (one event input, one stereo audio output) |
| `effects` | none | up to 16 stereo effects in order, each in the same form |
| `output_dir` | required | the directory every file goes into |
| `name` | `sample` | the pack's name (sanitised): `{name}`, and the manifest `NAME.json` and SFZ `NAME.sfz` |
| `name_pattern` | `{name}_{note}_{vel}_{param}` | tokens `{name}`, `{note}` (C4 = 60, sharps: `F#4`, `C-1`), `{midi}`, `{vel}`, `{param}`, `{index}`, `{rr}` (the round robin take from 1; empty without round robins, and `_rr{rr}` is added at the end when there are takes and the pattern has neither `{rr}` nor `{index}`); the result is sanitised, repeated underscores collapse and trailing `_`/`-` are dropped (so an empty `{param}` leaves no trace); `.wav` is added |
| `notes` | `[60]` | up to 128 notes, MIDI numbers or names (`"C3"`, `"Bb2"`), or `{"from", "to", "step"}` |
| `velocities` | `[100]` | 1..127, a list or `{"from", "to", "steps"}` (rounded) |
| `params` | none | up to 8 axes: `{"id", "values": [...]}` or `{"id", "from", "to", "steps"}` (at most 128 values), with `"effect": n` for `effects[n]`'s parameter (default: the instrument's). Values are clamped and stepped as in playback. The first axis changes slowest, then notes, then velocities |
| `hold_seconds` | 1 | 0.001..60 |
| `max_seconds` | the larger of 10 and hold + 5 (at most 60) | the longest a file may be |
| `tail_threshold_db` | -80 | where the tail ends |
| `sample_rate`, `block_size`, `tempo` | 48000, 64, 120 | 8000..192000, 1..8192, 20..999 |
| `bit_depth` | 24 | 16 or 24 (PCM, rounded and clipped to full scale) or 32 (float) |
| `dither` | `none` | `"tpdf"`: before rounding to 16 or 24 bits, add triangular noise of +-1 LSB, the difference of two uniform values (Lipshitz, Wannamaker and Vanderkooy, "Quantization and Dither: A Theoretical Survey", JAES 40(5), 1992), so the rounding error no longer depends on the signal: a fade or a quiet tail turns into steady noise instead of distortion. Seeded per file (the same request, the same bytes); samples that are exactly 0 stay 0; float files are never dithered |
| `round_robins` | 1 | 1..16 takes of every cell, or `{"count", "preroll_ms": 7, "vary": [{"id", "effect", "amount"}]}`. Take k (from 0) first runs the fresh chain on silence for k x `preroll_ms` (its free-running state moves on; the pre-roll is dropped), and each `vary` parameter (the instrument's, or `effects[effect]`'s) moves by up to +-`amount` from its value, by a deterministic amount per take. The first take is exactly the cell without round robins |
| `channels` | 2 | 1 writes the mid, (L + R) / 2 |
| `trim` | `{"start": true, "end": true, "threshold_db": -80}` | removes leading and trailing frames where every channel is below the threshold; `false` turns both off |
| `fade_in_ms`, `fade_out_ms` | 0 | linear fades after trimming, kept outside a loop |
| `normalize` | `none` | `"peak"`, `"rms"` or `{"mode", "target_db", "scope"}`: target -1 dBFS for peak, -18 dBFS for RMS (over all samples of all channels); scope `file` (each file its own gain) or `group` (one gain for the pack, set by its loudest file, keeping the files' relative levels; costs a measuring pass). For 16/24-bit the gain is held so the peak stays at or below 0 dBFS (`normalize_limited_by_peak`) |
| `loop` | false | `true` or `{"enabled", "min_seconds": 0.05, "max_seconds": half the hold, "crossfade_ms": 0, "crossfade": "equal_power"}`: a forward loop in the held part, as heard after the chain's latency. Its end is the frame before the last rising zero crossing (of the mid) at or before 20 ms (or a tenth of the hold, if less) before the note off; its start is the rising zero crossing, from `max_seconds` before the end to `min_seconds` before it, whose surrounding 64 frames either side best match the end's (both channels; ties keep the longer loop). Written to the `smpl` chunk; `no_loop_found` when there is none. With `crossfade_ms`, the loop's last frames are crossfaded with the frames just before its start (`equal_power`, sin/cos gains for unrelated material, or `linear`), so the wrap continues the sound as it first ran whatever the seam's match; held to the loop's length and to the frames before its start (`loop_crossfade_shortened`) |
| `sfz` | false | also write SFZ: one file per parameter combination (`NAME.sfz`, or `NAME_PARAMS.sfz` with axes) |
| `manifest` | true | write `NAME.json` |
| `max_files` | 256 | the grid may be at most this (at most 4096); a larger grid is refused before rendering |
| `overwrite` | false | replace existing files of the same names |

**Each WAV** has `fmt `, `fact` (float), a `smpl` chunk (MIDI unity note = the rendered note, the sample period, and the loop, if any, as start and inclusive end frames) and `data`.

**SFZ** (plain text, basic opcodes only): one `<region>` per file with `sample=` (the file name, in the same directory), `lokey`/`hikey` (each sampled note covers the keys halfway to its neighbours; the lowest from 0, the highest to 127), `pitch_keycenter`, `lovel`/`hivel` (each velocity layer from the one below it, exclusive, up to its own velocity; the lowest from 1, the highest to 127), `seq_length`/`seq_position` for round robins (the sampler plays the takes in turn), and `loop_mode=loop_continuous loop_start= loop_end=` or `loop_mode=no_loop`.

**Result and manifest.** The result has `ok` and `error` (on failure that is all), `output_dir` (canonical) and everything the manifest holds; the manifest has no absolute paths, so a pack can be moved: `schema` (1), `name`, `instrument` and `effects` (`id`, `source` builtin or library), `settings`, `file_count`, `files` (each: `file`, `note`, `note_name`, `velocity`, `round_robin` (with takes), `params` {id: value used}, `param_effects` (`"instrument"` or an effect index, per axis), `frames`, `seconds`, `trimmed_start_frames`, `peak_db`, `rms_db`, `gain_db`, `loop` (`start`, `end`, `match_db`: the seam's difference energy relative to the signal before any crossfade, lower is better; `crossfade_frames`) or null, `tail_truncated`, `hash` and `warnings`), `sfz` (file names), `manifest` and `warnings` (`tail_truncated`, `failed_blocks`, `no_loop_found`, `silent`, `normalize_limited_by_peak`, `clipped`, `over_full_scale`, `loop_crossfade_shortened`); `settings` also has `round_robins`, `dither` and, with a crossfade, `loop_crossfade_ms` and `loop_crossfade`. A dB of digital silence is `null`.

**CLI:** `caw sample-pack org.caw.synth.flagship --out pack --name lead --notes C2:C5:12 --vel 64,127 --step flt1_cutoff=600,3000 --set env1_release=0.4 --loop --sfz --normalize peak --group --bits 16`. `--request FILE.json` reads a request; the options override its keys. `--library PATH` takes the instrument from a built library and `--trust trusted|isolated|blocked` (default blocked) is the trust passed for every library; `--effect ID` (repeatable) adds built-in effects (library effects go in a request file). `--loop-xfade MS` loops with a crossfaded seam, `--rr N` renders N round robins and `--dither tpdf` dithers 16/24-bit files. `caw` with no arguments lists every option. It prints the result JSON and exits 1 when the pack fails.

**[Fact] Limits, as built:** a loop is found only where the held sound repeats: a detuned two-oscillator patch that beats slower than the hold gets a loop whose seam does not match well (`match_db` about -25 to -28 dB for `org.caw.synth.basic` at its default 7-cent detune, against below -80 dB with one sine oscillator); `loop.crossfade_ms` smooths such a seam. Notes are whole MIDI numbers. Round robins vary only by pre-roll and parameter offsets: an instrument whose state does not run free, given no `vary`, renders the same take each time.

## The Benchmark

The app's built-in stress test (beta plan C; the app calls it **Benchmark**). `src/stress_scenarios.cpp` holds the projects caw_bench's suite plays (moved there unchanged: caw_bench's output is the same, and `stress_test.cpp` checks each builder's project hash against caw_bench's own code at `d932771` and 1 s of each against `references/stress.cawref`), their tiers and two ramp builders; `src/stress_run.cpp` times them; `src/capi_stress.cpp` is the C API. **[Fact]** (`engine/tests/stress_test.cpp`, `stress_rt_test.cpp`, 2026-11-13).

- **Scenarios** (`benchmarkScenarios()`): first the ten ids section H2 names, Normal `first_loop`, `three_tracks`, `song8`, `song8_extras`; Heavy `song16`, `drums_kit`, `flagship_heavy16`; Extreme `flagship_heavy`, `song32_heavy`; Idle `tail_floor`; then the 20 feature scenarios (`featureScenarios()`, API 30, #197), each feature from its best case to its worst: `empty` (idle, baseline), `single_synth`, `single_sampler`, `single_drums` (normal, instrument), `single_audio` (normal, audio), `folders_shallow` (normal) and `folders_deep` (heavy; four chains of 8 nested folders, a test-tone track at the bottom of each: routing), `sends_few` (2 sends, normal), `sends_many` (heavy; 4 tracks each sending to 4 empty buses twice, pre and post: 32 sends) and `sends_chained` (heavy; 16 buses each sending on to the next: sends), `automation_light` (4 tracks, volume and pan lanes) and `automation_dense` (heavy; 16 tracks, 6 lanes each, 64 points a bar: automation), `modulation_dense` (heavy; 16 LFO, step and random modulators each on 8 tracks' cutoffs and faders, 256 links: modulation), `voice_storm` (extreme; a 12-note Flagship chord every 16th held two beats, past its 64 voices: voices), `sample_stream` (heavy; 8 tracks of 12 s clips, every other one stretched) and `resample` (heavy; files at 22.05 to 96 kHz, half re-pitched: samples), `every_instrument` and `every_effect` (heavy; every built-in instrument on its own track, every built-in effect over five Flagship tracks), `metering_heavy` (normal; 48 test-tone tracks under 8 groups: metering) and `worst_all` (extreme; the 20-track heavy song with deep folders, 32 sends, dense lanes, modulation, a voice storm and mixed-rate clips, about 230 components: all). The cheap sources are the engine's internal test signal (`org.caw.test.osc`, a free-running saw) so a feature's own cost shows. Every scenario has a `feature` (baseline, instrument, effect, routing, sends, automation, modulation, voices, samples, audio, metering, mix_pool, all; the ten: `song*` and `first_loop`, `three_tracks` all, `flagship*` and `drums_kit` instrument, `tail_floor` baseline) that the app groups them by. The feature scenarios are not caw_bench's (its suite and CI tables are unchanged) and not H2's. caw_bench's `tail` (three phases) is the suite's only. Each carries `memory_mib`, its peak RSS growth set up and playing on Linux x64 (2026-11-13) with about a third on top, for the app's budget check. The scenarios that write WAVs (`song8_extras`, `single_sampler`, `single_audio`, `sample_stream`, `resample`, `every_instrument`, `worst_all`; `makeChecked`) write them into the caller's folder (Android has no usable temporary folder) and fail, naming the problem, without one or on a failed write (`makeScenario`). Their projects are pinned by hash and their sound by `stress.cawref` (the feature entries appended on 2026-11-13, the first fifteen byte for byte as before).
- **A run** has its own `Engine` (48 kHz, the quality asked) and project, never the caller's; overload shedding is off on it (`Engine::setShedding(false)`: one check where a layer would be shed, and a change resets shedding, so layers already shed come back and the load history is forgotten), so the whole project is measured. Plain, sustained and ramp runs measure the app's figure: the mix pool with `MixPool::defaultWorkers()` on `MixParallel::Auto` (`Engine::useBenchmarkMixPool`: a test's pool stays, and while a live device runs the mix stays on one thread, as offline renders do), each callback's load passed to `Engine::reportLoad` as a device callback's (as `caw_bench --threads auto --mode auto`); a profile stays on one thread. Like caw_bench's suite: set up, 0.25 s stopped to apply, Play, then an untimed warm-up (`warmupSeconds`: 0.5 s, plus Auto's decision and settle, `kMixParallelSeconds + kMixSettleSeconds`, where the pool is used: 1.7 s), then `seconds` of `frames`-frame callbacks (256 by default: the CI bench's) timed one by one. Mean, p99 and worst (a percentage of the callback's budget), callbacks over budget, the worst's time (`worst_at_s`, from Play), the first callback after Play on its own (`first_callback_us`), the first and last tenth's means, the load's least-squares trend (`slope_pct_per_min`, % of the budget a minute), the mix pool's workers and those Auto had taking tasks at the end, and the run thread's core and its clock at the timed span's start and end (`core_khz_start`, `core_khz_end`, `core_max_khz`; -1 where unreadable). These are in the result JSON only: `CawStressResult` and API 29 are unchanged.
  - **Plain** and **sustained** (the same, long; the app's sustained run is live, in real time, the heat test).
  - **Ramps:** tracks (`bigSong(n)`, the typical song's tracks with its buses, master and LFOs; 1 to 128), Flagship voices (`flagshipVoices(n)`, its default patch holding n notes; 1 to 64, its polyphony) and effects on one track (`effectsChain(n)`, a Flagship chord track through EQ, compressor, saturator, chorus, delay, filter, phaser and reverb in turn; 1 to 64). Each step is a fresh engine timed for `seconds`; a step holds when its p99 callback is within the budget (`kHoldP99`). The counts go up until the first that doesn't hold, then halve the gap below it (never above the failed count), so the limit is the last count that held. A count the engine refuses for its own budget (`setProject`: its 256 native components or a memory budget, `rampCapRefusal`) after one that ran ends the ramp there (any other refusal is an error), capped at the last count that held: `stop_reason` `"capped"`, `capped_by` the engine's words, `ok` true, nothing above it tried (`kRampCapped`, `stress_ramp_stops_capped_at_the_engines_component_cap`; #234: the track ramp's 24-and-over songs reach 355 components, and the owner's S22+ run reported that as an error with limit 0). Refused at the first count, it is an error.
  - **Profile:** 1 s untimed (the warm-up and more), then each component's own time (`ChainProfile`, nested chains taken off their parents) and the host's share (all `Engine::process` does outside components), summed per component id; and **the engine parts** (API 30, `engine_parts.h`): `process()`'s time split by part, exclusively (one part is current at a time; a scope switches in and back, so nested parts keep only their own time and every callback's parts add up to its `process()` exactly, the rest of it `other`). `mix`: the mix graph's sums into the master and each track's gain and pan; `routing`: folder buses (with the graph's sum of a folder's children into its input, `claimSinceMark`) and latency compensation; `sends`: sends and keys, from the source's route gains to the destination's sum; `automation`: automation lanes and values resolved for each component (ramps, lanes, project modulation), the modulators and what they hear; `voices`: notes into instruments (pattern events, device MIDI, motion, per-note FX slots); `samples`: the audio-clip player and the sampler (their whole time, so they are not in `devices`); `metering`: peaks, scopes, correlation and loudness; `device_io`: a live run's callback outside `process()` (the FIFO and the input bridge, timed by `DeviceRender::timeProcess` while a live stress meter is installed); `mix_pool`: parking, waking and waiting for workers (near 0 in a profile, which is serial); `master`: the master stage, its chain and the limiter; `devices`: instruments and effects (the per-device `costs` break it down). Each part's `pct` (of the parts' sum), `mean_us` and `p99_us` per callback go in the result's `parts`. Not profiling, each part costs one predictable branch (a null check) and nothing else: no allocation, no lock, no clock read. **[Fact]** (Linux x64 VM, GCC 13 Release, 1 s profiles, 2026-11-13; shares vary run to run by a few points) the largest part: `devices` in every song and instrument scenario (song8 82 %, song32_heavy 88 %, every_effect 95 %, voice_storm 95 %), `routing` in folders_deep (50 %; metering 19 %, mix 13 %), `sends` in sends_many (41 %; mix 16 %), `samples` in sample_stream (55 %) and resample (37 %), `master` in empty (66 % of 4 µs); every table in [engine performance](engine-performance.md#a7-where-the-time-goes-engine-parts-197); test `stress_profile_parts_add_up_and_name_the_feature`. The profile is per Engine (`Engine::setProfile`): `process()` reads it once and hands it to every chain and modulator in its `ChainTime`, so it travels with the work, an engine without one adds nothing to another's on the same thread, and the audio path uses no thread-local storage (Android's emulated TLS can allocate on a thread's first use). A profile is filled by one thread at a time, so a profiling engine's mix stays serial: the pool runs a callback only when `wantsMixPool()` and the profile that call read (`chunkProfile_`) is null, so a profile set or cleared mid-callback never has workers filling it.
  - **Live** (plain and sustained only): a `CawEngine` of its own through the default output device (the low-latency profile at the size asked: 1024 frames is Auto while not recording, the app's playing buffer; 0 is Low latency, AAudio's own burst-sized callbacks), its mix pool on Auto as the app's device has it, output muted (`CawEngine::muteOutput`, zeroed after `process()`), the callback recording each callback's time, load and size into preallocated `CawStressStats` after its timed span. After Play, the same warm-up as the DSP runs' (1.7 s, real time) is untimed, its underruns apart (`warmup_xruns`) and its first callback in `first_callback_us`. The budget is the mean of the callbacks' own and the audio the sum of their frames, so varying callback sizes are measured right; but a callback's own length is not its deadline (that is the frames the device has queued, several callbacks at the playing buffer), so `over_budget` overstates what is heard, and the result says so (`budget_basis: "callback_length"`), and `device_sizes` says the device took its own callback sizes (Low latency; and the 1024 run on Android 11 and older, where a size never reaches the stream, so that run is at the burst). Dropouts are the platform's: AAudio's `AAudioStream_getXRunCount` over the timed span where it exists (**[Fact]** NDK r30 `AAudio.h`: underruns or overruns, API 26+), else the callbacks over budget. The callback's core (`sched_getcpu`, Windows `GetCurrentProcessorNumber`) is read on the first callback and every 64th, since it can be a system call, and its clock (`tuning::coreClock`) at the timed span's start and end on the control thread. Muted output is written as zeros, not scaled. One output per process: the caller stops its own engine's audio first; while a live run has the device, `caw_audio_start` on any other engine is refused, and a run whose device stops calling back for a second fails with that reason.
  - **Cancel** is read at every callback (DSP) or every 100 ms (live), so a run stops within a step. The timed loop allocates nothing (`caw_rt_tests`); the load history is a fixed ring of 8192 points, one per 100 ms of audio.
  - **Live parts:** a live run is not profiled (a profile runs the mix on one thread, which is not what plays): it reports `parts` with `device_io`, `master` (the live output's safety stage, API 35: the mono check and safety limiter, timed apart in the callback; the run's own engine has the limiter on, so the Benchmark always measures it) and the rest of the callback as `other`, and says so (`"parts_basis": "unprofiled"`; profiles say `"profile"`). Its JSON also has `audio_threads`, `caw_audio_threads` at the timed span's end (the performance hint's state, the callback's policy, nice and core; #234).
- **C API:** `caw_stress_scenarios()` (JSON: scenarios with tier, feature and memory, the ramps' counts, `hold_p99`); the run's JSON has `feature` and, profiled or live, `parts`; `caw_worker_pool_submit_stress(pool, CawStressRequest*, priority)` returns a `CawJob` (status, cancel, wait and release as for every job; `caw_job_result` the run's JSON, `caw_job_error` the reason, "cancelled"); `caw_stress_progress` (atomics: permille, ramp step, count, held, load, callbacks, over budget), `caw_stress_history` (the 100 ms load points from an index on), `caw_stress_result` and `caw_stress_costs` once it finished. `threads` must be 0 (the runs size the pool themselves, as above).

### The plug-in benchmark

One component timed offline (API 30, #197; `src/component_bench.cpp`, `src/component_profile.cpp`): `caw_worker_pool_submit_component_bench(pool, CawComponentBenchRequest*)` is a `CawJob` (status with progress 0..1000, cancel, wait, result and release as every job's; `caw_job_error` "cancelled" when cancelled; foreground priority; one bench at a time). It runs a built-in by id, or a built library's component only when the request's trust is `CAW_NATIVE_TRUST_TRUSTED` (the user's own build, or one they chose to trust): any other value is refused before the library is opened, and the loader refuses Blocked and Isolated again (CLAUDE.md: never an untrusted binary). It runs in the app's process (a Trusted plug-in is in-process code; the Isolated runner comes with #212), on a thread of its own that the job's worker watches: a block that doesn't return within max(1 s, 100 times its budget) fails the job ("did not return") and that thread is left behind (detached, never joined), so the worker and the pool go on; until it returns, another bench is refused at once ("never returned... restart the app"), and while one runs, another fails fast ("a benchmark is running"). A C++ exception reaching the bench (not the component: `process()` is `noexcept`, and the SDK's export turns a throw into a failed block) fails the job instead of ending the app; a crash in the plug-in still ends it. Each measurement is a fresh instance on the bench's thread, `seconds` of audio (0.1..10, default 1) timed block by block after 0.05 s untimed, the first twentieth of the timed blocks left out of the figures; flush-to-zero on, as `Engine::process` runs components, except where said:

- **Base:** 48 kHz, 64-frame blocks (the Engine's block), an effect hearing noise and a 220 Hz sine at about −10 dBFS, an instrument a four-note chord every half second (each note 0.4 s). `cost_pct` (mean of the 1,333 µs block), `mean_us`, `p99_us`, `budget_us`; the `CAW_PROFILE_SCOPE` sections recorded here.
- **Checks** (`checks`: `id`, `status` pass/warn/fail, `value`, `detail`, `measured`). A check whose costs all stay under 0.5 % of a block passes as too cheap to matter, except allocations and subnormal output. Each point of the voice, block-size and sample-rate curves is measured three times on fresh instances and the cheapest run kept (2026-11-14, after the gain fixture's block-size grade flipped under a loaded machine), so another process can't flip a grade; every run's allocations and failures still count.
  - `allocations`: allocations and frees in every `process()` call of every measurement, counted by the host's allocation counter (`bench::setAllocationCounter`: `caw_rt_tests` installs its counter of the global `operator new` and `delete`). Pass 0, fail any. Without a counter (the app's build: a library's own allocator can't be counted portably) it says `measured` false (`status` warn, `value` −1), which the app shows as "Not measured in the app (run the test build)", neither a pass nor a warning. A loaded library is also not counted on Windows and macOS, even with a counter: there a DLL or dylib binds its own `operator new`, so the host's replacement never sees its allocations (ELF interposes it on Linux and Android). Found when the Windows CI counted 0 for the fixture that allocates every block.
  - `denormals`: a quarter second of signal, then silence (a tail decaying), timed with flush-to-zero on and off (`ScopedKeepDenormals`, `fp_mode.h`): the percent slower unflushed; warn over 15 %, fail over 50 %; any subnormal output sample unflushed is at least a warning (C.A.W flushes them; other hosts, such as a CLAP export's, may not).
  - `cost_per_voice` (instruments): 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 notes held; the least-squares slope in percent of a block per voice; warn over 1.5, fail over 6 (16 voices over the whole block).
  - `block_size_scaling`: 32 to 512-frame blocks; how much more a frame costs at 32 than at 512; warn over 50 %, fail over 150 %.
  - `sample_rate_scaling`: 44.1, 48 and 96 kHz; the cost of a second of audio at 96 kHz over 48 kHz (2 is linear); warn over 2.4, fail over 3.2.
  - `param_change_cost`: every continuous automatable parameter ramping every block, by plus or minus 5 % of its range around its default (per-frame values, so the change is measured, not another setting); graded by the extra as a share of a block, warn over 2 %, fail over 10 %, so a component with hundreds of parameters isn't failed for their number; the detail also says how much more it costs.
  - `silence_cost`: a fresh instance hearing nothing (no notes), skipped while it says quiet as the Engine skips it; its cost as a share of the base cost; warns over 25 % unless it sleeps.
  - `process_failures` (only when some block returned failure): fail.
- **Curves:** `voices` (instruments), `block_frames` and `sample_rate`, each `[x, % of that block's budget]` (sample rate: of a 64-frame block at that rate). `thresholds` gives the numbers above.
- **Sections:** `CAW_PROFILE_SCOPE("name")` (SDK `caw/profile.hpp`; [SDK](native-module-sdk.md#time-sections-of-your-component)) times named parts of a component's `process()`, through the host extension `caw.profile.v1` (`caw_component.h`): a fixed table of 32 names, filled only on the bench's own thread (the same library playing in a project meanwhile adds nothing), no allocation or lock, recording only while a `profile::Recording` lives (so a throw can't leave it on). Sections are exclusive: a fixed stack of open sections (32 deep) takes each nested section's time off its parent's, so the shares add up to at most 100 %. Each: `name`, `pct` (of the base run's process time, untimed start included), `mean_ns` (its own time per call), `calls`. Outside a bench a section costs a relaxed load and a predictable branch on entry and a branch on exit.

Tests `component_bench_test.cpp` (the test component, Flagship's voices, the bad fixture's subnormals and sections, nested sections exclusive, other threads ignored, refusals, the job and cancel, a `process()` that never returns and one that fails its blocks) and `component_bench_rt_test.cpp` (allocations counted: the fixture `tests/bench_fixture_component.cpp`, which allocates every block and decays through subnormals, fails; the test component and a built-in pass).

## Tests

`engine/build/caw_tests` runs 958 tests, `engine/build/caw_rt_tests` 116 more and `engine/build/caw_exact_tests` 31 (CTest runs all three; counted 2026-11-19):
- **MIDI files:** round trips of the example patterns through `.mid` and `.midi2`, chords, fractions, microtones, exact clip and SMF bytes, hand-built files with running status and tempo changes, and thousands of truncated or corrupted files that must give errors without crashing.
- **Patterns:** the note format and its errors, sample-exact note starts and ends on the test signal, chords, repeatable probability, pattern length, notes released by seed and tempo changes, packed patterns, late-note warnings, pattern edits carrying sounding notes onto their new curve (or ending deleted ones) and the note matcher, and exports checking patterns.
- **Mixer:** (runs through the Engine's mix graph since F4.3) on the test signal (`engine/tests/test_signal.h`): block-size independence, a track removed from inside a folder fading out (issue #76), layer lengths, an instrument swap's crossfade, a value change keeping the instrument playing, a broken pattern keeping the old notes, mute, solo and pan, overload, stop and removal, effects, one graph node per track and the limiter.
- **Exports:** loop lengths, export equal to playback, free-length renders and cancel, one-shots (a C++ instrument's note to its tail, clips' length), wavetables (a C++ instrument's parameter swept), broken patterns and WAV bytes.
- **Test signal:** `test_signal_test.cpp`: each shape's exact samples, seeded noise, the sweep, Notes mode, that it is never listed, and the `test_signal.h` helpers on a track; no allocation in `caw_rt_tests`.
- **Modules and graph:** descriptor validation (layouts, the 64-channel port ceiling, identity version, role, choice parameters), the CP.2 capability flags (the executor refuses a modulation link, automation lane or CC mapping a parameter does not allow), the processor contract and its stereo adapter, graph compile errors and budgets, and the planar N-channel executor: distinct generated signals per channel for mono, stereo, quad, 5.1, 7.1 and Discrete(3), channel isolation, fan-in summing order (summing inputs), typed ports (sidechain, modulation, control, and event merge order, overflow and order checks), parameters (resolution, modulation links, automation lanes against the Engine's lane values, MIDI CC mappings), audio-thread re-routing (`setRouting`: validation, rejection keeps the old routing, unlisted nodes skipped, no allocation), host channel-count mismatches, processor failure reporting, re-prepare/reset, runtime publication, and no allocation or free in `process()`. These real-time tests are in `caw_rt_tests`, whose `engine/tests/alloc_counter.cpp` replaces the global `operator new`/`delete` to count calls; keeping it out of `caw_tests` keeps AddressSanitizer's new/delete mismatch checks there.
- **Flagship synth:** `flagship_test.cpp`: the unison layout (detune curve, mirror pans, blend levels, stacks), the comb, formant, multimode ladder and EQ kernels on noise, `Wavetable::levelData`, the descriptors (sections, curves, the matrix's destination list), unison pitches against the detune and its power, spread 0 exactly mono and wider at 1, each stack's intervals, each filter type on a saw (and series, parallel, bypass), the matrix moving a level, a pan and a cutoff, the same bits at blocks of 64, 128, 512 and 37 with every part on, finite output and silence after release (quiet), a bend, and a note above Nyquist staying silent; its reference render, perceptual reference and timing tests are with the other built-ins'.
- **Instruments:** `library_exact_test.cpp` (in `caw_exact_tests`): the Library's oscillators, fast maths, filters, envelopes, glide and drum voices; `builtin_instruments_test.cpp`: every built-in instrument listed with its name, starting on its frame and falling silent, the hats' open, closed and choke, the kit's key map, the mono synths' glide and legato, the morph synth's stereo spread and a reference render each; `instrument_test.cpp`: the project fields, the UMP note encoding, the bounded note list, notes reaching a recording fixture instrument on their exact frames across loop wraps and clip loops, slides, previews (ids, glides, notes off), Stop and Play, values, bypass and lanes, state, a rebuild taking over held notes, .caw effects after an instrument, wrong-shaped components refused, the built-in synth starting a note mid-block and its reference render, a bent note playing as loud as a plain one in live playback on the built-in synths, a sounding bent note following a pattern edit (a moved curve point; a moved, transposed and shortened note; a deleted note and one moved off the playhead ending) on the recorder, Morph and Flagship, and the C API; `engine_rt_test.cpp` plays, swaps, previews and stops instruments, the built-in ones included, and edits a pattern under sounding bent notes, without an allocation.
- **Pre-load scan:** `scan_test.cpp`: SHA-256 against the FIPS vectors; hand-built ELF64 and PE32+ files (`scan_fixtures.h`) with chosen imports, exports, ordinals, code, sections, TLS callbacks and strings, each finding and its capability; declarations (valid, every kind of invalid, two sections, the canonical form round trip); the policy (blocked and named, declared, legacy grace, plug-ins); every truncation of an ELF and a PE fixture, 6,000 seeded random corruptions and huge counts and offsets (no crash, malformed blocks); many findings of one kind never hide another. `scan_load_test.cpp`: the engine's built test components and the bass starter (built with `caw_add_component`, so it carries an empty section) pass and load; `caw_test_declared` (`engine/tests/fixtures/declared/`) carries exactly `engine/tests/references/capabilities.canonical`, which the manifest reader also gives; `libcaw` parses and shows `load_code` and threads; the loader refuses a blocked file with error code 11 before opening it and the grace lets it through; the cache by hash; tampering; the C API JSON. `scan_bypass_test.cpp` (the security review of PR #254, one test per fix, each failing without it): the dynamic table read at its address, non-system dependencies and `$ORIGIN`, `syscall()`, `svc` with any immediate and in the rounded pages, x86-64 system calls outside `.text` and `int 0x80`, overlapping segments, imports reached only by Android packed relocations, PE layouts Windows maps differently, ordinals, writes through an opened file, `clone`, `_r_debug`, ntdll, Windows thread entry points, only regular files read, and (Linux) the loader loading the descriptor it scanned. `scan_syscall_test.cpp`: hand-built AArch64 ELF call sites — the futex number is a notice, a disallowed number, a non-constant argument and `syscall`'s address taken each block.
- **Native SDK:** compiled components cross the plain-C interface v3 in `engine/include/caw_component.h` (installed with the SDK; `tests/component_header_check.c` compiles it as C99); `CAW_EXPORT_COMPONENT` in `engine/include/caw/component.hpp` builds it from a C++ `ModuleProcessor`. The C++ contract is `engine/include/caw/module.hpp`, exposed through the installable `CAW::SDK` package, which also checks with `static_assert` that its enums and event record match the C ones. `ModuleRegistry` and the Engine C API load explicit-trust shared libraries on Windows and POSIX through that one entry point, copy and validate the descriptor and stable ID, refuse a library without it (`MissingEntryPoint`), another interface version (`IncompatibleAbi`) or a missing function (`IncompleteTable`), and keep the library loaded while instances live. Tests: a fixture component, and a second build of it without the entry point. With `-DCAW_CLAP=ON`, `caw_clap_tests` (8 tests) loads the exported `.clap` plug-ins as a CLAP host and checks the factory, descriptors, ports, parameters (info, text, flush, sample-accurate changes and smoothing), state round trips, notes and MIDI in and out, the transport and zero allocations in process, against the same components run through `ModuleRegistry`, sample for sample. With `-DCAW_VST3=ON`, `caw_vst3_tests` (8 tests) loads the same components as `.vst3` bundles as a VST3 host and checks the factory, class ids and categories, buses and arrangements, parameters (info, normalized mapping and steps, text, sample-accurate queue points, a 0-frame flush), state (the CLAP blob, a load while active, the controller's copy), notes in and out with ids, the transport and zero allocations in process, sample for sample against `ModuleRegistry`.
- **Drums:** `drums_test.cpp`: the descriptor (64 × 162 grouped parameters, the 808 kit in bank A), every layer and effect on its own (sounds, ends, says quiet), Kit and Single mode, the note map (Auto, a note, Off, Any other, the first-pad note, channels), pitch tracking, the 808 bass's mono slide, choke groups (no click) and polyphony, pressure damping a cymbal, per-hit variation (deterministic at any block size, restarting at Play, another seed another sequence), block-size independence and sleeping with kits and the room, the quality modes, every preset (pitch, decay and centroid against reference ranges; the kits on a busy pattern), reference renders, randomize (a random kick stays in range and level-matched, a mutation moves a little), info and explode, the WAV decoder (formats, resampling, truncation, lying headers, random corruption) and the sample slots (load, swap, empty, bad references, a project's `samples`), the old drums' migration, the display data (with note learn's last note), the pitch shape's points (the drop as played and as drawn; velocity to pitch drop), the genre kicks' targets (808 long against short, the dubstep kick's 50..60 Hz and crest factor, every kick's pitch falling), velocity changing the spectrum and per-hit strike position, the sub staying mono under width, every drive type, the pad and kit clippers' ceiling and the kit EQ, the room as a send, and pressure to pitch and to choke. `the_old_drums_migrate_close_to_how_they_sounded` (`builtin_instruments_test.cpp`) compares each old drum's reference pattern with its pre-migration render; `drums_never_allocate_on_the_audio_thread` (`engine_rt_test.cpp`) plays a kit with every layer, effects and a sample while knobs, the quality and the sample change.
- **Mixer reference renders:** twelve multi-track projects in `engine/tests/mix_reference_test.cpp`, the test signal on patterns (pan, a folder bus, nested folders, mute and solo together and each alone, folder and track automation, the limiter driven through a bus and shown to engage, effect chains, short patterns, built-in effects on tracks and the master), and three chain swaps while playing, rendered in uneven blocks and compared with their reference renders (below); a second engine still renders the same bits. A companion test shows a muted or non-soloed track contributes exactly nothing: the mix equals the same project without it.
- **Component testing:** every measurement against known signals (a sine's level, crest and THD near zero, a known 1 % + 0.5 % harmonic mix, FFT bins and the Hann scaling, IR end and cross-correlation lag), the signals (levels, seeded noise, decorrelation), the fixture's echo IR length (3,361 frames) and denormals, its 100-frame delay measured against the declared latency with every broadband signal, exact nulls of identical components and of two loads of one library, gain changes in dB, parameter sweeps, the built-in effects at an odd block size with every signal, a tail longer than declared, refusals (no trust, blocked, unknown keys and parameters), a WAV round trip, the C API, the same JSON for the same request, an instrument played a note list (its tail after the last off), the ducker fed a sidechain and key notes, and thresholds that pass, fail and miss.
- **Sample packs:** note names both ways, the sanitiser (separators, `..`, leading dots, device names, length) and name patterns, trim, fades, a loop on whole cycles of a sine, the `smpl` chunk and samples read back at 16, 24 and 32 bits, SFZ key and velocity ranges, a real pack of `org.caw.synth.basic` (8 files, non-silent, looped, group peak, the same bytes in another directory and with `overwrite`), effects with an effect-parameter axis, mono float and RMS normalisation, refusals (a library without trust, a `trust` key, unknown parameters and keys, not an instrument, `max_files`, clashing names, an existing file, a symlinked target), names that try to leave the directory, the C API's trust, progress and cancel, a loop crossfade on a ramp (linear and equal power, held to the room before the loop), TPDF dither of a quarter-LSB level (its mean, its bound, silence kept, the same bytes), and three round robins of Flagship (different takes, the first equal to the plain render, SFZ `seq_position`).
- **Creative and Pitch & Time effects:** `fx_creative_test.cpp`: the eight registered with tapered knobs and 3 to 8 in-range presets each, the same bits at block sizes 1, 37, 64 and 500 (stopped and playing from an off-grid beat), finite and bounded under random knobs on loud music, a square and DC (the ring mod with a key), quiet after silence and awake on input, their display layouts, a reference render each at defaults and busy settings (`references/fx_creative.cawref`); the tape stop passing its input bit for bit, its pitch halving mid-stop, silence, a spin-up and the input again exactly, and a synced stop; the reverser's chunk coming back reversed one chunk late and its level kept across seams; the trance gate's steps, swing and song lock; the stutter's slice repeats (exact), chance 0 dry, seeded patterns, pitched repeats and hold; the bitcrush transparent at 24 bits and 48 kHz, 3 bits' levels, 1 kHz holds and the anti-image filter's effect; the pitch shifter's octaves and fifth and its reported latency (the dry delayed by exactly it); the frequency shifter's sidebands (over 50 dB apart at 1 kHz, 45 dB at 40 Hz and 15 kHz) in each direction; the ring mod's sum and difference, bias, the key as carrier and High quality's oversampling.
- **Benchmark:** `stress_test.cpp`: every builder's project unchanged by the move out of caw_bench (a hash of each field, recorded from caw_bench's code) and 1 s of each against its reference, the ten ids and their tiers, the ramp's search (the limit, only the first failing count above it, the cap, a stop), the ramp projects, a real ramp, cancel within one step (ramp and plain), callbacks and the 100 ms history, the history ring, two engines profiling on two threads at once with their own rows, a profile staying with its engine on a shared thread, the live callback record (frames summed, the core sampled), shedding switched off and switching it off bringing shed layers back, a profiling engine keeping its mix serial with a pool on Always; `stress_rt_test.cpp`: the timed loop allocates nothing.
- **Reference renders** (tolerance tests, owner 2026-11-12, [decisions](decisions.md); `engine/tests/tolerance.h`, `tolerance.cpp`): 156 renders (the Benchmark's 15 scenario and builder projects, 1 s each, recorded 2026-11-13 from the moved builders, whose renders were bit-identical to caw_bench's own; the mixer's 12 and its 3 chain swaps, the 8 built-in instruments, the Basic preset, 7 drum presets, the audio clips and sampler, and every effect at its defaults and busy settings: the effects suite 24, the stereo tools and ducker 12, fx-creative 16, fx-dynamics 17, fx-filters 18, fx-space 22) are compared with references recorded from the code the golden hashes pinned (2026-11-12, `c9197fb`; every hash matched when they were recorded). They are in `engine/tests/references/<suite>.cawref`, 23.7 MB in all (every render's whole waveform): binary (`*.cawref binary` in `.gitattributes`), little-endian on every platform, read with `rb`, each sample rounded to 2^-21 (at most −132.4 dBFS off), coded losslessly from there with per-block fixed or linear prediction (integer coefficients, so decoding is exact everywhere) and Rice codes; a render identical to an earlier one in its suite is stored once. A reference depends on nothing the test regenerates.
  - **Stable** (137 cases): the peak of render − reference over the whole render stays below −120 dBFS. A note, clip or automation event one frame off, another seeded choice (a stutter, a round robin, seeded noise), a polarity flip or a duck in another place moves some samples by far more than that, so in these cases timing and seeded choices are held exactly.
  - **Chaotic** (19 cases: four effect cases and the Benchmark's 15 whole songs): what a listener hears. Short-term loudness (RMS of 50 ms windows) and the same of the first difference (where clicks and brightness show), per channel, within 0.1 dB; the mean power below 20 Hz (DC included, so an offset or a lost DC blocker shows) and of each 1/3-octave band from 25 Hz to 20 kHz (8192-point Hann windows, half overlapping) within 0.5 dB; levels more than 60 dB below the reference's loudest window or band (or below −120 dBFS) count as that floor; the same onsets (the first frame above −60 dBFS after 10 ms at or below −80 dBFS), each within 2 frames: a level grown by a float step can cross the threshold a frame apart. So in a chaotic case timing is held to 2 frames and seeded choices only as far as they change loudness, spectrum or onsets. **[Assumption]** 0.5 dB a band is below what a listener notices: just-noticeable level differences are usually given as about 0.5 to 1 dB (not measured here); 0.1 dB of loudness is the owner's bar ([decisions](decisions.md)). Every probe build below moved the features by at most 0.033 dB of loudness and 0.0003 dB a band.
  - **Discrete** results (event frames, chosen indices) compare exactly (`compareExact`).
  - **Classification** (2026-11-12): three probes against the references. Built with `-mfma -ffp-contract=fast`, and with `-ffast-math -fno-finite-math-only -mfma`: every case within −129.7 dBFS. Every input sample of the effect suites moved one float step: four high-gain non-linear cases amplify it to within 6 dB of the limit (fx-dynamics' phase distortion busy −117.3 dBFS and default −125.9 dBFS, the distortion busy −123.3 dBFS, the transient shaper busy −122.9 dBFS), so they are chaotic; the rest stay at or below −128.9 dBFS. No feedback, self-resonant or free-running case diverged in any probe. The class of a case is in its test (`effectClass`, `dynamicsClass`, `instrumentClass`, `mixClass`), and a reference recorded as another class fails.
  - **The comparators** (`tolerance_test.cpp`): the codec round-trips music, a full-scale square, values over full scale and silence to the same steps and refuses a truncated stream; the stable comparison accepts a few float steps everywhere and rejects a 0.5 dB and a 0.01 dB gain change, a 4-sample click at −40 dBFS, a filter at 1300 Hz in place of 1200 Hz, a render one frame late, a frame short and a NaN, and a single sample 1.01e-6 off; the chaotic comparison accepts three partials at other phases (which the stable one rejects) and rejects 0.5 dB and 0.2 dB gain changes and a 4-sample click on a reverb, and the wrong cutoff, a three-frame shift and the click on a filter (a one-frame shift is within its onset slack). The reference files: a suite recorded and read back accepts its renders and rejects a changed one, another class, an unknown name and another length; a file cut anywhere, one with a byte too many or another magic is refused whole, and a stream whose residual would overflow does not decode. A stored reference that no case compares fails the suite, and a suite whose test failed while recording is not written.
  - **Re-recording:** `caw_tests --update-references` (`--update-golden` still works), unsharded, only for an intended sound change; say why in the commit. `CAW_REFERENCE_REPORT=1 caw_tests <name>` prints each comparison's measurements (`REF` lines: residual dBFS, largest loudness and band differences).

The suite passes under GCC 13, Clang 18, and GCC with AddressSanitizer and UBSan (`-DCAW_SANITIZE=ON`).
